# RRB NTPC CBT-1 General Awareness Question Bank

**Question count:** 1000 original PYQ-style MCQs

## Provenance and use

This bank follows the topics and question styles represented in the RRB NTPC CBT-1 syllabus and publicly listed past papers. Questions below are original practice questions; they are not claimed as verbatim official PYQs or reproduced paid question-bank content.

### Q001 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Brihadeshwara Temple at Thanjavur was built by which Chola king?

A. Rajendra Chola I
B. Rajaraja Chola I
C. Vijayalaya Chola
D. Parantaka Chola

**Answer:** B. Rajaraja Chola I
**Explanation:** The Brihadeshwara Temple (also called Peruvudaiyar Kovil) was built by Rajaraja Chola I in 1010 AD. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Brihadeshwara = Rajaraja; Gangaikonda Cholapuram = Rajendra.

### Q002 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

In which year was the Gandhi-Irwin Pact signed?

A. 1929
B. 1931
C. 1932
D. 1935

**Answer:** B. 1931
**Explanation:** The Gandhi-Irwin Pact was signed on 5 March 1931 between Mahatma Gandhi and Lord Irwin (Viceroy). It led to the suspension of the Civil Disobedience Movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** Gandhi-Irwin = 1931; Gandhi started CDM in 1930 (Salt March).

### Q003 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who introduced the "Subsidiary Alliance" system in British India?

A. Lord Dalhousie
B. Lord Wellesley
C. Lord Curzon
D. Warren Hastings

**Answer:** B. Lord Wellesley
**Explanation:** Lord Wellesley introduced the Subsidiary Alliance system in 1798 to bring Indian states under British control without direct annexation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Wellesley = Subsidiary Alliance; Dalhousie = Doctrine of Lapse.

### Q004 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The All India Forward Bloc was founded by whom?

A. Jawaharlal Nehru
B. Subhas Chandra Bose
C. Rash Behari Bose
D. Vallabhbhai Patel

**Answer:** B. Subhas Chandra Bose
**Explanation:** Subhas Chandra Bose founded the All India Forward Bloc in 1939 after his differences with the Congress leadership.
**Shortcut / rule:** Forward Bloc = Bose (1939); INA = Bose (1943).

### Q005 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the first Muslim President of the Indian National Congress?

A. Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
B. Badruddin Tyabji
C. Muhammad Ali Jinnah
D. Hakim Ajmal Khan

**Answer:** B. Badruddin Tyabji
**Explanation:** Badruddin Tyabji presided over the 3rd session of INC at Madras in 1887, making him the first Muslim president of the Congress.
**Shortcut / rule:** First INC President = W.C. Bonnerjee (1885); First Muslim = Badruddin Tyabji (1887).

### Q006 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The famous Kailash Temple at Ellora (Cave 16) was built under which dynasty?

A. Chalukya Dynasty
B. Rashtrakuta Dynasty
C. Pallava Dynasty
D. Chola Dynasty

**Answer:** B. Rashtrakuta Dynasty
**Explanation:** The Kailash Temple at Ellora was built by King Krishna I of the Rashtrakuta Dynasty in the 8th century. It is carved out of a single rock.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ellora = Rashtrakuta; Elephanta = Rashtrakuta; Ajanta = Vakataka/Satavahana.

### Q007 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who wrote the book 'Panchatantra'?

A. Kalidasa
B. Vishnu Sharma
C. Chanakya
D. Banabhatta

**Answer:** B. Vishnu Sharma
**Explanation:** Panchatantra is a collection of animal fables written by Vishnu Sharma around 200 BC. It is one of the most translated literary works in history.
**Shortcut / rule:** Panchatantra = Vishnu Sharma; Arthashastra = Chanakya; Meghaduta = Kalidasa.

### Q008 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Mahatma Gandhi returned to India from South Africa in which year?

A. 1905
B. 1910
C. 1915
D. 1920

**Answer:** C. 1915
**Explanation:** Gandhi returned to India on 9 January 1915. This date is now celebrated as Pravasi Bharatiya Divas.
**Shortcut / rule:** Gandhi returned 1915; Champaran Satyagraha 1917 (his first in India).

### Q009 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Battle of Plassey was fought in which year?

A. 1757
B. 1764
C. 1799
D. 1857

**Answer:** A. 1757
**Explanation:** The Battle of Plassey was fought on 23 June 1757 between the British East India Company (Robert Clive) and Siraj ud-Daulah, Nawab of Bengal.
**Shortcut / rule:** Plassey = 1757; Buxar = 1764; Seringapatam = 1799.

### Q010 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Swadeshi Movement was launched in response to which event?

A. Jallianwala Bagh Massacre
B. Partition of Bengal
C. Rowlatt Act
D. Simon Commission

**Answer:** B. Partition of Bengal
**Explanation:** The Swadeshi Movement (1905) was launched in protest against Lord Curzon's decision to partition Bengal.
**Shortcut / rule:** Partition of Bengal (1905) = Swadeshi; Rowlatt Act (1919) = Satyagraha.

### Q011 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the founder of the Maurya Empire?

A. Ashoka
B. Bindusara
C. Chandragupta Maurya
D. Samudragupta

**Answer:** C. Chandragupta Maurya
**Explanation:** Chandragupta Maurya founded the Maurya Empire around 321 BC with the guidance of Chanakya (Kautilya).
**Shortcut / rule:** Maurya founder = Chandragupta; Gupta founder = Sri Gupta; Mughal founder = Babur.

### Q012 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

In which session of INC was the demand for "Purna Swaraj" (Complete Independence) declared?

A. Calcutta Session 1928
B. Lahore Session 1929
C. Karachi Session 1931
D. Lucknow Session 1916

**Answer:** B. Lahore Session 1929
**Explanation:** The demand for Purna Swaraj was declared at the Lahore Session of INC in December 1929, presided by Jawaharlal Nehru. 26 January 1930 was celebrated as the first Independence Day.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lahore 1929 = Purna Swaraj; Karachi 1931 = Fundamental Rights resolution.

### Q013 · Indian & World Geography

The Sundarbans are chiefly associated with which river delta?

A. Mahanadi delta
B. Godavari delta
C. Ganga-Brahmaputra delta
D. Krishna delta

**Answer:** C. Ganga-Brahmaputra delta
**Explanation:** The Sundarbans is the world's largest mangrove forest, located in the Ganga-Brahmaputra delta across India (West Bengal) and Bangladesh.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sundarbans = Ganga-Brahmaputra; Royal Bengal Tiger habitat.

### Q014 · Indian & World Geography

The capital city of Australia is:

A. Sydney
B. Melbourne
C. Canberra
D. Perth

**Answer:** C. Canberra
**Explanation:** Canberra is the capital of Australia. Sydney and Melbourne are the largest cities, but Canberra was purpose-built as the capital.
**Shortcut / rule:** Australia capital ≠ Sydney (common trap); Canberra was a compromise between Sydney and Melbourne.

### Q015 · Indian & World Geography

Which region in India is famous for the cultivation of Saffron?

A. Brahmaputra Valley
B. Karewas of Kashmir
C. Doon Valley
D. Chota Nagpur Plateau

**Answer:** B. Karewas of Kashmir
**Explanation:** Saffron (Kesar) is primarily cultivated in the Karewa plateau of Kashmir, especially in Pampore. India is the 3rd largest producer of saffron.
**Shortcut / rule:** Saffron = Kashmir (Pampore); Tea = Assam/Darjeeling; Coffee = Karnataka (Coorg).

### Q016 · Indian & World Geography

Buenos Aires is the capital of which country?

A. Brazil
B. Chile
C. Argentina
D. Peru

**Answer:** C. Argentina
**Explanation:** Buenos Aires is the capital and largest city of Argentina.
**Shortcut / rule:** Argentina = Buenos Aires; Brazil = Brasilia (not Rio); Chile = Santiago.

### Q017 · Indian & World Geography

The Tropic of Cancer does NOT pass through which Indian state?

A. Rajasthan
B. Madhya Pradesh
C. Bihar
D. West Bengal

**Answer:** C. Bihar
**Explanation:** The Tropic of Cancer passes through 8 Indian states: Gujarat, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tripura, and Mizoram. Bihar is not among them.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mnemonic: "Go Right, My Child, Just Watch TV and Movies" (Gujarat, Rajasthan, MP, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, WB, Tripura, Mizoram).

### Q018 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the longest river in the world?

A. Amazon
B. Nile
C. Yangtze
D. Mississippi

**Answer:** B. Nile
**Explanation:** The Nile (6,650 km) is the longest river in the world, flowing through northeastern Africa. The Amazon is the largest by volume.
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest = Nile; Largest (volume) = Amazon; Longest in India = Ganga.

### Q019 · Indian & World Geography

Which plateau in India is known as the "mineral storehouse" or "Ruhr of India"?

A. Deccan Plateau
B. Malwa Plateau
C. Chota Nagpur Plateau
D. Meghalaya Plateau

**Answer:** C. Chota Nagpur Plateau
**Explanation:** The Chota Nagpur Plateau in Jharkhand is called the "Ruhr of India" due to its rich mineral resources including coal, iron ore, mica, and bauxite.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chota Nagpur = minerals + Damodar Valley; Deccan = basalt/lava soil.

### Q020 · Indian & World Geography

Which ocean current keeps the western coast of Europe warmer than expected for its latitude?

A. Labrador Current
B. Gulf Stream
C. Canary Current
D. Benguela Current

**Answer:** B. Gulf Stream
**Explanation:** The Gulf Stream (and its extension, the North Atlantic Drift) is a warm ocean current that carries heat from the Gulf of Mexico to Western Europe, keeping it warmer.
**Shortcut / rule:** Warm currents = Gulf Stream, Kuroshio; Cold currents = Labrador, Benguela.

### Q021 · Indian Polity & Governance

Which Article of the Indian Constitution deals with the President of India?

A. Article 36
B. Article 52
C. Article 74
D. Article 61

**Answer:** B. Article 52
**Explanation:** Article 52 states "There shall be a President of India." Articles 52-62 deal with the President's office, election, powers, and impeachment.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 52 = President; Art 74 = PM & Council of Ministers; Art 76 = Attorney General.

### Q022 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of Directive Principles of State Policy was borrowed from the constitution of which country?

A. USA
B. UK
C. Ireland
D. Canada

**Answer:** C. Ireland
**Explanation:** The DPSP in Part IV of the Indian Constitution were borrowed from the Irish Constitution. They are not justiciable but fundamental in governance.
**Shortcut / rule:** DPSP = Ireland; Fundamental Rights = USA; Parliamentary system = UK; Emergency = Germany.

### Q023 · Indian Polity & Governance

Which Constitutional Amendment Act is known as the "Mini Constitution"?

A. 42nd Amendment
B. 44th Amendment
C. 73rd Amendment
D. 86th Amendment

**Answer:** A. 42nd Amendment
**Explanation:** The 42nd Amendment (1976), enacted during the Emergency, made sweeping changes including adding "Socialist", "Secular" and "Integrity" to the Preamble, and Fundamental Duties.
**Shortcut / rule:** 42nd = Mini Constitution (1976); 44th = Undid many 42nd changes (1978).

### Q024 · Indian Polity & Governance

What is the minimum age to contest elections for the Lok Sabha?

A. 21 years
B. 25 years
C. 30 years
D. 35 years

**Answer:** B. 25 years
**Explanation:** The minimum age to contest Lok Sabha and State Legislative Assembly elections is 25 years. For Rajya Sabha and State Legislative Council, it is 30 years.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lok Sabha = 25 yrs; Rajya Sabha = 30 yrs; President = 35 yrs.

### Q025 · Indian Polity & Governance

Part IV-A of the Indian Constitution deals with:

A. Fundamental Rights
B. Directive Principles
C. Fundamental Duties
D. Union Territories

**Answer:** C. Fundamental Duties
**Explanation:** Part IV-A (Article 51A) contains the Fundamental Duties of citizens. It was added by the 42nd Amendment (1976) based on the Swaran Singh Committee recommendation. Originally 10 duties, 11th added by the 86th Amendment.
**Shortcut / rule:** Part III = FR; Part IV = DPSP; Part IV-A = FD (42nd Amendment).

### Q026 · Indian Polity & Governance

What is the maximum permissible gap between two sessions of Parliament?

A. 3 months
B. 6 months
C. 9 months
D. 12 months

**Answer:** B. 6 months
**Explanation:** Article 85 mandates that the gap between two sessions of Parliament should not exceed 6 months.
**Shortcut / rule:** Parliament sessions gap ≤ 6 months; Rajya Sabha is a permanent body (never dissolved).

### Q027 · Indian Polity & Governance

Who appoints the Chief Justice of India?

A. Prime Minister
B. President of India
C. Law Minister
D. Vice President

**Answer:** B. President of India
**Explanation:** Under Article 124, the Chief Justice and other judges of the Supreme Court are appointed by the President. By convention, the senior-most judge of the SC is appointed as CJI.
**Shortcut / rule:** All constitutional appointments = President; PM only "advises" President.

### Q028 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Right to Education (Article 21A) was added by which Amendment?

A. 73rd Amendment
B. 86th Amendment
C. 42nd Amendment
D. 91st Amendment

**Answer:** B. 86th Amendment
**Explanation:** The 86th Amendment (2002) inserted Article 21A making free and compulsory education for children aged 6-14 a Fundamental Right.
**Shortcut / rule:** 86th Amendment = Right to Education (Art 21A) + 11th Fundamental Duty.

### Q029 · Indian Economy

Which organization calculates the GDP of India?

A. Reserve Bank of India
B. National Statistical Office (NSO)
C. NITI Aayog
D. Finance Ministry

**Answer:** B. National Statistical Office (NSO)
**Explanation:** The National Statistical Office (earlier Central Statistics Office, CSO) under the Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation calculates India's GDP.
**Shortcut / rule:** GDP = NSO; Monetary Policy = RBI; Fiscal Policy = Finance Ministry.

### Q030 · Indian Economy

What is the full form of GST?

A. General Sales Tax
B. Goods and Services Tax
C. Government Service Tax
D. Gross Service Turnover

**Answer:** B. Goods and Services Tax
**Explanation:** GST (Goods and Services Tax) was implemented in India on 1 July 2017 through the 101st Constitutional Amendment. It replaced multiple indirect taxes.
**Shortcut / rule:** GST launched 1 July 2017; 101st Amendment; "One Nation, One Tax."

### Q031 · Indian Economy

SEBI (Securities and Exchange Board of India) was established in which year?

A. 1982
B. 1988
C. 1992
D. 1995

**Answer:** B. 1988
**Explanation:** SEBI was established on 12 April 1988 and was given statutory powers through the SEBI Act of 1992. Its headquarters is in Mumbai.
**Shortcut / rule:** SEBI established = 1988; Statutory = 1992; HQ = Mumbai.

### Q032 · Indian Economy

Deen Dayal Upadhyaya Gram Jyoti Yojana (DDUGJY) is related to which sector?

A. Rural Health
B. Rural Electrification
C. Rural Roads
D. Rural Education

**Answer:** B. Rural Electrification
**Explanation:** DDUGJY was launched in 2015 for rural electrification, including feeder separation and strengthening of sub-transmission and distribution infrastructure.
**Shortcut / rule:** DDUGJY = Rural Electricity; PMGSY = Rural Roads; PMJAY = Health.

### Q033 · Indian Economy

The Reserve Bank of India was established in which year?

A. 1935
B. 1947
C. 1949
D. 1950

**Answer:** A. 1935
**Explanation:** RBI was established on 1 April 1935 based on the recommendations of the Hilton Young Commission (1926). It was nationalized in 1949.
**Shortcut / rule:** RBI = 1935 (established), 1949 (nationalized); First Governor = Osborne Smith.

### Q034 · Indian Economy

Which Five Year Plan adopted the Mahalanobis Model focused on heavy industrialization?

A. First Five Year Plan
B. Second Five Year Plan
C. Third Five Year Plan
D. Fourth Five Year Plan

**Answer:** B. Second Five Year Plan
**Explanation:** The Second Five Year Plan (1956-61) was based on the Mahalanobis Model with focus on rapid industrialization and development of heavy industries.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Plan = Agriculture (Harrod-Domar); 2nd Plan = Industry (Mahalanobis); 3rd Plan = Agriculture + Industry.

### Q035 · Physics

What is the SI unit of Power?

A. Joule
B. Pascal
C. Watt
D. Newton

**Answer:** C. Watt
**Explanation:** The SI unit of Power is Watt (W). Power = Work/Time. 1 Watt = 1 Joule/second.
**Shortcut / rule:** Power = Watt; Energy = Joule; Force = Newton; Pressure = Pascal.

### Q036 · Physics

Newton's First Law of Motion is also known as:

A. Law of Acceleration
B. Law of Inertia
C. Law of Action-Reaction
D. Law of Gravitation

**Answer:** B. Law of Inertia
**Explanation:** Newton's First Law states that a body at rest stays at rest and a body in motion stays in motion unless acted upon by an external force. This property is called inertia.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Law = Inertia; 2nd Law = F=ma; 3rd Law = Action-Reaction.

### Q037 · Physics

What is the SI unit of Electric Current?

A. Volt
B. Ohm
C. Ampere
D. Coulomb

**Answer:** C. Ampere
**Explanation:** The SI unit of Electric Current is Ampere (A). Current = Charge/Time. 1 Ampere = 1 Coulomb/second.
**Shortcut / rule:** Current = Ampere; Voltage = Volt; Resistance = Ohm; Charge = Coulomb.

### Q038 · Physics

Which instrument is used to measure atmospheric pressure?

A. Thermometer
B. Barometer
C. Hygrometer
D. Ammeter

**Answer:** B. Barometer
**Explanation:** A barometer is used to measure atmospheric pressure. The mercury barometer was invented by Evangelista Torricelli in 1643.
**Shortcut / rule:** Barometer = pressure; Hygrometer = humidity; Thermometer = temperature; Ammeter = current.

### Q039 · Physics

The speed of light in vacuum is approximately:

A. 3 × 10⁶ m/s
B. 3 × 10⁸ m/s
C. 3 × 10¹⁰ m/s
D. 3 × 10⁴ m/s

**Answer:** B. 3 × 10⁸ m/s
**Explanation:** The speed of light in vacuum is approximately 3 × 10⁸ m/s (299,792,458 m/s exactly). It is denoted by 'c'.
**Shortcut / rule:** Speed of light = 3 × 10⁸ m/s; Speed of sound in air ≈ 343 m/s.

### Q040 · Physics

Which colour of visible light has the longest wavelength?

A. Violet
B. Blue
C. Green
D. Red

**Answer:** D. Red
**Explanation:** Red light has the longest wavelength (~620-750 nm) in the visible spectrum. Violet has the shortest wavelength (~380-450 nm).
**Shortcut / rule:** VIBGYOR: Violet (shortest λ, highest f) → Red (longest λ, lowest f).

### Q041 · Chemistry

What is the chemical formula of "Laughing Gas"?

A. NO
B. NO₂
C. N₂O
D. N₂O₅

**Answer:** C. N₂O
**Explanation:** Nitrous Oxide (N₂O) is commonly called "Laughing Gas." It is used as an anesthetic in dentistry and surgery.
**Shortcut / rule:** Laughing Gas = N₂O; Tear Gas = Chloroacetophenone; Marsh Gas = CH₄.

### Q042 · Chemistry

What is the chemical name of Baking Soda?

A. Sodium Carbonate
B. Sodium Bicarbonate
C. Sodium Chloride
D. Sodium Hydroxide

**Answer:** B. Sodium Bicarbonate
**Explanation:** Baking Soda is Sodium Bicarbonate (NaHCO₃). It is used in cooking, fire extinguishers, and as an antacid.
**Shortcut / rule:** Baking Soda = NaHCO₃; Washing Soda = Na₂CO₃·10H₂O; Caustic Soda = NaOH.

### Q043 · Chemistry

What is the chemical formula of Washing Soda?

A. NaOH
B. NaHCO₃
C. Na₂CO₃·10H₂O
D. CaCO₃

**Answer:** C. Na₂CO₃·10H₂O
**Explanation:** Washing Soda is Sodium Carbonate Decahydrate (Na₂CO₃·10H₂O). It is used in the glass industry, in soap making, and as a water softener.
**Shortcut / rule:** Washing Soda = Na₂CO₃·10H₂O (10 water molecules); Plaster of Paris = CaSO₄·½H₂O.

### Q044 · Chemistry

Rusting of iron requires which two substances?

A. Carbon dioxide and water
B. Oxygen and water
C. Nitrogen and water
D. Oxygen and carbon dioxide

**Answer:** B. Oxygen and water
**Explanation:** Rusting is the corrosion of iron, forming iron oxide (Fe₂O₃·xH₂O). It requires both oxygen and moisture (water).
**Shortcut / rule:** Rusting = O₂ + H₂O; prevented by galvanization (zinc coating), painting, or alloying.

### Q045 · Chemistry

Which gas is most abundant in Earth's atmosphere?

A. Oxygen
B. Carbon dioxide
C. Nitrogen
D. Argon

**Answer:** C. Nitrogen
**Explanation:** Nitrogen makes up approximately 78% of Earth's atmosphere, followed by Oxygen at 21%, and Argon at 0.93%.
**Shortcut / rule:** N₂ = 78%; O₂ = 21%; Ar = 0.93%; CO₂ = 0.04%.

### Q046 · Chemistry

The atomic number of Oxygen is:

A. 6
B. 7
C. 8
D. 9

**Answer:** C. 8
**Explanation:** Oxygen has atomic number 8. It has 8 protons and 8 electrons. Its atomic mass is approximately 16.
**Shortcut / rule:** C=6, N=7, O=8, F=9 — sequential in periodic table (Period 2).

### Q047 · Biology / Life Science

Which vitamin is synthesized in the human skin when exposed to sunlight?

A. Vitamin A
B. Vitamin B
C. Vitamin C
D. Vitamin D

**Answer:** D. Vitamin D
**Explanation:** Vitamin D (the "sunshine vitamin") is synthesized in the skin upon exposure to ultraviolet B (UVB) rays from sunlight. Its deficiency causes Rickets in children and Osteomalacia in adults.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sunlight vitamin = D; Night blindness = Vitamin A deficiency; Scurvy = Vitamin C deficiency.

### Q048 · Biology / Life Science

Bile juice is secreted by which organ?

A. Pancreas
B. Stomach
C. Liver
D. Small Intestine

**Answer:** C. Liver
**Explanation:** Bile juice is secreted by the Liver and stored in the Gallbladder. It helps in the emulsification (digestion) of fats.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bile = Liver (stored in Gallbladder); Insulin = Pancreas; HCl = Stomach.

### Q049 · Biology / Life Science

Deficiency of Vitamin D causes which disease in children?

A. Scurvy
B. Rickets
C. Beri-beri
D. Pellagra

**Answer:** B. Rickets
**Explanation:** Vitamin D deficiency causes Rickets in children (soft and weak bones) and Osteomalacia in adults.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rickets = Vit D; Scurvy = Vit C; Beri-beri = Vit B1; Pellagra = Vit B3 (Niacin).

### Q050 · Biology / Life Science

Which instrument is used to measure the purity of milk?

A. Hydrometer
B. Lactometer
C. Thermometer
D. Barometer

**Answer:** B. Lactometer
**Explanation:** A Lactometer is used to check the purity of milk by measuring its specific gravity/density. Pure milk has a specific gravity between 1.028 and 1.032.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lactometer = milk purity; Hydrometer = liquid density; Sphygmomanometer = blood pressure.

### Q051 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The traditional folk dance "Kalbeliya" belongs to which Indian state?

A. Gujarat
B. Punjab
C. Rajasthan
D. Haryana

**Answer:** C. Rajasthan
**Explanation:** Kalbeliya is a folk dance of the Kalbeliya tribe of Rajasthan. It was inscribed on UNESCO's Intangible Cultural Heritage list in 2010.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kalbeliya/Ghoomar = Rajasthan; Bhangra = Punjab; Garba = Gujarat; Bihu = Assam.

### Q052 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Khajuraho temples, famous for their Nagara-style architecture, are located in which state?

A. Uttar Pradesh
B. Madhya Pradesh
C. Rajasthan
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** B. Madhya Pradesh
**Explanation:** The Khajuraho Group of Monuments is located in Chhatarpur district, Madhya Pradesh. Built by the Chandela dynasty (950-1050 AD), they are a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Khajuraho = Chandela (MP); Konark Sun Temple = Eastern Ganga (Odisha).

### Q053 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

'Meghaduta' was written by which ancient Indian poet?

A. Vishnu Sharma
B. Kalidasa
C. Tulsidas
D. Valmiki

**Answer:** B. Kalidasa
**Explanation:** Meghaduta (The Cloud Messenger) is a lyric poem written by Kalidasa, one of the greatest Sanskrit poets. He also wrote Shakuntala and Raghuvamsha.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kalidasa = Meghaduta, Shakuntala; Valmiki = Ramayana; Vyasa = Mahabharata.

### Q054 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Ajanta Caves are famous for which type of art?

A. Rock-cut sculptures only
B. Paintings and frescoes
C. Metal engravings
D. Terracotta art

**Answer:** B. Paintings and frescoes
**Explanation:** The Ajanta Caves in Aurangabad, Maharashtra contain Buddhist paintings and sculptures from the 2nd century BC to 6th century AD. They are renowned for their exquisite murals.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ajanta = Paintings (Buddhist); Ellora = Sculptures (Hindu+Buddhist+Jain).

### Q055 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Classical dance form 'Kathakali' originates from which state?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Andhra Pradesh
C. Kerala
D. Karnataka

**Answer:** C. Kerala
**Explanation:** Kathakali is a classical dance-drama from Kerala known for elaborate costumes, face paint, and stories from Hindu epics.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kathakali = Kerala; Bharatanatyam = Tamil Nadu; Kuchipudi = Andhra Pradesh; Odissi = Odisha.

### Q056 · Games and Sports

Who was the first Indian woman to win an Olympic medal?

A. P.T. Usha
B. Saina Nehwal
C. Karnam Malleswari
D. Mary Kom

**Answer:** C. Karnam Malleswari
**Explanation:** Karnam Malleswari won a Bronze medal in Weightlifting (69 kg category) at the Sydney Olympics 2000, becoming the first Indian woman Olympic medalist.
**Shortcut / rule:** First Indian woman Olympic medal = Karnam Malleswari (2000); First Indian Olympic gold = Abhinav Bindra (2008).

### Q057 · Games and Sports

The Aga Khan Cup is associated with which sport?

A. Football
B. Hockey
C. Cricket
D. Tennis

**Answer:** B. Hockey
**Explanation:** The Aga Khan Cup is an international hockey tournament. It was first held in 1895 in Dhaka (now Bangladesh).
**Shortcut / rule:** Aga Khan Cup = Hockey; Davis Cup = Tennis; Thomas Cup = Badminton; Durand Cup = Football.

### Q058 · Games and Sports

The Olympic Games were originally held in ancient times at:

A. Athens, Greece
B. Olympia, Greece
C. Rome, Italy
D. Sparta, Greece

**Answer:** B. Olympia, Greece
**Explanation:** The ancient Olympic Games originated in Olympia, Greece around 776 BC. The modern Olympics were revived by Baron Pierre de Coubertin in 1896 at Athens.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ancient Olympics = Olympia; Modern Olympics revival = Athens 1896; Coubertin = founder.

### Q059 · Games and Sports

National Sports Day in India is celebrated on the birth anniversary of which sportsperson?

A. Sachin Tendulkar
B. Milkha Singh
C. Major Dhyan Chand
D. Kapil Dev

**Answer:** C. Major Dhyan Chand
**Explanation:** National Sports Day is celebrated on 29 August, the birthday of hockey legend Major Dhyan Chand. The highest sporting honor, the Khel Ratna, was renamed after him in 2021.
**Shortcut / rule:** 29 Aug = National Sports Day = Dhyan Chand; Khel Ratna = Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna Award.

### Q060 · Games and Sports

LBW, Googly, and Silly Point are terms associated with which sport?

A. Football
B. Hockey
C. Cricket
D. Badminton

**Answer:** C. Cricket
**Explanation:** LBW (Leg Before Wicket), Googly (a deceptive spin delivery), and Silly Point (a fielding position) are all cricket-specific terms.
**Shortcut / rule:** Cricket terms: LBW, Googly, Yorker, Duck; Football: Penalty, Offside; Hockey: Bully, Penalty corner.

### Q061 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

India's first engine-less semi-high-speed train is called:

A. Tejas Express
B. Vande Bharat Express
C. Gatimaan Express
D. Maharaja Express

**Answer:** B. Vande Bharat Express
**Explanation:** Vande Bharat Express (Train 18) is India's first semi-high-speed train. The first service ran between New Delhi and Varanasi in February 2019.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vande Bharat = semi-high-speed (engine-less); Gatimaan = fastest conventional train.

### Q062 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Which scheme was launched to replace incandescent bulbs with energy-efficient LED bulbs?

A. Saubhagya Scheme
B. UJALA Scheme
C. Kusum Scheme
D. Ujjwala Scheme

**Answer:** B. UJALA Scheme
**Explanation:** UJALA (Unnat Jyoti by Affordable LEDs for All) was launched on 5 January 2015 to promote energy-efficient LED bulbs at subsidized prices.
**Shortcut / rule:** UJALA = LED bulbs; Ujjwala = LPG connections; Saubhagya = household electrification.

### Q063 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

ASHA in the context of Indian health stands for:

A. Accredited Social Health Activist
B. Association of Social Health Agencies
C. All-India Social Health Alliance
D. Auxiliary Social Health Agent

**Answer:** A. Accredited Social Health Activist
**Explanation:** ASHA workers are community health workers under the National Rural Health Mission (NRHM). They serve as the first point of contact for healthcare in rural areas.
**Shortcut / rule:** ASHA = grassroots health worker; ANM = Auxiliary Nurse Midwife; AWW = Anganwadi Worker (ICDS).

### Q064 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

ISRO's Mars Orbiter Mission (Mangalyaan) was launched in which year?

A. 2012
B. 2013
C. 2014
D. 2015

**Answer:** B. 2013
**Explanation:** Mangalyaan was launched on 5 November 2013 and entered Mars orbit on 24 September 2014. India became the first country to reach Mars orbit on its maiden attempt.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mangalyaan launched = 2013; Mars orbit = 2014; Chandrayaan-3 = 2023 (Moon landing).

### Q065 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's first nuclear power plant was established at:

A. Kalpakkam
B. Tarapur
C. Narora
D. Kudankulam

**Answer:** B. Tarapur
**Explanation:** Tarapur Atomic Power Station (TAPS) in Maharashtra was India's first nuclear power plant, commissioned in 1969.
**Shortcut / rule:** First nuclear plant = Tarapur (1969); BARC = Mumbai; Nuclear tests = Pokhran (1974, 1998).

### Q066 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

Who is known as the "Father of the Indian Space Programme"?

A. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
B. Vikram Sarabhai
C. Homi J. Bhabha
D. Satish Dhawan

**Answer:** B. Vikram Sarabhai
**Explanation:** Dr. Vikram Sarabhai is called the Father of the Indian Space Programme. He established the Indian National Committee for Space Research (INCOSPAR) in 1962, which later became ISRO.
**Shortcut / rule:** Space = Vikram Sarabhai; Nuclear = Homi Bhabha; Missile = A.P.J. Abdul Kalam.

### Q067 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

Chandrayaan-3 successfully landed on the Moon in which year?

A. 2019
B. 2021
C. 2023
D. 2024

**Answer:** C. 2023
**Explanation:** Chandrayaan-3 successfully soft-landed on the Moon's south pole on 23 August 2023, making India the 4th country to land on the Moon and the 1st to land near the south pole.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chandrayaan-1 = 2008 (orbiter); Chandrayaan-2 = 2019 (crash); Chandrayaan-3 = 2023 (success).

### Q068 · UN & World Organizations

The International Labour Organization (ILO) was established in which year?

A. 1919
B. 1945
C. 1950
D. 1920

**Answer:** A. 1919
**Explanation:** The ILO was established on 11 April 1919 as part of the Treaty of Versailles. Its headquarters is in Geneva, Switzerland.
**Shortcut / rule:** ILO = 1919 (Geneva); UN = 1945 (New York); WHO = 1948 (Geneva).

### Q069 · UN & World Organizations

The headquarters of the United Nations is located in:

A. Geneva
B. Vienna
C. New York
D. The Hague

**Answer:** C. New York
**Explanation:** The United Nations headquarters is in New York City, USA. The UN was established on 24 October 1945 with 51 founding members.
**Shortcut / rule:** UN = New York; ICJ = The Hague; WHO/ILO = Geneva; IAEA = Vienna.

### Q070 · UN & World Organizations

Which organization publishes the Human Development Index (HDI)?

A. World Bank
B. UNDP
C. IMF
D. WHO

**Answer:** B. UNDP
**Explanation:** The Human Development Index is published by the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) in its annual Human Development Report since 1990.
**Shortcut / rule:** HDI = UNDP; World Development Report = World Bank; Global Hunger Index = IFPRI.

### Q071 · UN & World Organizations

The World Health Organization (WHO) was established in which year?

A. 1945
B. 1946
C. 1948
D. 1950

**Answer:** C. 1948
**Explanation:** WHO was established on 7 April 1948. World Health Day is celebrated on 7 April each year. Its headquarters is in Geneva, Switzerland.
**Shortcut / rule:** WHO = 7 April 1948 (Geneva); UNESCO = 1945 (Paris); UNICEF = 1946 (New York).

### Q072 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Montreal Protocol is related to:

A. Climate change
B. Ozone layer depletion
C. Biodiversity conservation
D. Desertification

**Answer:** B. Ozone layer depletion
**Explanation:** The Montreal Protocol (1987) aims to phase out the production of ozone-depleting substances like CFCs. It is considered the most successful environmental treaty.
**Shortcut / rule:** Montreal = Ozone; Kyoto = Greenhouse gases; Ramsar = Wetlands; CITES = Wildlife trade.

### Q073 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Kaziranga National Park, famous for the One-horned Rhinoceros, is located in which state?

A. West Bengal
B. Assam
C. Meghalaya
D. Arunachal Pradesh

**Answer:** B. Assam
**Explanation:** Kaziranga National Park in Assam is a UNESCO World Heritage Site (1985) and home to two-thirds of the world's Great Indian One-horned Rhinoceros population.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kaziranga = Rhino (Assam); Jim Corbett = Tiger (Uttarakhand); Gir = Lion (Gujarat).

### Q074 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Chipko Movement for forest conservation originated in which state?

A. Himachal Pradesh
B. Uttarakhand
C. Jharkhand
D. Madhya Pradesh

**Answer:** B. Uttarakhand
**Explanation:** The Chipko Movement started in 1973 in Chamoli district of Uttarakhand (then UP). Gaura Devi and Sunderlal Bahuguna were key figures. "Chipko" means "to hug/embrace" trees.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chipko = Uttarakhand (trees); Appiko = Karnataka (forests); Narmada Bachao = Gujarat/MP (dam).

### Q075 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

What does BOD stand for in environmental science?

A. Biological Output Demand
B. Biochemical Oxygen Demand
C. Basic Oxygen Deficit
D. Biological Organic Decomposition

**Answer:** B. Biochemical Oxygen Demand
**Explanation:** BOD measures the amount of dissolved oxygen consumed by biological organisms to decompose organic matter in water. Higher BOD = more polluted water.
**Shortcut / rule:** High BOD = polluted; Low BOD = clean; COD (Chemical Oxygen Demand) is always ≥ BOD.

### Q076 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

World Environment Day is celebrated on:

A. 22 April
B. 5 June
C. 21 March
D. 16 September

**Answer:** B. 5 June
**Explanation:** World Environment Day is celebrated on 5 June every year since 1973. It was established by the UNGA at the 1972 Stockholm Conference.
**Shortcut / rule:** 5 June = Environment Day; 22 April = Earth Day; 22 March = World Water Day; 16 Sept = Ozone Day.

### Q077 · Computers & Applications

Who is known as the "Father of Computers"?

A. Alan Turing
B. Charles Babbage
C. Bill Gates
D. Tim Berners-Lee

**Answer:** B. Charles Babbage
**Explanation:** Charles Babbage is called the Father of Computers for designing the Analytical Engine in 1837, the first general-purpose mechanical computer concept.
**Shortcut / rule:** Babbage = Father of Computer; Ada Lovelace = First Programmer; Turing = Father of AI.

### Q078 · Computers & Applications

CPU stands for:

A. Central Processing Unit
B. Computer Personal Unit
C. Central Program Utility
D. Computer Processing Unit

**Answer:** A. Central Processing Unit
**Explanation:** The CPU is the brain of the computer. It performs all calculations and logical operations. It has two main components: ALU (Arithmetic Logic Unit) and CU (Control Unit).
**Shortcut / rule:** CPU = ALU + CU + Registers; GPU = Graphics Processing Unit.

### Q079 · Computers & Applications

Which memory is considered volatile?

A. ROM
B. Hard Disk
C. RAM
D. SSD

**Answer:** C. RAM
**Explanation:** RAM (Random Access Memory) is volatile — it loses all data when power is turned off. ROM, Hard Disk, and SSD are non-volatile (persistent) storage.
**Shortcut / rule:** Volatile = RAM; Non-volatile = ROM, HDD, SSD, Flash drive.

### Q080 · Computers & Applications

HTTP stands for:

A. HyperText Transfer Protocol
B. High Transfer Text Protocol
C. HyperText Transmission Program
D. High Text Transfer Process

**Answer:** A. HyperText Transfer Protocol
**Explanation:** HTTP is the protocol used for transmitting web pages over the Internet. HTTPS is the secure version using SSL/TLS encryption.
**Shortcut / rule:** HTTP = web protocol; FTP = file transfer; SMTP = email sending; TCP/IP = internet backbone.

### Q081 · Computers & Applications

The extension of a default Microsoft Word file (2007+) is:

A. .doc
B. .docx
C. .pdf
D. .txt

**Answer:** B. .docx
**Explanation:** Since Microsoft Office 2007, Word files use the .docx format (based on XML). Older versions used .doc format.
**Shortcut / rule:** Word = .docx; Excel = .xlsx; PowerPoint = .pptx; PDF = Adobe.

### Q082 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What is the full form of ISRO?

A. Indian Space Research Organisation
B. International Space Research Organisation
C. Indian Satellite Research Organisation
D. Indian Scientific Research Organisation

**Answer:** A. Indian Space Research Organisation
**Explanation:** ISRO was established on 15 August 1969. Its headquarters is in Bengaluru. It is under the Department of Space (DOS).
**Shortcut / rule:** ISRO = Space (Bengaluru); DRDO = Defence (New Delhi); BARC = Atomic (Mumbai).

### Q083 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The first Metro Rail in India was started in which city?

A. Delhi
B. Mumbai
C. Kolkata
D. Chennai

**Answer:** C. Kolkata
**Explanation:** Kolkata Metro (now Line 1) was the first underground metro railway in India, opened on 24 October 1984.
**Shortcut / rule:** First Metro = Kolkata (1984); Longest Metro network = Delhi Metro.

### Q084 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What is the full form of UNESCO?

A. United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization
B. United Nations Economic, Social and Cultural Organization
C. Universal National Education, Science and Culture Organization
D. United Nations Environmental and Social Committee Organization

**Answer:** A. United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization
**Explanation:** UNESCO was established on 16 November 1945. Its headquarters is in Paris, France. It designates World Heritage Sites.
**Shortcut / rule:** UNESCO = Paris; UNICEF = New York; WHO = Geneva; FAO = Rome.

### Q085 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

NH-44 is the longest National Highway in India connecting:

A. Mumbai to Chennai
B. Srinagar to Kanyakumari
C. Delhi to Kolkata
D. Porbandar to Silchar

**Answer:** B. Srinagar to Kanyakumari
**Explanation:** NH-44 (previously NH-1A, NH-1, NH-2, NH-3, NH-7) is India's longest national highway at approximately 3,745 km, connecting Srinagar (J&K) to Kanyakumari (Tamil Nadu).
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest NH = NH-44 (Srinagar-Kanyakumari); Shortest NH = NH-47A (6 km, Kerala).

### Q086 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana (PMJDY) was launched in which year?

A. 2013
B. 2014
C. 2015
D. 2016

**Answer:** B. 2014
**Explanation:** PMJDY was launched on 28 August 2014 for financial inclusion. It aimed to provide every household with a bank account, along with RuPay debit card and ₹1 lakh accident insurance.
**Shortcut / rule:** PMJDY = 2014 (financial inclusion); Mudra = 2015 (micro loans); Stand-up India = 2016 (SC/ST/women entrepreneurs).

### Q087 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Swachh Bharat Abhiyan was launched on:

A. 15 August 2014
B. 2 October 2014
C. 26 January 2015
D. 5 June 2015

**Answer:** B. 2 October 2014
**Explanation:** Swachh Bharat Mission was launched on 2 October 2014 (Gandhi Jayanti) with the goal of making India open-defecation free by 2 October 2019 (Gandhi's 150th birth anniversary).
**Shortcut / rule:** Swachh Bharat = 2 Oct 2014 (Gandhi Jayanti); Target = ODF by 2019.

### Q088 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

"Make in India" initiative was launched in which year?

A. 2013
B. 2014
C. 2015
D. 2016

**Answer:** B. 2014
**Explanation:** Make in India was launched on 25 September 2014 to encourage manufacturing in India. Its logo is the Ashoka Chakra inspired lion.
**Shortcut / rule:** Make in India = Sept 2014 (manufacturing); Digital India = July 2015; Startup India = Jan 2016.

### Q089 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana provides:

A. Free electricity connections
B. Free LPG connections
C. Free LED bulbs
D. Free solar panels

**Answer:** B. Free LPG connections
**Explanation:** PM Ujjwala Yojana was launched on 1 May 2016 to provide free LPG connections to women from Below Poverty Line (BPL) households to reduce indoor air pollution.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ujjwala = LPG; UJALA = LED; Saubhagya = electricity; Kusum = solar pumps.

### Q090 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Jallianwala Bagh Massacre took place in which year?

A. 1917
B. 1919
C. 1920
D. 1922

**Answer:** B. 1919
**Explanation:** The Jallianwala Bagh Massacre occurred on 13 April 1919 in Amritsar, when General Dyer ordered troops to fire on a peaceful gathering, killing hundreds.
**Shortcut / rule:** Jallianwala Bagh = 13 April 1919; Rowlatt Act = March 1919; Hunter Commission investigated it.

### Q091 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the Viceroy of India at the time of the Quit India Movement (1942)?

A. Lord Mountbatten
B. Lord Linlithgow
C. Lord Wavell
D. Lord Irwin

**Answer:** B. Lord Linlithgow
**Explanation:** Lord Linlithgow was the Viceroy when the Quit India Movement was launched on 8 August 1942. He served the longest term as Viceroy (1936-1943).
**Shortcut / rule:** Quit India 1942 = Linlithgow; Partition 1947 = Mountbatten; CDM 1930 = Irwin.

### Q092 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the largest desert in the world?

A. Sahara Desert
B. Arabian Desert
C. Antarctic Desert
D. Gobi Desert

**Answer:** C. Antarctic Desert
**Explanation:** The Antarctic Desert is the largest desert (14.2 million km²) followed by the Arctic Desert. The Sahara is the largest hot desert (9.2 million km²).
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest desert = Antarctic (cold); Largest hot desert = Sahara; Largest in India = Thar.

### Q093 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Preamble of the Indian Constitution starts with which words?

A. "In the name of God"
B. "We, the People of India"
C. "The Republic of India"
D. "India, that is Bharat"

**Answer:** B. "We, the People of India"
**Explanation:** The Preamble begins with "We, the People of India" indicating that ultimate sovereignty lies with the people. It declares India as a Sovereign, Socialist, Secular, Democratic Republic.
**Shortcut / rule:** "Socialist" and "Secular" were added by the 42nd Amendment (1976).

### Q094 · Indian Economy

NITI Aayog replaced which body?

A. Reserve Bank of India
B. Planning Commission
C. Finance Commission
D. National Development Council

**Answer:** B. Planning Commission
**Explanation:** NITI Aayog (National Institution for Transforming India) replaced the Planning Commission on 1 January 2015. The PM is its chairperson.
**Shortcut / rule:** Planning Commission (1950-2014) → NITI Aayog (2015); Chairman PM; Vice Chairman is CEO equivalent.

### Q095 · Physics

A convex mirror is commonly used in:

A. Telescopes
B. Headlights
C. Rear-view mirrors of vehicles
D. Solar cookers

**Answer:** C. Rear-view mirrors of vehicles
**Explanation:** Convex mirrors are used as rear-view mirrors because they provide a wider field of view (diverging mirror). They always form virtual, erect, and diminished images.
**Shortcut / rule:** Convex mirror = rear view (wider view); Concave mirror = headlights, shaving mirrors; Convex lens = magnifying glass.

### Q096 · Chemistry

pH value of pure water at 25°C is:

A. 0
B. 5
C. 7
D. 14

**Answer:** C. 7
**Explanation:** Pure water is neutral with a pH of 7. Acids have pH < 7 and bases have pH > 7. The pH scale ranges from 0 to 14.
**Shortcut / rule:** pH 7 = neutral; < 7 = acidic; > 7 = basic/alkaline; Stomach HCl ≈ pH 1-2.

### Q097 · Biology / Life Science

The largest organ of the human body is:

A. Liver
B. Brain
C. Skin
D. Heart

**Answer:** C. Skin
**Explanation:** The skin is the largest organ of the human body by surface area (about 1.5-2 m²). The liver is the largest internal organ.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest organ = Skin; Largest internal organ = Liver; Largest gland = Liver; Smallest bone = Stapes (ear).

### Q098 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who is known as the "Missile Man of India"?

A. Vikram Sarabhai
B. Homi Bhabha
C. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
D. C.V. Raman

**Answer:** C. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
**Explanation:** Dr. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam is known as the "Missile Man of India" for his work on ballistic missile and launch vehicle technology. He served as the 11th President of India (2002-2007).
**Shortcut / rule:** Missile Man = Kalam; Father of Nuclear = Bhabha; Father of Space = Sarabhai; Raman Effect = C.V. Raman.

### Q099 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Revolt of 1857 started from which place?

A. Delhi
B. Lucknow
C. Kanpur
D. Meerut

**Answer:** D. Meerut
**Explanation:** The Revolt of 1857 (also called the First War of Independence) started on 10 May 1857 at Meerut when Indian sepoys refused to use the new Enfield rifle cartridges.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1857 started = Meerut; Mangal Pandey = Barrackpore (29 March 1857); Last Mughal Emperor = Bahadur Shah Zafar led Delhi revolt.

### Q100 · Indian & World Geography

The Siachen Glacier is located in:

A. Himachal Pradesh
B. Uttarakhand
C. Ladakh (Karakoram Range)
D. Sikkim

**Answer:** C. Ladakh (Karakoram Range)
**Explanation:** The Siachen Glacier is located in the eastern Karakoram Range in Ladakh. It is the world's highest battlefield at over 5,400 m. It is the second longest glacier in the non-polar world.
**Shortcut / rule:** Siachen = Karakoram (Ladakh); Gangotri = Uttarakhand (source of Ganga).

### Q101 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Simon Commission visited India in which year?

A. 1927
B. 1928
C. 1930
D. 1932

**Answer:** B. 1928
**Explanation:** The Simon Commission arrived in India on 3 February 1928 to study constitutional reforms. It was boycotted because it had no Indian members. Lala Lajpat Rai was fatally injured during protests against it.
**Shortcut / rule:** Simon Commission = 1928; "Simon Go Back"; Lala Lajpat Rai = Lion of Punjab (died 1928).

### Q102 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who gave the slogan "Do or Die" during the Quit India Movement?

A. Subhas Chandra Bose
B. Jawaharlal Nehru
C. Mahatma Gandhi
D. Sardar Patel

**Answer:** C. Mahatma Gandhi
**Explanation:** Mahatma Gandhi gave the famous call "Do or Die" (Karo ya Maro) on 8 August 1942 at the Gowalia Tank Maidan, Mumbai, launching the Quit India Movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** "Do or Die" = Gandhi (1942); "Give me blood, I'll give you freedom" = Bose; "Jai Hind" = Bose.

### Q103 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The first session of the Indian National Congress was held in which city?

A. Calcutta
B. Bombay
C. Madras
D. Allahabad

**Answer:** B. Bombay
**Explanation:** The first session of INC was held at Gokuldas Tejpal Sanskrit College, Bombay on 28 December 1885, presided by W.C. Bonnerjee. 72 delegates attended.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st INC session = Bombay (1885); President = W.C. Bonnerjee; Founded by A.O. Hume.

### Q104 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Dandi March was undertaken to protest against which law?

A. Rowlatt Act
B. Salt Tax
C. Land Revenue Act
D. Press Act

**Answer:** B. Salt Tax
**Explanation:** The Dandi March (Salt March) was led by Gandhi from Sabarmati Ashram to Dandi (12 March – 6 April 1930) to protest the British monopoly on salt and salt tax.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dandi March = 1930 (Salt Tax, CDM); 240 miles; 24 days; 78 followers.

### Q105 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the highest peak in India?

A. Nanda Devi
B. Kanchenjunga
C. K2 (Godwin-Austen)
D. Mount Everest

**Answer:** C. K2 (Godwin-Austen)
**Explanation:** K2 (8,611 m) in the Karakoram Range (PoK) is the highest peak in India. Kanchenjunga (8,586 m) is the highest peak entirely within India's administered territory.
**Shortcut / rule:** K2 = highest in India (disputed); Kanchenjunga = highest within Indian territory; Everest = world's highest.

### Q106 · Indian & World Geography

Which soil type is best suited for cotton cultivation in India?

A. Alluvial soil
B. Red soil
C. Black soil (Regur)
D. Laterite soil

**Answer:** C. Black soil (Regur)
**Explanation:** Black soil (Regur soil) is best for cotton cultivation. It is found mainly in the Deccan Plateau (Maharashtra, Gujarat, MP). It is self-ploughing and retains moisture well.
**Shortcut / rule:** Black soil = Cotton; Alluvial = Rice/Wheat; Red soil = Groundnut; Laterite = Tea/Coffee.

### Q107 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian state has the longest coastline?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Andhra Pradesh
C. Gujarat
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** C. Gujarat
**Explanation:** Gujarat has the longest coastline among Indian states at approximately 1,600 km, followed by Andhra Pradesh. Among UTs, Andaman & Nicobar Islands has the longest coastline.
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest coastline state = Gujarat; Longest among UTs = Andaman & Nicobar.

### Q108 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Attorney General of India is appointed under which Article?

A. Article 74
B. Article 76
C. Article 78
D. Article 148

**Answer:** B. Article 76
**Explanation:** Article 76 provides for the Attorney General of India. The AG is the highest law officer of the country, appointed by the President on advice of the government.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 76 = AG of India; Art 148 = CAG; Art 165 = Advocate General of State.

### Q109 · Indian Polity & Governance

How many Fundamental Duties are listed in the Indian Constitution?

A. 9
B. 10
C. 11
D. 12

**Answer:** C. 11
**Explanation:** Originally 10 Fundamental Duties were added by the 42nd Amendment (1976). The 11th duty (providing education to children aged 6-14) was added by the 86th Amendment (2002).
**Shortcut / rule:** 10 original FDs (42nd Amendment) + 1 added (86th Amendment) = 11 total.

### Q110 · Indian Economy

Which committee recommended the introduction of GST in India?

A. Kelkar Committee
B. Raja Chelliah Committee
C. Vijay Kelkar Task Force
D. All of the above contributed

**Answer:** A. Kelkar Committee
**Explanation:** The Kelkar Task Force (2004) recommended GST. The Empowered Committee of State Finance Ministers further developed it. The 101st Amendment Act (2016) enabled GST from 1 July 2017.
**Shortcut / rule:** GST recommendation = Kelkar; Implementation = 1 July 2017; Amendment = 101st.

### Q111 · Indian Economy

What is the base year for the current GDP calculation in India?

A. 2004-05
B. 2011-12
C. 2015-16
D. 2017-18

**Answer:** B. 2011-12
**Explanation:** The current base year for GDP calculation in India is 2011-12. The new method uses Gross Value Added (GVA) at basic prices instead of GDP at factor cost.
**Shortcut / rule:** GDP base year = 2011-12; WPI base year = 2011-12; CPI base year = 2012.

### Q112 · Physics

The unit of frequency is:

A. Newton
B. Hertz
C. Pascal
D. Decibel

**Answer:** B. Hertz
**Explanation:** Frequency is measured in Hertz (Hz). 1 Hz = 1 cycle per second. It is named after Heinrich Hertz who proved the existence of electromagnetic waves.
**Shortcut / rule:** Frequency = Hertz; Sound intensity = Decibel; Force = Newton; Pressure = Pascal.

### Q113 · Physics

Myopia (near-sightedness) is corrected using which type of lens?

A. Convex lens
B. Concave lens
C. Bifocal lens
D. Cylindrical lens

**Answer:** B. Concave lens
**Explanation:** Myopia occurs when the image forms in front of the retina. A concave (diverging) lens corrects it by diverging light rays so the image falls on the retina.
**Shortcut / rule:** Myopia = Concave lens (diverging); Hypermetropia = Convex lens (converging).

### Q114 · Physics

The phenomenon of breaking of white light into seven colours is called:

A. Reflection
B. Refraction
C. Dispersion
D. Diffraction

**Answer:** C. Dispersion
**Explanation:** Dispersion is the splitting of white light into its component colours (VIBGYOR) when passed through a prism, due to different wavelengths bending at different angles.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dispersion = splitting light (prism); Diffraction = bending around obstacles; Scattering = blue sky (Rayleigh).

### Q115 · Chemistry

Which acid is found in vinegar?

A. Hydrochloric acid
B. Sulphuric acid
C. Acetic acid
D. Citric acid

**Answer:** C. Acetic acid
**Explanation:** Vinegar contains 4-8% acetic acid (CH₃COOH). It is also called ethanoic acid.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vinegar = Acetic acid; Lemon = Citric acid; Stomach = HCl; Ant sting = Formic acid.

### Q116 · Chemistry

The hardest natural substance known is:

A. Iron
B. Platinum
C. Diamond
D. Quartz

**Answer:** C. Diamond
**Explanation:** Diamond (an allotrope of carbon) is the hardest known natural substance, with a hardness of 10 on the Mohs scale. It has a tetrahedral crystal structure.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hardest = Diamond (10 on Mohs); Softest mineral = Talc (1 on Mohs); Both are carbon allotropes: Diamond + Graphite.

### Q117 · Chemistry

The process of conversion of sugar into alcohol is called:

A. Oxidation
B. Fermentation
C. Distillation
D. Hydrogenation

**Answer:** B. Fermentation
**Explanation:** Fermentation is an anaerobic process where yeast converts sugar (glucose) into ethanol (alcohol) and carbon dioxide. It is catalyzed by the enzyme zymase.
**Shortcut / rule:** Fermentation = sugar → alcohol + CO₂ (by yeast); Used in bread, beer, wine making.

### Q118 · Biology / Life Science

The powerhouse of the cell is:

A. Nucleus
B. Ribosome
C. Mitochondria
D. Golgi body

**Answer:** C. Mitochondria
**Explanation:** Mitochondria are called the "powerhouse of the cell" because they produce ATP (adenosine triphosphate) through cellular respiration, which is the energy currency of the cell.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mitochondria = energy (ATP); Nucleus = control center (DNA); Ribosome = protein synthesis.

### Q119 · Biology / Life Science

Which blood group is known as the "Universal Donor"?

A. A
B. B
C. AB
D. O

**Answer:** D. O
**Explanation:** Blood group O (especially O-negative) is the universal donor because it has no A or B antigens on red blood cells, so it can be transfused to anyone.
**Shortcut / rule:** Universal Donor = O; Universal Recipient = AB; Blood groups discovered by Karl Landsteiner.

### Q120 · Biology / Life Science

Photosynthesis takes place in which part of the plant cell?

A. Mitochondria
B. Nucleus
C. Chloroplast
D. Cell wall

**Answer:** C. Chloroplast
**Explanation:** Chloroplasts contain chlorophyll (green pigment) and are the site of photosynthesis, where CO₂ and H₂O are converted into glucose and O₂ using sunlight energy.
**Shortcut / rule:** Photosynthesis = Chloroplast (6CO₂ + 6H₂O → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂); Respiration = Mitochondria.

### Q121 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Meenakshi Temple is located in which city?

A. Chennai
B. Madurai
C. Thanjavur
D. Tirupati

**Answer:** B. Madurai
**Explanation:** The Meenakshi Amman Temple is a historic Hindu temple located in Madurai, Tamil Nadu. It is dedicated to Goddess Meenakshi (Parvati) and Lord Sundareswarar (Shiva).
**Shortcut / rule:** Meenakshi = Madurai; Jagannath = Puri; Somnath = Gujarat; Tirupati = Andhra Pradesh.

### Q122 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

'Geet Govinda' was written by:

A. Jayadeva
B. Surdas
C. Tulsidas
D. Kabir

**Answer:** A. Jayadeva
**Explanation:** Geet Govinda is a Sanskrit poem written by Jayadeva in the 12th century. It describes the relationship between Lord Krishna and Radha.
**Shortcut / rule:** Geet Govinda = Jayadeva; Ramcharitmanas = Tulsidas; Padmavat = Malik Muhammad Jayasi.

### Q123 · Games and Sports

The Thomas Cup is associated with which sport?

A. Cricket
B. Football
C. Badminton
D. Table Tennis

**Answer:** C. Badminton
**Explanation:** The Thomas Cup is the premier men's international team badminton championship. India won its maiden Thomas Cup in 2022.
**Shortcut / rule:** Thomas Cup = Men's Badminton; Uber Cup = Women's Badminton; Sudirman Cup = Mixed Badminton.

### Q124 · Games and Sports

The Durand Cup is associated with which sport?

A. Hockey
B. Football
C. Cricket
D. Tennis

**Answer:** B. Football
**Explanation:** The Durand Cup is the oldest football tournament in Asia, first held in 1888 in Shimla. It is named after Sir Mortimer Durand.
**Shortcut / rule:** Durand Cup = Football (oldest in Asia, 1888); Santosh Trophy = Football (national); Ranji Trophy = Cricket.

### Q125 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who is known as the "Iron Man of India"?

A. Subhas Chandra Bose
B. Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
C. Lal Bahadur Shastri
D. Bhagat Singh

**Answer:** B. Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
**Explanation:** Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel is known as the "Iron Man of India" for integrating over 560 princely states into the Indian Union. The Statue of Unity (182 m) in Gujarat is the world's tallest statue dedicated to him.
**Shortcut / rule:** Iron Man = Patel; Statue of Unity = Gujarat (182m, tallest statue in world); National Unity Day = 31 Oct (Patel's birthday).

### Q126 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's first satellite launched into space was:

A. Bhaskara
B. Aryabhata
C. Rohini
D. INSAT-1A

**Answer:** B. Aryabhata
**Explanation:** Aryabhata was India's first satellite, launched on 19 April 1975 by the Soviet Union using a Kosmos-3M launch vehicle. It was named after the ancient mathematician.
**Shortcut / rule:** First satellite = Aryabhata (1975); First launched by Indian rocket = Rohini (1980, SLV-3).

### Q127 · UN & World Organizations

FAO (Food and Agriculture Organization) is headquartered in:

A. New York
B. Geneva
C. Rome
D. Paris

**Answer:** C. Rome
**Explanation:** FAO is headquartered in Rome, Italy. It was established on 16 October 1945 (World Food Day). It leads international efforts to defeat hunger.
**Shortcut / rule:** FAO = Rome; WHO = Geneva; UNESCO = Paris; IMF/World Bank = Washington DC.

### Q128 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which gas is mainly responsible for the greenhouse effect on Earth?

A. Nitrogen
B. Oxygen
C. Carbon dioxide
D. Hydrogen

**Answer:** C. Carbon dioxide
**Explanation:** CO₂ is the primary greenhouse gas contributing to global warming. Other greenhouse gases include methane (CH₄), nitrous oxide (N₂O), and water vapour.
**Shortcut / rule:** Main greenhouse gas by volume = H₂O (water vapour); By human contribution = CO₂; Most potent per molecule = SF₆.

### Q129 · Computers & Applications

Which generation of computers used Integrated Circuits (ICs)?

A. First Generation
B. Second Generation
C. Third Generation
D. Fourth Generation

**Answer:** C. Third Generation
**Explanation:** Third Generation computers (1964-1971) used Integrated Circuits (ICs), making them smaller, faster, and more reliable. ICs were invented by Jack Kilby.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Gen = Vacuum Tubes; 2nd Gen = Transistors; 3rd Gen = ICs; 4th Gen = Microprocessors; 5th Gen = AI.

### Q130 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What is the full form of GDP?

A. Gross Domestic Product
B. General Development Plan
C. Government Domestic Policy
D. Gross Development Proceeds

**Answer:** A. Gross Domestic Product
**Explanation:** GDP is the total monetary value of all finished goods and services produced within a country's borders in a specific time period.
**Shortcut / rule:** GDP = within borders; GNP = by nationals (anywhere); NNP = GNP − Depreciation.

### Q131 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

NITI Aayog was established on:

A. 1 January 2014
B. 1 January 2015
C. 15 August 2015
D. 26 January 2016

**Answer:** B. 1 January 2015
**Explanation:** NITI Aayog (National Institution for Transforming India) was established on 1 January 2015, replacing the Planning Commission. Its first Vice Chairman was Arvind Panagariya.
**Shortcut / rule:** NITI Aayog = 1 Jan 2015; Planning Commission abolished = 2014; PM = Chairperson of NITI Aayog.

### Q132 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the last Governor-General of free India?

A. Lord Mountbatten
B. C. Rajagopalachari
C. Jawaharlal Nehru
D. Dr. Rajendra Prasad

**Answer:** B. C. Rajagopalachari
**Explanation:** C. Rajagopalachari (Rajaji) was the last Governor-General of India (1948-1950). Lord Mountbatten was the first Governor-General of free India (1947-48), and the last Viceroy.
**Shortcut / rule:** Last Viceroy = Mountbatten; First GG of free India = Mountbatten; Last GG = Rajagopalachari; First President = Dr. Rajendra Prasad.

### Q133 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Lucknow Pact of 1916 was an agreement between:

A. Congress and Muslim League
B. Congress and British
C. Muslim League and British
D. Hindu Mahasabha and Muslim League

**Answer:** A. Congress and Muslim League
**Explanation:** The Lucknow Pact (1916) was a joint agreement between INC and the All-India Muslim League for constitutional reforms. It marked a rare instance of Hindu-Muslim unity.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lucknow Pact 1916 = INC + Muslim League; Presided by Ambika Charan Mazumdar (Congress).

### Q134 · Indian & World Geography

The 'Ring of Fire' is associated with:

A. Atlantic Ocean
B. Pacific Ocean
C. Indian Ocean
D. Arctic Ocean

**Answer:** B. Pacific Ocean
**Explanation:** The Ring of Fire is a horseshoe-shaped zone of frequent earthquakes and volcanic eruptions around the basin of the Pacific Ocean. It contains about 75% of the world's active volcanoes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ring of Fire = Pacific Ocean; 75% of world's volcanoes; 90% of earthquakes.

### Q135 · Indian & World Geography

Which pass connects Srinagar to Leh?

A. Banihal Pass
B. Zoji La Pass
C. Rohtang Pass
D. Nathu La Pass

**Answer:** B. Zoji La Pass
**Explanation:** Zoji La Pass (3,528 m) on NH-1 connects Srinagar (Kashmir Valley) to Leh (Ladakh). It is located in the Greater Himalayas on the route from Srinagar to Kargil.
**Shortcut / rule:** Zoji La = Srinagar-Leh; Banihal = Jammu-Srinagar; Rohtang = Manali-Leh; Nathu La = India-China (Sikkim).

### Q136 · Indian Polity & Governance

Which Schedule of the Indian Constitution deals with the allocation of seats in the Rajya Sabha?

A. Second Schedule
B. Third Schedule
C. Fourth Schedule
D. Seventh Schedule

**Answer:** C. Fourth Schedule
**Explanation:** The Fourth Schedule contains the allocation of seats to each state and UT in the Rajya Sabha.
**Shortcut / rule:** 4th Schedule = Rajya Sabha seats; 7th Schedule = Union/State/Concurrent lists; 8th Schedule = Languages.

### Q137 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Eighth Schedule of the Constitution currently recognizes how many languages?

A. 18
B. 20
C. 22
D. 24

**Answer:** C. 22
**Explanation:** The Eighth Schedule currently has 22 recognized languages. The 92nd Amendment (2003) added Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, and Santhali, bringing the total from 18 to 22.
**Shortcut / rule:** 8th Schedule = 22 languages; Originally 14 → 15 (Sindhi) → 18 (Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali) → 22 (Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali).

### Q138 · Indian Economy

The National Rural Employment Guarantee Act (NREGA/MGNREGA) guarantees how many days of employment per year?

A. 50 days
B. 75 days
C. 100 days
D. 150 days

**Answer:** C. 100 days
**Explanation:** MGNREGA guarantees 100 days of wage employment per year to every rural household whose adult members volunteer to do unskilled manual work. It was enacted in 2005.
**Shortcut / rule:** MGNREGA = 100 days; Enacted 2005; Started 2 Feb 2006 (from Anantapur, AP).

### Q139 · Indian Economy

Which bank is known as the "Banker's Bank" in India?

A. State Bank of India
B. Reserve Bank of India
C. NABARD
D. IDBI

**Answer:** B. Reserve Bank of India
**Explanation:** RBI is called the "Banker's Bank" because all commercial banks maintain accounts with RBI and it acts as the lender of last resort. It also regulates the money supply.
**Shortcut / rule:** RBI = Banker's Bank + Lender of last resort; SBI = largest commercial bank.

### Q140 · Physics

Sound waves cannot travel through:

A. Solids
B. Liquids
C. Gases
D. Vacuum

**Answer:** D. Vacuum
**Explanation:** Sound waves are mechanical waves that require a medium (solid, liquid, or gas) to propagate. They cannot travel through a vacuum (empty space).
**Shortcut / rule:** Sound = needs medium; Light = can travel in vacuum; Sound fastest in solids > liquids > gases.

### Q141 · Physics

Which type of mirror is used in the headlights of a car?

A. Plane mirror
B. Convex mirror
C. Concave mirror
D. Cylindrical mirror

**Answer:** C. Concave mirror
**Explanation:** Concave mirrors are used in car headlights because they can produce a powerful parallel beam of light when the bulb is placed at the focus.
**Shortcut / rule:** Concave = headlights, searchlights, solar cookers; Convex = rear-view mirrors; Plane = regular mirrors.

### Q142 · Chemistry

Which gas is used in the preparation of soft drinks and fire extinguishers?

A. Oxygen
B. Nitrogen
C. Carbon dioxide
D. Hydrogen

**Answer:** C. Carbon dioxide
**Explanation:** CO₂ is dissolved in water under pressure to make carbonated soft drinks. In fire extinguishers, CO₂ smothers fire by cutting off oxygen supply.
**Shortcut / rule:** CO₂ = soft drinks + fire extinguisher; N₂ = food packaging; O₂ = welding + hospitals.

### Q143 · Chemistry

Galvanization is the process of coating iron with:

A. Copper
B. Tin
C. Zinc
D. Chromium

**Answer:** C. Zinc
**Explanation:** Galvanization involves coating iron or steel with a thin layer of zinc to prevent rusting (corrosion). The zinc acts as a sacrificial anode.
**Shortcut / rule:** Galvanization = Zinc coating; Electroplating = any metal coating; Anodization = Aluminium oxide coating.

### Q144 · Biology / Life Science

Which disease is caused by Plasmodium parasite transmitted by Anopheles mosquito?

A. Dengue
B. Malaria
C. Chikungunya
D. Typhoid

**Answer:** B. Malaria
**Explanation:** Malaria is caused by Plasmodium parasites (P. vivax, P. falciparum, etc.) transmitted by the bite of female Anopheles mosquitoes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Malaria = Anopheles (Plasmodium); Dengue = Aedes (virus); Sleeping sickness = Tsetse fly (Trypanosoma).

### Q145 · Biology / Life Science

The normal blood pressure of a healthy adult human is approximately:

A. 80/120 mmHg
B. 120/80 mmHg
C. 140/90 mmHg
D. 100/60 mmHg

**Answer:** B. 120/80 mmHg
**Explanation:** Normal blood pressure is 120/80 mmHg (systolic/diastolic). Hypertension is diagnosed when BP is consistently 140/90 mmHg or higher.
**Shortcut / rule:** Normal BP = 120/80; Systolic (heart contracts) / Diastolic (heart relaxes); Measured by Sphygmomanometer.

### Q146 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Rabindranath Tagore won the Nobel Prize in Literature in which year?

A. 1905
B. 1910
C. 1913
D. 1920

**Answer:** C. 1913
**Explanation:** Rabindranath Tagore became the first non-European to win the Nobel Prize in Literature in 1913 for "Gitanjali" (Song Offerings). He also wrote India's national anthem.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tagore = Nobel 1913 (Literature); C.V. Raman = Nobel 1930 (Physics); Amartya Sen = Nobel 1998 (Economics).

### Q147 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

The full form of PSLV is:

A. Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle
B. Polar Space Launch Vehicle
C. Primary Satellite Launch Vehicle
D. Programmable Space Launch Vehicle

**Answer:** A. Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle
**Explanation:** PSLV is ISRO's workhorse launch vehicle for placing satellites into polar and sun-synchronous orbits. PSLV-C37 set a record by launching 104 satellites in one mission (2017).
**Shortcut / rule:** PSLV = Polar orbits (reliable workhorse); GSLV = Geosynchronous orbits (heavier payloads).

### Q148 · UN & World Organizations

The International Court of Justice (ICJ) is located in:

A. New York
B. Geneva
C. The Hague
D. Vienna

**Answer:** C. The Hague
**Explanation:** The ICJ is the principal judicial organ of the United Nations, located at the Peace Palace in The Hague, Netherlands. It settles disputes between states.
**Shortcut / rule:** ICJ = The Hague; ICC (Criminal) = The Hague; UN General Assembly = New York.

### Q149 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Ramsar Convention is related to conservation of:

A. Forests
B. Wetlands
C. Deserts
D. Mountains

**Answer:** B. Wetlands
**Explanation:** The Ramsar Convention (1971) is an international treaty for the conservation and sustainable use of wetlands. It is named after the city of Ramsar in Iran where it was signed.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ramsar = Wetlands (1971, Iran); CITES = Wildlife trade; CBD = Biodiversity; UNFCCC = Climate change.

### Q150 · Computers & Applications

The binary number system uses which digits?

A. 0 to 7
B. 0 to 9
C. 0 and 1
D. 0 to F

**Answer:** C. 0 and 1
**Explanation:** The binary (base-2) number system uses only two digits: 0 and 1. All computer data is ultimately stored and processed in binary form.
**Shortcut / rule:** Binary = 0, 1 (base 2); Octal = 0-7 (base 8); Decimal = 0-9 (base 10); Hexadecimal = 0-F (base 16).

### Q151 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The capital of the Vijayanagara Empire was:

A. Madurai
B. Hampi
C. Warangal
D. Bijapur

**Answer:** B. Hampi
**Explanation:** Hampi (in present-day Karnataka) was the capital of the Vijayanagara Empire (1336-1646). It was founded by Harihara I and Bukka I. Hampi is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vijayanagara = Hampi; Founded by Harihara & Bukka (1336); Krishna Deva Raya = greatest ruler.

### Q152 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Treaty of Salbai (1782) was signed between:

A. British and Marathas
B. British and Mysore
C. British and Sikhs
D. British and Mughals

**Answer:** A. British and Marathas
**Explanation:** The Treaty of Salbai was signed in 1782, ending the First Anglo-Maratha War. It maintained status quo and ensured 20 years of peace between the British and Marathas.
**Shortcut / rule:** Salbai 1782 = 1st Maratha War end; Bassein 1802 = 2nd Maratha War cause; Seringapatam 1799 = Tipu Sultan.

### Q153 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Permanent Settlement of Bengal was introduced by:

A. Lord Cornwallis
B. Lord Wellesley
C. Warren Hastings
D. Lord Bentinck

**Answer:** A. Lord Cornwallis
**Explanation:** Lord Cornwallis introduced the Permanent Settlement (Zamindari System) in Bengal in 1793. It fixed the land revenue to be paid by Zamindars permanently.
**Shortcut / rule:** Permanent Settlement = Cornwallis (1793); Ryotwari = Thomas Munro; Mahalwari = Holt Mackenzie.

### Q154 · Indian & World Geography

Which country is called the "Land of the Rising Sun"?

A. China
B. South Korea
C. Japan
D. Thailand

**Answer:** C. Japan
**Explanation:** Japan is called the "Land of the Rising Sun" because it is situated in the far east where the sun rises first relative to other major Asian nations. In Japanese, Japan is "Nihon" meaning "origin of the sun."
**Shortcut / rule:** Japan = Rising Sun; Australia = Land Down Under; Finland = Land of Thousand Lakes.

### Q155 · Indian & World Geography

The Brahmaputra River is known by which name in Tibet?

A. Meghna
B. Tsangpo
C. Padma
D. Jamuna

**Answer:** B. Tsangpo
**Explanation:** The Brahmaputra is called Tsangpo in Tibet, Siang/Dihang in Arunachal Pradesh, and Jamuna in Bangladesh.
**Shortcut / rule:** Brahmaputra: Tibet = Tsangpo; Arunachal = Siang; Assam = Brahmaputra; Bangladesh = Jamuna.

### Q156 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of "Judicial Review" in India is borrowed from the constitution of which country?

A. UK
B. USA
C. France
D. Ireland

**Answer:** B. USA
**Explanation:** Judicial Review — the power of courts to examine the constitutionality of legislative acts and executive orders — was borrowed from the US Constitution.
**Shortcut / rule:** Judicial Review = USA; DPSP = Ireland; Emergency = Germany; Rule of Law = UK.

### Q157 · Indian Polity & Governance

Who is the ex-officio Chairman of the Rajya Sabha?

A. President of India
B. Prime Minister
C. Vice President
D. Speaker of Lok Sabha

**Answer:** C. Vice President
**Explanation:** Under Article 89(1), the Vice President of India is the ex-officio Chairman of the Rajya Sabha. The Deputy Chairman is elected from among the members.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rajya Sabha Chairman = Vice President; Lok Sabha Speaker = elected MP; Vice President votes only in case of tie.

### Q158 · Indian Economy

Which institution is called the 'lender of last resort' in India?

A. SBI
B. NABARD
C. RBI
D. SEBI

**Answer:** C. RBI
**Explanation:** RBI acts as the lender of last resort by providing emergency loans to commercial banks facing liquidity crises, ensuring financial system stability.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lender of last resort = RBI; Agricultural credit = NABARD; Stock market regulation = SEBI.

### Q159 · Indian Economy

The fiscal year in India runs from:

A. January to December
B. April to March
C. July to June
D. October to September

**Answer:** B. April to March
**Explanation:** The Indian fiscal year runs from 1 April to 31 March. The Union Budget is presented on 1 February for the upcoming fiscal year.
**Shortcut / rule:** India fiscal year = April-March; Budget on 1 Feb; Economic Survey released before Budget.

### Q160 · Physics

Fuse wire is made of an alloy of:

A. Copper and Iron
B. Lead and Tin
C. Aluminium and Zinc
D. Silver and Gold

**Answer:** B. Lead and Tin
**Explanation:** Fuse wire is made of an alloy of lead (37%) and tin (63%) because it has a low melting point. It melts and breaks the circuit when current exceeds the safe limit.
**Shortcut / rule:** Fuse = lead + tin (low melting point); Purpose = circuit protection; Higher rating fuse = thicker wire.

### Q161 · Physics

Echo is caused by:

A. Refraction of sound
B. Reflection of sound
C. Diffraction of sound
D. Interference of sound

**Answer:** B. Reflection of sound
**Explanation:** An echo is produced when sound waves reflect off a surface and return to the listener. The minimum distance required for an echo to be heard is approximately 17.2 meters (at 20°C).
**Shortcut / rule:** Echo = reflection of sound; Min distance ≈ 17m; Time gap ≈ 0.1 sec for distinct echo.

### Q162 · Chemistry

Which metal is liquid at room temperature?

A. Sodium
B. Potassium
C. Mercury
D. Gallium

**Answer:** C. Mercury
**Explanation:** Mercury (Hg) is the only metal that is liquid at room temperature (melting point -38.83°C). Gallium melts at 29.76°C (near body temperature). Bromine is a non-metal liquid.
**Shortcut / rule:** Liquid metal = Mercury (-39°C); Liquid non-metal = Bromine; Mercury used in thermometers.

### Q163 · Chemistry

Plaster of Paris is chemically:

A. Calcium Carbonate
B. Calcium Sulphate Hemihydrate
C. Calcium Hydroxide
D. Calcium Chloride

**Answer:** B. Calcium Sulphate Hemihydrate
**Explanation:** Plaster of Paris (PoP) is CaSO₄·½H₂O. It is obtained by heating Gypsum (CaSO₄·2H₂O) to about 373 K. When mixed with water, it hardens back to Gypsum.
**Shortcut / rule:** PoP = CaSO₄·½H₂O; Gypsum = CaSO₄·2H₂O; Heat Gypsum → PoP; PoP + Water → Gypsum.

### Q164 · Biology / Life Science

Which organ in the human body produces insulin?

A. Liver
B. Kidney
C. Pancreas
D. Spleen

**Answer:** C. Pancreas
**Explanation:** Insulin is produced by the beta cells of the Islets of Langerhans in the Pancreas. Insulin regulates blood sugar levels. Its deficiency causes Diabetes Mellitus.
**Shortcut / rule:** Insulin = Pancreas (beta cells); Diabetes = insulin deficiency; Discovered by Banting & Best (1921).

### Q165 · Biology / Life Science

The study of fungi is called:

A. Mycology
B. Virology
C. Bacteriology
D. Phycology

**Answer:** A. Mycology
**Explanation:** Mycology is the branch of biology that deals with the study of fungi. The term comes from the Greek word "mykes" meaning mushroom.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mycology = fungi; Virology = viruses; Bacteriology = bacteria; Phycology = algae; Ornithology = birds.

### Q166 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Sun Temple at Konark was built by:

A. Raja Raja Chola
B. Narasimhadeva I
C. Krishna Deva Raya
D. Ashoka

**Answer:** B. Narasimhadeva I
**Explanation:** The Konark Sun Temple in Odisha was built by King Narasimhadeva I of the Eastern Ganga dynasty in the 13th century (around 1250 AD). It is designed as a giant chariot with 24 wheels.
**Shortcut / rule:** Konark = Narasimhadeva I (Eastern Ganga, 13th c.); 24 wheels = 24 hours; UNESCO World Heritage Site.

### Q167 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Which Indian classical dance form originated in Andhra Pradesh?

A. Kathak
B. Odissi
C. Kuchipudi
D. Mohiniyattam

**Answer:** C. Kuchipudi
**Explanation:** Kuchipudi originated in the village of Kuchipudi in Andhra Pradesh. It combines elements of dance, drama, and music.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kuchipudi = Andhra Pradesh; Kathak = North India (Uttar Pradesh); Mohiniyattam = Kerala; Sattriya = Assam.

### Q168 · Games and Sports

Wimbledon is associated with which sport?

A. Football
B. Golf
C. Tennis
D. Cricket

**Answer:** C. Tennis
**Explanation:** Wimbledon is the oldest and most prestigious tennis tournament in the world, held annually in London since 1877. It is one of the four Grand Slam tournaments.
**Shortcut / rule:** Grand Slams: Australian Open, French Open (Roland Garros), Wimbledon, US Open; Only Wimbledon is on grass.

### Q169 · Games and Sports

The Davis Cup is related to which sport?

A. Badminton
B. Tennis
C. Hockey
D. Golf

**Answer:** B. Tennis
**Explanation:** The Davis Cup is the premier international men's team tennis competition, started in 1900. India reached the finals in 1966, 1974, and 1987.
**Shortcut / rule:** Davis Cup = Men's Tennis; Fed Cup (Billie Jean King Cup) = Women's Tennis.

### Q170 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who was the first Indian to go to space?

A. Kalpana Chawla
B. Rakesh Sharma
C. Sunita Williams
D. Vikram Sarabhai

**Answer:** B. Rakesh Sharma
**Explanation:** Squadron Leader Rakesh Sharma became the first Indian in space on 3 April 1984, aboard the Soviet spacecraft Soyuz T-11. When PM Indira Gandhi asked how India looked from space, he famously replied "Sare Jahan Se Achha."
**Shortcut / rule:** First Indian in space = Rakesh Sharma (1984); Kalpana Chawla = first Indian-origin woman in space (1997).

### Q171 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India conducted its first nuclear test (Smiling Buddha) at:

A. Kalpakkam
B. Pokhran
C. Tarapur
D. Trombay

**Answer:** B. Pokhran
**Explanation:** India's first nuclear test "Smiling Buddha" was conducted on 18 May 1974 at Pokhran, Rajasthan under PM Indira Gandhi. The second test "Operation Shakti" was conducted on 11 May 1998 under PM Atal Bihari Vajpayee.
**Shortcut / rule:** Pokhran-I = 1974 (Indira); Pokhran-II = 1998 (Vajpayee); Both at Pokhran, Rajasthan.

### Q172 · UN & World Organizations

UNICEF stands for:

A. United Nations International Children's Emergency Fund
B. United Nations Integrated Child Education Fund
C. United Nations Industrial and Commercial Economic Forum
D. Universal Nations Information Centre for Education

**Answer:** A. United Nations International Children's Emergency Fund
**Explanation:** UNICEF was created in 1946 to provide emergency food and healthcare to children in war-devastated countries. Its HQ is in New York. It was renamed "United Nations Children's Fund" but retained the UNICEF acronym.
**Shortcut / rule:** UNICEF = Children (New York, 1946); UNESCO = Education/Culture (Paris); UNHCR = Refugees (Geneva).

### Q173 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Jim Corbett National Park, India's first national park, is located in:

A. Madhya Pradesh
B. Uttarakhand
C. Rajasthan
D. Kerala

**Answer:** B. Uttarakhand
**Explanation:** Jim Corbett National Park was established in 1936 as Hailey National Park. It is located in the Nainital district of Uttarakhand and was India's first national park. It is famous for Bengal Tigers.
**Shortcut / rule:** First NP = Jim Corbett (1936, Uttarakhand); Project Tiger started here (1973).

### Q174 · Computers & Applications

The first computer programmer in history is considered to be:

A. Charles Babbage
B. Ada Lovelace
C. Alan Turing
D. John von Neumann

**Answer:** B. Ada Lovelace
