**Explanation:** Ada Lovelace (Augusta Ada King) is considered the first computer programmer for writing an algorithm for Charles Babbage's Analytical Engine in 1843.
**Shortcut / rule:** First programmer = Ada Lovelace; Babbage = designed computer; Turing = AI/code-breaking.

### Q175 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The full form of NABARD is:

A. National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development
B. National Board for Agricultural Research and Development
C. National Bureau for Agricultural and Rural Development
D. National Bank for Agro and Rural Deposits

**Answer:** A. National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development
**Explanation:** NABARD was established on 12 July 1982 based on the recommendations of the B. Sivaraman Committee. Its HQ is in Mumbai.
**Shortcut / rule:** NABARD = Agriculture + Rural (1982, Mumbai); SIDBI = Small Industries; EXIM Bank = Export-Import.

### Q176 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Ayushman Bharat (PMJAY) provides health insurance coverage of up to:

A. ₹1 lakh per family
B. ₹3 lakh per family
C. ₹5 lakh per family
D. ₹10 lakh per family

**Answer:** C. ₹5 lakh per family
**Explanation:** Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (PMJAY) under Ayushman Bharat provides health insurance of up to ₹5 lakh per family per year for secondary and tertiary care hospitalization. Launched on 23 September 2018.
**Shortcut / rule:** PMJAY = ₹5 lakh health cover; Launched 23 Sept 2018; Covers 10.74 crore poor families.

### Q177 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Rowlatt Act was passed in which year?

A. 1917
B. 1919
C. 1920
D. 1922

**Answer:** B. 1919
**Explanation:** The Rowlatt Act (also called "Black Act") was passed in March 1919, allowing the British to imprison any person without trial for up to 2 years. Gandhi called it the "beginning of the end" for British rule.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rowlatt Act = March 1919; Jallianwala Bagh = 13 April 1919 (response to protests); Both = 1919.

### Q178 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Morley-Minto Reforms (1909) introduced the concept of:

A. Diarchy
B. Separate Electorates
C. Provincial Autonomy
D. Federal System

**Answer:** B. Separate Electorates
**Explanation:** The Indian Councils Act 1909 (Morley-Minto Reforms) introduced separate electorates for Muslims for the first time, allowing Muslims to elect their own representatives.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1909 = Separate Electorates; 1919 (Montagu-Chelmsford) = Diarchy; 1935 = Provincial Autonomy.

### Q179 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian river is called "Dakshin Ganga" (Ganga of the South)?

A. Krishna
B. Kaveri
C. Godavari
D. Narmada

**Answer:** C. Godavari
**Explanation:** The Godavari (1,465 km) is the longest peninsular river in India and is called "Dakshin Ganga" or "Vridh Ganga" (Old Ganga). It originates from Trimbakeshwar, Nasik (Maharashtra).
**Shortcut / rule:** Godavari = Dakshin Ganga (longest peninsular river); Krishna = 2nd longest; Narmada = longest west-flowing peninsular river.

### Q180 · Indian & World Geography

The Palk Strait separates India from:

A. Myanmar
B. Bangladesh
C. Sri Lanka
D. Maldives

**Answer:** C. Sri Lanka
**Explanation:** The Palk Strait is a narrow stretch of water between Tamil Nadu (India) and Jaffna (Sri Lanka). It connects the Bay of Bengal with the Gulf of Mannar.
**Shortcut / rule:** Palk Strait = India-Sri Lanka; Adam's Bridge (Ram Setu) = also India-Sri Lanka; 10° Channel = Andaman-Nicobar.

### Q181 · Indian Polity & Governance

Which Article of the Constitution provides for the formation of new states?

A. Article 1
B. Article 2
C. Article 3
D. Article 4

**Answer:** C. Article 3
**Explanation:** Article 3 empowers Parliament to form new states, alter boundaries, change names, etc. A bill for this must be referred to the concerned state legislature for its views (not consent).
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 1 = India = Union of States; Art 2 = Admission of new states; Art 3 = Formation/alteration of states.

### Q182 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Finance Commission is constituted every:

A. 3 years
B. 5 years
C. 7 years
D. 10 years

**Answer:** B. 5 years
**Explanation:** Under Article 280, the President constitutes a Finance Commission every 5 years to recommend the distribution of tax revenues between the Centre and States.
**Shortcut / rule:** Finance Commission = 5 years (Art 280); Recommends tax sharing; Currently 16th FC (headed by Arvind Panagariya).

### Q183 · Indian Economy

Which sector contributes the most to India's GDP currently?

A. Agriculture
B. Industry
C. Services (Tertiary)
D. Mining

**Answer:** C. Services (Tertiary)
**Explanation:** The Services sector contributes over 50% to India's GDP. It includes IT, banking, transport, education, healthcare, and tourism. Agriculture contributes about 15-17%.
**Shortcut / rule:** Services > Industry > Agriculture in GDP contribution; But Agriculture employs ~42% of workforce.

### Q184 · Physics

The device used to convert mechanical energy into electrical energy is:

A. Motor
B. Generator (Dynamo)
C. Transformer
D. Battery

**Answer:** B. Generator (Dynamo)
**Explanation:** A generator (dynamo) converts mechanical energy into electrical energy based on electromagnetic induction (Faraday's law). A motor does the reverse.
**Shortcut / rule:** Generator = mechanical → electrical; Motor = electrical → mechanical; Transformer = changes voltage.

### Q185 · Physics

Which law states that "for every action, there is an equal and opposite reaction"?

A. Newton's First Law
B. Newton's Second Law
C. Newton's Third Law
D. Law of Conservation of Energy

**Answer:** C. Newton's Third Law
**Explanation:** Newton's Third Law of Motion states that for every action force, there is an equal and opposite reaction force. Examples: rocket propulsion, walking, swimming.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st = Inertia; 2nd = F=ma; 3rd = Action-Reaction (forces on different bodies).

### Q186 · Chemistry

The chemical formula of common salt is:

A. KCl
B. NaCl
C. CaCl₂
D. MgCl₂

**Answer:** B. NaCl
**Explanation:** Common salt (table salt) is Sodium Chloride (NaCl). It is an ionic compound formed by the reaction of sodium (Na) and chlorine (Cl).
**Shortcut / rule:** Common salt = NaCl; Rock salt = NaCl (mined); Sea water = ~3.5% NaCl.

### Q187 · Chemistry

Which gas is produced when zinc reacts with dilute hydrochloric acid?

A. Oxygen
B. Carbon dioxide
C. Hydrogen
D. Chlorine

**Answer:** C. Hydrogen
**Explanation:** Zn + 2HCl → ZnCl₂ + H₂. When zinc reacts with dilute HCl, zinc chloride and hydrogen gas are produced. The hydrogen gas burns with a "pop" sound.
**Shortcut / rule:** Active metal + acid → salt + H₂; "Pop test" = hydrogen; "Lime water turns milky" = CO₂.

### Q188 · Biology / Life Science

Which vitamin deficiency causes Night Blindness?

A. Vitamin A
B. Vitamin B
C. Vitamin C
D. Vitamin K

**Answer:** A. Vitamin A
**Explanation:** Vitamin A (Retinol) deficiency causes Night Blindness (Nyctalopia) and Xerophthalmia. Vitamin A is found in carrots, liver, fish oil, and green leafy vegetables.
**Shortcut / rule:** Night blindness = Vit A; Scurvy = Vit C; Beri-beri = Vit B1; Pernicious anemia = Vit B12.

### Q189 · Biology / Life Science

Red blood cells (RBCs) are produced in the:

A. Liver
B. Kidney
C. Bone marrow
D. Spleen

**Answer:** C. Bone marrow
**Explanation:** Red blood cells (erythrocytes) are produced in the red bone marrow through a process called erythropoiesis. Mature RBCs lack a nucleus and live for about 120 days.
**Shortcut / rule:** RBC production = Bone marrow; RBC destruction = Spleen ("graveyard of RBCs"); RBC lifespan = 120 days.

### Q190 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Qutub Minar in Delhi was started by:

A. Iltutmish
B. Qutub-ud-din Aibak
C. Alauddin Khilji
D. Firoz Shah Tughlaq

**Answer:** B. Qutub-ud-din Aibak
**Explanation:** Qutub Minar was started by Qutub-ud-din Aibak in 1193 and completed by Iltutmish. It is the tallest brick minaret in the world (72.5 m) and a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Started = Aibak; Completed = Iltutmish; 5 storeys; Made of red sandstone and marble.

### Q191 · Games and Sports

The Ranji Trophy is associated with which sport?

A. Football
B. Hockey
C. Cricket
D. Badminton

**Answer:** C. Cricket
**Explanation:** The Ranji Trophy is India's premier first-class domestic cricket tournament, named after Ranjitsinhji (Kumar Shri Ranjitsinhji Vibhaji Jadeja). It started in 1934-35.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ranji Trophy = Cricket (domestic); Irani Cup = Ranji winner vs Rest of India; Duleep Trophy = Cricket (zonal).

### Q192 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

C.V. Raman won the Nobel Prize in Physics in which year?

A. 1928
B. 1930
C. 1932
D. 1935

**Answer:** B. 1930
**Explanation:** Sir C.V. Raman won the Nobel Prize in Physics in 1930 for his discovery of the Raman Effect (inelastic scattering of light). National Science Day (28 February) celebrates this discovery.
**Shortcut / rule:** C.V. Raman = Nobel 1930; Raman Effect discovered = 28 Feb 1928; National Science Day = 28 Feb.

### Q193 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

GSLV stands for:

A. Geo-Stationary Launch Vehicle
B. Geosynchronous Satellite Launch Vehicle
C. Global Satellite Launch Vehicle
D. Geo-Synchronous Light Vehicle

**Answer:** B. Geosynchronous Satellite Launch Vehicle
**Explanation:** GSLV is designed to place heavier satellites into Geosynchronous Transfer Orbit (GTO). GSLV Mk III (LVM3) was used for the Chandrayaan-3 mission.
**Shortcut / rule:** GSLV = heavy satellites (geosynchronous); PSLV = lighter satellites (polar orbit); LVM3 = GSLV Mk III.

### Q194 · UN & World Organizations

The World Bank is headquartered in:

A. New York
B. Geneva
C. Washington DC
D. London

**Answer:** C. Washington DC
**Explanation:** The World Bank Group is headquartered in Washington DC, USA. It was established in 1944 (Bretton Woods Conference). Its main goal is to reduce poverty and promote development.
**Shortcut / rule:** World Bank = Washington DC (1944); IMF = Washington DC (1945); Both from Bretton Woods.

### Q195 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Gir National Park is famous for which animal?

A. Bengal Tiger
B. Asiatic Lion
C. Indian Rhinoceros
D. Snow Leopard

**Answer:** B. Asiatic Lion
**Explanation:** Gir National Park in Gujarat is the only natural habitat of the Asiatic Lion (Panthera leo persica) in the world.
**Shortcut / rule:** Gir = Lion (Gujarat); Kaziranga = Rhino (Assam); Ranthambore = Tiger (Rajasthan); Hemis = Snow Leopard (Ladakh).

### Q196 · Computers & Applications

LAN stands for:

A. Large Area Network
B. Local Area Network
C. Long Access Network
D. Light Amplitude Network

**Answer:** B. Local Area Network
**Explanation:** A LAN connects computers within a small geographic area like an office, school, or building. It uses technologies like Ethernet and Wi-Fi.
**Shortcut / rule:** LAN = small area (office); MAN = city; WAN = country/global; Internet = largest WAN.

### Q197 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does PIN in PIN code stand for?

A. Postal Index Number
B. Post Identification Number
C. Postal Information Network
D. Post Internal Number

**Answer:** A. Postal Index Number
**Explanation:** PIN (Postal Index Number) is a 6-digit code used by India Post. It was introduced on 15 August 1972. The first digit represents the region.
**Shortcut / rule:** PIN = 6 digits (introduced 1972); First digit = region (1=Delhi, 2=AP/TN, 3=WB, etc.).

### Q198 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Beti Bachao Beti Padhao scheme was launched in:

A. 2013
B. 2014
C. 2015
D. 2016

**Answer:** C. 2015
**Explanation:** Beti Bachao Beti Padhao was launched on 22 January 2015 from Panipat, Haryana to address the declining child sex ratio and promote education for girls.
**Shortcut / rule:** Beti Bachao = Jan 2015 (Panipat); Sukanya Samriddhi = savings for girl child (part of same initiative).

### Q199 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Indian National Army (INA) was formed by:

A. Subhas Chandra Bose
B. Rash Behari Bose
C. Mohan Singh
D. Both B and C initially; Bose took over later

**Answer:** D. Both B and C initially; Bose took over later
**Explanation:** The INA was first formed by Captain Mohan Singh in 1942 with Japanese help. After it was dissolved, Rash Behari Bose revived it and later handed leadership to Subhas Chandra Bose in 1943.
**Shortcut / rule:** INA first = Mohan Singh (1942); Reorganized = Rash Behari Bose; Led to glory = Subhas Bose (1943, "Chalo Dilli").

### Q200 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the largest freshwater lake in India?

A. Chilika Lake
B. Wular Lake
C. Dal Lake
D. Loktak Lake

**Answer:** B. Wular Lake
**Explanation:** Wular Lake in Jammu & Kashmir is the largest freshwater lake in India. It is fed by the Jhelum River. Chilika Lake (Odisha) is the largest brackish water lagoon.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest freshwater = Wular (J&K); Largest brackish = Chilika (Odisha); Largest saline = Sambhar (Rajasthan).

### Q201 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the first woman President of the Indian National Congress?

A. Sarojini Naidu
B. Annie Besant
C. Kasturba Gandhi
D. Aruna Asaf Ali

**Answer:** B. Annie Besant
**Explanation:** Annie Besant was the first woman President of INC (Calcutta session, 1917). Sarojini Naidu was the first Indian-born woman president (Kanpur session, 1925).
**Shortcut / rule:** First woman INC President = Annie Besant (1917); First Indian woman = Sarojini Naidu (1925).

### Q202 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Cripps Mission visited India in which year?

A. 1940
B. 1942
C. 1944
D. 1946

**Answer:** B. 1942
**Explanation:** The Cripps Mission (March 1942) was sent by the British government under Sir Stafford Cripps to secure Indian cooperation during WWII. It was rejected by both Congress and Muslim League.
**Shortcut / rule:** Cripps Mission = 1942; Cabinet Mission = 1946; Simon Commission = 1928.

### Q203 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who is known as the "Grand Old Man of India"?

A. Lala Lajpat Rai
B. Dadabhai Naoroji
C. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
D. Bal Gangadhar Tilak

**Answer:** B. Dadabhai Naoroji
**Explanation:** Dadabhai Naoroji is known as the "Grand Old Man of India." He was the first Indian to be elected to the British Parliament (1892) and propounded the "Drain of Wealth" theory.
**Shortcut / rule:** Grand Old Man = Naoroji; Drain Theory = Naoroji; "Poverty and Un-British Rule in India" = Naoroji.

### Q204 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Vernacular Press Act was passed in which year?

A. 1875
B. 1878
C. 1882
D. 1885

**Answer:** B. 1878
**Explanation:** The Vernacular Press Act was enacted in 1878 by Lord Lytton to suppress freedom of the Indian language press. It was repealed in 1882 by Lord Ripon.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vernacular Press Act = 1878 (Lytton); Repealed 1882 (Ripon); Ripon = "Father of Local Self-Government."

### Q205 · Indian & World Geography

Which layer of the atmosphere contains the ozone layer?

A. Troposphere
B. Stratosphere
C. Mesosphere
D. Thermosphere

**Answer:** B. Stratosphere
**Explanation:** The ozone layer is located in the stratosphere (about 15-35 km above Earth's surface). It absorbs 97-99% of the Sun's harmful ultraviolet radiation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Troposphere = weather; Stratosphere = ozone; Mesosphere = meteoroids burn; Thermosphere = ISS orbits; Exosphere = outermost.

### Q206 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the largest continent by area?

A. Africa
B. North America
C. Asia
D. Europe

**Answer:** C. Asia
**Explanation:** Asia is the largest continent by both area (~44.58 million km²) and population (~4.7 billion). It covers about 30% of Earth's total land area.
**Shortcut / rule:** By area: Asia > Africa > N. America > S. America > Antarctica > Europe > Australia/Oceania.

### Q207 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian river originates from Amarkantak?

A. Godavari
B. Narmada
C. Krishna
D. Tapti

**Answer:** B. Narmada
**Explanation:** The Narmada River originates from Amarkantak plateau in Madhya Pradesh. It is the longest west-flowing river in peninsular India (1,312 km) and flows into the Gulf of Khambhat (Cambay).
**Shortcut / rule:** Narmada = Amarkantak (MP); Godavari = Trimbakeshwar (MH); Krishna = Mahabaleshwar (MH); Ganga = Gangotri (UK).

### Q208 · Indian Polity & Governance

The term "Secular" was added to the Preamble by which Amendment?

A. 1st Amendment
B. 42nd Amendment
C. 44th Amendment
D. 73rd Amendment

**Answer:** B. 42nd Amendment
**Explanation:** The 42nd Amendment (1976) added "Socialist," "Secular," and "Integrity" to the Preamble. It is called the "Mini Constitution" for its sweeping changes.
**Shortcut / rule:** 42nd Amendment added: Socialist + Secular + Integrity to Preamble (1976, during Emergency).

### Q209 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG) of India is appointed under which Article?

A. Article 124
B. Article 148
C. Article 280
D. Article 324

**Answer:** B. Article 148
**Explanation:** Article 148 provides for the CAG of India. The CAG audits all government expenditure and reports to the Parliament. The CAG is the guardian of the public purse.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 148 = CAG; Art 324 = Election Commission; Art 280 = Finance Commission; Art 338 = SC Commission.

### Q210 · Indian Economy

What is the current repo rate tool used by RBI called?

A. Bank Rate
B. Repo Rate
C. Cash Reserve Ratio
D. Statutory Liquidity Ratio

**Answer:** B. Repo Rate
**Explanation:** Repo Rate is the rate at which RBI lends money to commercial banks against government securities. It is the primary tool for controlling inflation under the Monetary Policy Framework.
**Shortcut / rule:** Repo = RBI lends to banks; Reverse Repo = banks deposit with RBI; CRR = cash banks must keep with RBI.

### Q211 · Indian Economy

The concept of "Mixed Economy" means:

A. Only public sector
B. Only private sector
C. Coexistence of public and private sectors
D. Only cooperative sector

**Answer:** C. Coexistence of public and private sectors
**Explanation:** India follows a Mixed Economy model where both public and private sectors coexist. This was adopted in the Industrial Policy Resolution of 1948 and 1956.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mixed Economy = Public + Private; India adopted from Industrial Policy 1956; LPG reforms 1991 tilted towards private.

### Q212 · Physics

Joule is the unit of:

A. Power
B. Force
C. Energy/Work
D. Pressure

**Answer:** C. Energy/Work
**Explanation:** Joule (J) is the SI unit of energy and work. 1 Joule = 1 Newton × 1 meter. It is also equal to 1 Watt × 1 second.
**Shortcut / rule:** Energy = Joule; Power = Watt (J/s); Force = Newton; Pressure = Pascal (N/m²).

### Q213 · Physics

Fleming's Left Hand Rule is used to determine:

A. Direction of induced current
B. Direction of force on a current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field
C. Direction of magnetic field
D. Resistance in a circuit

**Answer:** B. Direction of force on a current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field
**Explanation:** Fleming's Left Hand Rule: if the forefinger points to the magnetic field and the middle finger to the current, the thumb shows the direction of force (motion).
**Shortcut / rule:** Left Hand Rule = Force on conductor (motor); Right Hand Rule = Induced current (generator).

### Q214 · Chemistry

Which element has the chemical symbol 'Au'?

A. Silver
B. Aluminium
C. Gold
D. Copper

**Answer:** C. Gold
**Explanation:** Au (from Latin 'Aurum') is the chemical symbol for Gold. It has atomic number 79 and is a noble/precious metal.
**Shortcut / rule:** Au = Gold (Aurum); Ag = Silver (Argentum); Fe = Iron (Ferrum); Cu = Copper (Cuprum); Hg = Mercury (Hydrargyrum).

### Q215 · Chemistry

The process of conversion of iron into its oxide in the presence of moisture is called:

A. Smelting
B. Corrosion (Rusting)
C. Reduction
D. Calcination

**Answer:** B. Corrosion (Rusting)
**Explanation:** Corrosion is the degradation of metals by chemical reaction with the environment. Rusting of iron (forming Fe₂O₃) is the most common example, requiring both O₂ and H₂O.
**Shortcut / rule:** Corrosion = slow oxidation of metals; Rusting specific to iron; Prevention: galvanization, painting, oiling.

### Q216 · Biology / Life Science

The largest gland in the human body is:

A. Thyroid
B. Pancreas
C. Liver
D. Pituitary

**Answer:** C. Liver
**Explanation:** The Liver is the largest gland (and largest internal organ) in the human body, weighing about 1.5 kg. It produces bile, detoxifies blood, stores glycogen, and performs 500+ functions.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest gland = Liver; Master gland = Pituitary; Largest endocrine gland = Thyroid.

### Q217 · Biology / Life Science

DNA stands for:

A. Deoxy Nucleic Acid
B. Deoxyribonucleic Acid
C. Di-Nucleotide Acid
D. Dynamic Nucleic Acid

**Answer:** B. Deoxyribonucleic Acid
**Explanation:** DNA carries the genetic instructions for life. Its double-helix structure was discovered by Watson and Crick in 1953. DNA is found in the nucleus of cells.
**Shortcut / rule:** DNA = double helix (Watson & Crick, 1953); RNA = single strand; DNA in nucleus; Mitochondria also has DNA.

### Q218 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The famous Hawa Mahal is located in:

A. Delhi
B. Jodhpur
C. Jaipur
D. Udaipur

**Answer:** C. Jaipur
**Explanation:** Hawa Mahal (Palace of Winds) is in Jaipur, Rajasthan. It was built in 1799 by Maharaja Sawai Pratap Singh. It has 953 small windows (jharokhas) for ventilation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hawa Mahal = Jaipur (1799); Taj Mahal = Agra (Shah Jahan); Red Fort = Delhi (Shah Jahan).

### Q219 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Which Mughal emperor built the Taj Mahal?

A. Akbar
B. Jahangir
C. Shah Jahan
D. Aurangzeb

**Answer:** C. Shah Jahan
**Explanation:** Shah Jahan built the Taj Mahal in Agra (1632-1653) in memory of his wife Mumtaz Mahal. It was designed by Ustad Ahmad Lahori and is one of the Seven Wonders of the World.
**Shortcut / rule:** Taj Mahal = Shah Jahan (Agra, 1632-53); Fatehpur Sikri = Akbar; Red Fort Delhi = Shah Jahan.

### Q220 · Games and Sports

The FIFA World Cup is associated with:

A. Cricket
B. Hockey
C. Football
D. Tennis

**Answer:** C. Football
**Explanation:** The FIFA World Cup is the most prestigious international football tournament, held every 4 years since 1930. Brazil has won it a record 5 times.
**Shortcut / rule:** FIFA WC = Football (every 4 yrs); Cricket WC = ICC; Hockey WC = FIH; Most FIFA titles = Brazil (5).

### Q221 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who wrote the book "The Discovery of India"?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Jawaharlal Nehru
C. Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
D. Rabindranath Tagore

**Answer:** B. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Explanation:** "The Discovery of India" was written by Jawaharlal Nehru during his imprisonment at Ahmednagar Fort (1944). His other books include "Glimpses of World History" and his autobiography.
**Shortcut / rule:** Discovery of India = Nehru; My Experiments with Truth = Gandhi; Annihilation of Caste = Ambedkar.

### Q222 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

Satish Dhawan Space Centre is located at:

A. Thumba
B. Sriharikota
C. Bengaluru
D. Ahmedabad

**Answer:** B. Sriharikota
**Explanation:** Satish Dhawan Space Centre (SDSC SHAR) is located in Sriharikota, Andhra Pradesh. It is ISRO's primary launch centre for satellite launch vehicles.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sriharikota = launch centre; Bengaluru = ISRO HQ; Thumba = first sounding rocket launch (1963).

### Q223 · UN & World Organizations

The International Monetary Fund (IMF) is headquartered in:

A. New York
B. Washington DC
C. Geneva
D. London

**Answer:** B. Washington DC
**Explanation:** The IMF was established in 1944 at the Bretton Woods Conference and is headquartered in Washington DC. It promotes global monetary cooperation and financial stability.
**Shortcut / rule:** IMF = Washington DC (1944); Special Drawing Rights (SDR) = IMF's currency; India = founding member.

### Q224 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which protocol deals with reduction of greenhouse gas emissions?

A. Montreal Protocol
B. Kyoto Protocol
C. Ramsar Convention
D. Basel Convention

**Answer:** B. Kyoto Protocol
**Explanation:** The Kyoto Protocol (1997) is an international treaty that commits parties to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. It was adopted in Kyoto, Japan and entered into force in 2005.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kyoto = greenhouse gases (1997); Montreal = ozone (1987); Paris Agreement = climate (2015); Basel = hazardous waste.

### Q225 · Computers & Applications

What is the full form of URL?

A. Uniform Resource Locator
B. Universal Resource Link
C. Unified Resource Locator
D. Universal Reference Link

**Answer:** A. Uniform Resource Locator
**Explanation:** URL is the address used to access resources on the Internet. It was invented by Tim Berners-Lee in 1994 along with HTTP and HTML.
**Shortcut / rule:** URL = web address; HTML = web page structure; CSS = web styling; JavaScript = web interactivity.

### Q226 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does BHEL stand for?

A. Bharat Heavy Electricals Limited
B. Bharat Heavy Engineering Limited
C. Bharat Hydroelectric Limited
D. Basic Heavy Electronics Limited

**Answer:** A. Bharat Heavy Electricals Limited
**Explanation:** BHEL is a Maharatna PSU under the Ministry of Heavy Industries. It was founded in 1964 and manufactures power plant equipment and heavy electrical machinery.
**Shortcut / rule:** BHEL = heavy electricals (1964); ONGC = oil & gas; NTPC = thermal power; SAIL = steel.

### Q227 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Skill India Mission was launched in which year?

A. 2014
B. 2015
C. 2016
D. 2017

**Answer:** B. 2015
**Explanation:** Skill India Mission was launched on 15 July 2015 (World Youth Skills Day) to train youth in industry-relevant skills. PMKVY (Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana) is its flagship scheme.
**Shortcut / rule:** Skill India = 15 July 2015; PMKVY = skill training scheme; Target = 40 crore skilled youth by 2022.

### Q228 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Champaran Satyagraha (1917) was related to:

A. Salt tax
B. Indigo cultivation
C. Land revenue
D. Textile workers

**Answer:** B. Indigo cultivation
**Explanation:** The Champaran Satyagraha was Gandhi's first civil disobedience movement in India. He went to Champaran (Bihar) in 1917 to address the grievances of indigo farmers exploited by European planters under the Tinkathia system.
**Shortcut / rule:** Champaran (1917) = Indigo (Bihar) = Gandhi's first Indian satyagraha; Kheda (1918) = farmers + flood; Ahmedabad (1918) = mill workers.

### Q229 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who gave the slogan "Swaraj is my birthright"?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Lala Lajpat Rai
C. Bal Gangadhar Tilak
D. Subhas Chandra Bose

**Answer:** C. Bal Gangadhar Tilak
**Explanation:** Bal Gangadhar Tilak (Lokmanya Tilak) gave the famous slogan "Swaraj is my birthright, and I shall have it." He is considered the father of Indian unrest.
**Shortcut / rule:** "Swaraj is my birthright" = Tilak; "Inquilab Zindabad" = Bhagat Singh; "Jai Hind" = Bose; "Tum mujhe khoon do" = Bose.

### Q230 · Indian & World Geography

Which canal connects the Mediterranean Sea to the Red Sea?

A. Panama Canal
B. Suez Canal
C. Kiel Canal
D. Erie Canal

**Answer:** B. Suez Canal
**Explanation:** The Suez Canal in Egypt connects the Mediterranean Sea to the Red Sea. It was opened in 1869 and is one of the world's most important waterways, shortening the Europe-Asia route.
**Shortcut / rule:** Suez = Mediterranean-Red Sea (Egypt, 1869); Panama = Atlantic-Pacific (Panama, 1914).

### Q231 · Indian Polity & Governance

Writ jurisdiction of the Supreme Court is provided under which Article?

A. Article 12
B. Article 21
C. Article 32
D. Article 226

**Answer:** C. Article 32
**Explanation:** Article 32 gives the Supreme Court the power to issue writs (Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Certiorari, Quo Warranto) for enforcement of Fundamental Rights. Dr. Ambedkar called it the "heart and soul" of the Constitution.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 32 = SC writ (FR enforcement); Art 226 = HC writ (FR + other rights); 5 writs: Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Certiorari, Quo Warranto.

### Q232 · Indian Economy

The Goods and Services Tax (GST) was implemented from:

A. 1 April 2017
B. 1 July 2017
C. 1 January 2018
D. 1 April 2018

**Answer:** B. 1 July 2017
**Explanation:** GST was implemented from 1 July 2017 through the 101st Constitutional Amendment Act. It subsumed 17 indirect taxes and 23 cesses into one unified tax.
**Shortcut / rule:** GST = 1 July 2017; 101st Amendment; "One Nation, One Tax, One Market"; GST Council chaired by Finance Minister.

### Q233 · Physics

The instrument used to measure the depth of the ocean is:

A. Altimeter
B. Fathometer
C. Barometer
D. Anemometer

**Answer:** B. Fathometer
**Explanation:** A Fathometer (or Echo Sounder) measures ocean depth using sound waves (SONAR principle). It sends sound pulses and measures the time for the echo to return.
**Shortcut / rule:** Fathometer = ocean depth; Altimeter = altitude; Anemometer = wind speed; Seismograph = earthquake.

### Q234 · Chemistry

The noble gas used in advertising signs (neon signs) is:

A. Helium
B. Neon
C. Argon
D. Krypton

**Answer:** B. Neon
**Explanation:** Neon gas emits a bright reddish-orange glow when electrically excited, making it ideal for illuminated signs. Other noble gases produce different colours.
**Shortcut / rule:** Neon = red-orange signs; Helium = balloons/airships; Argon = welding; Krypton = flash photography.

### Q235 · Biology / Life Science

Which part of the brain controls balance and coordination?

A. Cerebrum
B. Cerebellum
C. Medulla oblongata
D. Hypothalamus

**Answer:** B. Cerebellum
**Explanation:** The Cerebellum (little brain) controls balance, posture, and coordination of voluntary movements. It is located at the back of the brain below the cerebrum.
**Shortcut / rule:** Cerebrum = thinking/memory; Cerebellum = balance; Medulla = involuntary (breathing, heartbeat); Hypothalamus = temperature/hunger.

### Q236 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Which dance form is associated with Manipur?

A. Bihu
B. Manipuri
C. Kathakali
D. Bharatanatyam

**Answer:** B. Manipuri
**Explanation:** Manipuri dance (also called Raas Leela) is a major Indian classical dance form from Manipur. It depicts scenes from the life of Lord Krishna and Radha.
**Shortcut / rule:** Manipuri = Manipur; Bihu = Assam (folk dance); Sattriya = Assam (classical); Lavani = Maharashtra.

### Q237 · Games and Sports

The Olympics are held every:

A. 2 years
B. 3 years
C. 4 years
D. 5 years

**Answer:** C. 4 years
**Explanation:** The Summer and Winter Olympic Games are each held every 4 years. They are staggered so an Olympic event occurs every 2 years. The IOC organizes the games.
**Shortcut / rule:** Olympics = every 4 years; IOC = International Olympic Committee (Lausanne); Olympic motto = "Faster, Higher, Stronger — Together."

### Q238 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who is the author of "Wings of Fire"?

A. Vikram Sarabhai
B. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
C. Jawaharlal Nehru
D. Amartya Sen

**Answer:** B. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
**Explanation:** "Wings of Fire" is the autobiography of Dr. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam, written with Arun Tiwari. It chronicles his life from a humble background to becoming a leading scientist and President.
**Shortcut / rule:** Wings of Fire = Kalam (autobiography); India 2020 = Kalam; Ignited Minds = Kalam.

### Q239 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's lunar mission Chandrayaan-1 was launched in which year?

A. 2006
B. 2008
C. 2010
D. 2013

**Answer:** B. 2008
**Explanation:** Chandrayaan-1 was launched on 22 October 2008 from Sriharikota using PSLV-C11. It discovered evidence of water molecules on the Moon's surface using the Moon Impact Probe (MIP).
**Shortcut / rule:** Chandrayaan-1 = 2008 (water discovery); Chandrayaan-2 = 2019 (orbiter success); Chandrayaan-3 = 2023 (soft landing).

### Q240 · UN & World Organizations

NATO stands for:

A. North Atlantic Treaty Organization
B. North Asian Trade Organization
C. National Alliance for Trade Operations
D. North American Treaty Organization

**Answer:** A. North Atlantic Treaty Organization
**Explanation:** NATO is a military alliance formed on 4 April 1949. Its headquarters is in Brussels, Belgium. It was formed for collective defense against the Soviet Union.
**Shortcut / rule:** NATO = 1949 (Brussels); Warsaw Pact = 1955 (Soviet bloc, dissolved 1991); NATO has 32 members (2024).

### Q241 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Sundarbans is a habitat of which critically important species?

A. Asiatic Lion
B. Royal Bengal Tiger
C. Snow Leopard
D. Indian Rhinoceros

**Answer:** B. Royal Bengal Tiger
**Explanation:** The Sundarbans is famous for the Royal Bengal Tiger that has adapted to swimming and mangrove habitats. It is the largest tiger reserve in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sundarbans = Royal Bengal Tiger (mangrove) + Gangetic Dolphin; Gir = Asiatic Lion; Kaziranga = Indian Rhino.

### Q242 · Computers & Applications

MS-Excel is primarily used for:

A. Word processing
B. Spreadsheet/data analysis
C. Presentation making
D. Database management

**Answer:** B. Spreadsheet/data analysis
**Explanation:** Microsoft Excel is a spreadsheet application used for data entry, calculation, analysis, and visualization using formulas, charts, and pivot tables.
**Shortcut / rule:** Excel = spreadsheet (.xlsx); Word = documents (.docx); PowerPoint = presentations (.pptx); Access = database (.accdb).

### Q243 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does NHAI stand for?

A. National Highway Authority of India
B. National Highways Act Implementation
C. National Highway and Infrastructure Authority
D. National Highways Association of India

**Answer:** A. National Highway Authority of India
**Explanation:** NHAI was established in 1988 under the NHAI Act. It is responsible for the development, maintenance, and management of National Highways in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** NHAI = National Highways (1988); NHPC = Hydroelectric Power; NTPC = Thermal Power.

### Q244 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Digital India Programme was launched in which year?

A. 2014
B. 2015
C. 2016
D. 2017

**Answer:** B. 2015
**Explanation:** Digital India was launched on 1 July 2015 to transform India into a digitally empowered society and knowledge economy. Key initiatives include BharatNet, DigiLocker, and UMANG.
**Shortcut / rule:** Digital India = 1 July 2015; Pillars: digital infrastructure, governance, digital empowerment.

### Q245 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Ryotwari System of land revenue was introduced by:

A. Lord Cornwallis
B. Thomas Munro
C. Warren Hastings
D. Lord Wellesley

**Answer:** B. Thomas Munro
**Explanation:** The Ryotwari System was introduced by Thomas Munro and Captain Read in Madras Presidency. Under this system, individual cultivators (ryots) directly paid revenue to the government.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ryotwari = Thomas Munro (Madras); Permanent = Cornwallis (Bengal); Mahalwari = Holt Mackenzie (NW Provinces).

### Q246 · Indian & World Geography

Which river forms the boundary between India and Pakistan in Punjab?

A. Jhelum
B. Chenab
C. Ravi
D. Sutlej

**Answer:** C. Ravi
**Explanation:** The Ravi River forms part of the India-Pakistan border in Punjab. Under the Indus Water Treaty (1960), India has rights over the eastern rivers (Ravi, Beas, Sutlej).
**Shortcut / rule:** Indus Water Treaty 1960: India = Ravi, Beas, Sutlej; Pakistan = Indus, Jhelum, Chenab; Brokered by World Bank.

### Q247 · Indian Polity & Governance

Anti-Defection Law is contained in which Schedule of the Constitution?

A. Eighth Schedule
B. Ninth Schedule
C. Tenth Schedule
D. Eleventh Schedule

**Answer:** C. Tenth Schedule
**Explanation:** The Anti-Defection Law was added by the 52nd Amendment Act (1985) as the Tenth Schedule. It disqualifies members who defect from their party.
**Shortcut / rule:** 10th Schedule = Anti-Defection (52nd Amendment, 1985); Decision by Speaker/Chairman; Reviewed by judiciary.

### Q248 · Indian Economy

What is Fiscal Deficit?

A. Total revenue minus total expenditure
B. Total expenditure minus total receipts excluding borrowings
C. Revenue receipts minus revenue expenditure
D. Capital expenditure minus capital receipts

**Answer:** B. Total expenditure minus total receipts excluding borrowings
**Explanation:** Fiscal Deficit = Total Expenditure − (Revenue Receipts + Non-debt Capital Receipts). It indicates the total borrowing requirements of the government.
**Shortcut / rule:** Fiscal Deficit = borrowing needed; Revenue Deficit = revenue gap; Primary Deficit = Fiscal Deficit − Interest payments.

### Q249 · Physics

Total internal reflection is the principle behind:

A. Telescope
B. Microscope
C. Optical Fiber
D. Periscope

**Answer:** C. Optical Fiber
**Explanation:** Optical fibers use total internal reflection to transmit light signals over long distances. Light enters the fiber and bounces along the core due to total internal reflection.
**Shortcut / rule:** Optical fiber = total internal reflection; Mirage = total internal reflection; Periscope = plane mirror reflection.

### Q250 · Chemistry

Which acid is present in the stomach for digestion?

A. Sulphuric acid
B. Acetic acid
C. Hydrochloric acid
D. Nitric acid

**Answer:** C. Hydrochloric acid
**Explanation:** The stomach secretes Hydrochloric acid (HCl) to aid digestion and kill bacteria. The stomach lining is protected from the acid by a thick layer of mucus.
**Shortcut / rule:** Stomach acid = HCl (pH 1-2); Excess acid = acidity/ulcer; Antacids (like Mg(OH)₂) neutralize it.

### Q251 · Biology / Life Science

Which hormone is known as the "emergency hormone"?

A. Insulin
B. Thyroxine
C. Adrenaline
D. Testosterone

**Answer:** C. Adrenaline
**Explanation:** Adrenaline (Epinephrine) is called the emergency/fight-or-flight hormone. It is secreted by the adrenal glands (medulla) during stress, increasing heart rate, blood pressure, and energy supply.
**Shortcut / rule:** Adrenaline = emergency (adrenal gland); Insulin = sugar (pancreas); Thyroxine = metabolism (thyroid).

### Q252 · Biology / Life Science

The functional unit of the kidney is:

A. Neuron
B. Nephron
C. Alveolus
D. Villus

**Answer:** B. Nephron
**Explanation:** The Nephron is the structural and functional unit of the kidney. Each kidney has about 1 million nephrons that filter blood and produce urine.
**Shortcut / rule:** Nephron = kidney; Neuron = nervous system; Alveolus = lungs; Sarcomere = muscle.

### Q253 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who presided over the Constituent Assembly of India?

A. Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
B. Dr. Rajendra Prasad
C. Jawaharlal Nehru
D. Sardar Patel

**Answer:** B. Dr. Rajendra Prasad
**Explanation:** Dr. Rajendra Prasad was the permanent President of the Constituent Assembly. Dr. Sachidanand Sinha was the temporary/provisional chairman. The Assembly met from 9 December 1946.
**Shortcut / rule:** CA President = Rajendra Prasad; Drafting Committee Chairman = Ambedkar; CA first met = 9 Dec 1946.

### Q254 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Poona Pact (1932) was between:

A. Congress and Muslim League
B. Gandhi and Ambedkar
C. Gandhi and Jinnah
D. Nehru and Patel

**Answer:** B. Gandhi and Ambedkar
**Explanation:** The Poona Pact (24 September 1932) was signed between Mahatma Gandhi and Dr. B.R. Ambedkar. It replaced the separate electorates for Depressed Classes with reserved seats under joint electorate.
**Shortcut / rule:** Poona Pact = Gandhi + Ambedkar (1932); Communal Award by Ramsay MacDonald preceded it; Gandhi fasted against separate electorates.

### Q255 · Indian & World Geography

The Chilika Lake is located in which state?

A. West Bengal
B. Odisha
C. Andhra Pradesh
D. Tamil Nadu

**Answer:** B. Odisha
**Explanation:** Chilika Lake is the largest brackish water lagoon in Asia, located in Odisha. It is a Ramsar site and home to Irrawaddy Dolphins and many migratory birds.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chilika = Odisha (largest brackish); Wular = J&K (largest freshwater); Vembanad = Kerala (longest lake).

### Q256 · Indian & World Geography

Which state is known as the "Spice Garden of India"?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Karnataka
C. Kerala
D. Andhra Pradesh

**Answer:** C. Kerala
**Explanation:** Kerala is called the "Spice Garden of India" due to its large-scale production of spices like pepper, cardamom, cinnamon, cloves, and nutmeg.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kerala = Spice Garden; Meghalaya = Abode of Clouds; Assam = Land of Red River & Blue Hills; Punjab = Land of Five Rivers.

### Q257 · Indian Polity & Governance

Money Bill can be introduced only in:

A. Rajya Sabha
B. Lok Sabha
C. Either House
D. Joint Session

**Answer:** B. Lok Sabha
**Explanation:** Under Article 109, a Money Bill can be introduced only in the Lok Sabha and only on the recommendation of the President. Rajya Sabha can only suggest amendments within 14 days.
**Shortcut / rule:** Money Bill = Lok Sabha only; Rajya Sabha = 14 days to suggest; Ordinary Bill = either house.

### Q258 · Indian Polity & Governance

Right to Information (RTI) Act was enacted in which year?

A. 2003
B. 2005
C. 2007
D. 2010

**Answer:** B. 2005
**Explanation:** The Right to Information Act was enacted on 15 June 2005 and came into force on 12 October 2005. It empowers citizens to seek information from public authorities.
**Shortcut / rule:** RTI Act = 2005; Information Commission = Central + State; Reply within 30 days; Fee = ₹10.

### Q259 · Indian Economy

What was the objective of LPG reforms of 1991?

A. Land, Population, Growth
B. Liberalization, Privatization, Globalization
C. Literacy, Productivity, Governance
D. Labour, Production, Growth

**Answer:** B. Liberalization, Privatization, Globalization
**Explanation:** The LPG reforms were introduced by PM P.V. Narasimha Rao and Finance Minister Manmohan Singh in 1991 to open up the Indian economy, reduce government control, and integrate with the global market.
**Shortcut / rule:** LPG 1991: PM = Narasimha Rao; FM = Manmohan Singh; Triggered by BoP crisis; Industrial Policy 1991.

### Q260 · Physics

Archimedes' principle is related to:

A. Gravity
B. Buoyancy
C. Magnetism
D. Electricity

**Answer:** B. Buoyancy
**Explanation:** Archimedes' Principle states that when a body is immersed in a fluid, it experiences an upward buoyant force equal to the weight of the fluid displaced by the body.
**Shortcut / rule:** Archimedes = buoyancy; Why ships float = buoyant force > weight; Hydrometer works on this principle.

### Q261 · Physics

The resistance of a conductor is measured in:

A. Ampere
B. Volt
C. Ohm
D. Watt

**Answer:** C. Ohm
**Explanation:** Resistance is measured in Ohm (Ω). Ohm's Law: V = IR (Voltage = Current × Resistance). The symbol Ω is named after Georg Simon Ohm.
**Shortcut / rule:** Resistance = Ohm; Conductance = Siemens (1/Ohm); Resistivity = Ohm·meter.

### Q262 · Chemistry

Bleaching powder is chemically:

A. Calcium Hypochlorite
B. Sodium Hypochlorite
C. Calcium Carbonate
D. Sodium Bicarbonate

**Answer:** A. Calcium Hypochlorite
**Explanation:** Bleaching powder is Calcium Hypochlorite [Ca(OCl)₂ or CaOCl₂]. It is made by passing chlorine gas through dry slaked lime. It is used for water purification and bleaching.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bleaching powder = Ca(OCl)₂; Slaked lime = Ca(OH)₂; Quick lime = CaO.

### Q263 · Chemistry

The gas used in the manufacture of ammonia by Haber's Process is:

A. Oxygen and Hydrogen
B. Nitrogen and Hydrogen
C. Carbon dioxide and Hydrogen
D. Nitrogen and Oxygen

**Answer:** B. Nitrogen and Hydrogen
**Explanation:** Haber's Process: N₂ + 3H₂ → 2NH₃ (at high temperature 400-500°C, high pressure 200 atm, and iron catalyst). Ammonia is used in making fertilizers.
**Shortcut / rule:** Haber = N₂ + H₂ → NH₃; Contact Process = SO₃ (H₂SO₄); Ostwald Process = HNO₃.

### Q264 · Biology / Life Science

Which blood component helps in clotting?

A. Red Blood Cells
B. White Blood Cells
C. Platelets (Thrombocytes)
D. Plasma

**Answer:** C. Platelets (Thrombocytes)
**Explanation:** Platelets (thrombocytes) are cell fragments that play a crucial role in blood clotting (coagulation). They aggregate at wound sites and release clotting factors.
**Shortcut / rule:** Platelets = clotting; RBC = O₂ transport; WBC = immunity; Plasma = 55% of blood (liquid part).

### Q265 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Hampi, the capital of Vijayanagara Empire, is in which state?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Andhra Pradesh
C. Karnataka
D. Kerala

**Answer:** C. Karnataka
**Explanation:** Hampi is located in Bellary (Vijayanagara) district of Karnataka. It was the capital of the Vijayanagara Empire from the 14th to 16th century. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hampi = Karnataka; Fatehpur Sikri = UP (Akbar); Golconda Fort = Telangana; Mysore Palace = Karnataka.

### Q266 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Sanchi Stupa was built by:

A. Chandragupta Maurya
B. Ashoka
C. Kanishka
D. Harsha

**Answer:** B. Ashoka
**Explanation:** The Great Stupa at Sanchi was originally built by Emperor Ashoka in the 3rd century BC to enshrine relics of Buddha. It is located in Madhya Pradesh and is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sanchi Stupa = Ashoka (MP); Four lions capital = Ashoka (Sarnath); Ashoka's edicts spread Buddhism.

### Q267 · Games and Sports

The term "Grand Slam" in tennis refers to winning:

A. 3 major tournaments
B. 4 major tournaments in a calendar year
C. Any 2 major tournaments
D. 5 major tournaments

**Answer:** B. 4 major tournaments in a calendar year
**Explanation:** A Grand Slam in tennis means winning all four major tournaments (Australian Open, French Open, Wimbledon, US Open) in a single calendar year.
**Shortcut / rule:** 4 Grand Slams: AO (hard), FO (clay), Wimbledon (grass), USO (hard); Calendar Grand Slam = all 4 same year.

### Q268 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Bharat Ratna is India's:

A. Highest military decoration
B. Highest civilian award
C. Highest sports award
D. Highest literary award

**Answer:** B. Highest civilian award
**Explanation:** Bharat Ratna is India's highest civilian honor, instituted in 1954. It recognizes exceptional service in any field excluding sports (sports was added in 2011).
**Shortcut / rule:** Bharat Ratna = highest civilian (1954); Padma Vibhushan > Padma Bhushan > Padma Shri; First recipient = C. Rajagopalachari.

### Q269 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

The Bhabha Atomic Research Centre (BARC) is located in:

A. Bengaluru
B. Mumbai (Trombay)
C. Hyderabad
D. Chennai

**Answer:** B. Mumbai (Trombay)
**Explanation:** BARC is located at Trombay, Mumbai. It was established in 1954 as the Atomic Energy Establishment, Trombay (AEET) and renamed BARC after Homi J. Bhabha's death in 1966.
**Shortcut / rule:** BARC = Mumbai (Trombay); ISRO HQ = Bengaluru; DRDO HQ = New Delhi; IGCAR = Kalpakkam.

### Q270 · UN & World Organizations

The Red Cross was founded by:

A. Florence Nightingale
B. Henry Dunant
C. Mother Teresa
D. Albert Schweitzer

**Answer:** B. Henry Dunant
**Explanation:** The International Red Cross was founded by Henry Dunant of Switzerland. He was inspired by the suffering of soldiers at the Battle of Solferino (1859). He received the first Nobel Peace Prize in 1901.
**Shortcut / rule:** Red Cross = Henry Dunant (Switzerland, 1863); HQ = Geneva; World Red Cross Day = 8 May (Dunant's birthday).

### Q271 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Project Tiger was launched in India in which year?

A. 1970
B. 1972
C. 1973
D. 1975

**Answer:** C. 1973
**Explanation:** Project Tiger was launched on 1 April 1973 to protect the Bengal Tiger and its habitat. Jim Corbett National Park was the first tiger reserve under this project.
**Shortcut / rule:** Project Tiger = 1973; Project Elephant = 1992; National Tiger Conservation Authority (NTCA) = 2005.

### Q272 · Computers & Applications

Which device is used to connect a computer to the internet via telephone lines?

A. Router
B. Modem
C. Switch
D. Hub

**Answer:** B. Modem
**Explanation:** A Modem (Modulator-Demodulator) converts digital signals to analog for transmission over telephone lines and vice versa. It enables internet connectivity.
**Shortcut / rule:** Modem = digital ↔ analog; Router = directs network traffic; Switch = connects devices in LAN.

### Q273 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What is the full form of SBI?

A. State Bank of India
B. Standard Bank of India
C. Savings Bank of India
D. Secondary Bank of India

**Answer:** A. State Bank of India
**Explanation:** SBI is India's largest public sector bank. It was established on 1 July 1955 (originally Imperial Bank of India, nationalized). Its headquarters is in Mumbai.
**Shortcut / rule:** SBI = largest PSU bank (1955); Imperial Bank → SBI; RBI nationalized = 1949.

### Q274 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Atal Pension Yojana is aimed at:

A. Rural housing
B. Old age pension for unorganized sector
C. Education loans
D. Crop insurance

**Answer:** B. Old age pension for unorganized sector
**Explanation:** Atal Pension Yojana was launched on 9 May 2015 to provide old age income security to workers in the unorganized sector. It guarantees a minimum pension of ₹1,000-₹5,000 per month after age 60.
**Shortcut / rule:** Atal Pension = unorganized workers (2015); PM Mudra = micro loans; PM Jeevan Jyoti = life insurance.

### Q275 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Round Table Conferences were held in:

A. Delhi
B. Calcutta
C. London
D. Paris

**Answer:** C. London
**Explanation:** Three Round Table Conferences were held in London (1930-31, 1931, 1932) to discuss constitutional reforms for India. Gandhi attended only the Second RTC.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st RTC = 1930 (Gandhi absent); 2nd RTC = 1931 (Gandhi attended); 3rd RTC = 1932 (Gandhi absent); All in London.

### Q276 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Doctrine of Lapse was introduced by:

A. Lord Wellesley
B. Lord Dalhousie
C. Lord Cornwallis
D. Lord Canning

**Answer:** B. Lord Dalhousie
**Explanation:** Lord Dalhousie introduced the Doctrine of Lapse (1848-1856), which annexed princely states where rulers died without a natural male heir. States like Satara, Jhansi, and Nagpur were annexed.
**Shortcut / rule:** Doctrine of Lapse = Dalhousie; Subsidiary Alliance = Wellesley; Permanent Settlement = Cornwallis.

### Q277 · Indian & World Geography

Which country has the maximum number of time zones?

A. Russia
B. USA
C. France
D. China

**Answer:** C. France
**Explanation:** France has 12 time zones (including overseas territories), the most of any country. Russia has 11 time zones. India has only one time zone (IST = UTC+5:30).
**Shortcut / rule:** Most time zones: France (12) > Russia (11) > USA (9); India = single time zone (IST, Mirzapur).

### Q278 · Indian Polity & Governance

The 73rd Constitutional Amendment is related to:

A. Municipalities
B. Panchayati Raj
C. Anti-Defection Law
D. Scheduled Areas

**Answer:** B. Panchayati Raj
**Explanation:** The 73rd Amendment (1992) gave constitutional status to Panchayati Raj Institutions (PRIs). It added Part IX and 11th Schedule to the Constitution. It introduced a 3-tier system: Gram Panchayat, Panchayat Samiti, Zila Parishad.
**Shortcut / rule:** 73rd = Panchayati Raj (rural, 1992); 74th = Municipalities (urban, 1992).

### Q279 · Indian Economy

Which bank was the first to be nationalized in India?

A. State Bank of India
B. Reserve Bank of India
C. Imperial Bank of India
D. Punjab National Bank

**Answer:** B. Reserve Bank of India
**Explanation:** RBI was the first bank nationalized in India in 1949. Then Imperial Bank of India was nationalized to form SBI in 1955. 14 major commercial banks were nationalized in 1969.
**Shortcut / rule:** RBI nationalized = 1949; SBI formed = 1955; 14 banks nationalized = 1969; 6 banks nationalized = 1980.

### Q280 · Physics

The phenomenon of scattering of light by colloidal particles is called:

A. Raman Effect
B. Tyndall Effect
C. Photoelectric Effect
D. Compton Effect

**Answer:** B. Tyndall Effect
**Explanation:** The Tyndall Effect is the scattering of light by colloidal particles, making the beam of light visible. Example: light passing through fog or smoke.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tyndall = colloids scatter light; Rayleigh scattering = sky appears blue; Raman = inelastic scattering.

### Q281 · Chemistry

The gas responsible for depletion of the ozone layer is:

A. Carbon dioxide
B. Chlorofluorocarbon (CFC)
C. Methane
D. Sulphur dioxide

**Answer:** B. Chlorofluorocarbon (CFC)
**Explanation:** CFCs release chlorine atoms in the stratosphere, which catalytically destroy ozone (O₃) molecules. One Cl atom can destroy thousands of ozone molecules.
**Shortcut / rule:** CFC = ozone depletion; Montreal Protocol (1987) = phase out CFCs; Ozone hole = Antarctica.

### Q282 · Biology / Life Science

Which element is essential for thyroid hormone production?

A. Iron
B. Iodine
C. Calcium
D. Potassium

**Answer:** B. Iodine
**Explanation:** Iodine is essential for the production of thyroid hormones (T3 and T4). Iodine deficiency causes Goitre (enlarged thyroid gland). Iodized salt prevents this deficiency.
**Shortcut / rule:** Iodine = thyroid (deficiency = goitre); Iron = hemoglobin (deficiency = anemia); Calcium = bones.

### Q283 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Bharatanatyam classical dance originates from which state?

A. Kerala
B. Tamil Nadu
C. Andhra Pradesh
D. Karnataka

**Answer:** B. Tamil Nadu
**Explanation:** Bharatanatyam is one of the oldest classical dance forms, originating in Tamil Nadu. It was traditionally performed in Hindu temples as a form of worship (Devadasi system).
**Shortcut / rule:** Bharatanatyam = TN; Kathak = UP/North; Odissi = Odisha; Kathakali = Kerala; Kuchipudi = AP; Manipuri = Manipur; Sattriya = Assam; Mohiniyattam = Kerala.

### Q284 · Games and Sports

The Dronacharya Award is given for excellence in:

A. Sports performance
B. Sports coaching
C. Sports journalism
D. Lifetime achievement in sports

**Answer:** B. Sports coaching
**Explanation:** The Dronacharya Award is given to outstanding sports coaches in India. It is named after Dronacharya, the royal guru of Pandavas and Kauravas in the Mahabharata.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dronacharya = coaching; Arjuna = sports performance; Khel Ratna = highest sports honor; Dhyan Chand = lifetime achievement.

### Q285 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Satyajit Ray was famous as a:

A. Scientist
B. Film Director
C. Musician
D. Painter

**Answer:** B. Film Director
**Explanation:** Satyajit Ray was a legendary Indian film director known for the Apu Trilogy (Pather Panchali, Aparajito, Apur Sansar). He received an honorary Oscar in 1992 and the Bharat Ratna in 1992.
