### Q286 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

Aditya-L1 mission is related to the study of:

A. Moon
B. Mars
C. Sun
D. Jupiter

**Answer:** C. Sun
**Explanation:** Aditya-L1 is India's first space mission to study the Sun. Launched on 2 September 2023, it reached the L1 Lagrange point (1.5 million km from Earth) on 6 January 2024.
**Shortcut / rule:** Aditya-L1 = Sun; Chandrayaan = Moon; Mangalyaan = Mars; L1 point = stable orbit between Sun-Earth.

### Q287 · UN & World Organizations

ASEAN stands for:

A. Association of South East Asian Nations
B. Alliance of South East Asian Nationalities
C. Association of South and East Asian Networks
D. Asian Strategic Economic Alliance Nations

**Answer:** A. Association of South East Asian Nations
**Explanation:** ASEAN was established on 8 August 1967 in Bangkok. It has 10 member states. Its secretariat is in Jakarta, Indonesia.
**Shortcut / rule:** ASEAN = 10 members (Jakarta HQ, 1967); SAARC = 8 members (Kathmandu, 1985); BRICS = 5 original + new members.

### Q288 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The concept of "Carbon Credit" is associated with:

A. Banking sector
B. Climate change mitigation
C. Forest conservation
D. Water management

**Answer:** B. Climate change mitigation
**Explanation:** Carbon credits are tradable permits that allow the holder to emit a certain amount of CO₂. One credit = one tonne of CO₂. It is a market-based mechanism under the Kyoto Protocol.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1 Carbon Credit = 1 tonne CO₂; Kyoto Protocol introduced trading; Clean Development Mechanism (CDM).

### Q289 · Computers & Applications

The shortcut key for Copy in Windows is:

A. Ctrl + X
B. Ctrl + V
C. Ctrl + C
D. Ctrl + Z

**Answer:** C. Ctrl + C
**Explanation:** Ctrl+C is the universal shortcut for Copy in Windows. Ctrl+X = Cut, Ctrl+V = Paste, Ctrl+Z = Undo, Ctrl+A = Select All.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ctrl+C = Copy; Ctrl+X = Cut; Ctrl+V = Paste; Ctrl+Z = Undo; Ctrl+S = Save.

### Q290 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does RADAR stand for?

A. Radio Detection And Ranging
B. Radio Direction And Receiving
C. Rapid Detection And Ranging
D. Remote Automated Detection And Response

**Answer:** A. Radio Detection And Ranging
**Explanation:** RADAR uses radio waves to detect objects, determine their distance, speed, and direction. It was developed during World War II and is used in aviation, weather, and defense.
**Shortcut / rule:** RADAR = radio waves; SONAR = sound waves (underwater); LIDAR = laser light.

### Q291 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

PM Kisan Samman Nidhi provides:

A. ₹3,000 per year to all farmers
B. ₹6,000 per year in 3 installments
C. ₹10,000 per year to marginal farmers
D. ₹12,000 per year in 4 installments

**Answer:** B. ₹6,000 per year in 3 installments
**Explanation:** PM-KISAN provides ₹6,000 per year (₹2,000 in 3 equal installments) to small and marginal farmer families. Launched on 24 February 2019.
**Shortcut / rule:** PM-KISAN = ₹6,000/year (₹2,000 × 3); Launched Feb 2019; Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT).

### Q292 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Khilafat Movement (1919-1924) was led by:

A. Gandhi and Jinnah
B. Ali Brothers (Muhammad Ali and Shaukat Ali)
C. Abul Kalam Azad and Nehru
D. Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan

**Answer:** B. Ali Brothers (Muhammad Ali and Shaukat Ali)
**Explanation:** The Khilafat Movement was led by Muhammad Ali Jouhar and Shaukat Ali to protest against British treatment of the Ottoman Caliphate after WWI. Gandhi supported it and linked it with the Non-Cooperation Movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** Khilafat = Ali Brothers + Gandhi support; Merged with Non-Cooperation (1920); Ended after Turkey abolished Caliphate (1924).

### Q293 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Chandragupta II (Vikramaditya) was a ruler of which dynasty?

A. Maurya Dynasty
B. Gupta Dynasty
C. Chola Dynasty
D. Mughal Dynasty

**Answer:** B. Gupta Dynasty
**Explanation:** Chandragupta II (Vikramaditya) ruled the Gupta Empire from 375-415 AD. His court had the famous "Navratnas" (Nine Gems) including Kalidasa. The Gupta period is called the "Golden Age of India."
**Shortcut / rule:** Gupta = Golden Age; Chandragupta II = Vikramaditya; Navratnas included Kalidasa, Varahamihira, Aryabhata.

### Q294 · Indian & World Geography

The Thar Desert is located primarily in which state?

A. Gujarat
B. Rajasthan
C. Madhya Pradesh
D. Haryana

**Answer:** B. Rajasthan
**Explanation:** The Thar Desert (Great Indian Desert) covers about 2 lakh km² and is primarily in Rajasthan (about 60%) with parts in Gujarat, Punjab, and Haryana. It is the most densely populated desert in the world.
**Shortcut / rule:** Thar = Rajasthan (mostly); Most populated desert; Luni = only river in Thar; Sambhar Lake = largest salt lake.

### Q295 · Indian Polity & Governance

Emergency provisions in the Indian Constitution are borrowed from:

A. USA
B. UK
C. Germany (Weimar Constitution)
D. Canada

**Answer:** C. Germany (Weimar Constitution)
**Explanation:** Emergency provisions (Articles 352-360) were inspired by the Weimar Constitution of Germany. Three types: National Emergency (Art 352), State Emergency (Art 356), Financial Emergency (Art 360).
**Shortcut / rule:** Emergency = Germany; DPSP = Ireland; FR = USA; Federal with unitary bias = Canada; Concurrent List = Australia.

### Q296 · Indian Economy

National Income of India is calculated by:

A. RBI
B. NITI Aayog
C. National Statistical Office (NSO)
D. Ministry of Finance

**Answer:** C. National Statistical Office (NSO)
**Explanation:** The National Statistical Office (formerly Central Statistics Office) calculates National Income. It publishes advance estimates, revised estimates, and final figures.
**Shortcut / rule:** National Income = NSO; Index of Industrial Production (IIP) = NSO; CPI = NSO; WPI = Office of Economic Adviser (Commerce Ministry).

### Q297 · Physics

Which scientist discovered the Law of Gravitation?

A. Albert Einstein
B. Isaac Newton
C. Galileo Galilei
D. Archimedes

**Answer:** B. Isaac Newton
**Explanation:** Sir Isaac Newton discovered the Universal Law of Gravitation (1687). It states that every mass attracts every other mass with a force proportional to the product of their masses and inversely proportional to the square of the distance between them.
**Shortcut / rule:** Newton = Gravity + 3 Laws of Motion; Einstein = Relativity; Galileo = telescope/falling bodies.

### Q298 · Chemistry

Brass is an alloy of:

A. Copper and Tin
B. Copper and Zinc
C. Iron and Carbon
D. Aluminium and Copper

**Answer:** B. Copper and Zinc
**Explanation:** Brass is an alloy of Copper (Cu) and Zinc (Zn). It is used in making utensils, musical instruments, and decorative items.
**Shortcut / rule:** Brass = Cu + Zn; Bronze = Cu + Sn (Tin); Steel = Fe + C; Stainless Steel = Fe + Cr + Ni.

### Q299 · Biology / Life Science

Which type of tissue covers the outer surface of the body?

A. Connective tissue
B. Muscular tissue
C. Epithelial tissue
D. Nervous tissue

**Answer:** C. Epithelial tissue
**Explanation:** Epithelial tissue covers the outer surface of the body (skin) and lines internal organs and cavities. It protects against mechanical injury, pathogens, and fluid loss.
**Shortcut / rule:** Epithelial = covering/lining; Connective = support (bone, blood, cartilage); Muscular = movement; Nervous = signal transmission.

### Q300 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Indus Valley Civilization is also known as:

A. Vedic Civilization
B. Harappan Civilization
C. Aryan Civilization
D. Dravidian Civilization

**Answer:** B. Harappan Civilization
**Explanation:** The Indus Valley Civilization (c. 3300-1300 BC) is also called the Harappan Civilization because Harappa was the first site to be discovered (1921 by Daya Ram Sahni). Mohenjo-daro was discovered in 1922 by R.D. Banerji.
**Shortcut / rule:** Harappa = Daya Ram Sahni (1921); Mohenjo-daro = R.D. Banerji (1922); Lothal = Gujarat (dockyard); Great Bath = Mohenjo-daro.

### Q301 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian state has the largest area?

A. Madhya Pradesh
B. Maharashtra
C. Rajasthan
D. Uttar Pradesh

**Answer:** C. Rajasthan
**Explanation:** Rajasthan is the largest state of India by area (342,239 km²), followed by Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, and Uttar Pradesh.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest area = Rajasthan; Largest population = UP; Smallest area = Goa; Smallest population = Sikkim.

### Q302 · Indian Polity & Governance

Fundamental Rights are enshrined in which Part of the Indian Constitution?

A. Part II
B. Part III
C. Part IV
D. Part V

**Answer:** B. Part III
**Explanation:** Part III (Articles 12-35) contains the Fundamental Rights. Originally there were 7 FRs; now 6 remain after the 44th Amendment removed Right to Property.
**Shortcut / rule:** Part III = FR; Part IV = DPSP; Part IV-A = FD; Right to Property now a legal right under Art 300A.

### Q303 · Indian Economy

Which body recommends the Minimum Support Price (MSP) for crops in India?

A. NITI Aayog
B. CACP (Commission for Agricultural Costs & Prices)
C. FCI
D. NABARD

**Answer:** B. CACP (Commission for Agricultural Costs & Prices)
**Explanation:** CACP recommends MSP for 23 crops to the government. The final decision on MSP is taken by the Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA).
**Shortcut / rule:** CACP = MSP recommendation; FCI = procurement & storage; NABARD = rural credit; APMC = mandis.

### Q304 · Physics

The SI unit of luminous intensity is:

A. Lumen
B. Candela
C. Lux
D. Watt

**Answer:** B. Candela
**Explanation:** Candela (cd) is one of the 7 SI base units. It measures luminous intensity — how much light is emitted in a particular direction.
**Shortcut / rule:** 7 SI base units: meter, kilogram, second, ampere, kelvin, mole, candela.

### Q305 · Physics

Which phenomenon explains why a straw appears bent in water?

A. Reflection
B. Refraction
C. Diffraction
D. Total internal reflection

**Answer:** B. Refraction
**Explanation:** Refraction is the bending of light as it passes from one medium to another (water to air) due to a change in speed. This makes the straw appear bent or broken at the water surface.
**Shortcut / rule:** Refraction = bending of light (speed change); Snell's Law: n₁ sin θ₁ = n₂ sin θ₂.

### Q306 · Chemistry

What is the chemical name of Vitamin C?

A. Retinol
B. Thiamine
C. Ascorbic acid
D. Tocopherol

**Answer:** C. Ascorbic acid
**Explanation:** Vitamin C is Ascorbic acid. Its deficiency causes Scurvy (bleeding gums, weakness). It is found in citrus fruits, amla, guava, and tomatoes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vit A = Retinol; Vit B1 = Thiamine; Vit C = Ascorbic acid; Vit D = Calciferol; Vit E = Tocopherol; Vit K = Phylloquinone.

### Q307 · Chemistry

Which ore is used for the extraction of aluminium?

A. Haematite
B. Bauxite
C. Chalcopyrite
D. Galena

**Answer:** B. Bauxite
**Explanation:** Bauxite (Al₂O₃·2H₂O) is the primary ore of aluminium. Aluminium is extracted by Hall-Héroult process (electrolysis of alumina dissolved in cryolite).
**Shortcut / rule:** Bauxite = Aluminium; Haematite = Iron; Chalcopyrite = Copper; Galena = Lead; Cinnabar = Mercury.

### Q308 · Biology / Life Science

The normal body temperature of a healthy human being is:

A. 95°F
B. 98.6°F (37°C)
C. 100°F
D. 104°F

**Answer:** B. 98.6°F (37°C)
**Explanation:** The normal body temperature is 98.6°F or 37°C. It is regulated by the hypothalamus in the brain. Fever is usually above 100.4°F (38°C).
**Shortcut / rule:** Normal = 98.6°F / 37°C; Hypothalamus = thermostat; Fever > 100.4°F.

### Q309 · Biology / Life Science

The process of cell division that reduces chromosome number by half is:

A. Mitosis
B. Meiosis
C. Binary fission
D. Budding

**Answer:** B. Meiosis
**Explanation:** Meiosis is a type of cell division that produces gametes (sex cells) with half the chromosome number (haploid). Humans: 46 chromosomes → 23 in gametes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Meiosis = gametes (haploid, n); Mitosis = somatic cells (diploid, 2n); Humans: 46 chromosomes (23 pairs).

### Q310 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Shore Temple at Mahabalipuram was built by the:

A. Chola Dynasty
B. Pallava Dynasty
C. Pandya Dynasty
D. Chalukya Dynasty

**Answer:** B. Pallava Dynasty
**Explanation:** The Shore Temple at Mahabalipuram (Mamallapuram) was built by Narasimhavarman II (Rajasimha) of the Pallava dynasty in the 8th century AD. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mahabalipuram = Pallavas; Thanjavur = Cholas; Badami/Pattadakal = Chalukyas; Madurai = Pandyas.

### Q311 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The national emblem of India is adopted from the:

A. Ajanta Caves
B. Sarnath Lion Capital of Ashoka
C. Sanchi Stupa
D. Konark Temple

**Answer:** B. Sarnath Lion Capital of Ashoka
**Explanation:** India's national emblem is an adaptation of the Lion Capital of Ashoka at Sarnath. It was adopted on 26 January 1950. The base has the inscription "Satyameva Jayate" (Truth Alone Triumphs) from Mundaka Upanishad.
**Shortcut / rule:** National Emblem = Sarnath Lion Capital; "Satyameva Jayate" = Mundaka Upanishad; Adopted 26 Jan 1950.

### Q312 · Games and Sports

Which country has won the most FIFA World Cup titles?

A. Germany
B. Italy
C. Argentina
D. Brazil

**Answer:** D. Brazil
**Explanation:** Brazil has won the FIFA World Cup a record 5 times (1958, 1962, 1970, 1994, 2002). Germany and Italy have won it 4 times each.
**Shortcut / rule:** Brazil = 5; Germany = 4; Italy = 4; Argentina = 3; France = 2; Uruguay = 2.

### Q313 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Mother Teresa was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize in which year?

A. 1975
B. 1979
C. 1985
D. 1990

**Answer:** B. 1979
**Explanation:** Mother Teresa received the Nobel Peace Prize in 1979 for her work among the poorest in Kolkata. She founded the Missionaries of Charity in 1950 and was canonized as Saint Teresa in 2016.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mother Teresa: Nobel Peace = 1979; Bharat Ratna = 1980; Canonized = 2016; Missionaries of Charity = 1950.

### Q314 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

Which rocket successfully placed Chandrayaan-3 into orbit?

A. PSLV-C57
B. GSLV Mk III (LVM3)
C. PSLV-C37
D. SSLV

**Answer:** B. GSLV Mk III (LVM3)
**Explanation:** Chandrayaan-3 was launched by LVM3-M4 (GSLV Mk III) on 14 July 2023 from SDSC, Sriharikota. It successfully soft-landed on the Moon's south pole on 23 August 2023.
**Shortcut / rule:** LVM3 = heavy-lift (Chandrayaan-2 & 3, Gaganyaan); PSLV = medium (Chandrayaan-1, Mangalyaan).

### Q315 · UN & World Organizations

The Universal Declaration of Human Rights was adopted in:

A. 1945
B. 1947
C. 1948
D. 1950

**Answer:** C. 1948
**Explanation:** The UDHR was adopted by the UN General Assembly on 10 December 1948 in Paris. 10 December is celebrated as Human Rights Day. It has 30 articles.
**Shortcut / rule:** UDHR = 10 Dec 1948 (30 articles); Human Rights Day = 10 December; Drafted by Eleanor Roosevelt committee.

### Q316 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which national park is known for the floating islands called "Phumdis"?

A. Kaziranga
B. Keibul Lamjao
C. Manas
D. Sundarbans

**Answer:** B. Keibul Lamjao
**Explanation:** Keibul Lamjao National Park in Manipur is the world's only floating national park. It is located on Loktak Lake with floating islands called phumdis. It is home to the endangered Sangai (Manipur brow-antlered deer).
**Shortcut / rule:** Keibul Lamjao = floating NP (Manipur, Loktak Lake); Sangai = endemic deer.

### Q317 · Computers & Applications

Which programming language is primarily used for web page development?

A. Java
B. Python
C. HTML
D. C++

**Answer:** C. HTML
**Explanation:** HTML (HyperText Markup Language) is the standard language for creating web pages. It defines the structure of web content. CSS handles styling and JavaScript handles interactivity.
**Shortcut / rule:** HTML = structure; CSS = style; JavaScript = interactivity; PHP/Python = backend.

### Q318 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does DRDO stand for?

A. Defence Research and Development Organisation
B. Department of Rural Development Operations
C. Defence Reform and Deployment Office
D. Directorate of Research and Development Overseas

**Answer:** A. Defence Research and Development Organisation
**Explanation:** DRDO was established in 1958 and is headquartered in New Delhi. It develops defence technologies including missiles (Agni, Prithvi, BrahMos), radars, and military systems.
**Shortcut / rule:** DRDO = defence R&D (1958, Delhi); ISRO = space; BARC = atomic; CSIR = scientific research.

### Q319 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana (PMAY) aims to provide:

A. Free education
B. Housing for all
C. Employment guarantee
D. Health insurance

**Answer:** B. Housing for all
**Explanation:** PMAY was launched on 25 June 2015 with the target of "Housing for All" by 2022. It has two components: PMAY-Urban and PMAY-Gramin (rural).
**Shortcut / rule:** PMAY = Housing; PMJDY = Banking; PMJAY = Health; PMKVY = Skill; PMGSY = Rural Roads.

### Q320 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Cabinet Mission Plan of 1946 proposed:

A. Partition of India
B. A three-tier federal structure
C. Separate nation for Muslims
D. Direct rule by British

**Answer:** B. A three-tier federal structure
**Explanation:** The Cabinet Mission (1946) rejected the demand for Pakistan and proposed a three-tier structure: Union at the centre, groups of provinces, and provinces. It also proposed an interim government and a Constituent Assembly.
**Shortcut / rule:** Cabinet Mission 1946 = three-tier structure (rejected Pakistan); Mountbatten Plan 1947 = Partition accepted.

### Q321 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Indian Independence Act was passed by British Parliament in:

A. 1945
B. 1946
C. 1947
D. 1948

**Answer:** C. 1947
**Explanation:** The Indian Independence Act was passed on 18 July 1947 by the British Parliament. It provided for the creation of two independent dominions — India and Pakistan — from 15 August 1947.
**Shortcut / rule:** Indian Independence Act = 18 July 1947; Independence = 15 Aug 1947; Constitution adopted = 26 Nov 1949; Republic Day = 26 Jan 1950.

### Q322 · Indian & World Geography

Which volcano is known as the "Light of the Mediterranean"?

A. Mount Vesuvius
B. Stromboli
C. Mount Etna
D. Krakatoa

**Answer:** B. Stromboli
**Explanation:** Stromboli volcano in Italy is called the "Lighthouse of the Mediterranean" because it has been in almost continuous eruption for over 2,000 years, its glow visible from the sea.
**Shortcut / rule:** Stromboli = "Lighthouse" (always active, Italy); Vesuvius = destroyed Pompeii (79 AD); Etna = largest active in Europe.

### Q323 · Indian & World Geography

The Deccan Plateau is bounded by which ranges?

A. Himalayas and Vindhyas
B. Western Ghats and Eastern Ghats
C. Aravallis and Satpuras
D. Nilgiris and Vindhyas

**Answer:** B. Western Ghats and Eastern Ghats
**Explanation:** The Deccan Plateau is a triangular plateau bounded by the Western Ghats (Sahyadri) on the west, Eastern Ghats on the east, and the Satpura-Vindhya ranges on the north.
**Shortcut / rule:** Deccan: W = Western Ghats; E = Eastern Ghats; N = Satpura/Vindhya; Highest peak WG = Anamudi (2695m, Kerala).

### Q324 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of "Single Citizenship" in India is borrowed from:

A. USA
B. UK
C. Canada
D. Australia

**Answer:** B. UK
**Explanation:** India follows the concept of single citizenship (only Indian citizenship, no state citizenship) borrowed from the UK, promoting national unity.
**Shortcut / rule:** Single citizenship = UK; Dual citizenship example = USA; Indian citizenship = Part II, Art 5-11.

### Q325 · Indian Polity & Governance

Rajya Sabha members are elected for a term of:

A. 4 years
B. 5 years
C. 6 years
D. No fixed term

**Answer:** C. 6 years
**Explanation:** Rajya Sabha members serve 6-year terms with one-third retiring every 2 years. Rajya Sabha is a permanent body and is never dissolved.
**Shortcut / rule:** RS = 6 years (1/3 retire every 2 years); LS = 5 years; RS max = 250 (12 nominated); LS max = 552.

### Q326 · Indian Economy

Inflation means:

A. Decrease in the value of money
B. Increase in the value of money
C. Stable prices
D. Decrease in production

**Answer:** A. Decrease in the value of money
**Explanation:** Inflation is a sustained increase in the general price level, leading to a decrease in the purchasing power (value) of money. CPI is the main measure of inflation in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** Inflation = prices rise, money value falls; Deflation = prices fall; Stagflation = inflation + stagnation + unemployment.

### Q327 · Physics

Which mirror is used in solar cookers?

A. Plane mirror
B. Convex mirror
C. Concave mirror
D. Cylindrical mirror

**Answer:** C. Concave mirror
**Explanation:** Concave mirrors are used in solar cookers because they converge sunlight to a focal point, generating intense heat for cooking.
**Shortcut / rule:** Solar cooker = concave mirror; Rear-view = convex mirror; Dressing table = plane mirror.

### Q328 · Chemistry

Which acid is known as "Oil of Vitriol"?

A. Nitric acid
B. Hydrochloric acid
C. Sulphuric acid
D. Acetic acid

**Answer:** C. Sulphuric acid
**Explanation:** Sulphuric acid (H₂SO₄) is known as the "Oil of Vitriol" and is called the "King of Chemicals" due to its widespread industrial use.
**Shortcut / rule:** Oil of Vitriol = H₂SO₄; Aqua Fortis = HNO₃; Muriatic acid = HCl; Aqua Regia = HCl + HNO₃ (3:1).

### Q329 · Biology / Life Science

The smallest bone in the human body is:

A. Femur
B. Stapes
C. Radius
D. Phalanges

**Answer:** B. Stapes
**Explanation:** The Stapes (stirrup bone) in the middle ear is the smallest bone in the human body, measuring about 3 mm. The ear has 3 tiny bones: Malleus, Incus, and Stapes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Smallest bone = Stapes (ear); Largest bone = Femur (thigh); Ear bones: Hammer, Anvil, Stirrup.

### Q330 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Natyashastra', an ancient treatise on performing arts, was written by:

A. Kalidasa
B. Bharata Muni
C. Panini
D. Patanjali

**Answer:** B. Bharata Muni
**Explanation:** Natyashastra was written by Bharata Muni (between 200 BC – 200 AD). It is considered the foundational text on drama, dance, and music in Indian classical tradition.
**Shortcut / rule:** Natyashastra = Bharata Muni; Arthashastra = Kautilya/Chanakya; Ashtadhyayi = Panini (grammar).

### Q331 · Games and Sports

Which country hosted the 2024 Summer Olympics?

A. Japan
B. USA
C. France
D. Australia

**Answer:** C. France
**Explanation:** The 2024 Summer Olympics were held in Paris, France (26 July – 11 August 2024). It was the third time Paris hosted the Olympics (previous: 1900, 1924).
**Shortcut / rule:** 2024 = Paris; 2028 = Los Angeles; 2032 = Brisbane; 2020 (held 2021) = Tokyo.

### Q332 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who was the first Prime Minister of India?

A. Dr. Rajendra Prasad
B. Sardar Patel
C. Jawaharlal Nehru
D. Lal Bahadur Shastri

**Answer:** C. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Explanation:** Jawaharlal Nehru served as the first PM of India from 15 August 1947 until his death on 27 May 1964 — the longest-serving PM at 16 years, 286 days.
**Shortcut / rule:** First PM = Nehru; First President = Rajendra Prasad; First woman PM = Indira Gandhi; Shortest PM tenure = Atal B. Vajpayee (13 days, 1996).

### Q333 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

Gaganyaan is India's planned:

A. Mars mission
B. Human spaceflight programme
C. Lunar rover mission
D. Space station

**Answer:** B. Human spaceflight programme
**Explanation:** Gaganyaan is ISRO's crewed orbital spacecraft programme to send Indian astronauts (Gaganauts/Vyomanauts) to low Earth orbit. It aims to make India the 4th nation with indigenous human spaceflight capability.
**Shortcut / rule:** Gaganyaan = human spaceflight (ISRO); GSLV Mk III (LVM3) = launch vehicle; 3 crew, 3-day mission planned.

### Q334 · UN & World Organizations

SAARC was established in which year?

A. 1980
B. 1985
C. 1990
D. 1995

**Answer:** B. 1985
**Explanation:** SAARC (South Asian Association for Regional Cooperation) was established on 8 December 1985 in Dhaka. It has 8 members: India, Pakistan, Bangladesh, Sri Lanka, Nepal, Bhutan, Maldives, and Afghanistan.
**Shortcut / rule:** SAARC = 1985 (Dhaka); 8 members; Secretariat = Kathmandu; Founded by Ziaur Rahman (Bangladesh).

### Q335 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Kyoto Protocol was adopted in which year?

A. 1992
B. 1997
C. 2005
D. 2015

**Answer:** B. 1997
**Explanation:** The Kyoto Protocol was adopted on 11 December 1997 in Kyoto, Japan, and entered into force on 16 February 2005. It set binding emission reduction targets for developed countries.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kyoto = 1997 (greenhouse gas); Paris Agreement = 2015; UNFCCC = 1992 (Rio Earth Summit).

### Q336 · Computers & Applications

The full form of PDF is:

A. Printed Document Format
B. Portable Document Format
C. Personal Data File
D. Public Document Framework

**Answer:** B. Portable Document Format
**Explanation:** PDF was developed by Adobe Systems in 1993. It preserves document formatting across different platforms and devices, making it ideal for sharing documents.
**Shortcut / rule:** PDF = Adobe (1993); DOC/DOCX = Microsoft Word; HTML = web pages; XML = data exchange.

### Q337 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What is the full form of BSNL?

A. Bharat Sanchar Nigam Limited
B. Bharat Satellite Network Limited
C. Bharat Service Network Limited
D. Basic Services Networking Limited

**Answer:** A. Bharat Sanchar Nigam Limited
**Explanation:** BSNL is a government-owned telecom company established on 1 October 2000. It provides telephone, mobile, broadband, and internet services across India (except Delhi and Mumbai).
**Shortcut / rule:** BSNL = telecom (2000); MTNL = Delhi & Mumbai; ONGC = oil & gas; IOCL = petroleum refining.

### Q338 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Pradhan Mantri Fasal Bima Yojana provides:

A. Crop insurance
B. Seed subsidy
C. Irrigation support
D. Farm equipment loans

**Answer:** A. Crop insurance
**Explanation:** PMFBY was launched on 13 January 2016 to provide insurance coverage and financial support to farmers in case of crop failure due to natural calamities, pests, or diseases.
**Shortcut / rule:** PMFBY = crop insurance (2016); Premium: Kharif = 2%, Rabi = 1.5%, Commercial = 5%.

### Q339 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who established the Brahmo Samaj?

A. Swami Vivekananda
B. Raja Ram Mohan Roy
C. Dayanand Saraswati
D. Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar

**Answer:** B. Raja Ram Mohan Roy
**Explanation:** Raja Ram Mohan Roy founded the Brahmo Sabha in 1828 (later Brahmo Samaj). He is called the "Father of Indian Renaissance." He campaigned against Sati and promoted modern education.
**Shortcut / rule:** Brahmo Samaj = Ram Mohan Roy (1828); Arya Samaj = Dayanand Saraswati (1875); Ramakrishna Mission = Vivekananda (1897).

### Q340 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Partition of Bengal was annulled in which year?

A. 1908
B. 1911
C. 1915
D. 1920

**Answer:** B. 1911
**Explanation:** The Partition of Bengal (1905) by Lord Curzon was annulled in 1911 by King George V at the Delhi Durbar. At the same time, the capital was shifted from Calcutta to Delhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** Partition of Bengal = 1905 (Curzon); Annulled = 1911 (George V); Capital shift = Calcutta → Delhi (1911).

### Q341 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the highest plateau in the world?

A. Deccan Plateau
B. Tibetan Plateau (Pamir)
C. Colorado Plateau
D. Bolivian Plateau

**Answer:** B. Tibetan Plateau (Pamir)
**Explanation:** The Tibetan Plateau (also called the "Roof of the World") is the highest and largest plateau in the world, with an average elevation of about 4,500 meters above sea level.
**Shortcut / rule:** Highest plateau = Tibetan (Roof of the World); Largest plateau = Antarctic; India's largest = Deccan.

### Q342 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Election Commission of India is established under:

A. Article 280
B. Article 324
C. Article 352
D. Article 368

**Answer:** B. Article 324
**Explanation:** Article 324 provides for the Election Commission of India which supervises elections to Parliament, State Legislatures, and the offices of President and Vice President.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 324 = EC; CEC + Election Commissioners; CEC removed only by impeachment (like SC judge).

### Q343 · Indian Economy

The first Five Year Plan in India was launched in:

A. 1947
B. 1950
C. 1951
D. 1955

**Answer:** C. 1951
**Explanation:** The first Five Year Plan (1951-1956) was based on the Harrod-Domar model and prioritized agriculture, irrigation, and power. The Planning Commission was established in 1950.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st FYP = 1951-56 (agriculture, Harrod-Domar); Planning Commission = 1950; Last FYP = 12th (2012-17); Replaced by NITI Aayog 2015.

### Q344 · Physics

Transformer works on the principle of:

A. Electromagnetic induction
B. Electrostatic induction
C. Nuclear fission
D. Photoelectric effect

**Answer:** A. Electromagnetic induction
**Explanation:** A transformer works on Faraday's Law of Electromagnetic Induction. It transfers electrical energy between two circuits through mutual induction. It can step up or step down voltage.
**Shortcut / rule:** Transformer = AC only (electromagnetic induction); Step-up = increases voltage; Step-down = decreases voltage.

### Q345 · Chemistry

The process of converting raw sugar into refined sugar involves:

A. Evaporation
B. Centrifugation
C. Distillation
D. Crystallization and purification

**Answer:** D. Crystallization and purification
**Explanation:** Sugar refining involves dissolving raw sugar, purifying with chemicals, filtering, decolorizing, and recrystallization to produce white refined sugar.
**Shortcut / rule:** Crystallization = sugar refining; Distillation = separating liquids (oil refining, alcohol); Evaporation = salt from seawater.

### Q346 · Biology / Life Science

Haemoglobin is present in:

A. White Blood Cells
B. Red Blood Cells
C. Platelets
D. Plasma

**Answer:** B. Red Blood Cells
**Explanation:** Haemoglobin (Hb) is the iron-containing protein in RBCs that transports oxygen from the lungs to body tissues. It gives blood its red colour. Iron deficiency causes anaemia.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hb = Fe + protein (in RBCs); O₂ transport; CO binds to Hb 200× stronger than O₂ (CO poisoning).

### Q347 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who composed India's national song "Vande Mataram"?

A. Rabindranath Tagore
B. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay
C. Muhammad Iqbal
D. Subramania Bharati

**Answer:** B. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay
**Explanation:** "Vande Mataram" was written by Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay in his novel Anandamath (1882). It was adopted as the national song on 24 January 1950.
**Shortcut / rule:** National Song = Vande Mataram (Bankim Chandra); National Anthem = Jana Gana Mana (Tagore); Adopted = 24 Jan 1950.

### Q348 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's first indigenous cryogenic engine was used in:

A. PSLV
B. GSLV-D5
C. SSLV
D. SLV-3

**Answer:** B. GSLV-D5
**Explanation:** GSLV-D5 (5 January 2014) was the first successful flight using an indigenous cryogenic upper stage (CE-7.5). This made India the 6th country to develop cryogenic engine technology.
**Shortcut / rule:** GSLV-D5 = first indigenous cryo success (2014); Cryo engine = liquid hydrogen + liquid oxygen; Very high efficiency.

### Q349 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which is the first biosphere reserve in India?

A. Nanda Devi
B. Nilgiri
C. Sundarbans
D. Gulf of Mannar

**Answer:** B. Nilgiri
**Explanation:** Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve (5,520 km²) was established in 1986 as India's first biosphere reserve. It spans parts of Tamil Nadu, Karnataka, and Kerala.
**Shortcut / rule:** First biosphere reserve = Nilgiri (1986); Total = 18 biosphere reserves in India; 12 are in UNESCO MAB network.

### Q350 · Computers & Applications

What is the capacity of a standard CD-ROM?

A. 256 MB
B. 512 MB
C. 700 MB
D. 1 GB

**Answer:** C. 700 MB
**Explanation:** A standard CD-ROM can hold approximately 700 MB (80 minutes of audio). A DVD holds 4.7 GB and a Blu-ray disc holds 25-50 GB.
**Shortcut / rule:** CD = 700 MB; DVD = 4.7 GB; Blu-ray = 25-50 GB; Floppy = 1.44 MB.

### Q351 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Home Rule Movement in India was started by:

A. Annie Besant and Bal Gangadhar Tilak
B. Mahatma Gandhi and Nehru
C. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
D. Lala Lajpat Rai

**Answer:** A. Annie Besant and Bal Gangadhar Tilak
**Explanation:** Two Home Rule Leagues were founded in 1916: Tilak's in April (Poona) and Annie Besant's in September (Madras). They demanded self-governance within the British Empire.
**Shortcut / rule:** Home Rule 1916: Tilak (Poona, April) + Besant (Madras, September); Inspired by Irish Home Rule movement.

### Q352 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Chauri Chaura incident (1922) led to the withdrawal of which movement?

A. Civil Disobedience Movement
B. Non-Cooperation Movement
C. Quit India Movement
D. Swadeshi Movement

**Answer:** B. Non-Cooperation Movement
**Explanation:** On 4 February 1922, a violent mob set fire to a police station at Chauri Chaura (UP), killing 22 policemen. Gandhi immediately withdrew the Non-Cooperation Movement due to this violence.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chauri Chaura = 4 Feb 1922 (UP); NCM withdrawn; Gandhi = strict non-violence principle.

### Q353 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the deepest lake in India?

A. Dal Lake
B. Manasbal Lake
C. Pangong Lake
D. Sheshnag Lake

**Answer:** B. Manasbal Lake
**Explanation:** Manasbal Lake in J&K is considered the deepest lake in India with a maximum depth of about 13 meters. Among high-altitude lakes, Pangong Tso (Ladakh) is notable.
**Shortcut / rule:** Deepest = Manasbal (J&K); Highest = Cholamu (Sikkim, 5330m); Largest brackish = Chilika; World deepest = Lake Baikal (Russia, 1642m).

### Q354 · Indian & World Geography

Which state in India receives rainfall from both southwest and northeast monsoons?

A. Kerala
B. Tamil Nadu
C. Rajasthan
D. Assam

**Answer:** B. Tamil Nadu
**Explanation:** Tamil Nadu receives rainfall from the northeast monsoon (October-December) primarily, and also some from the southwest monsoon. It is the only state that gets most rain from the retreating monsoon.
**Shortcut / rule:** TN = northeast monsoon (winter rain); Rest of India = southwest monsoon (June-Sept); Mawsynram/Cherrapunji = highest rainfall.

### Q355 · Indian Polity & Governance

Under which Article can the President of India be impeached?

A. Article 56
B. Article 61
C. Article 72
D. Article 143

**Answer:** B. Article 61
**Explanation:** Article 61 provides for the impeachment of the President for "violation of the Constitution." The charge is initiated in either House by at least 1/4 of members and must be passed by 2/3 majority of both Houses.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 61 = President impeachment; No President has been impeached in India; Process similar to removing SC judge.

### Q356 · Indian Economy

Which tax is levied and collected by the Central Government but entirely transferred to States?

A. Income Tax
B. GST
C. Excise Duty on medicinal products
D. Taxes on income other than agricultural income

**Answer:** D. Taxes on income other than agricultural income
**Explanation:** Under the recommendations of the Finance Commission, certain central taxes are shared with states. The share of states in central taxes is recommended by each Finance Commission.
**Shortcut / rule:** Finance Commission = tax devolution; 16th FC; Art 268 = duties levied by Centre, collected & kept by States.

### Q357 · Physics

The filament of an electric bulb is made of:

A. Copper
B. Aluminium
C. Tungsten
D. Iron

**Answer:** C. Tungsten
**Explanation:** Tungsten is used for bulb filaments because it has the highest melting point (3,422°C) of any metal, allowing it to glow white-hot without melting.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tungsten = highest melting point metal (3422°C); Used in bulb filaments + welding electrodes.

### Q358 · Physics

Ultrasound has frequency greater than:

A. 10 Hz
B. 1,000 Hz
C. 20,000 Hz
D. 100,000 Hz

**Answer:** C. 20,000 Hz
**Explanation:** Sound above 20,000 Hz (20 kHz) is called ultrasound — beyond the range of human hearing. It is used in medical imaging (sonography), cleaning, and SONAR.
**Shortcut / rule:** Infrasound < 20 Hz; Audible = 20 Hz – 20 kHz; Ultrasound > 20 kHz; Bats/dolphins use ultrasound.

### Q359 · Chemistry

Which vitamin is water-soluble?

A. Vitamin A
B. Vitamin D
C. Vitamin C
D. Vitamin K

**Answer:** C. Vitamin C
**Explanation:** Vitamins B and C are water-soluble (not stored in the body, need daily intake). Vitamins A, D, E, and K are fat-soluble (stored in liver and fatty tissues).
**Shortcut / rule:** Water-soluble = B & C; Fat-soluble = A, D, E, K (mnemonic: "ADEK"); Excess water-soluble excreted in urine.

### Q360 · Chemistry

The gas used in refrigerators as a coolant is:

A. Ammonia
B. Freon (CFC)
C. Nitrogen
D. Carbon dioxide

**Answer:** B. Freon (CFC)
**Explanation:** Freon (chlorofluorocarbon) was traditionally used as a refrigerant. Due to ozone depletion, CFCs are being replaced by HFCs (hydrofluorocarbons) like R-134a.
**Shortcut / rule:** Old refrigerant = CFC (Freon); New = HFC; Montreal Protocol banned CFCs; CFCs deplete ozone.

### Q361 · Biology / Life Science

Which organ purifies blood in the human body?

A. Heart
B. Liver
C. Kidney
D. Lungs

**Answer:** C. Kidney
**Explanation:** The kidneys filter blood, remove waste products (urea, creatinine) and excess water, and produce urine. Each kidney contains about 1 million nephrons. Dialysis replaces kidney function.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kidney = filters blood (nephron); Liver = detoxifies; Lungs = gas exchange; Heart = pumps blood.

### Q362 · Biology / Life Science

The gas we exhale during respiration is mainly:

A. Oxygen
B. Nitrogen
C. Carbon dioxide
D. Carbon monoxide

**Answer:** C. Carbon dioxide
**Explanation:** We inhale oxygen-rich air and exhale carbon dioxide-rich air. Cellular respiration in mitochondria uses O₂ to produce energy (ATP) and releases CO₂ as a waste product.
**Shortcut / rule:** Inhale: O₂ (21%); Exhale: CO₂ (4%), O₂ (16%); Plants use CO₂ for photosynthesis.

### Q363 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Ajmer Sharif Dargah is the tomb of which Sufi saint?

A. Nizamuddin Auliya
B. Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti
C. Salim Chishti
D. Baba Farid

**Answer:** B. Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti
**Explanation:** The Ajmer Sharif Dargah in Rajasthan is the shrine of Sufi saint Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti (1141-1236 AD). He founded the Chishti order of Sufism in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ajmer = Moinuddin Chishti (Chishti order); Delhi = Nizamuddin Auliya; Fatehpur Sikri = Salim Chishti.

### Q364 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Which cave paintings are considered among the oldest in India?

A. Ajanta
B. Bhimbetka
C. Ellora
D. Bagh

**Answer:** B. Bhimbetka
**Explanation:** Bhimbetka rock shelters in Madhya Pradesh contain some of the oldest known rock art in India, dating back to the Mesolithic period (~30,000 years ago). It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bhimbetka = oldest rock art (MP, Stone Age); Ajanta = Buddhist paintings (MH, 2nd c. BC); Both = UNESCO sites.

### Q365 · Games and Sports

Sachin Tendulkar is the only cricketer to score:

A. 50 centuries in Tests
B. 100 international centuries
C. 200 wickets and 10,000 runs
D. Triple century in ODIs

**Answer:** B. 100 international centuries
**Explanation:** Sachin Tendulkar scored 100 centuries in international cricket (51 in Tests + 49 in ODIs). He holds records for most runs in both Test (15,921) and ODI (18,426) cricket.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sachin = 100 centuries; First double century in ODI (200* vs SA, 2010); "Master Blaster"; Bharat Ratna 2014.

### Q366 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who is known as the "Father of the Indian Constitution"?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Jawaharlal Nehru
C. Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
D. Sardar Patel

**Answer:** C. Dr. B.R. Ambedkar
**Explanation:** Dr. B.R. Ambedkar was the Chairman of the Drafting Committee of the Indian Constitution. He is called the chief architect/father of the Indian Constitution. His birthday (14 April) is celebrated as Ambedkar Jayanti.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ambedkar = Drafting Committee Chairman; Constitution adopted = 26 Nov 1949; Commenced = 26 Jan 1950.

### Q367 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's first successful satellite launch vehicle was:

A. SLV-3
B. PSLV
C. GSLV
D. ASLV

**Answer:** A. SLV-3
**Explanation:** SLV-3 (Satellite Launch Vehicle-3) successfully launched the Rohini satellite into orbit on 18 July 1980 from Sriharikota. Dr. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam was the project director.
**Shortcut / rule:** SLV-3 = first success (1980, Kalam); ASLV = augmented SLV; PSLV = 1993 (workhorse); GSLV = 2001.

### Q368 · UN & World Organizations

The Paris Agreement on climate change was adopted in:

A. 2010
B. 2012
C. 2015
D. 2017

**Answer:** C. 2015
**Explanation:** The Paris Agreement was adopted on 12 December 2015 at COP21 (Conference of the Parties). It aims to limit global warming to well below 2°C, preferably 1.5°C, above pre-industrial levels.
**Shortcut / rule:** Paris Agreement = 2015 (COP21); UNFCCC = 1992; Kyoto = 1997; India ratified Paris Agreement in 2016.

### Q369 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which is the national aquatic animal of India?

A. Blue whale
B. Gangetic Dolphin
C. Indian Star Tortoise
D. Olive Ridley Turtle

**Answer:** B. Gangetic Dolphin
**Explanation:** The Ganges River Dolphin (Platanista gangetica) was declared India's national aquatic animal in 2009. It is an endangered species found mainly in the Ganga-Brahmaputra river systems.
**Shortcut / rule:** National aquatic animal = Gangetic Dolphin (2009); National animal = Bengal Tiger; National bird = Peacock; National flower = Lotus.

### Q370 · Computers & Applications

BIOS stands for:

A. Basic Input Output System
B. Binary Integrated Operating System
C. Basic Internal Operating System
D. Binary Input Output Service

**Answer:** A. Basic Input Output System
**Explanation:** BIOS is firmware that initializes hardware during the booting process and provides runtime services for operating systems. It is stored on a chip on the motherboard.
**Shortcut / rule:** BIOS = boot firmware; POST = Power-On Self-Test (BIOS function); Modern replacement = UEFI.

### Q371 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does GPS stand for?

A. Global Positioning System
B. General Position Service
C. Geographic Point System
D. Global Processing Signal

**Answer:** A. Global Positioning System
**Explanation:** GPS is a satellite-based navigation system operated by the US. It uses 24+ satellites to provide location, navigation, and timing services. India's own system is NavIC (IRNSS).
**Shortcut / rule:** GPS = USA (24 satellites); GLONASS = Russia; Galileo = EU; BeiDou = China; NavIC (IRNSS) = India.

### Q372 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Startup India initiative was launched in which year?

A. 2014
B. 2015
C. 2016
D. 2017

**Answer:** C. 2016
**Explanation:** Startup India was launched on 16 January 2016 to build an ecosystem for nurturing innovation and startups. It offers tax exemptions, easy compliance, and a fund of funds (₹10,000 crore).
**Shortcut / rule:** Startup India = Jan 2016; Make in India = Sept 2014; Stand-up India = April 2016 (SC/ST/women entrepreneurs).

### Q373 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who founded the Servants of India Society?

A. Bal Gangadhar Tilak
B. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
C. Dadabhai Naoroji
D. Surendranath Banerjee

**Answer:** B. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
**Explanation:** Gopal Krishna Gokhale founded the Servants of India Society in 1905 in Pune. It promoted Indian education, sanitation, and worked for the welfare of underprivileged sections.
**Shortcut / rule:** Servants of India = Gokhale (1905); Gokhale = political guru of Gandhi; Tilak = Gokhale's contemporary (Extremist wing).

### Q374 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Non-Aligned Movement (NAM) was co-founded by Nehru and:

A. Nasser, Sukarno, Tito
B. Churchill, Roosevelt
C. Mao, Stalin
D. Mandela, Nkrumah

**Answer:** A. Nasser, Sukarno, Tito
**Explanation:** NAM was co-founded by Nehru (India), Nasser (Egypt), Tito (Yugoslavia), Sukarno (Indonesia), and Nkrumah (Ghana). The first NAM summit was held in Belgrade, 1961.
**Shortcut / rule:** NAM = 1961 (Belgrade); Founders: Nehru, Nasser, Tito, Sukarno, Nkrumah; India = key member.

### Q375 · Indian & World Geography

The Duncan Passage separates:

A. Andaman and Nicobar Islands
B. India and Sri Lanka
C. South Andaman and Little Andaman
D. India and Myanmar

**Answer:** C. South Andaman and Little Andaman
**Explanation:** Duncan Passage separates South Andaman from Little Andaman. The Ten Degree Channel separates the Andaman Islands from the Nicobar Islands.
**Shortcut / rule:** Duncan Passage = S. Andaman & Little Andaman; 10° Channel = Andaman & Nicobar; Palk Strait = India & Sri Lanka.

### Q376 · Indian Polity & Governance

The minimum age for becoming the President of India is:

A. 25 years
B. 30 years
C. 35 years
D. 40 years

**Answer:** C. 35 years
**Explanation:** Under Article 58, a person must be at least 35 years old to be eligible for the office of President. They must also be a citizen of India and qualified to be a member of the Lok Sabha.
**Shortcut / rule:** President = 35 yrs; Vice President = 35 yrs; Lok Sabha = 25 yrs; Rajya Sabha = 30 yrs.

### Q377 · Indian Economy

What percentage of the population was below the poverty line as per the Tendulkar Committee?

A. 15.5%
B. 21.9%
C. 29.8%
D. 37.2%

**Answer:** B. 21.9%
**Explanation:** According to the Tendulkar Committee methodology, 21.9% of India's population was below the poverty line in 2011-12 (25.7% rural, 13.7% urban).
**Shortcut / rule:** Tendulkar Committee: BPL = 21.9% (2011-12); Rangarajan Committee: 29.5% (2011-12); Earlier: Lakdawala methodology.

### Q378 · Physics

Radar works on the principle of:

A. Reflection of radio waves
B. Refraction of light
C. Diffraction of sound
D. Nuclear fission

**Answer:** A. Reflection of radio waves
**Explanation:** RADAR sends radio waves and detects their reflection from objects to determine distance, speed, and direction. It was extensively developed during World War II.
**Shortcut / rule:** RADAR = radio waves (reflection); SONAR = sound waves (underwater); LIDAR = laser pulses (3D mapping).

### Q379 · Chemistry

Which element is common to all acids?

A. Oxygen
B. Hydrogen
C. Sulphur
D. Carbon

**Answer:** B. Hydrogen
**Explanation:** Hydrogen is the common element in all acids. According to Arrhenius theory, an acid is a substance that produces H⁺ ions in aqueous solution.
**Shortcut / rule:** All acids contain H; HCl, H₂SO₄, HNO₃, CH₃COOH all have H; Bases produce OH⁻ ions.

### Q380 · Biology / Life Science

The longest bone in the human body is:

A. Tibia
B. Femur
C. Humerus
D. Radius

**Answer:** B. Femur
**Explanation:** The Femur (thighbone) is the longest and strongest bone in the human body. It extends from the hip to the knee and supports the body's weight.
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest = Femur (thigh); Smallest = Stapes (ear); Total bones in adult = 206; Babies born with ~270 bones.

### Q381 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Iron Pillar at Mehrauli (Delhi) dates back to which dynasty?

A. Maurya Dynasty
B. Gupta Dynasty
C. Mughal Dynasty
D. Delhi Sultanate

**Answer:** B. Gupta Dynasty
**Explanation:** The Iron Pillar near Qutub Minar is from the Gupta period (c. 400 AD), attributed to Chandragupta II. It is famous for its resistance to rust despite being over 1,600 years old.
**Shortcut / rule:** Iron Pillar = Gupta period (~400 AD); Rust-resistant due to high phosphorus content; Located at Qutub complex.

### Q382 · Games and Sports

P.V. Sindhu won a medal in which sport at the Olympics?

A. Tennis
B. Badminton
C. Boxing
D. Wrestling

**Answer:** B. Badminton
**Explanation:** P.V. Sindhu won Silver at the 2016 Rio Olympics and Bronze at the 2020 Tokyo Olympics in badminton, becoming the first Indian woman to win two individual Olympic medals.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sindhu: Silver (2016 Rio) + Bronze (2020 Tokyo) = 2 Olympic medals; World Championship Gold = 2019.

### Q383 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who wrote "Arthashastra"?

A. Ashoka
B. Chanakya (Kautilya)
C. Kalidasa
D. Banabhatta

**Answer:** B. Chanakya (Kautilya)
**Explanation:** Arthashastra was written by Chanakya (also called Kautilya or Vishnugupta), the advisor to Chandragupta Maurya. It is an ancient Indian treatise on statecraft, economics, and military strategy.
**Shortcut / rule:** Arthashastra = Chanakya/Kautilya; Indica = Megasthenes; Mudrarakshasa = Vishakhadatta.

### Q384 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The concept of "Biodiversity Hotspot" was given by:

A. Charles Darwin
B. Norman Myers
C. Alexander von Humboldt
D. E.O. Wilson

**Answer:** B. Norman Myers
**Explanation:** British ecologist Norman Myers coined the term "Biodiversity Hotspot" in 1988. Currently, there are 36 hotspots worldwide. India has 4 hotspots: Western Ghats, Eastern Himalayas, Indo-Burma, and Sundaland.
**Shortcut / rule:** 36 hotspots globally; India = 4 (Western Ghats, E. Himalayas, Indo-Burma, Sundaland); Myers = 1988.

### Q385 · Computers & Applications

What is the full form of Wi-Fi?

A. Wireless Fidelity
B. Wired Facility
C. Wireless Frequency Internet
D. It is a brand name, not an acronym

**Answer:** D. It is a brand name, not an acronym
**Explanation:** Wi-Fi is a trademark of the Wi-Fi Alliance. It is commonly believed to stand for "Wireless Fidelity" but the Wi-Fi Alliance has stated it does not stand for anything — it's just a brand name for wireless networking standards (IEEE 802.11).
**Shortcut / rule:** Wi-Fi = brand name (not "Wireless Fidelity"); Based on IEEE 802.11 standards; Uses radio waves (2.4/5 GHz).

### Q386 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

IATA stands for:

A. International Air Transport Association
B. Indian Air Traffic Authority
C. International Aviation and Travel Association
D. Indian Air Transport Agency

**Answer:** A. International Air Transport Association
**Explanation:** IATA was founded in 1945 in Havana, Cuba. It represents about 300 airlines. It assigns 3-letter airport codes (e.g., DEL for Delhi, BOM for Mumbai).
**Shortcut / rule:** IATA = airlines (3-letter codes); ICAO = aviation safety (4-letter codes); HQ = Montreal, Canada.

### Q387 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The National Pension System (NPS) was launched for all citizens in which year?

A. 2004
B. 2009
C. 2012
D. 2015

**Answer:** B. 2009
**Explanation:** NPS was introduced for central government employees from 1 January 2004 and opened to all Indian citizens from 1 May 2009. It is a voluntary retirement savings scheme regulated by PFRDA.
**Shortcut / rule:** NPS: Govt employees = 2004; All citizens = 2009; Regulator = PFRDA; Atal Pension = unorganized sector.

### Q388 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Indian Constitution was adopted on:

A. 15 August 1947
B. 26 January 1950
C. 26 November 1949
D. 30 January 1948

**Answer:** C. 26 November 1949
**Explanation:** The Constitution was adopted by the Constituent Assembly on 26 November 1949 (celebrated as Constitution Day/Samvidhan Divas). It came into effect on 26 January 1950 (Republic Day).
**Shortcut / rule:** Adopted = 26 Nov 1949; Commenced = 26 Jan 1950; Constitution Day = 26 Nov; Republic Day = 26 Jan.

### Q389 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the largest island in the world?

A. Madagascar
B. Borneo
C. Greenland
D. Sumatra

**Answer:** C. Greenland
**Explanation:** Greenland is the world's largest island (2.166 million km²). It is an autonomous territory of Denmark. Australia is not counted as an island because it is a continent.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest island = Greenland (Denmark); Largest in India = Middle Andaman; Largest river island = Majuli (Assam).

### Q390 · Indian Polity & Governance

The first woman Chief Justice of a High Court in India was:

A. Fatima Beevi
B. Leila Seth
C. Anna Chandy
D. Sujata Manohar

**Answer:** C. Anna Chandy
**Explanation:** Justice Anna Chandy became the first woman judge of a High Court in India (Kerala HC, 1959). Justice Leila Seth was the first woman Chief Justice of a State HC (HP, 1991). Justice Fatima Beevi was the first woman SC judge (1989).
**Shortcut / rule:** First woman HC judge = Anna Chandy (1959); First woman SC judge = Fatima Beevi (1989); First woman CJ of HC = Leila Seth (1991).

### Q391 · Indian Economy

Which organization regulates the insurance sector in India?

A. RBI
B. SEBI
C. IRDAI
D. NABARD

**Answer:** C. IRDAI
**Explanation:** IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India) was established in 1999 under the IRDA Act. It regulates and promotes the insurance industry in India. HQ: Hyderabad.
**Shortcut / rule:** IRDAI = insurance (Hyderabad); SEBI = securities (Mumbai); RBI = banking (Mumbai); PFRDA = pensions (New Delhi).

### Q392 · Physics

The working principle of a hydraulic press is based on:

A. Archimedes' principle
B. Pascal's law
C. Bernoulli's principle
D. Newton's third law

**Answer:** B. Pascal's law
**Explanation:** Pascal's Law states that pressure applied to a confined fluid is transmitted equally in all directions. Hydraulic machines (press, brake, jack) work on this principle.
**Shortcut / rule:** Pascal's Law = hydraulics; Bernoulli = aerodynamics (airplane lift); Archimedes = buoyancy.

### Q393 · Chemistry

Dry ice is the solid form of:

A. Carbon monoxide
B. Carbon dioxide
C. Nitrogen
D. Oxygen

**Answer:** B. Carbon dioxide
**Explanation:** Dry ice is solid CO₂ at -78.5°C. It is called "dry" because it sublimes directly from solid to gas without passing through the liquid phase at normal atmospheric pressure.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dry ice = solid CO₂ (-78.5°C); Sublimation = solid → gas directly; Used in cold storage and fog effects.

### Q394 · Biology / Life Science

Penicillin was discovered by:

A. Louis Pasteur
B. Alexander Fleming
C. Edward Jenner
D. Robert Koch

**Answer:** B. Alexander Fleming
**Explanation:** Alexander Fleming discovered Penicillin in 1928 from the fungus Penicillium notatum. It was the first antibiotic and revolutionized medicine. Fleming shared the 1945 Nobel Prize for this discovery.
**Shortcut / rule:** Penicillin = Fleming (1928); Vaccination = Jenner (1796, smallpox); Pasteurization = Pasteur; TB bacillus = Koch.

### Q395 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Charminar is located in which city?

A. Hyderabad
B. Lucknow
C. Ahmedabad
D. Delhi

**Answer:** A. Hyderabad
**Explanation:** Charminar was built in 1591 by Muhammad Quli Qutb Shah, the fifth ruler of the Qutb Shahi dynasty, to mark the end of a plague epidemic in Hyderabad.
**Shortcut / rule:** Charminar = Hyderabad (1591, Qutb Shahi); Gateway of India = Mumbai; India Gate = Delhi; Victoria Memorial = Kolkata.

### Q396 · Games and Sports

Which country has won the most Cricket World Cups (ODI)?

A. India
B. West Indies
C. Australia
D. England

**Answer:** C. Australia
**Explanation:** Australia has won the Cricket World Cup (ODI) a record 6 times (1987, 1999, 2003, 2007, 2023). India has won it 2 times (1983, 2011).
**Shortcut / rule:** Most ODI WC titles: Australia = 6; India = 2; West Indies = 2; First WC = 1975 (England); Won by WI.

### Q397 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Amartya Sen won the Nobel Prize in Economics for his work on:

A. Game theory
B. Welfare economics and famine
C. International trade
D. Monetary policy

**Answer:** B. Welfare economics and famine
**Explanation:** Amartya Sen won the Nobel Prize in Economics in 1998 for his contributions to welfare economics, social choice theory, and his work on famines and poverty.
**Shortcut / rule:** Amartya Sen = Nobel Economics 1998; "Development as Freedom"; Capability approach; Bengali Indian.

### Q398 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

The first Indian to walk in space (EVA) is planned under which mission?

A. Chandrayaan
B. Mangalyaan
C. Gaganyaan
D. Aditya

**Answer:** C. Gaganyaan
**Explanation:** India's Gaganyaan programme includes planned EVA (Extra-Vehicular Activity) capabilities. ISRO has been developing space suits and EVA systems for future missions beyond the initial crewed orbital flight.
**Shortcut / rule:** Gaganyaan = human spaceflight; Chandrayaan = Moon; Mangalyaan = Mars; Aditya = Sun.

### Q399 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Red Data Book' provides information about species that are:

A. Abundant
B. Endangered
C. Extinct
D. Newly discovered

**Answer:** B. Endangered
**Explanation:** The Red Data Book (Red List) is published by IUCN (International Union for Conservation of Nature). It lists species that are endangered, vulnerable, or threatened with extinction.
**Shortcut / rule:** Red Data Book = IUCN; Categories: Extinct, Endangered, Vulnerable, Near Threatened, Least Concern.

### Q400 · Computers & Applications

Which is the most widely used operating system for smartphones?

A. iOS
B. Windows Phone
C. Android
D. BlackBerry OS

**Answer:** C. Android
**Explanation:** Android, developed by Google, is the most widely used mobile operating system with about 72% global market share. It is based on the Linux kernel and is open-source.
**Shortcut / rule:** Android = Google (Linux-based, open source); iOS = Apple (closed source); ~72% Android vs ~27% iOS globally.

### Q401 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Regulating Act was passed in which year?

A. 1757
B. 1773
C. 1784
D. 1793

**Answer:** B. 1773
**Explanation:** The Regulating Act of 1773 was the first step by the British Parliament to control and regulate the affairs of the East India Company in India. It established the Supreme Court at Calcutta and made Warren Hastings the first Governor-General of Bengal.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1773 = Regulating Act; 1784 = Pitt's India Act; 1793 = Charter Act; 1813 = End of EIC trade monopoly.

### Q402 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Pitt's India Act was passed in which year?

A. 1773
B. 1784
C. 1793
D. 1813

**Answer:** B. 1784
**Explanation:** Pitt's India Act (1784) established dual control over Indian affairs: Board of Control (British government) + Court of Directors (EIC). It distinguished commercial and political functions of EIC.
**Shortcut / rule:** Pitt's India Act = 1784 (dual control); Named after PM William Pitt the Younger.

### Q403 · Indian & World Geography

Which tropic passes through India?

A. Tropic of Capricorn
B. Equator
C. Tropic of Cancer
D. Arctic Circle

**Answer:** C. Tropic of Cancer
**Explanation:** The Tropic of Cancer (23.5°N) passes through 8 Indian states: Gujarat, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tripura, and Mizoram.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tropic of Cancer = 23.5°N (through India); Tropic of Capricorn = 23.5°S (through Australia, South Africa, Brazil).

### Q404 · Indian & World Geography

The Suez Canal connects which two bodies of water?

A. Atlantic and Pacific
B. Mediterranean and Red Sea
C. Arabian Sea and Bay of Bengal
D. Black Sea and Mediterranean

**Answer:** B. Mediterranean and Red Sea
**Explanation:** The Suez Canal (193 km) connects the Mediterranean Sea to the Red Sea through Egypt. Opened in 1869, it shortens the sea route between Europe and Asia by ~7,000 km.
**Shortcut / rule:** Suez = 1869 (Egypt, 193 km); Panama Canal = 1914 (Panama, 82 km); Suez is sea-level (no locks); Panama has locks.

### Q405 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Inter-State Council is established under which Article?

A. Article 262
B. Article 263
C. Article 280
D. Article 300

**Answer:** B. Article 263
**Explanation:** Article 263 empowers the President to establish an Inter-State Council to investigate and discuss subjects of common interest between the Centre and States and make recommendations.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 263 = Inter-State Council; Art 262 = Inter-State water disputes; Art 280 = Finance Commission.

### Q406 · Indian Economy

Who is considered the architect of India's economic reforms (LPG) of 1991?

A. P.V. Narasimha Rao
B. Manmohan Singh
C. Atal Bihari Vajpayee
D. Rajiv Gandhi

**Answer:** B. Manmohan Singh
**Explanation:** Dr. Manmohan Singh, as Finance Minister under PM P.V. Narasimha Rao, introduced the economic reforms of 1991 (Liberalization, Privatization, Globalization) during the balance of payments crisis.
**Shortcut / rule:** LPG 1991: FM = Manmohan Singh; PM = Narasimha Rao; Triggered by BoP crisis (gold pledged to IMF).

### Q407 · Physics

The SI unit of electric charge is:

A. Volt
B. Ampere
C. Coulomb
D. Ohm

**Answer:** C. Coulomb
**Explanation:** Electric charge is measured in Coulombs (C). One Coulomb = charge carried by 6.25 × 10¹⁸ electrons. 1 Ampere = 1 Coulomb per second.
**Shortcut / rule:** Charge = Coulomb; Current = Ampere (C/s); Voltage = Volt (J/C); 1 electron charge = 1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C.

### Q408 · Physics

Which lens is used to correct Hypermetropia (far-sightedness)?

A. Concave lens
B. Convex lens
C. Bifocal lens
D. Cylindrical lens

**Answer:** B. Convex lens
**Explanation:** Hypermetropia (long-sightedness) occurs when the image forms behind the retina. A convex (converging) lens corrects it by converging light rays to form the image on the retina.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hypermetropia = Convex lens; Myopia = Concave lens; Astigmatism = Cylindrical lens; Presbyopia = Bifocal lens.

### Q409 · Chemistry

LPG mainly consists of:

A. Methane
B. Butane and Propane
C. Ethane
D. Hydrogen

**Answer:** B. Butane and Propane
**Explanation:** LPG (Liquefied Petroleum Gas) is mainly a mixture of butane (C₄H₁₀) and propane (C₃H₈). Ethyl mercaptan is added for the distinctive smell to detect leaks.
**Shortcut / rule:** LPG = Butane + Propane; CNG = Methane (CH₄); Smell agent = Ethyl Mercaptan; LPG cylinder colour = red.

### Q410 · Chemistry

Which metal is stored in kerosene oil?

A. Gold
B. Silver
C. Sodium
D. Aluminium

**Answer:** C. Sodium
**Explanation:** Sodium (Na) and Potassium (K) are highly reactive alkali metals that react vigorously with water and air. They are stored in kerosene oil to prevent contact with moisture and oxygen.
**Shortcut / rule:** Na/K stored in kerosene; Phosphorus stored in water; Na + Water → NaOH + H₂ (vigorous, fire risk).

### Q411 · Biology / Life Science

Which enzyme in saliva helps in digestion of starch?

A. Pepsin
B. Trypsin
C. Amylase (Ptyalin)
D. Lipase

**Answer:** C. Amylase (Ptyalin)
**Explanation:** Salivary amylase (Ptyalin) begins the digestion of starch into maltose in the mouth. This is why food tastes sweeter when chewed for a long time.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mouth: Amylase (starch); Stomach: Pepsin (protein); Small intestine: Trypsin (protein), Lipase (fat).

### Q412 · Biology / Life Science

The total number of bones in an adult human body is:

A. 196
B. 206
C. 216
D. 300

**Answer:** B. 206
**Explanation:** An adult human body has 206 bones. Babies are born with about 270-300 bones; many fuse together during growth. The skeletal system provides support, protection, and enables movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** Adult = 206 bones; Baby = ~270-300; Skull = 22 bones; Spine = 33 vertebrae; Ribs = 24 (12 pairs).

### Q413 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Jatakas' are stories related to the previous births of:

A. Lord Krishna
B. Lord Rama
C. Lord Buddha
D. Lord Mahavira

**Answer:** C. Lord Buddha
**Explanation:** Jataka tales are a collection of 547 stories about the previous births of Lord Buddha (Bodhisattva). They are written in Pali and provide insight into ancient Indian society and culture.
**Shortcut / rule:** Jatakas = Buddha's previous births (Pali); Tripitaka = Buddhist scriptures; Agamas = Jain scriptures.

### Q414 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Golden Temple (Harmandir Sahib) is located in which city?

A. Delhi
B. Chandigarh
C. Amritsar
D. Jalandhar

**Answer:** C. Amritsar
**Explanation:** The Golden Temple (Sri Harmandir Sahib) in Amritsar, Punjab is the holiest Gurdwara of Sikhism. It was built by Guru Arjan Dev (5th Sikh Guru) in 1604. The foundation was laid by Sufi saint Mian Mir.
**Shortcut / rule:** Golden Temple = Amritsar (Guru Arjan Dev, 1604); Akal Takht = highest Sikh authority; Sarovar = sacred pool.

### Q415 · Games and Sports

Viswanathan Anand is associated with which sport?

A. Cricket
B. Chess
C. Badminton
D. Tennis

**Answer:** B. Chess
**Explanation:** Viswanathan Anand is a five-time World Chess Champion (2000, 2007, 2008, 2010, 2012). He became India's first Grandmaster in 1988 and was awarded the Padma Vibhushan.
**Shortcut / rule:** Anand = Chess (5× World Champion); First Indian GM = Anand (1988); Padma Vibhushan 2007.

### Q416 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who gave the slogan "Jai Jawan, Jai Kisan"?

A. Jawaharlal Nehru
B. Lal Bahadur Shastri
C. Indira Gandhi
D. Atal Bihari Vajpayee

**Answer:** B. Lal Bahadur Shastri
**Explanation:** PM Lal Bahadur Shastri gave the slogan "Jai Jawan, Jai Kisan" (Hail the Soldier, Hail the Farmer) during the 1965 Indo-Pakistani War to boost morale. Vajpayee later added "Jai Vigyan."
**Shortcut / rule:** "Jai Jawan, Jai Kisan" = Shastri (1965); "Jai Vigyan" added by Vajpayee (1998 Pokhran); "Jai Anusandhan" = PM Modi.

### Q417 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

ISRO was established in which year?

A. 1962
B. 1969
C. 1972
D. 1975

**Answer:** B. 1969
**Explanation:** ISRO was established on 15 August 1969 by Vikram Sarabhai. It replaced the earlier Indian National Committee for Space Research (INCOSPAR, 1962). ISRO HQ is in Bengaluru.
**Shortcut / rule:** ISRO = 15 Aug 1969 (Independence Day); INCOSPAR (predecessor) = 1962; Founder = Vikram Sarabhai.

### Q418 · UN & World Organizations

WTO (World Trade Organization) replaced which organization?

A. IMF
B. GATT
C. World Bank
D. UNCTAD

**Answer:** B. GATT
**Explanation:** WTO replaced GATT (General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade) on 1 January 1995. WTO is headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland. It deals with rules of trade between nations.
**Shortcut / rule:** GATT (1947) → WTO (1995); WTO HQ = Geneva; India = founding member of both GATT and WTO.

### Q419 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which gas causes acid rain?

A. Carbon dioxide
B. Sulphur dioxide and Nitrogen oxides
C. Methane
D. Ozone

**Answer:** B. Sulphur dioxide and Nitrogen oxides
**Explanation:** Acid rain is caused primarily by SO₂ and NOₓ emissions (from burning fossil fuels). These gases react with water vapour to form sulphuric and nitric acids (pH < 5.6).
**Shortcut / rule:** Acid rain: SO₂ + NOₓ → H₂SO₄ + HNO₃; Normal rain pH = 5.6; Acid rain pH < 5.6; Damages buildings, crops, aquatic life.

### Q420 · Computers & Applications

The first generation of computers used:

A. Transistors
B. Integrated Circuits
C. Vacuum Tubes
D. Microprocessors

**Answer:** C. Vacuum Tubes
**Explanation:** First generation computers (1940s-1950s) used vacuum tubes for processing. They were large, expensive, and generated enormous heat. Examples: ENIAC, UNIVAC.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st = Vacuum Tubes (ENIAC); 2nd = Transistors; 3rd = ICs; 4th = Microprocessors; 5th = AI/ULSI.

### Q421 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does ONGC stand for?

A. Oil and Natural Gas Corporation
B. Oil and National Gas Commission
C. Offshore Natural Gas Corporation
D. Oil and Natural Gas Commission

**Answer:** A. Oil and Natural Gas Corporation
**Explanation:** ONGC (Oil and Natural Gas Corporation Limited) is a Maharatna PSU and India's largest oil and gas exploration company. It was established in 1956 and HQ is in New Delhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** ONGC = oil exploration (Maharatna, 1956); IOCL = refining; GAIL = gas distribution; BPCL = petroleum marketing.

### Q422 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Jal Jeevan Mission aims to provide:

A. Irrigation to all farms
B. Tap water to every rural household
C. Clean rivers
D. Rainwater harvesting

**Answer:** B. Tap water to every rural household
**Explanation:** Jal Jeevan Mission was launched on 15 August 2019 to provide Functional Household Tap Connection (FHTC) to every rural household by 2024. Budget: ₹3.60 lakh crore.
**Shortcut / rule:** Jal Jeevan = tap water to rural homes (2019); Swachh Bharat = sanitation; Namami Gange = Ganga cleaning.

### Q423 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Ilbert Bill controversy occurred during the tenure of which Viceroy?

A. Lord Ripon
B. Lord Curzon
C. Lord Lytton
D. Lord Dufferin

**Answer:** A. Lord Ripon
**Explanation:** The Ilbert Bill (1883) during Lord Ripon's tenure proposed allowing Indian judges to try European offenders. It faced fierce opposition from Europeans and was eventually watered down.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ilbert Bill = Ripon (1883); Ripon = "Father of Local Self-Government"; Vernacular Press Act repealed by Ripon.

### Q424 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the last Mughal Emperor?

A. Aurangzeb
B. Shah Alam II
C. Bahadur Shah Zafar
D. Muhammad Shah

**Answer:** C. Bahadur Shah Zafar
**Explanation:** Bahadur Shah Zafar (Bahadur Shah II) was the last Mughal Emperor (1837-1857). He was declared the leader of the 1857 revolt. After its failure, he was exiled to Rangoon (Myanmar) where he died in 1862.
**Shortcut / rule:** Last Mughal = Bahadur Shah Zafar (exiled to Rangoon); First Mughal = Babur (1526); Greatest = Akbar.

### Q425 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian state is bordered by the maximum number of states?

A. Madhya Pradesh
B. Uttar Pradesh
C. Assam
D. Both A and B

**Answer:** B. Uttar Pradesh
**Explanation:** Uttar Pradesh borders 9 states (Uttarakhand, Himachal Pradesh, Haryana, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Bihar, plus a part of Delhi NCT). It borders the most states.

### Q426 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Speaker of Lok Sabha is elected by:

A. The President
B. Members of Lok Sabha
C. Members of both Houses
D. Electoral College

**Answer:** B. Members of Lok Sabha
**Explanation:** The Speaker is elected by the members of Lok Sabha from among its members. The Speaker remains in office until the first meeting of the next Lok Sabha (even if their term as MP ends).
**Shortcut / rule:** Speaker = elected by LS members; Casting vote only; Cannot vote in first instance; Resigns by writing to Deputy Speaker.

### Q427 · Indian Economy

The Pradhan Mantri Mudra Yojana provides loans up to:

A. ₹5 lakh
B. ₹10 lakh
C. ₹15 lakh
D. ₹25 lakh

**Answer:** B. ₹10 lakh
**Explanation:** PMMY provides loans up to ₹10 lakh to non-corporate, non-farm small businesses. Three categories: Shishu (up to ₹50,000), Kishore (₹50,000-₹5 lakh), Tarun (₹5-10 lakh).
**Shortcut / rule:** Mudra: Shishu ≤ 50K; Kishore ≤ 5L; Tarun ≤ 10L; No collateral required; Launched 8 April 2015.

### Q428 · Physics

Specific heat capacity is highest for which substance?

A. Iron
B. Copper
C. Water
D. Mercury

**Answer:** C. Water
**Explanation:** Water has the highest specific heat capacity (4,200 J/kg·K) of common substances. This is why water is used as a coolant and why coastal areas have moderate climates.
**Shortcut / rule:** Water = highest specific heat; Why oceans moderate climate; Water as coolant in engines and power plants.

### Q429 · Chemistry

Which compound is called "Quicklite" or "Quick Lime"?

A. CaCO₃
B. CaO
C. Ca(OH)₂
D. CaSO₄

**Answer:** B. CaO
**Explanation:** Quick Lime is Calcium Oxide (CaO). When water is added to it, it forms slaked lime [Ca(OH)₂] with a vigorous exothermic reaction. CaCO₃ is limestone.
**Shortcut / rule:** CaO = Quick lime; Ca(OH)₂ = Slaked lime; CaCO₃ = Limestone/Marble; CaO + H₂O → Ca(OH)₂ (exothermic).

### Q430 · Biology / Life Science

Which plant hormone promotes growth (cell elongation)?

A. Ethylene
B. Auxin
C. Abscisic acid
D. Cytokinin

**Answer:** B. Auxin
**Explanation:** Auxin is a plant hormone that promotes cell elongation, root formation, and phototropism. It is primarily produced in the shoot tips. Ethylene promotes fruit ripening.
**Shortcut / rule:** Auxin = growth/elongation; Gibberellin = stem growth; Cytokinin = cell division; Ethylene = ripening; Abscisic acid = inhibition/dormancy.

### Q431 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Which Mughal Emperor is associated with the Peacock Throne?

A. Akbar
B. Jahangir
C. Shah Jahan
D. Aurangzeb

**Answer:** C. Shah Jahan
**Explanation:** The Peacock Throne (Takht-e-Taus) was commissioned by Mughal Emperor Shah Jahan. It was adorned with precious gems. Nadir Shah of Persia took it away in 1739 after invading Delhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** Peacock Throne = Shah Jahan; Taken by Nadir Shah (1739); Kohinoor diamond also taken by Nadir Shah.

### Q432 · Games and Sports

The first Olympic medal for independent India was won by:

A. Khashaba Dadasaheb Jadhav
B. Leander Paes
C. Karnam Malleswari
D. Abhinav Bindra

**Answer:** A. Khashaba Dadasaheb Jadhav
**Explanation:** K.D. Jadhav won a Bronze medal in Wrestling (Bantamweight) at the 1952 Helsinki Olympics, making him the first individual Olympic medalist for independent India.

### Q433 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who was the first Indian woman to climb Mount Everest?

A. Arunima Sinha
B. Bachendri Pal
C. Santosh Yadav
D. Malavath Purna

**Answer:** B. Bachendri Pal
**Explanation:** Bachendri Pal became the first Indian woman to summit Mount Everest on 23 May 1984. Santosh Yadav was the first woman to climb Everest twice (1992, 1993).

### Q434 · UN & World Organizations

OPEC is associated with:

A. Nuclear energy
B. Petroleum exporting countries
C. Environmental protection
D. Education

**Answer:** B. Petroleum exporting countries
**Explanation:** OPEC (Organization of the Petroleum Exporting Countries) was founded in 1960 in Baghdad. HQ is in Vienna, Austria. It has 13 member countries and coordinates petroleum policies.

### Q435 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Coral reefs in India are found in:

A. Gulf of Kutch, Lakshadweep, Andaman & Nicobar, Gulf of Mannar
B. Only Lakshadweep
C. Only Andaman & Nicobar
D. Only Gulf of Mannar

**Answer:** A. Gulf of Kutch, Lakshadweep, Andaman & Nicobar, Gulf of Mannar
**Explanation:** India has coral reefs in four major regions: Gulf of Kutch (Gujarat), Gulf of Mannar (Tamil Nadu), Andaman & Nicobar Islands, and Lakshadweep Islands. Lakshadweep is an atoll coral reef.
