### Q436 · Computers & Applications

What is an IP address?

A. Internet Protocol address — unique identifier for devices on a network
B. Internal Processing address
C. Integrated Program address
D. Internet Provider address

**Answer:** A. Internet Protocol address — unique identifier for devices on a network
**Explanation:** An IP address is a unique numerical label assigned to each device connected to a computer network. IPv4 uses 32-bit addresses (e.g., 192.168.1.1), while IPv6 uses 128-bit.
**Shortcut / rule:** IPv4 = 32-bit (4.3 billion addresses); IPv6 = 128-bit (practically unlimited); Every device on internet has an IP.

### Q437 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

NTPC stands for:

A. National Thermal Power Corporation
B. National Transport Planning Commission
C. National Technology and Power Centre
D. National Telecommunications and Power Corporation

**Answer:** A. National Thermal Power Corporation
**Explanation:** NTPC Limited is India's largest power generating company (Maharatna PSU). Established in 1975, it generates about 1/4 of India's total power. HQ: New Delhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** NTPC = power generation (Maharatna, 1975); NHPC = hydropower; NPCIL = nuclear power; Power Grid = transmission.

### Q438 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Namami Gange Programme is related to:

A. Solar energy
B. Cleaning and conservation of river Ganga
C. Rural electrification
D. Forest conservation

**Answer:** B. Cleaning and conservation of river Ganga
**Explanation:** Namami Gange was launched in June 2014 as an Integrated Conservation Mission for the Ganga River. It has a budget of ₹20,000 crore and covers activities like sewage treatment, river surface cleaning, and afforestation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Namami Gange = Ganga cleaning (2014); National Mission for Clean Ganga (NMCG) = implementing body.

### Q439 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Swadeshi Movement was associated with which national leader's ideology?

A. Moderate ideology
B. Extremist ideology (Bal, Pal, Lal)
C. Gandhian ideology
D. Communist ideology

**Answer:** B. Extremist ideology (Bal, Pal, Lal)
**Explanation:** The Swadeshi Movement (1905) was led by Extremist leaders — Bal Gangadhar Tilak, Bipin Chandra Pal, and Lala Lajpat Rai (Lal, Bal, Pal) who advocated boycott of British goods and self-reliance.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lal Bal Pal = Extremists (Swadeshi); Moderates = Gokhale, Naoroji, Surendranath; Swadeshi = boycott British + use Indian goods.

### Q440 · Indian & World Geography

The Cherrapunji/Mawsynram region receives heavy rainfall due to:

A. Western disturbances
B. Northeast monsoon
C. Funnel-shaped hills trapping southwest monsoon
D. Tropical cyclones

**Answer:** C. Funnel-shaped hills trapping southwest monsoon
**Explanation:** Mawsynram and Cherrapunji in Meghalaya receive the highest rainfall because the funnel-shaped Khasi Hills trap moisture-laden southwest monsoon winds from the Bay of Bengal, forcing them to rise and release rain.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mawsynram = highest average rainfall (11,872 mm); Cherrapunji = 2nd; Both in Meghalaya (Khasi Hills).

### Q441 · Indian Polity & Governance

Which Part of the Constitution deals with Panchayats?

A. Part VIII
B. Part IX
C. Part X
D. Part XI

**Answer:** B. Part IX
**Explanation:** Part IX (Articles 243-243O) deals with Panchayats. It was added by the 73rd Amendment (1992). Part IX-A deals with Municipalities (74th Amendment).
**Shortcut / rule:** Part IX = Panchayats (73rd Amdt); Part IX-A = Municipalities (74th Amdt); 11th Schedule = Panchayat subjects; 12th Schedule = Municipal subjects.

### Q442 · Indian Economy

Disinvestment means:

A. Increasing government investment
B. Government selling its stake in PSUs
C. Banning foreign investment
D. Increasing FDI

**Answer:** B. Government selling its stake in PSUs
**Explanation:** Disinvestment is the process by which the government sells its shares in public sector enterprises to private investors. It is done to raise revenue and improve efficiency.
**Shortcut / rule:** Disinvestment = selling govt stake in PSUs; Privatization = selling majority stake (>51%); Strategic sale = selling to a specific buyer.

### Q443 · Physics

The nuclear reactor at a nuclear power plant uses which process?

A. Nuclear fusion
B. Nuclear fission
C. Chemical combustion
D. Electrolysis

**Answer:** B. Nuclear fission
**Explanation:** Nuclear power plants use nuclear fission — splitting heavy atoms (usually Uranium-235 or Plutonium-239) to release enormous energy. Nuclear fusion (combining light atoms) powers the Sun.
**Shortcut / rule:** Fission = splitting heavy atoms (power plants, bombs); Fusion = combining light atoms (Sun, hydrogen bomb); E = mc².

### Q444 · Chemistry

Stainless steel is an alloy of:

A. Iron and Carbon
B. Iron, Chromium and Nickel
C. Copper and Zinc
D. Iron and Manganese

**Answer:** B. Iron, Chromium and Nickel
**Explanation:** Stainless steel is an alloy of Iron (Fe), Chromium (Cr ≥ 10.5%), and Nickel (Ni). The chromium forms a passive oxide layer that prevents rusting.
**Shortcut / rule:** Stainless Steel = Fe + Cr + Ni; Steel = Fe + C; Bronze = Cu + Sn; Brass = Cu + Zn; Solder = Pb + Sn.

### Q445 · Biology / Life Science

The vaccine for smallpox was developed by:

A. Alexander Fleming
B. Louis Pasteur
C. Edward Jenner
D. Robert Koch

**Answer:** C. Edward Jenner
**Explanation:** Edward Jenner developed the smallpox vaccine in 1796 using cowpox virus. He is called the "Father of Immunology." Smallpox was declared eradicated by WHO in 1980.
**Shortcut / rule:** Jenner = smallpox vaccine (1796); Pasteur = rabies vaccine (1885); Salk = polio vaccine (1955); Smallpox eradicated = 1980.

### Q446 · Games and Sports

The 'Hockey India League' and 'Pro Kabaddi League' are examples of:

A. International tournaments
B. Professional sports leagues in India
C. Olympic events
D. School sports competitions

**Answer:** B. Professional sports leagues in India
**Explanation:** India has several professional sports leagues: IPL (Cricket), ISL (Football), PKL (Kabaddi), HIL (Hockey), PBL (Badminton). These franchise-based leagues have boosted sports popularity.
**Shortcut / rule:** IPL = Cricket (2008); PKL = Kabaddi (2014); ISL = Football (2014); HIL = Hockey; PBL = Badminton.

### Q447 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who was the first woman President of India?

A. Indira Gandhi
B. Pratibha Patil
C. Sushma Swaraj
D. Droupadi Murmu

**Answer:** B. Pratibha Patil
**Explanation:** Pratibha Devisingh Patil was the first woman President of India, serving from 2007 to 2012 (12th President). Droupadi Murmu (2022) is the first tribal woman President.
**Shortcut / rule:** First woman President = Pratibha Patil (2007); First woman PM = Indira Gandhi (1966); First tribal woman President = Droupadi Murmu (2022).

### Q448 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Mangrove forests in India are mostly found in:

A. Western Ghats
B. Sundarbans
C. Thar Desert
D. Deccan Plateau

**Answer:** B. Sundarbans
**Explanation:** The Sundarbans in West Bengal has the largest mangrove forest in India and the world. India has about 4,975 km² of mangrove cover. Other mangrove areas: Gujarat, Maharashtra, Andaman & Nicobar.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest mangrove = Sundarbans (India + Bangladesh); Mangroves = coastal, salt-tolerant; Protect from tsunami/cyclone.

### Q449 · Computers & Applications

Which topology connects all computers to a single cable?

A. Star topology
B. Bus topology
C. Ring topology
D. Mesh topology

**Answer:** B. Bus topology
**Explanation:** In Bus topology, all devices are connected to a single central cable (the bus). Data travels along the bus and each device checks if the data is addressed to it.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bus = single cable; Star = central hub; Ring = circular; Mesh = every device connected to every other; Star is most common today.

### Q450 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Goods and Services Tax (GST) Council is headed by:

A. Prime Minister
B. Finance Minister of India
C. RBI Governor
D. Chief Justice of India

**Answer:** B. Finance Minister of India
**Explanation:** The GST Council is a constitutional body (Article 279A) chaired by the Union Finance Minister. Members include the Union Minister of State for Finance and the Finance Ministers of all states/UTs.
**Shortcut / rule:** GST Council: Chairman = Union FM; Art 279A; Decisions by 3/4 majority; Centre = 1/3 vote; States = 2/3 vote.

### Q451 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms (1919) introduced the system of:

A. Separate Electorates
B. Diarchy in Provinces
C. Direct Elections
D. Universal Suffrage

**Answer:** B. Diarchy in Provinces
**Explanation:** The Government of India Act 1919 (Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms) introduced Diarchy in provinces — subjects were divided into 'Transferred' (Indian ministers) and 'Reserved' (Governor).
**Shortcut / rule:** 1919 = Diarchy (Montagu-Chelmsford); 1909 = Separate Electorates (Morley-Minto); 1935 = Provincial Autonomy.

### Q452 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Government of India Act, 1935 was based on the recommendations of:

A. Simon Commission
B. Cripps Mission
C. Cabinet Mission
D. Nehru Report

**Answer:** A. Simon Commission
**Explanation:** The Government of India Act 1935 was based on the Simon Commission Report and the Round Table Conferences. It introduced Provincial Autonomy, Federal structure, and established RBI (1935).
**Shortcut / rule:** GOI Act 1935: Provincial Autonomy + Federal Court + RBI established + Abolished Diarchy in provinces.

### Q453 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian river is known as the "Sorrow of Bengal"?

A. Hooghly
B. Damodar
C. Teesta
D. Mahanadi

**Answer:** B. Damodar
**Explanation:** The Damodar River was called the "Sorrow of Bengal" due to its frequent devastating floods. The Damodar Valley Corporation (DVC) was established in 1948 to control floods.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sorrow of Bengal = Damodar; Sorrow of Bihar = Kosi; Sorrow of Assam = Brahmaputra; DVC modeled on TVA (USA).

### Q454 · Indian & World Geography

Which strait connects the Red Sea to the Indian Ocean?

A. Strait of Hormuz
B. Bab-el-Mandeb
C. Strait of Malacca
D. Strait of Gibraltar

**Answer:** B. Bab-el-Mandeb
**Explanation:** Bab-el-Mandeb strait connects the Red Sea to the Gulf of Aden (Indian Ocean), between Yemen and Djibouti/Eritrea. It is a vital chokepoint for global maritime trade.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bab-el-Mandeb = Red Sea-Indian Ocean; Hormuz = Persian Gulf-Arabian Sea; Malacca = Indian Ocean-Pacific; Gibraltar = Mediterranean-Atlantic.

### Q455 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Concurrent List contains how many subjects?

A. 97
B. 52
C. 66
D. 47

**Answer:** B. 52
**Explanation:** The Concurrent List (7th Schedule, List III) originally had 47 subjects, now has 52. Both Centre and State can legislate on these subjects, but in case of conflict, Central law prevails.
**Shortcut / rule:** Union List = 100 (defence, foreign); State List = 61 (police, health); Concurrent = 52 (education, forests); Central law prevails on Concurrent.

### Q456 · Indian Economy

Census in India is conducted every:

A. 5 years
B. 10 years
C. 15 years
D. 20 years

**Answer:** B. 10 years
**Explanation:** Census in India is conducted every 10 years under the Census Act 1948. The first census was in 1872 (non-synchronous) and 1881 (first synchronous). The last completed census was 2011.
**Shortcut / rule:** Census = every 10 years; First = 1872; First complete = 1881; Registrar General of India conducts it.

### Q457 · Physics

The image formed by a plane mirror is:

A. Real and inverted
B. Virtual and erect
C. Real and erect
D. Virtual and inverted

**Answer:** B. Virtual and erect
**Explanation:** A plane mirror forms a virtual, erect, laterally inverted image of the same size as the object. The image appears to be behind the mirror at the same distance as the object.
**Shortcut / rule:** Plane mirror: virtual, erect, same size, laterally inverted; Image distance = Object distance.

### Q458 · Chemistry

The pH of lemon juice is approximately:

A. 2-3
B. 5-6
C. 7
D. 9-10

**Answer:** A. 2-3
**Explanation:** Lemon juice has a pH of about 2-3 due to citric acid content. It is acidic. For comparison: vinegar ~3, milk ~6.5, pure water = 7, soap ~9-10.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lemon = pH 2-3 (acidic); Milk = 6.5; Water = 7; Blood = 7.4; Soap = 9-10 (basic).

### Q459 · Biology / Life Science

Which gland is called the "Master Gland"?

A. Thyroid
B. Adrenal
C. Pituitary
D. Pancreas

**Answer:** C. Pituitary
**Explanation:** The Pituitary gland is called the "Master Gland" because it controls other endocrine glands through its hormones (TSH, ACTH, GH, etc.). It is located at the base of the brain.
**Shortcut / rule:** Pituitary = Master Gland (controls others); Growth Hormone (GH) from pituitary; Size = pea-sized; Located: base of brain (sella turcica).

### Q460 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Which dynasty built the temples at Pattadakal?

A. Pallava Dynasty
B. Chalukya Dynasty
C. Rashtrakuta Dynasty
D. Hoysala Dynasty

**Answer:** B. Chalukya Dynasty
**Explanation:** The temples at Pattadakal in Karnataka were built by the Chalukya dynasty (7th-8th century). Pattadakal is a UNESCO World Heritage Site with both Nagara and Dravidian style temples.
**Shortcut / rule:** Pattadakal/Badami/Aihole = Chalukya (Karnataka); Belur/Halebid = Hoysala; Hampi = Vijayanagara.

### Q461 · Games and Sports

The term "Checkmate" is used in which game?

A. Cricket
B. Chess
C. Hockey
D. Polo

**Answer:** B. Chess
**Explanation:** "Checkmate" in chess means the king is in a position to be captured (in "check") and cannot escape. The game ends immediately when checkmate occurs. The word comes from Persian "Shah Mat" (the king is dead).
**Shortcut / rule:** Chess terms: Checkmate, Stalemate, Castling, En passant, Gambit; Chess origin = India (Chaturanga).

### Q462 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Kailash Satyarthi won the Nobel Peace Prize in which year?

A. 2012
B. 2014
C. 2016
D. 2018

**Answer:** B. 2014
**Explanation:** Kailash Satyarthi shared the Nobel Peace Prize 2014 with Malala Yousafzai for their struggle against child labour and for children's right to education. He founded Bachpan Bachao Andolan.
**Shortcut / rule:** Satyarthi = Nobel Peace 2014 (child rights); Shared with Malala; Bachpan Bachao Andolan = 1980.

### Q463 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's navigation satellite system is called:

A. GPS
B. GLONASS
C. NavIC (IRNSS)
D. Galileo

**Answer:** C. NavIC (IRNSS)
**Explanation:** NavIC (Navigation with Indian Constellation), also known as IRNSS (Indian Regional Navigation Satellite System), is India's autonomous regional satellite navigation system with 7 satellites providing coverage over India and 1,500 km around it.
**Shortcut / rule:** NavIC = India's GPS (7 satellites); GPS = USA (31); GLONASS = Russia; Galileo = EU; BeiDou = China.

### Q464 · UN & World Organizations

The International Court of Justice has how many judges?

A. 9
B. 12
C. 15
D. 20

**Answer:** C. 15
**Explanation:** The ICJ has 15 judges elected for 9-year terms. No two judges can be from the same nationality. Five judges are elected every 3 years. It is the principal judicial organ of the UN.
**Shortcut / rule:** ICJ = 15 judges, 9-year term; UN Security Council = 15 members (5 permanent + 10 non-permanent).

### Q465 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Great Barrier Reef is located off the coast of:

A. Brazil
B. India
C. Australia
D. South Africa

**Answer:** C. Australia
**Explanation:** The Great Barrier Reef is the world's largest coral reef system, stretching over 2,300 km along the northeast coast of Australia (Queensland). It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Great Barrier Reef = Australia (largest reef); Coral bleaching = major threat; UNESCO World Heritage.

### Q466 · Computers & Applications

What is the full form of HTML?

A. Hyper Text Markup Language
B. High Text Machine Language
C. Hyper Transfer Markup Language
D. Home Text Markup Language

**Answer:** A. Hyper Text Markup Language
**Explanation:** HTML is the standard markup language for creating web pages. It was created by Tim Berners-Lee in 1991. HTML uses tags to structure content on web pages.
**Shortcut / rule:** HTML = web structure (Berners-Lee, 1991); CSS = styling; JavaScript = interactivity; PHP = server-side.

### Q467 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

SAIL stands for:

A. Steel Authority of India Limited
B. Steel and Aluminium Industries Limited
C. South Asian Iron and Lead Corporation
D. Steel Alliance of Indian Labourers

**Answer:** A. Steel Authority of India Limited
**Explanation:** SAIL is a Maharatna PSU and India's largest steel-producing company. It was incorporated in 1973 and operates several steel plants across India (Bhilai, Durgapur, Rourkela, Bokaro).
**Shortcut / rule:** SAIL = steel (Maharatna, 1973); Major plants: Bhilai (MP→CG), Rourkela (Odisha), Durgapur (WB), Bokaro (Jharkhand).

### Q468 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana is a savings scheme for:

A. Senior citizens
B. Girl children
C. Farmers
D. Students

**Answer:** B. Girl children
**Explanation:** Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana was launched on 22 January 2015 under Beti Bachao Beti Padhao. Parents/guardians can open an account for a girl child below 10 years. It offers one of the highest interest rates among small savings schemes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sukanya Samriddhi = girl child savings (2015); Part of Beti Bachao; Max 2 accounts (2 daughters); Matures at age 21.

### Q469 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who is known as the "Frontier Gandhi"?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan
C. Abdul Kalam Azad
D. Maulana Hasrat Mohani

**Answer:** B. Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan
**Explanation:** Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan, a Pashtun leader from the NWFP (now Pakistan), was called the "Frontier Gandhi" or "Badshah Khan." He founded the Khudai Khidmatgar movement and was a close ally of Gandhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** Frontier Gandhi = Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan; Bharat Ratna 1987 (first non-Indian); Khudai Khidmatgar = "Servants of God."

### Q470 · Indian & World Geography

The Siwaliks are part of which mountain range?

A. Greater Himalayas
B. Lesser Himalayas
C. Outer Himalayas
D. Trans Himalayas

**Answer:** C. Outer Himalayas
**Explanation:** The Siwaliks (Shivaliks) form the outermost and lowest range of the Himalayas, with elevation 600-1500m. They extend from Jammu to Arunachal Pradesh. The duns (valleys) like Dehradun lie between Siwaliks and Lesser Himalayas.
**Shortcut / rule:** Himalayas (south to north): Siwaliks (Outer) → Lesser/Middle (Pir Panjal) → Greater (Himadri) → Trans (Karakoram, Ladakh).

### Q471 · Indian Polity & Governance

What is a "Lame Duck" session in the context of Indian Parliament?

A. Session with no business
B. Last session of outgoing Lok Sabha after election of new one
C. Budget session
D. Joint session

**Answer:** B. Last session of outgoing Lok Sabha after election of new one
**Explanation:** A "Lame Duck" session occurs when the outgoing Lok Sabha meets after a new Lok Sabha has been elected but before it convenes. Members who lost re-election still participate.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lame Duck = outgoing house after new elected; Anti-defection law (10th Schedule) reduced this.

### Q472 · Indian Economy

The concept of "Economic Planning" in India was influenced by:

A. USA
B. USSR (Soviet Union)
C. UK
D. Japan

**Answer:** B. USSR (Soviet Union)
**Explanation:** India's Five Year Plans were influenced by the USSR's planned economy model. The Planning Commission (1950) was modeled on Soviet planning. However, India adopted a mixed economy rather than fully planned.
**Shortcut / rule:** Planning = Soviet influence; Mixed economy = Indian approach; 1st FYP = 1951; Planning Commission → NITI Aayog (2015).

### Q473 · Physics

Optical fibre works on the principle of:

A. Refraction
B. Reflection
C. Total Internal Reflection
D. Diffraction

**Answer:** C. Total Internal Reflection
**Explanation:** Optical fibres transmit data as light pulses through glass or plastic fibres using total internal reflection. Light enters one end and bounces along the core until it exits the other end.
**Shortcut / rule:** Optical fibre = total internal reflection; Used in internet, medical endoscopy; Faster than copper cables.

### Q474 · Chemistry

The hardest substance made by humans is:

A. Diamond
B. Carborundum
C. Aggregated Diamond Nanorods
D. Boron Nitride

**Answer:** C. Aggregated Diamond Nanorods
**Explanation:** Aggregated diamond nanorods (ADNRs or hyperdiamond) are harder than natural diamond. Among commonly known substances, diamond remains the hardest natural substance (10 on Mohs scale).
**Shortcut / rule:** Natural hardest = Diamond; Synthetic harder = ADNRs; Mohs scale: 1 (talc) to 10 (diamond).

### Q475 · Biology / Life Science

The pH of human blood is:

A. 6.8
B. 7.0
C. 7.35-7.45
D. 8.0

**Answer:** C. 7.35-7.45
**Explanation:** Human blood is slightly alkaline with a pH of 7.35-7.45. The body maintains this narrow range through buffer systems. Deviation causes acidosis (pH < 7.35) or alkalosis (pH > 7.45).
**Shortcut / rule:** Blood pH = 7.35-7.45 (slightly alkaline); Stomach = pH 1-2; Urine = pH 4.5-8; Tears = pH 7.

### Q476 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Dilwara Temples' at Mount Abu are associated with which religion?

A. Hinduism
B. Buddhism
C. Jainism
D. Sikhism

**Answer:** C. Jainism
**Explanation:** The Dilwara Temples at Mount Abu, Rajasthan are exquisite Jain temples built between the 11th-13th centuries. They are famous for their intricate marble carvings, among the finest in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dilwara = Jain (Mount Abu, marble); Ranakpur = Jain (Rajasthan); Khajuraho = Hindu + Jain; Bodhgaya = Buddhist.

### Q477 · Games and Sports

The Hockey World Cup is organized by:

A. IOC
B. FIH (Fédération Internationale de Hockey)

D. ICC

**Explanation:** FIH (International Hockey Federation) organizes the Hockey World Cup, first held in 1971 (men) and 1974 (women). India won the 1975 Hockey World Cup.

Har Gobind Khorana received the Nobel Prize for work in:

A. Economics
B. Physics
C. Medicine/Physiology
D. Peace

**Answer:** C. Medicine/Physiology
**Explanation:** Har Gobind Khorana (Indian-born American biochemist) shared the Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine in 1968 for his work on the interpretation of the genetic code and its function in protein synthesis.
**Shortcut / rule:** Khorana = Nobel Medicine 1968 (genetic code); Born in Raipur, Punjab (now Pakistan); Indian-origin.

### Q479 · UN & World Organizations

The UN Security Council has how many permanent members?

A. 3
B. 5
C. 10
D. 15

**Answer:** B. 5
**Explanation:** The UNSC has 5 permanent members (P5): USA, UK, France, Russia, and China. Each has veto power. There are also 10 non-permanent members elected for 2-year terms.
**Shortcut / rule:** P5 = USA, UK, France, Russia, China (veto power); 10 non-permanent = 2-year terms; Total UNSC = 15.

### Q480 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Narmada Bachao Andolan' was led by:

A. Sunderlal Bahuguna
B. Medha Patkar

**Answer:** B. Medha Patkar
**Explanation:** Medha Patkar led the Narmada Bachao Andolan (Save Narmada Movement) against the Sardar Sarovar Dam on the Narmada River, advocating for displaced people and environmental conservation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Narmada Bachao = Medha Patkar; Chipko = Sunderlal Bahuguna + Gaura Devi; Appiko = Pandurang Hegde.

### Q481 · Computers & Applications

What does RAM stand for?

A. Read Access Memory
B. Random Access Memory
C. Rapid Application Memory
D. Remote Access Module

**Answer:** B. Random Access Memory
**Explanation:** RAM is the computer's short-term memory where data being actively used is stored. It is volatile — data is lost when power is off. More RAM = faster multitasking.
**Shortcut / rule:** RAM = volatile (temporary); ROM = non-volatile (permanent); DRAM = main memory; SRAM = cache.

### Q482 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

HAL stands for:

A. Hindustan Aeronautics Limited
B. Heavy Aviation Laboratory
C. Hindustan Airlines Limited
D. High Altitude Laboratory

**Answer:** A. Hindustan Aeronautics Limited
**Explanation:** HAL is India's premier aerospace and defense company (Maharatna PSU). Founded in 1940, HQ in Bengaluru. It designs and manufactures aircraft like Tejas (LCA), helicopters, and engines.
**Shortcut / rule:** HAL = aerospace (Maharatna, 1940, Bengaluru); Tejas = HAL's light combat aircraft; BEL = electronics; BDL = missiles.

### Q483 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Aspirational Districts Programme aims to:

A. Develop metropolitan cities
B. Rapidly develop India's most underdeveloped districts
C. Build smart cities
D. Promote exports

**Answer:** B. Rapidly develop India's most underdeveloped districts
**Explanation:** Launched in January 2018, the Aspirational Districts Programme identifies 112 of India's most underdeveloped districts for rapid improvement in health, nutrition, education, agriculture, water, and infrastructure.
**Shortcut / rule:** Aspirational Districts = 112 districts (2018); Monitored by NITI Aayog; Focus: health, education, agriculture, infrastructure.

### Q484 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The 'Drain of Wealth' theory was propounded by:

A. R.C. Dutt
B. Dadabhai Naoroji
C. Mahatma Gandhi
D. Surendranath Banerjee

**Answer:** B. Dadabhai Naoroji
**Explanation:** Dadabhai Naoroji propounded the 'Drain of Wealth' theory in his book "Poverty and Un-British Rule in India" (1901). He argued that British policies systematically drained India's wealth to Britain.
**Shortcut / rule:** Drain theory = Naoroji; "Poverty and Un-British Rule" = Naoroji; R.C. Dutt also expanded on it in "Economic History of India."

### Q485 · Indian & World Geography

Which state has the highest literacy rate in India?

A. Mizoram
B. Kerala
C. Goa
D. Tripura

**Explanation:** Kerala has the highest literacy rate in India at 93.91% (Census 2011). Bihar has the lowest (63.82%). The national average is 74.04%.
**Shortcut / rule:** Highest literacy = Kerala (93.91%); Lowest = Bihar (63.82%); National avg = 74.04% (2011 Census).

### Q486 · Indian Polity & Governance

The first Lokpal (national ombudsman) of India was:

A. T.N. Seshan
B. Justice Pinaki Chandra Ghose
C. Anna Hazare
D. Arvind Kejriwal

**Answer:** B. Justice Pinaki Chandra Ghose
**Explanation:** Justice Pinaki Chandra Ghose was appointed as India's first Lokpal on 23 March 2019 under the Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act, 2013.

### Q487 · Indian Economy

Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) means:

A. Investment by Indian companies abroad
B. Investment by foreign entities in Indian businesses
C. Government lending to foreign countries
D. Import of foreign goods

**Answer:** B. Investment by foreign entities in Indian businesses
**Explanation:** FDI is investment by a foreign entity in a company or project in another country, typically implying long-term interest and control (≥10% equity). India allows up to 100% FDI in many sectors.
**Shortcut / rule:** FDI = long-term foreign investment (≥10% equity); FPI = portfolio investment (short-term); FDI routes: Automatic + Government approval.

### Q488 · Physics

Which colour of light is scattered the most by the atmosphere (why sky appears blue)?

A. Red
B. Green
C. Yellow
D. Violet/Blue

**Answer:** D. Violet/Blue
**Explanation:** Blue light is scattered more by the atmosphere due to Rayleigh scattering (scattering ∝ 1/λ⁴). Though violet scatters even more, our eyes are more sensitive to blue, so the sky appears blue.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rayleigh scattering: short λ scattered more; Blue sky = scattering; Red sunset = blue scattered away, red passes through.

### Q489 · Chemistry

Which is the most abundant element in the Earth's crust?

A. Iron
B. Silicon
C. Oxygen
D. Aluminium

**Answer:** C. Oxygen
**Explanation:** Oxygen is the most abundant element in the Earth's crust (~46% by weight), mainly in oxide and silicate minerals. Silicon is second (~28%), followed by Aluminium (~8%).
**Shortcut / rule:** Earth's crust: O (46%) > Si (28%) > Al (8%) > Fe (5%); Universe: H > He; Atmosphere: N₂ > O₂.

### Q490 · Biology / Life Science

Which organ stores glycogen in the human body?

A. Heart
B. Kidney
C. Liver
D. Lungs

**Answer:** C. Liver
**Explanation:** The liver stores glucose as glycogen and releases it into the blood when needed to maintain blood sugar levels. Muscles also store glycogen for local energy use.
**Shortcut / rule:** Liver stores glycogen; Insulin (pancreas) → glucose to glycogen; Glucagon → glycogen to glucose; Diabetes = insulin problem.

A. Lata Mangeshkar

A. New Delhi

### Q495 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Silent Valley' is located in which state?

A. Kerala
B. Karnataka

### Q496 · Computers & Applications

What is Cloud Computing?

A. Computing using weather data
B. Storing and accessing data/services over the internet instead of local hardware
C. Using computers in aircraft
D. A type of supercomputer

### Q497 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

A. Rural development
B. Urban infrastructure (water supply, sewerage)

**Explanation:** AMRUT was launched on 25 June 2015 to provide basic services like water supply, sewerage, urban transport, and green spaces in 500 cities. AMRUT 2.0 launched in 2021 aims for water supply to all urban households.

### Q498 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Wood's Despatch (1854) was related to:

A. Military reforms
B. Education reforms
C. Land revenue reforms
D. Judicial reforms

**Explanation:** Wood's Despatch (1854) is called the "Magna Carta of English Education in India." It recommended creating universities at Calcutta, Bombay, and Madras; establishing a system from primary schools to universities.

### Q499 · Indian & World Geography

The Andaman Islands are separated from the Nicobar Islands by:

A. Palk Strait
B. Duncan Passage
C. Ten Degree Channel
D. Nine Degree Channel

**Answer:** C. Ten Degree Channel
**Explanation:** The Ten Degree Channel (10°N latitude) separates the Andaman Islands from the Nicobar Islands. The Nine Degree Channel separates Lakshadweep's Minicoy Island from the rest of Lakshadweep.
**Shortcut / rule:** 10° Channel = Andaman & Nicobar; 9° Channel = within Lakshadweep; 8° Channel = Maldives & Minicoy; Duncan = S. Andaman & Little Andaman.

### Q500 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of "Basic Structure" of the Constitution was established in which case?

A. Golaknath Case (1967)
B. Kesavananda Bharati Case (1973)
C. Minerva Mills Case (1980)
D. Maneka Gandhi Case (1978)

**Answer:** B. Kesavananda Bharati Case (1973)
**Explanation:** In the Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala case (1973), the Supreme Court held that Parliament can amend the Constitution but cannot alter its "Basic Structure." This landmark doctrine protects fundamental features like secularism, federalism, judicial review, etc.
**Shortcut / rule:** Basic Structure = Kesavananda (1973); 13-judge bench; Parliament can amend but not destroy basic structure; Reinforced in Minerva Mills (1980).

### Q501 · Indian Economy

The term 'Budget' is derived from which language?

A. Latin
B. French
C. Greek
D. German

**Answer:** B. French
**Explanation:** The word "Budget" comes from the French word "bougette" meaning a small leather bag. In India, the Union Budget is presented on 1 February by the Finance Minister.
**Shortcut / rule:** Budget = French (bougette); First Indian Budget = 7 April 1860 (James Wilson); First Republic Budget = R.K. Shanmukham Chetty (1947).

### Q502 · Physics

The unit of work is the same as the unit of:

A. Power
B. Force
C. Energy
D. Momentum

**Answer:** C. Energy
**Explanation:** Both work and energy are measured in Joules (J) in SI units. Work = Force × Displacement. Energy is the capacity to do work.
**Shortcut / rule:** Work = Energy = Joule; Power = Watt (J/s); Force = Newton; Momentum = kg·m/s.

### Q503 · Chemistry

Ethanol (drinking alcohol) has the chemical formula:

A. CH₃OH
B. C₂H₅OH
C. C₃H₇OH
D. C₆H₁₂O₆

**Answer:** B. C₂H₅OH
**Explanation:** Ethanol (ethyl alcohol) has the formula C₂H₅OH. It is produced by fermentation of sugars. It is used in alcoholic beverages, as a solvent, and as a biofuel.
**Shortcut / rule:** Methanol (CH₃OH) = poisonous (wood alcohol); Ethanol (C₂H₅OH) = drinking alcohol; Glycerol = C₃H₈O₃.

### Q504 · Biology / Life Science

Polio vaccine was developed by:

A. Edward Jenner
B. Jonas Salk
C. Alexander Fleming
D. Louis Pasteur

**Answer:** B. Jonas Salk
**Explanation:** Jonas Salk developed the inactivated polio vaccine (IPV) in 1955. Albert Sabin later developed the oral polio vaccine (OPV). India was declared polio-free in 2014.
**Shortcut / rule:** Salk = IPV (injection, 1955); Sabin = OPV (oral drops); India polio-free = 2014; Pulse Polio = mass immunization.

### Q505 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Pattachitra' painting style is associated with which state?

A. Rajasthan
B. Madhya Pradesh
C. Odisha
D. Kerala

**Answer:** C. Odisha
**Explanation:** Pattachitra is a traditional cloth-based scroll painting from Odisha (also practiced in West Bengal). The name comes from Sanskrit "patta" (cloth) and "chitra" (picture). Themes are mainly mythological.
**Shortcut / rule:** Pattachitra = Odisha/WB; Madhubani = Bihar; Warli = Maharashtra; Phad = Rajasthan; Tanjore = Tamil Nadu.

### Q506 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Salt Satyagraha was part of which larger movement?

A. Non-Cooperation Movement
B. Civil Disobedience Movement
C. Quit India Movement
D. Swadeshi Movement

**Answer:** B. Civil Disobedience Movement
**Explanation:** The Salt March (Dandi March, March-April 1930) launched the Civil Disobedience Movement. Gandhi walked 241 miles from Sabarmati Ashram to Dandi to make salt and defy British salt tax.
**Shortcut / rule:** CDM = 1930 (Salt March); NCM = 1920; QIM = 1942; CDM was suspended after Gandhi-Irwin Pact (1931).

### Q507 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian state shares the longest international border?

A. Rajasthan
B. West Bengal
C. Arunachal Pradesh
D. Jammu & Kashmir

**Answer:** B. West Bengal
**Explanation:** West Bengal shares the longest international border with Bangladesh (~2,217 km). Rajasthan has the longest border with Pakistan (~1,170 km, the longest India-Pakistan border by state).
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest int'l border by state: WB with Bangladesh (~2,217 km); India-Bangladesh = longest bilateral border (~4,096 km total).

### Q508 · Indian Polity & Governance

Writs in the Indian Constitution are inspired by:

A. American Constitution
B. British practice/English law
C. French Constitution
D. Soviet Constitution

**Answer:** B. British practice/English law
**Explanation:** The 5 writs (Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Certiorari, Quo Warranto) in the Indian Constitution are borrowed from British/English law. Art 32 (SC) and Art 226 (HC) provide writ jurisdiction.
**Shortcut / rule:** 5 writs: Habeas Corpus (produce body); Mandamus (command to act); Prohibition (stop lower court); Certiorari (transfer case); Quo Warranto (by what authority).

### Q509 · Indian Economy

What is CRR (Cash Reserve Ratio)?

A. Percentage of deposits banks must keep with themselves
B. Percentage of deposits banks must maintain with RBI
C. Interest rate charged by RBI
D. Tax on bank profits

**Answer:** B. Percentage of deposits banks must maintain with RBI
**Explanation:** CRR is the percentage of a bank's total deposits that must be maintained as cash reserves with the RBI. It controls money supply and inflation.
**Shortcut / rule:** CRR = cash with RBI; SLR = liquid assets (govt securities); Repo = RBI lends; Reverse Repo = banks deposit with RBI.

### Q510 · Physics

The acceleration due to gravity on Earth is approximately:

A. 8.9 m/s²
B. 9.8 m/s²
C. 10.8 m/s²
D. 11.8 m/s²

**Answer:** B. 9.8 m/s²
**Explanation:** The acceleration due to gravity (g) at Earth's surface is approximately 9.8 m/s² (often rounded to 10 m/s² for calculations). It varies slightly with latitude and altitude.
**Shortcut / rule:** g = 9.8 m/s²; g maximum at poles; g minimum at equator; g decreases with altitude; Moon g = 1.6 m/s² (1/6th Earth).

### Q511 · Chemistry

Blue vitriol is the common name for:

A. Copper Sulphate (CuSO₄·5H₂O)
B. Iron Sulphate (FeSO₄·7H₂O)
C. Zinc Sulphate (ZnSO₄·7H₂O)
D. Sodium Sulphate (Na₂SO₄·10H₂O)

**Answer:** A. Copper Sulphate (CuSO₄·5H₂O)
**Explanation:** Blue Vitriol (Neela Thotha) is hydrated Copper Sulphate with 5 water molecules. It is blue in colour and used in agriculture as a fungicide and in electroplating.
**Shortcut / rule:** Blue Vitriol = CuSO₄; Green Vitriol = FeSO₄ (Hara Kasis); White Vitriol = ZnSO₄; Glauber's Salt = Na₂SO₄.

### Q512 · Biology / Life Science

The study of bones is called:

A. Osteology
B. Oncology
C. Nephrology
D. Cardiology

**Answer:** A. Osteology
**Explanation:** Osteology is the branch of anatomy dealing with the study of bones. The human skeletal system has 206 bones in adults.
**Shortcut / rule:** Osteology = bones; Oncology = cancer; Nephrology = kidneys; Cardiology = heart; Neurology = nervous system; Dermatology = skin.

### Q513 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who composed India's National Anthem "Jana Gana Mana"?

A. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay
B. Rabindranath Tagore
C. Muhammad Iqbal
D. Subramania Bharati

**Answer:** B. Rabindranath Tagore
**Explanation:** "Jana Gana Mana" was written by Rabindranath Tagore in Bengali. It was first sung at the Calcutta session of INC on 27 December 1911. It was adopted as the National Anthem on 24 January 1950.
**Shortcut / rule:** National Anthem = Jana Gana Mana (Tagore); Duration = 52 seconds; National Song = Vande Mataram (Bankim Chandra).

### Q514 · Games and Sports

Mary Kom is associated with which sport?

A. Wrestling
B. Boxing
C. Weightlifting
D. Athletics

**Answer:** B. Boxing
**Explanation:** MC Mary Kom is a six-time World Amateur Boxing Champion from Manipur. She won a Bronze medal at the 2012 London Olympics (Flyweight). She is known as "Magnificent Mary."
**Shortcut / rule:** Mary Kom = Boxing (6× World Champion, Bronze 2012 Olympics); From Manipur; Padma Vibhushan.

### Q515 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's first Mars orbiter mission "Mangalyaan" made India the __ country to reach Mars:

A. 2nd
B. 3rd
C. 4th
D. 5th

**Answer:** C. 4th
**Explanation:** India became the 4th space agency to reach Mars orbit (after NASA, Roscosmos, ESA) and the first to do so on its maiden attempt. ISRO spent only ~₹450 crore — less than the budget of the movie "Gravity."
**Shortcut / rule:** Mangalyaan = 4th to Mars, 1st on maiden attempt; Cost = ~₹450 crore ($74 million); Launch = 5 Nov 2013.

### Q516 · UN & World Organizations

BRICS originally stood for:

A. Brazil, Russia, India, China, South Africa
B. Britain, Russia, India, China, Singapore
C. Brazil, Russia, Indonesia, China, South Africa
D. Brazil, Russia, India, Canada, South Africa

**Answer:** A. Brazil, Russia, India, China, South Africa
**Explanation:** BRICS was originally BRIC (coined by Jim O'Neill, 2001; formal alliance 2009). South Africa joined in 2010, making it BRICS. The group expanded further in 2024.
**Shortcut / rule:** BRIC coined = 2001 (Jim O'Neill); First summit = 2009; +South Africa = 2010 (BRICS); Expanded 2024 (Egypt, Ethiopia, Iran, UAE, Saudi Arabia).

### Q517 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Rann of Kutch' is famous for which ecosystem?

A. Tropical rainforest
B. Mangrove forest
C. Salt marsh/desert and wild ass sanctuary
D. Coral reef

**Answer:** C. Salt marsh/desert and wild ass sanctuary
**Explanation:** The Rann of Kutch in Gujarat is a vast salt marsh famous for the Indian Wild Ass Sanctuary (Little Rann). The Great Rann is one of the largest salt deserts in the world.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rann of Kutch = Gujarat; Wild Ass Sanctuary = Little Rann; Rann Utsav = cultural festival; Flamingos breed here.

### Q518 · Computers & Applications

The father of the World Wide Web is:

A. Charles Babbage
B. Tim Berners-Lee
C. Bill Gates
D. Steve Jobs

**Answer:** B. Tim Berners-Lee
**Explanation:** Sir Tim Berners-Lee invented the World Wide Web (WWW) in 1989 at CERN. He also created the first web browser and web server. The first website went live on 6 August 1991.
**Shortcut / rule:** WWW = Tim Berners-Lee (1989, CERN); First website = 1991; Internet ≠ WWW (Internet = network; WWW = application on internet).

### Q519 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

NITI Aayog's full form is:

A. National Institution for Transforming India
B. National Information Technology and Innovation
C. National Industrial and Trade Institute
D. National Infrastructure and Technology Improvement

**Answer:** A. National Institution for Transforming India
**Explanation:** NITI Aayog (National Institution for Transforming India) replaced the Planning Commission on 1 January 2015. Its CEO is appointed by the government; PM is the chairperson.
**Shortcut / rule:** NITI = National Institution for Transforming India; Chairperson = PM; Vice Chairperson = appointed; CEO = appointed.

### Q520 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Stand-Up India scheme is specifically for:

A. All entrepreneurs
B. SC/ST and women entrepreneurs
C. Senior citizens
D. NRIs

**Answer:** B. SC/ST and women entrepreneurs
**Explanation:** Stand-Up India was launched on 5 April 2016 to promote entrepreneurship among SC, ST, and women by providing bank loans between ₹10 lakh and ₹1 crore for greenfield enterprises.
**Shortcut / rule:** Stand-Up India = SC/ST/women (₹10L-₹1Cr, 2016); Startup India = general startups (2016); Mudra = small loans (up to ₹10L).

### Q521 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Indian Civil Service (ICS) was opened to Indians by:

A. Charter Act 1813
B. Charter Act 1833
C. Government of India Act 1858
D. Indian Councils Act 1892

**Answer:** B. Charter Act 1833
**Explanation:** The Charter Act of 1833 threw open the Indian Civil Service to Indians by stating that no Indian would be debarred from holding any office on grounds of religion, race, or colour. However, actual entry exams were held only in London until 1922.
**Shortcut / rule:** Charter Act 1833 = opened ICS to Indians (in theory); First Indian ICS = Satyendranath Tagore (1864).

### Q522 · Indian & World Geography

The Sundarbans delta is formed by which rivers?

A. Ganga and Yamuna
B. Brahmaputra and Meghna
C. Ganga and Brahmaputra
D. Godavari and Krishna

**Answer:** C. Ganga and Brahmaputra
**Explanation:** The Sundarbans is formed by the confluence of the Ganga, Brahmaputra, and Meghna rivers in the Bay of Bengal. It spans India (West Bengal) and Bangladesh.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sundarbans = Ganga + Brahmaputra delta; World's largest mangrove; World's largest delta; UNESCO World Heritage (1987).

### Q523 · Indian Polity & Governance

Under which Article is the Vice President of India elected?

A. Article 63
B. Article 66
C. Article 67
D. Article 69

**Answer:** B. Article 66
**Explanation:** Article 66 provides for the election of the Vice President by an electoral college consisting of members of both Houses of Parliament (Lok Sabha + Rajya Sabha) through proportional representation by single transferable vote.
**Shortcut / rule:** VP elected by: LS + RS members; President elected by: LS + RS + State LA members; VP = ex-officio RS Chairman.

### Q524 · Indian Economy

What does SLR (Statutory Liquidity Ratio) mean?

A. Percentage of deposits banks must keep as liquid assets
B. Interest rate on savings accounts
C. Tax rate on luxury goods
D. Rate of exchange between currencies

**Answer:** A. Percentage of deposits banks must keep as liquid assets
**Explanation:** SLR is the percentage of Net Demand and Time Liabilities (NDTL) that banks must maintain in the form of liquid assets like cash, gold, and government securities.
**Shortcut / rule:** SLR = liquid assets (govt securities); CRR = cash with RBI; Both control credit creation; RBI sets both.

### Q525 · Physics

Which device converts electrical energy into mechanical energy?

A. Generator
B. Motor
C. Battery
D. Transformer

**Answer:** B. Motor
**Explanation:** An electric motor converts electrical energy into mechanical (rotational) energy using the principle of electromagnetic induction. It is the reverse of a generator.
**Shortcut / rule:** Motor = electrical → mechanical; Generator = mechanical → electrical; Both use electromagnetic induction.

### Q526 · Chemistry

Which non-metal is liquid at room temperature?

A. Mercury
B. Bromine
C. Gallium
D. Phosphorus

**Answer:** B. Bromine
**Explanation:** Bromine (Br) is the only non-metal that is liquid at room temperature. It is a reddish-brown liquid with a pungent smell. Mercury is a metal that is liquid at room temperature.
**Shortcut / rule:** Liquid non-metal = Bromine (Br₂); Liquid metal = Mercury (Hg); Both liquid at room temperature (~25°C).

### Q527 · Biology / Life Science

Mendel is known as the "Father of":

A. Modern Physics
B. Genetics
C. Microbiology
D. Evolution

**Answer:** B. Genetics
**Explanation:** Gregor Johann Mendel is called the "Father of Genetics" for his pioneering work on heredity using pea plants (1865-66). He established the Laws of Inheritance: Law of Dominance, Law of Segregation, Law of Independent Assortment.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mendel = Genetics (pea plants); Darwin = Evolution (Natural Selection); Pasteur = Microbiology; Hippocrates = Medicine.

### Q528 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Which fort is known as the "Red Fort of the South"?

A. Golconda Fort
B. Bidar Fort
C. Fort St. George
D. Daulatabad Fort

**Answer:** A. Golconda Fort
**Explanation:** Golconda Fort near Hyderabad (Telangana) was built by the Kakatiya dynasty and later expanded by the Qutb Shahi dynasty. It is known for its acoustics and was once famous for the diamond trade (Kohinoor).
**Shortcut / rule:** Golconda = Hyderabad (Qutb Shahi, diamond trade); Red Fort = Delhi (Shah Jahan); Agra Fort = Agra (Akbar).

### Q529 · Games and Sports

Milkha Singh was known as:

A. Flying Sikh
B. Sprint King
C. Golden Boy
D. Blade Runner

**Answer:** A. Flying Sikh
**Explanation:** Milkha Singh, known as the "Flying Sikh," was a legendary Indian sprinter. He narrowly missed a bronze in the 400m at the 1960 Rome Olympics (finished 4th). He held the 400m national record for 38 years.
**Shortcut / rule:** Flying Sikh = Milkha Singh (400m); Payyoli Express = P.T. Usha; Golden Girl = P.T. Usha; Blade Runner = Oscar Pistorius.

### Q530 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who was India's first Field Marshal?

A. K.M. Cariappa
B. Sam Manekshaw
C. Arjan Singh
D. Kodandera Subayya Thimayya

**Answer:** B. Sam Manekshaw
**Explanation:** Sam Hormusji Framji Jamshedji Manekshaw was India's first Field Marshal (highest rank in the Indian Army). He led India to victory in the 1971 Indo-Pakistani War (Bangladesh Liberation).
**Shortcut / rule:** First Field Marshal = Sam Manekshaw (1973); First CDS = Gen. Bipin Rawat; First Indian C-in-C Army = K.M. Cariappa.

### Q531 · UN & World Organizations

WHO's headquarters is in:

A. New York
B. Vienna
C. Geneva
D. Paris

**Answer:** C. Geneva
**Explanation:** The World Health Organization is headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland. It was established on 7 April 1948 (World Health Day). Current Director-General is Dr. Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus.
**Shortcut / rule:** WHO = Geneva (7 April 1948); World Health Day = 7 April; 194 member states.

### Q532 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which act governs wildlife protection in India?

A. Forest Conservation Act 1980
B. Wildlife Protection Act 1972
C. Environment Protection Act 1986
D. Biological Diversity Act 2002

**Answer:** B. Wildlife Protection Act 1972
**Explanation:** The Wildlife (Protection) Act 1972 provides for the protection of wild animals, birds, and plants. It established National Parks, Wildlife Sanctuaries, and created schedules of protected species.
**Shortcut / rule:** Wildlife Protection Act = 1972; Forest Conservation Act = 1980; EPA = 1986; Biodiversity Act = 2002; National Board for Wildlife chaired by PM.

### Q533 · Computers & Applications

USB stands for:

A. Universal Serial Bus
B. Unified System Base
C. Ultra Speed Buffer
D. Universal Storage Buffer

**Answer:** A. Universal Serial Bus
**Explanation:** USB is an industry standard for connecting peripherals to computers. USB 1.0 was released in 1996. Current versions: USB 3.0 (5 Gbps), USB 3.1 (10 Gbps), USB4 (40 Gbps).
**Shortcut / rule:** USB = Universal Serial Bus; USB-A = standard; USB-C = reversible; Thunderbolt = fastest (40-80 Gbps).

### Q534 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does FIFA stand for?

A. Fédération Internationale de Football Association
B. Federation of Indian Football Associations
C. Football International Federation Alliance
D. Federal International Football Agency

**Answer:** A. Fédération Internationale de Football Association
**Explanation:** FIFA is the international governing body of football (soccer), founded on 21 May 1904 in Paris. Its headquarters is in Zurich, Switzerland. It organizes the FIFA World Cup.
**Shortcut / rule:** FIFA = football (1904, Zurich); ICC = cricket (1909, Dubai); FIH = hockey (1924, Lausanne).

### Q535 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Goods and Services Tax Network (GSTN) is:

A. A government department
B. A not-for-profit IT company providing the technology backbone for GST
C. A bank
D. A court

**Answer:** B. A not-for-profit IT company providing the technology backbone for GST
**Explanation:** GSTN was set up as a Section 8 company to provide the IT infrastructure for GST implementation. It manages the common GST portal (gst.gov.in) for registration, filing returns, and payments.
**Shortcut / rule:** GSTN = IT backbone for GST; GST portal = gst.gov.in; GSTIN = 15-digit GST Identification Number.

### Q536 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the first Indian to become a member of the British Parliament?

A. Pherozeshah Mehta
B. Dadabhai Naoroji
C. W.C. Bonnerjee
D. Surendranath Banerjee

**Answer:** B. Dadabhai Naoroji
**Explanation:** Dadabhai Naoroji was elected to the British House of Commons in 1892 from Central Finsbury constituency (Liberal Party), becoming the first Indian MP in Britain.
**Shortcut / rule:** First Indian in British Parliament = Naoroji (1892); "Grand Old Man of India"; Drain of Wealth theory.

### Q537 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian city is called the "Silicon Valley of India"?

A. Hyderabad
B. Pune
C. Bengaluru
D. Chennai

**Answer:** C. Bengaluru
**Explanation:** Bengaluru (Bangalore) is called the "Silicon Valley of India" due to its concentration of IT companies and tech startups. It hosts offices of major tech companies like Infosys, Wipro, TCS, Google, Microsoft.

### Q538 · Indian Polity & Governance

The 74th Amendment of the Constitution deals with:

A. Panchayati Raj
B. Municipalities
C. Cooperative Societies
D. Anti-Defection Law

**Answer:** B. Municipalities
**Explanation:** The 74th Amendment (1992) gave constitutional status to urban local bodies. It added Part IX-A and the 12th Schedule to the Constitution. Three types: Nagar Panchayat, Municipal Council, Municipal Corporation.
**Shortcut / rule:** 73rd = Panchayati Raj (rural); 74th = Municipalities (urban); Both in 1992; 11th Schedule = Panchayat; 12th Schedule = Municipality.

### Q539 · Indian Economy

Which committee recommended reforms in the banking sector in India (1991)?

A. Narsimham Committee
B. Kelkar Committee
C. Tendulkar Committee
D. Rangarajan Committee

**Answer:** A. Narsimham Committee
**Explanation:** The Narsimham Committee (1991) on Financial System Reforms recommended reducing SLR and CRR, capital adequacy norms, deregulation of interest rates, and setting up of asset reconstruction companies.
**Shortcut / rule:** Narsimham I = 1991 (financial reforms); Narsimham II = 1998 (banking reforms); Basel norms = capital adequacy.

### Q540 · Physics

The phenomenon due to which a hot metal ball shrinks on cooling is called:

A. Expansion
B. Contraction
C. Conduction
D. Convection

**Answer:** B. Contraction
**Explanation:** When metals cool, they contract (decrease in size) due to reduction in the kinetic energy of atoms, causing them to move closer together. This is thermal contraction, the opposite of thermal expansion.
**Shortcut / rule:** Heat → Expansion; Cool → Contraction; Exception: Water expands when frozen (anomalous expansion, 0-4°C).

### Q541 · Chemistry

The process of a solid directly converting to gas without becoming liquid is called:

A. Evaporation
B. Condensation
C. Sublimation
D. Deposition

**Answer:** C. Sublimation
**Explanation:** Sublimation is the transition from solid directly to gas phase. Examples: dry ice (CO₂), naphthalene balls, camphor. The reverse process (gas → solid) is called deposition.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sublimation = solid → gas; Deposition = gas → solid; Evaporation = liquid → gas; Condensation = gas → liquid.

### Q542 · Biology / Life Science

What is the gestation period of humans?

A. 7 months
B. 9 months (approximately 280 days)
C. 11 months
D. 12 months

**Answer:** B. 9 months (approximately 280 days)
**Explanation:** The human gestation period is approximately 280 days (40 weeks or 9 months). The developing baby is called an embryo for the first 8 weeks and a fetus thereafter.
**Shortcut / rule:** Human gestation = 280 days; Elephant = 22 months (longest); Mouse = 20 days; Dog = 63 days.

### Q543 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Jallianwala Bagh' memorial is located in which city?

A. Lahore
B. Delhi
C. Amritsar
D. Ludhiana

**Explanation:** The Jallianwala Bagh memorial in Amritsar, Punjab marks the site of the 1919 massacre where General Dyer ordered troops to fire on a peaceful gathering, killing hundreds.

### Q544 · Games and Sports

Which country has won the most Olympic gold medals overall (summer + winter)?

A. Russia/USSR
B. United States

D. Great Britain

**Explanation:** The United States has won the most Olympic gold medals in history (over 1,000 combined summer and winter games), followed by the Soviet Union/Russia and Germany.

### Q545 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Subrahmanyan Chandrasekhar won the Nobel Prize in which field?

A. Chemistry
B. Physics
C. Medicine
D. Literature

**Answer:** B. Physics
**Explanation:** Subrahmanyan Chandrasekhar won the Nobel Prize in Physics in 1983 for his theoretical studies of the physical processes of importance to the structure and evolution of stars (Chandrasekhar limit).

### Q546 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Nilgiri Tahr' is found exclusively in:

A. Eastern Himalayas
B. Western Ghats
C. Northeast India
D. Central India

**Explanation:** The Nilgiri Tahr (Nilgiritragus hylocrius) is an endangered mountain goat endemic to the Western Ghats (Nilgiri Hills, Anamalai Hills, Palani Hills) of southern India. Eravikulam NP (Kerala) is a key habitat.

### Q547 · Computers & Applications

What is an operating system?

A. A type of hardware
B. System software that manages hardware and software resources
C. An internet browser
D. A programming language

**Answer:** B. System software that manages hardware and software resources
**Explanation:** An OS manages hardware resources, provides common services for applications, and acts as an intermediary between users and hardware. Examples: Windows, macOS, Linux, Android, iOS.
**Shortcut / rule:** OS types: Windows, macOS, Linux (open source), Android (mobile); Functions: process, memory, file, device management.

### Q548 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The National Education Policy (NEP) 2020 replaced:

A. National Policy on Education 1968
B. National Policy on Education 1986
C. Education Commission Report 1964
D. Kothari Commission recommendations

**Answer:** B. National Policy on Education 1986
**Explanation:** NEP 2020 replaced the 34-year-old NPE 1986. Key features: 5+3+3+4 structure (replacing 10+2), multi-disciplinary education, flexibility, mother tongue instruction till Grade 5, and renaming MHRD to Ministry of Education.
**Shortcut / rule:** NEP 2020: 5+3+3+4 structure; Board exams in Grades 10 & 12 retained; Coding from Grade 6; Academic Bank of Credits.

### Q549 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the first Governor-General of Bengal?

A. Robert Clive
B. Warren Hastings
C. Lord Cornwallis
D. Lord Wellesley

**Answer:** B. Warren Hastings
**Explanation:** Warren Hastings was the first Governor-General of Bengal (1773-1785), appointed under the Regulating Act 1773. He was impeached but acquitted. He founded the Asiatic Society (1784).
**Shortcut / rule:** First GG of Bengal = Warren Hastings (1773); First GG of India = Lord William Bentinck (1833); Last GG (British) = Lord Mountbatten; Last GG (Indian) = C. Rajagopalachari.

### Q550 · Indian & World Geography

Which is the highest peak in the world?

A. K2
B. Kanchenjunga
C. Mount Everest
D. Makalu

**Answer:** C. Mount Everest
**Explanation:** Mount Everest (8,848.86 m) in the Himalayas (Nepal-China border) is the highest peak in the world. It was first summited by Tenzing Norgay and Edmund Hillary on 29 May 1953.
**Shortcut / rule:** Everest = 8849m (highest); K2 = 8611m (2nd); Kanchenjunga = 8586m (3rd); First ascent = 29 May 1953 (Tenzing + Hillary).

### Q551 · Indian Polity & Governance

Zero Hour in Parliament is:

A. Time for question hour
B. Time after question hour for raising urgent matters without prior notice
C. Closing time
D. Budget presentation time

**Answer:** B. Time after question hour for raising urgent matters without prior notice
**Explanation:** Zero Hour starts immediately after Question Hour (12 noon in Lok Sabha, hence "zero"). It is an Indian parliamentary innovation where members can raise matters of urgent public importance without prior notice.
**Shortcut / rule:** Zero Hour = after Question Hour (Indian innovation, not in rules originally); Question Hour = first hour (11 AM-12 PM in LS).

### Q552 · Indian Economy

NABARD was established based on the recommendations of which committee?

A. Narsimham Committee
B. B. Sivaraman Committee (CRAFICARD)
C. Kelkar Committee
D. Tendulkar Committee

**Answer:** B. B. Sivaraman Committee (CRAFICARD)
**Explanation:** NABARD was established on 12 July 1982 based on recommendations of the Committee to Review Arrangements for Institutional Credit for Agriculture and Rural Development (CRAFICARD), chaired by B. Sivaraman.
**Shortcut / rule:** NABARD = 1982 (Sivaraman/CRAFICARD); HQ = Mumbai; Apex institution for rural credit; Regulates RRBs and cooperative banks.

### Q553 · Physics

What is the principle behind the working of a washing machine?

A. Centripetal force
B. Centrifugal force
C. Gravitational force
D. Electromagnetic force

**Answer:** B. Centrifugal force
**Explanation:** Washing machines use centrifugal force during the spin cycle to separate water from clothes. The drum spins rapidly, pushing water outward through the drum holes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Centrifugal = outward (washing machine, cream separator); Centripetal = inward (keeps object in circular path).

### Q554 · Chemistry

The catalyst used in the Haber process for ammonia production is:

A. Platinum
B. Iron
C. Vanadium pentoxide
D. Nickel

**Answer:** B. Iron
**Explanation:** Finely divided iron is used as a catalyst in the Haber process (N₂ + 3H₂ → 2NH₃) at 400-500°C and 200 atm pressure. Molybdenum is used as a promoter.
**Shortcut / rule:** Haber (NH₃) = Iron; Contact (H₂SO₄) = V₂O₅; Ostwald (HNO₃) = Platinum; Hydrogenation = Nickel.

### Q555 · Biology / Life Science

Which part of the eye is responsible for color vision?

A. Rods
B. Cones
C. Cornea
D. Iris

**Answer:** B. Cones
**Explanation:** Cone cells in the retina are responsible for color vision and work best in bright light. Rod cells are responsible for dim-light (night) vision and cannot distinguish colors.
**Shortcut / rule:** Cones = color vision (bright light); Rods = dim-light vision (B&W); Retina = screen of eye; Lens focuses light.

### Q556 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Madhubani' painting style originates from:

A. Rajasthan
B. Odisha
C. Bihar
D. Gujarat

**Answer:** C. Bihar
**Explanation:** Madhubani painting (Mithila painting) is a traditional art form from the Mithila region of Bihar. It uses natural dyes and pigments with geometric patterns and mythological themes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Madhubani = Bihar (Mithila); Pattachitra = Odisha; Warli = Maharashtra; Phad = Rajasthan; Miniature = Rajasthan/Mughal.

### Q557 · Games and Sports

The 'Santosh Trophy' is associated with which sport?

A. Cricket
B. Hockey
C. Football
D. Badminton

**Answer:** C. Football
**Explanation:** The Santosh Trophy is India's national football championship for state teams. It was first held in 1941 and is named after the late Maharaja of Santosh (now in Bangladesh).
**Shortcut / rule:** Santosh Trophy = Football (national, states); Durand Cup = Football (oldest, clubs); Subroto Cup = Football (school).

### Q558 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who is known as the "Birdman of India"?

A. Salim Ali
B. M.S. Swaminathan
C. E.K. Janaki Ammal
D. Sunderlal Bahuguna

**Answer:** A. Salim Ali
**Explanation:** Dr. Salim Ali (1896-1987) is called the "Birdman of India" for his pioneering work in ornithology. He conducted systematic bird surveys across India and wrote "The Book of Indian Birds."
**Shortcut / rule:** Birdman of India = Salim Ali; Father of Green Revolution = M.S. Swaminathan; Father of White Revolution = Verghese Kurien.

### Q559 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

DRDO's missile 'Agni-V' has a range of approximately:

A. 1,000 km
B. 3,000 km
C. 5,000-5,500 km
D. 10,000 km

**Answer:** C. 5,000-5,500 km
**Explanation:** Agni-V is an intercontinental ballistic missile (ICBM) with a range of over 5,000 km. It is a three-stage solid-fueled missile, making India one of the few countries with ICBM capability.
**Shortcut / rule:** Agni-I = 700 km; Agni-II = 2000 km; Agni-III = 3000 km; Agni-IV = 4000 km; Agni-V = 5000+ km (ICBM class).

### Q560 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Kyoto Protocol' established the concept of:

A. Carbon dating
B. Carbon trading/emission credits
C. Carbon capture
D. Carbon footprint measurement

**Answer:** B. Carbon trading/emission credits
**Explanation:** The Kyoto Protocol (1997) established emission trading (carbon market) where countries that reduce emissions below their targets can sell excess credits to countries that exceed theirs.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kyoto = emission trading + CDM; Paris Agreement (2015) = successor; COP = Conference of Parties (annual).

### Q561 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Swadeshi movement gave rise to which educational institutions?

A. Central Universities
B. National Education Institutions (like Bengal National College)
C. IITs
D. Medical Colleges

**Answer:** B. National Education Institutions (like Bengal National College)
**Explanation:** The Swadeshi Movement (1905) promoted national education. Bengal National College was founded with Aurobindo Ghosh as principal. The movement emphasized boycott of British goods and educational institutions.
**Shortcut / rule:** Swadeshi education: Bengal National College (Aurobindo); National Council of Education (1906); Tagore's Shantiniketan.

### Q562 · Indian & World Geography

The Western Ghats are also known as:

A. Nilgiris
B. Sahyadri
C. Vindhyas
D. Aravallis

**Answer:** B. Sahyadri
**Explanation:** The Western Ghats are also called Sahyadri Mountains. They run parallel to the western coast of India from Gujarat to Tamil Nadu (1,600 km). They are a UNESCO World Heritage Site and biodiversity hotspot.
**Shortcut / rule:** Western Ghats = Sahyadri; Eastern Ghats = discontinuous; Highest peak WG = Anamudi (2695m, Kerala).

### Q563 · Indian Polity & Governance

The first Chief Election Commissioner of India was:

A. T.N. Seshan
B. Sukumar Sen
C. K.V.K. Sundaram
D. S.P. Sen Verma

**Answer:** B. Sukumar Sen
**Explanation:** Sukumar Sen was the first Chief Election Commissioner of India (1950-1958). He successfully conducted India's first general elections in 1951-52 — the largest democratic exercise ever at that time.
**Shortcut / rule:** First CEC = Sukumar Sen (1950); T.N. Seshan = reformed electoral system (strict enforcement); Art 324 = Election Commission.

### Q564 · Indian Economy

Which plan is known as the 'People's Plan' or 'Bombay Plan'?

A. First Five Year Plan
B. Second Five Year Plan
C. The industrial plan proposed by Indian industrialists in 1944
D. NITI Aayog's Vision 2030

**Answer:** C. The industrial plan proposed by Indian industrialists in 1944
**Explanation:** The Bombay Plan (1944) was an economic plan proposed by 8 Indian industrialists including J.R.D. Tata and G.D. Birla. It advocated government intervention and planned industrialization for India's post-independence economy.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bombay Plan = 1944 (Tata, Birla); People's Plan = M.N. Roy; Gandhian Plan = Shriman Narayan Agarwal; All pre-independence proposals.

### Q565 · Physics

The law of conservation of energy states that:

A. Energy can be created
B. Energy can be destroyed
C. Energy can neither be created nor destroyed, only transformed
D. Energy always increases

**Answer:** C. Energy can neither be created nor destroyed, only transformed
**Explanation:** The Law of Conservation of Energy (First Law of Thermodynamics) states that energy can be converted from one form to another, but the total energy of an isolated system remains constant.
**Shortcut / rule:** Energy conserved (1st Law of Thermodynamics); Mass-energy equivalence: E = mc²; KE + PE = Total mechanical energy (constant in absence of friction).

### Q566 · Chemistry

Aqua Regia is a mixture of:

A. HCl and H₂SO₄
B. HNO₃ and H₂SO₄
C. HCl and HNO₃ in 3:1 ratio
D. HF and HNO₃

**Answer:** C. HCl and HNO₃ in 3:1 ratio
**Explanation:** Aqua Regia ("Royal Water") is a mixture of concentrated HCl and HNO₃ in 3:1 ratio. It can dissolve noble metals like gold and platinum, which individual acids cannot.
**Shortcut / rule:** Aqua Regia = 3 HCl + 1 HNO₃; Dissolves gold & platinum; Name = "Royal Water" (Latin).

### Q567 · Biology / Life Science

The basic unit of classification in taxonomy is:

A. Genus
B. Species
C. Family
D. Order

**Answer:** B. Species
**Explanation:** Species is the basic unit of biological classification. It is defined as a group of organisms that can interbreed and produce fertile offspring. The taxonomic hierarchy: Kingdom > Phylum > Class > Order > Family > Genus > Species.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mnemonic: "King Philip Came Over For Good Spaghetti" (K > P > C > O > F > G > S); Binomial nomenclature by Linnaeus.

### Q568 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The Victoria Memorial is located in:

A. Mumbai
B. Delhi
C. Kolkata
D. Chennai

**Answer:** C. Kolkata
**Explanation:** The Victoria Memorial in Kolkata was built between 1906-1921 in memory of Queen Victoria. It is now a museum and tourist destination, designed in Indo-Saracenic style using white Makrana marble.
**Shortcut / rule:** Victoria Memorial = Kolkata; Gateway of India = Mumbai; India Gate = Delhi; Charminar = Hyderabad; Hawa Mahal = Jaipur.

### Q569 · Games and Sports

The 'Irani Trophy' in cricket is played between:

A. India and Iran
B. Two top domestic teams
C. Ranji Trophy winners and Rest of India
D. India and Pakistan

**Answer:** C. Ranji Trophy winners and Rest of India
**Explanation:** The Irani Trophy is an annual cricket match between the reigning Ranji Trophy champions and the Rest of India. Named after Z.R. Irani (a former BCCI official), it was first held in 1959-60.
**Shortcut / rule:** Irani Trophy = Ranji winners vs Rest; Deodhar Trophy = domestic ODI; Duleep Trophy = zonal; NKP Salve Challenger = limited overs.

### Q570 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Verghese Kurien is known as the "Father of":

A. Green Revolution
B. White Revolution
C. Blue Revolution
D. Golden Revolution

**Answer:** B. White Revolution
**Explanation:** Dr. Verghese Kurien is the "Father of the White Revolution" (Operation Flood) in India. He founded Amul (GCMMF) and transformed India from a milk-deficient nation into the world's largest milk producer.
**Shortcut / rule:** White Revolution = Kurien (milk); Green Revolution = Swaminathan (food grains); Blue Revolution = fish; Golden Revolution = honey/horticulture.

### Q571 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

The IRNSS (Indian Regional Navigation Satellite System) has how many satellites?

A. 5
B. 7
C. 9
D. 12

**Answer:** B. 7
**Explanation:** IRNSS (now NavIC) has 7 satellites: 3 in geostationary orbit and 4 in geosynchronous orbit. It provides coverage over India and 1,500 km around it.
**Shortcut / rule:** NavIC/IRNSS = 7 satellites; GPS = 31; Coverage = India + 1,500 km; Accuracy = 10m (public), better for military.

### Q572 · UN & World Organizations

The World Bank Group consists of how many institutions?

A. 2
B. 3
C. 5
D. 7

**Answer:** C. 5
**Explanation:** The World Bank Group has 5 institutions: IBRD, IDA, IFC, MIGA, and ICSID. IBRD and IDA together are commonly called "The World Bank."
**Shortcut / rule:** WBG = IBRD + IDA (World Bank proper) + IFC + MIGA + ICSID; HQ = Washington DC; Founded 1944.

### Q573 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which is the national tree of India?

A. Neem
B. Peepal
C. Banyan (Ficus benghalensis)
D. Teak

**Answer:** C. Banyan (Ficus benghalensis)
**Explanation:** The Indian Banyan (Ficus benghalensis) is the national tree of India. It symbolizes longevity and is known for its aerial roots. The Great Banyan Tree in Howrah, Kolkata is one of the largest.
**Shortcut / rule:** National tree = Banyan; National flower = Lotus; National bird = Peacock; National animal = Bengal Tiger; National fruit = Mango.

### Q574 · Computers & Applications

What is Artificial Intelligence (AI)?

A. A type of virus
B. Simulation of human intelligence by machines/computers
C. A programming language
D. A type of hardware

**Answer:** B. Simulation of human intelligence by machines/computers
**Explanation:** AI is the simulation of human intelligence processes by computer systems, including learning, reasoning, and self-correction. It includes machine learning, deep learning, and natural language processing.
**Shortcut / rule:** AI coined by John McCarthy (1956); Types: Narrow AI (specific), General AI (human-like), Super AI (beyond human); Machine Learning ⊂ AI.

### Q575 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

IRCTC stands for:

A. Indian Railway Catering and Tourism Corporation
B. Indian Railway Central Transport Corporation
C. Indian Regional Communication and Transport Centre
D. International Railway and Commercial Travel Corporation

**Answer:** A. Indian Railway Catering and Tourism Corporation
**Explanation:** IRCTC was incorporated on 27 September 1999. It handles catering, tourism, and online ticketing for Indian Railways. Its website (irctc.co.in) is one of the most visited in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** IRCTC = catering + tourism + tickets (1999); IRFC = financing; RITES = consulting; CRIS = IT; All under Indian Railways.

### Q576 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The 'August Declaration' (1917) stated that the British aim was:

A. Full independence for India
B. Gradual development of self-governing institutions
C. Partition of India
D. Complete British rule forever

**Answer:** B. Gradual development of self-governing institutions
**Explanation:** The August Declaration (20 August 1917) by Secretary of State Edwin Montagu announced the British policy of "increasing association of Indians in every branch of administration and the gradual development of self-governing institutions."
**Shortcut / rule:** August Declaration 1917 = self-governing institutions (Montagu); Led to Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms (1919).

### Q577 · Indian & World Geography

The Aravalli Range is one of the oldest mountain ranges in the world. Its highest peak is:

A. Nanga Parbat
B. Guru Shikhar (Mount Abu)
C. Nanda Devi
D. Dhaulagiri

**Answer:** B. Guru Shikhar (Mount Abu)
**Explanation:** Guru Shikhar (1,722 m) at Mount Abu in Rajasthan is the highest peak of the Aravalli Range. The Aravallis extend from Gujarat to Delhi (692 km) and are one of the oldest fold mountains.
**Shortcut / rule:** Aravalli = oldest Indian range; Guru Shikhar = highest (1722m); Delhi Ridge = northern extension; Rich in zinc, lead, marble.

### Q578 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Governor of a state is appointed by:

A. Chief Minister
B. Prime Minister
C. President
D. Chief Justice of India

**Answer:** C. President
**Explanation:** Under Article 155, the Governor of each state is appointed by the President (on advice of the Union government). The Governor serves at the "pleasure of the President" and has a 5-year term.
**Shortcut / rule:** Governor = appointed by President (Art 155); No fixed tenure (pleasure doctrine); Can be transferred; Acts on CM's advice normally.

### Q579 · Indian Economy

What is the full form of SEBI?

A. Securities and Exchange Board of India
B. Stock Exchange Bureau of India
C. Securities and Equity Bureau of India
D. Share Exchange Board of India

**Answer:** A. Securities and Exchange Board of India
**Explanation:** SEBI was established on 12 April 1988 and given statutory powers by the SEBI Act 1992. It regulates the securities market, protects investor interests, and promotes development of the market.
**Shortcut / rule:** SEBI = securities market regulator; Established 1988; Statutory 1992; HQ = Mumbai; Chairman appointed by Union Govt.

### Q580 · Physics

The device used to measure potential difference (voltage) is:

A. Ammeter
B. Voltmeter
C. Galvanometer
D. Wattmeter

**Answer:** B. Voltmeter
**Explanation:** A voltmeter measures the potential difference (voltage) between two points in a circuit. It is connected in parallel with the component. It has high resistance.
**Shortcut / rule:** Voltmeter = voltage (parallel, high resistance); Ammeter = current (series, low resistance); Galvanometer = small currents.

### Q581 · Chemistry

The process of extracting metals from their ores is called:

A. Mining
B. Metallurgy
C. Alloying
D. Smelting

**Answer:** B. Metallurgy
**Explanation:** Metallurgy is the science and technology of extracting metals from ores and refining them for use. It includes concentration, roasting/calcination, reduction, and refining.
**Shortcut / rule:** Metallurgy steps: Ore → Concentration → Roasting/Calcination → Reduction → Refining; Smelting = specific reduction step.

### Q582 · Biology / Life Science

Which organ is affected by Jaundice?

A. Heart
B. Kidney
C. Liver
D. Lungs

**Answer:** C. Liver
**Explanation:** Jaundice is caused by excess bilirubin (a waste product from breakdown of RBCs) in the blood, usually due to liver dysfunction. It causes yellowing of skin and eyes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Jaundice = liver (bilirubin); Hepatitis = liver infection; Cirrhosis = liver scarring; Yellow color = bilirubin buildup.

### Q583 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

Which Sikh Guru compiled the Adi Granth?

A. Guru Nanak
B. Guru Angad
C. Guru Arjan Dev
D. Guru Gobind Singh

**Answer:** C. Guru Arjan Dev
**Explanation:** Guru Arjan Dev (5th Sikh Guru) compiled the Adi Granth (original Sikh scripture) in 1604. Guru Gobind Singh (10th Guru) later expanded it into the Guru Granth Sahib.
**Shortcut / rule:** Adi Granth = Guru Arjan Dev (1604); Guru Granth Sahib = Guru Gobind Singh; Khalsa = Guru Gobind Singh (1699).

### Q584 · Games and Sports

The term 'Hat-trick' in cricket means:

A. Scoring three centuries
B. Taking three wickets in three consecutive balls
C. Hitting three sixes
D. Winning three matches in a row

**Answer:** B. Taking three wickets in three consecutive balls
**Explanation:** A hat-trick in cricket is when a bowler takes three wickets on three successive deliveries. The term originated from cricket in the 19th century when a bowler achieving this feat was awarded a new hat.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hat-trick = 3 wickets in 3 balls; Origin = cricket (new hat award); Also used in football (3 goals) and hockey.

### Q585 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who is known as the "Father of the Green Revolution" in India?

A. Norman Borlaug
B. M.S. Swaminathan
C. Verghese Kurien
D. C. Subramaniam

**Answer:** B. M.S. Swaminathan
**Explanation:** Dr. M.S. Swaminathan is called the "Father of the Green Revolution in India." He introduced high-yielding varieties of wheat in the 1960s. Norman Borlaug (USA) is the Father of the Green Revolution globally.
**Shortcut / rule:** Swaminathan = Green Revolution (India); Norman Borlaug = Green Revolution (world, Nobel Peace 1970).

### Q586 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Dodo' bird, now extinct, was native to:

A. Australia
B. New Zealand
C. Mauritius
D. Madagascar

**Answer:** C. Mauritius
**Explanation:** The Dodo was a flightless bird native to the island of Mauritius in the Indian Ocean. It went extinct in the late 17th century (by 1681) due to hunting and introduced predators.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dodo = Mauritius (extinct ~1681); "Dead as a dodo" = completely extinct; Caused by Dutch sailors + introduced animals.

### Q587 · Computers & Applications

What is the difference between RAM and ROM?

A. No difference
B. RAM is volatile (data lost on power off); ROM is non-volatile (data retained)
C. ROM is faster than RAM
D. RAM is permanent; ROM is temporary

**Answer:** B. RAM is volatile (data lost on power off); ROM is non-volatile (data retained)
**Explanation:** RAM is volatile temporary memory used for active processes. ROM is non-volatile permanent memory that retains data even without power (stores BIOS/firmware).
**Shortcut / rule:** RAM = volatile, read-write, temporary; ROM = non-volatile, read-only, permanent; RAM is faster; ROM stores boot instructions.

### Q588 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The term 'Dyarchy' was introduced by which Act?

A. Indian Councils Act 1909
B. Government of India Act 1919
C. Government of India Act 1935
D. Indian Independence Act 1947

**Answer:** B. Government of India Act 1919
**Explanation:** Dyarchy (dual government in provinces) was introduced by the Government of India Act 1919 (Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms). Provincial subjects were divided into 'Reserved' (Governor) and 'Transferred' (Indian ministers).
**Shortcut / rule:** 1919 Act = Dyarchy in provinces; 1935 Act = abolished dyarchy in provinces, introduced at centre (not implemented).

### Q589 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian state is the largest producer of tea?

A. West Bengal
B. Assam
C. Kerala
D. Tamil Nadu

**Answer:** B. Assam
**Explanation:** Assam is India's largest tea-producing state, accounting for over 50% of India's tea production. India is the second largest tea producer in the world after China.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tea: Assam (largest) > West Bengal > Tamil Nadu; India = 2nd largest producer (after China); Darjeeling = "Champagne of Teas."

### Q590 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Inter-State Water Disputes Act was enacted in which year?

A. 1950
B. 1956
C. 1962
D. 1972

**Answer:** B. 1956
**Explanation:** The Inter-State River Water Disputes Act was enacted in 1956 (under Article 262). It provides for the adjudication of disputes relating to waters of inter-state rivers by tribunals.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 262 = water disputes; Tribunals: Krishna, Godavari, Narmada, Cauvery, Ravi-Beas; Supreme Court excluded from jurisdiction.

### Q591 · Indian Economy

The term 'Repo Rate' means:
