A. The rate at which banks lend to customers
B. The rate at which RBI lends money to commercial banks against government securities
C. The fixed deposit rate
D. The exchange rate

**Answer:** B. The rate at which RBI lends money to commercial banks against government securities
**Explanation:** Repo (Repurchase Option) Rate is the rate at which banks borrow from RBI for short-term needs by selling government securities with an agreement to repurchase them. It is the main tool for controlling inflation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Repo = RBI lends to banks; Reverse Repo = banks deposit with RBI; Higher Repo = tighter money supply = lower inflation.

### Q592 · Physics

What type of wave is a sound wave?

A. Electromagnetic wave
B. Longitudinal wave
C. Transverse wave
D. Surface wave

**Answer:** B. Longitudinal wave
**Explanation:** Sound waves are longitudinal (mechanical) waves where particles vibrate parallel to the direction of wave propagation, creating compressions and rarefactions. They require a medium to travel.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sound = longitudinal + mechanical; Light = transverse + electromagnetic; Water surface = both longitudinal + transverse.

### Q593 · Chemistry

Soap is a salt of:

A. Strong acid and weak base
B. Weak acid (fatty acid) and strong base
C. Strong acid and strong base
D. Weak acid and weak base

**Answer:** B. Weak acid (fatty acid) and strong base
**Explanation:** Soap is made by saponification — the reaction of fats/oils (fatty acids, weak organic acids) with a strong base like NaOH or KOH. Example: Sodium stearate (C₁₇H₃₅COONa).
**Shortcut / rule:** Soap = fatty acid + NaOH/KOH; Saponification = soap making; Soap doesn't work in hard water (forms scum); Detergents work in hard water.

### Q594 · Biology / Life Science

The process by which green plants make their food is called:

A. Respiration
B. Transpiration
C. Photosynthesis
D. Osmosis

**Answer:** C. Photosynthesis
**Explanation:** Photosynthesis is the process by which green plants convert CO₂ and H₂O into glucose and oxygen using sunlight energy and chlorophyll. 6CO₂ + 6H₂O → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂.
**Shortcut / rule:** Photosynthesis = food making (chloroplast); Respiration = energy release (mitochondria); Transpiration = water loss from leaves.

### Q595 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Sangam Literature' belongs to which language?

A. Sanskrit
B. Tamil
C. Kannada
D. Telugu

**Answer:** B. Tamil
**Explanation:** Sangam Literature is a collection of ancient Tamil literary works produced during the Sangam period (300 BC – 300 AD). The three Sangams were held at Madurai and other Tamil cities. Major works include Tolkappiyam and Ettuthogai.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sangam = Tamil (oldest secular literature); Tolkappiyam = oldest Tamil grammar; Tirukkural = by Thiruvalluvar.

### Q596 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who was the architect of Chandigarh city?

A. Edwin Lutyens
B. Le Corbusier
C. Charles Correa
D. Laurie Baker

**Answer:** B. Le Corbusier
**Explanation:** Le Corbusier (French-Swiss architect) designed Chandigarh, India's first planned city, in the 1950s. The Capitol Complex in Chandigarh is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chandigarh = Le Corbusier; New Delhi (Lutyens' Delhi) = Edwin Lutyens + Herbert Baker; IIM Ahmedabad = Louis Kahn.

### Q597 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which day is celebrated as 'Earth Day'?

A. 5 June
B. 22 March
C. 22 April
D. 16 September

**Answer:** C. 22 April
**Explanation:** Earth Day is celebrated annually on 22 April since 1970. It was founded by US Senator Gaylord Nelson to promote environmental awareness and protection.
**Shortcut / rule:** 22 April = Earth Day; 5 June = Environment Day; 22 March = World Water Day; 21 March = World Forest Day; 16 Sept = Ozone Day.

### Q598 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who presided over the Lahore Session of INC (1929)?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Jawaharlal Nehru
C. Subhas Chandra Bose
D. Maulana Azad

**Answer:** B. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Explanation:** Jawaharlal Nehru presided over the Lahore Session (December 1929) where the resolution for 'Purna Swaraj' (Complete Independence) was passed. 26 January 1930 was first observed as Independence Day.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lahore 1929 = Nehru (Purna Swaraj); Tripuri 1939 = Bose (defeated Pattabhi Sitaramayya); Karachi 1931 = Sardar Patel.

### Q599 · Indian & World Geography

Which line divides India and China?

A. Durand Line
B. McMahon Line
C. Radcliffe Line
D. 17th Parallel

**Answer:** B. McMahon Line
**Explanation:** The McMahon Line is the border between India and China (Tibet) in the northeast, drawn at the Simla Conference (1914). China does not recognize this line.
**Shortcut / rule:** McMahon = India-China; Radcliffe = India-Pakistan; Durand = Pakistan-Afghanistan; LOC = India-Pakistan (Kashmir).

### Q600 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of 'Fundamental Rights' was borrowed from:

A. UK
B. USA
C. Ireland
D. Canada

**Answer:** B. USA
**Explanation:** The concept of Fundamental Rights (Part III, Articles 12-35) was borrowed from the Bill of Rights of the US Constitution. They are justiciable (enforceable by courts).
**Shortcut / rule:** FR = USA; DPSP = Ireland; Emergency = Germany; Concurrent List = Australia; Parliamentary system = UK; Amendment procedure = South Africa.

### Q601 · Indian Economy

The largest public sector bank in India is:

A. Punjab National Bank
B. Bank of Baroda
C. State Bank of India
D. Canara Bank

**Answer:** C. State Bank of India
**Explanation:** SBI is India's largest public sector bank by assets, deposits, branches, customers, and employees. It was formed in 1955 from the Imperial Bank of India.
**Shortcut / rule:** SBI = largest PSB; Merged 5 associate banks + Bharatiya Mahila Bank in 2017; HQ = Mumbai.

### Q602 · Physics

A body at rest will remain at rest unless acted upon by an external force. This is:

A. Newton's Second Law
B. Newton's First Law (Law of Inertia)
C. Newton's Third Law
D. Law of Gravitation

**Answer:** B. Newton's First Law (Law of Inertia)
**Explanation:** Newton's First Law states that a body continues in its state of rest or uniform motion in a straight line unless compelled by an external force to change that state. This property is called inertia.
**Shortcut / rule:** Inertia depends on mass; More mass = more inertia; Seatbelts prevent injury due to inertia during sudden stops.

### Q603 · Chemistry

The chemical formula of common table sugar (sucrose) is:

A. C₆H₁₂O₆
B. C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁
C. C₂H₅OH
D. CH₃COOH

**Answer:** B. C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁
**Explanation:** Sucrose (table sugar) has the formula C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁. It is a disaccharide made of glucose and fructose. Glucose (C₆H₁₂O₆) is a monosaccharide.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sucrose = C₁₂H₂₂O₁₁ (glucose + fructose); Glucose = C₆H₁₂O₆; Lactose = glucose + galactose; Maltose = glucose + glucose.

### Q604 · Biology / Life Science

Which vitamin deficiency causes Scurvy?

A. Vitamin A
B. Vitamin B12
C. Vitamin C
D. Vitamin D

**Answer:** C. Vitamin C
**Explanation:** Scurvy is caused by severe Vitamin C (Ascorbic acid) deficiency. Symptoms include bleeding gums, weakness, and slow wound healing. It was common among sailors who lacked fresh fruits.
**Shortcut / rule:** Scurvy = Vit C; Night blindness = Vit A; Beri-beri = Vit B1; Rickets = Vit D; Anaemia = Vit B12/Iron.

### Q605 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Gateway of India' is located in:

A. Delhi
B. Mumbai
C. Kolkata
D. Chennai

**Answer:** B. Mumbai
**Explanation:** The Gateway of India is an arch-monument on the waterfront in Mumbai. Built in Indo-Saracenic style, it was erected to commemorate the landing of King George V and Queen Mary at Apollo Bunder in 1911.
**Shortcut / rule:** Gateway of India = Mumbai (1924); India Gate = Delhi (1931, WWI memorial); Victoria Memorial = Kolkata.

### Q606 · Games and Sports

Which country hosted the first Cricket World Cup?

A. India
B. England
C. Australia
D. West Indies

**Answer:** B. England
**Explanation:** The first Cricket World Cup was held in England in 1975. West Indies won the inaugural tournament, defeating Australia in the final at Lord's.
**Shortcut / rule:** First Cricket WC = England (1975); Won by West Indies; India's first WC win = 1983 (Kapil Dev).

### Q607 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's nuclear doctrine follows which policy?

A. First-use policy
B. No First Use (NFU) policy
C. Pre-emptive strike policy
D. Nuclear disarmament

**Answer:** B. No First Use (NFU) policy
**Explanation:** India's nuclear doctrine, formalized in 2003, follows a "No First Use" policy — India will not be the first to use nuclear weapons but maintains credible minimum deterrence for massive retaliation if attacked.
**Shortcut / rule:** India's nuclear doctrine: No First Use + Credible Minimum Deterrence + Massive Retaliation; Nuclear triad: land, air, sea.

### Q608 · UN & World Organizations

The International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA) is headquartered in:

A. Geneva
B. New York
C. Vienna
D. The Hague

**Answer:** C. Vienna
**Explanation:** The IAEA was established in 1957 and is headquartered in Vienna, Austria. It promotes peaceful use of nuclear energy and monitors nuclear proliferation through safeguards.
**Shortcut / rule:** IAEA = Vienna (1957); "Atoms for Peace"; Nobel Peace Prize 2005; Nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty (NPT) = 1968.

### Q609 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which act governs environmental protection in India?

A. Forest Conservation Act 1980
B. Wildlife Protection Act 1972
C. Environment Protection Act 1986
D. Water Prevention and Control of Pollution Act 1974

**Answer:** C. Environment Protection Act 1986
**Explanation:** The EPA 1986 was enacted after the Bhopal Gas Tragedy (1984). It is an umbrella legislation giving the central government power to protect and improve environmental quality.
**Shortcut / rule:** EPA = 1986 (post-Bhopal, umbrella law); Wildlife = 1972; Forest = 1980; Water Pollution = 1974; Air Pollution = 1981.

### Q610 · Computers & Applications

What is a firewall in computing?

A. A physical wall around servers
B. A network security system that monitors and controls incoming/outgoing traffic
C. A type of virus
D. A cooling system

**Answer:** B. A network security system that monitors and controls incoming/outgoing traffic
**Explanation:** A firewall is a network security system that monitors and controls traffic based on predetermined security rules. It acts as a barrier between a trusted internal network and untrusted external networks.
**Shortcut / rule:** Firewall = security barrier; Types: hardware firewall, software firewall; Antivirus ≠ Firewall (but complementary).

### Q611 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Arya Samaj was founded by:

A. Raja Ram Mohan Roy
B. Dayanand Saraswati
C. Swami Vivekananda
D. Ramakrishna Paramahamsa

**Answer:** B. Dayanand Saraswati
**Explanation:** Swami Dayanand Saraswati founded the Arya Samaj on 10 April 1875 in Bombay. His motto was "Back to the Vedas." He opposed idol worship, caste system, and advocated women's education.
**Shortcut / rule:** Arya Samaj = Dayanand (1875, Bombay); "Satyarth Prakash" = his book; "Back to Vedas"; Shuddhi movement.

### Q612 · Indian & World Geography

The 'Konkan' region is the coastal area of:

A. Kerala
B. Karnataka
C. Maharashtra and Goa
D. Tamil Nadu

**Answer:** C. Maharashtra and Goa
**Explanation:** The Konkan coast stretches along western Maharashtra and Goa between the Western Ghats and the Arabian Sea. It has a tropical climate with heavy monsoon rainfall and is famous for Alphonso mangoes and coconut.
**Shortcut / rule:** Konkan = Maharashtra/Goa coast; Malabar = Kerala coast; Coromandel = Tamil Nadu east coast; Canara = Karnataka coast.

### Q613 · Indian Polity & Governance

Residuary powers in India belong to:

A. State Government
B. Central Government
C. Both equally
D. Local bodies

**Answer:** B. Central Government
**Explanation:** Under Article 248 and Entry 97 of the Union List, residuary powers (subjects not in any list) belong to the Central Government/Parliament. This is similar to Canada and unlike the USA.
**Shortcut / rule:** Residuary powers = Centre (Art 248); Like Canada; Unlike USA (residuary to states); 7th Schedule = 3 lists.

### Q614 · Indian Economy

Which is the highest denomination currency note currently in circulation in India?

A. ₹500
B. ₹1,000
C. ₹2,000
D. ₹5,000

**Answer:** A. ₹500
**Explanation:** After demonetization (8 November 2016), ₹500 and ₹1,000 notes were withdrawn. New ₹500 and ₹2,000 notes were introduced. The ₹2,000 note was subsequently withdrawn from circulation in 2023, making ₹500 the highest denomination.
**Shortcut / rule:** Demonetization = 8 Nov 2016 (₹500 & ₹1000 old); ₹2000 withdrawn 2023; Current highest = ₹500.

### Q615 · Physics

Diamonds shine due to:

A. Reflection
B. Refraction
C. Total Internal Reflection
D. Dispersion

**Answer:** C. Total Internal Reflection
**Explanation:** Diamonds have a very high refractive index (2.42), causing light entering the diamond to undergo multiple total internal reflections before emerging, creating the brilliant sparkle.
**Shortcut / rule:** Diamond brilliance = total internal reflection + dispersion; High refractive index = 2.42; Cut matters for sparkle.

### Q616 · Chemistry

Marsh gas is:

A. Ethane
B. Methane
C. Propane
D. Butane

**Answer:** B. Methane
**Explanation:** Methane (CH₄) is called Marsh gas because it is produced by decomposition of organic matter in marshes and swamps. It is also the main component of natural gas and a potent greenhouse gas.
**Shortcut / rule:** Marsh gas = CH₄ (methane); Natural gas = mainly CH₄; CNG = compressed CH₄; Coal mine gas = CH₄ (firedamp).

### Q617 · Biology / Life Science

Insulin was discovered by:

A. Edward Jenner
B. Alexander Fleming
C. Frederick Banting and Charles Best
D. Louis Pasteur

**Answer:** C. Frederick Banting and Charles Best
**Explanation:** Insulin was discovered by Frederick Banting and Charles Best in 1921 at the University of Toronto. Banting received the Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine in 1923.
**Shortcut / rule:** Insulin = Banting & Best (1921); Produced by beta cells of Islets of Langerhans (pancreas); Deficiency = Diabetes.

### Q618 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Warli' painting tradition belongs to which state?

A. Madhya Pradesh
B. Bihar
C. Maharashtra
D. Rajasthan

**Answer:** C. Maharashtra
**Explanation:** Warli painting is a tribal art form from the Warli tribe of Maharashtra (Thane and Nashik districts). It uses simple geometric shapes (circles, triangles, squares) depicting daily life and nature.
**Shortcut / rule:** Warli = Maharashtra (tribal, geometric); Madhubani = Bihar; Phad = Rajasthan; Pattachitra = Odisha; Gond = MP.

### Q619 · Games and Sports

Which martial art form originated in Kerala?

A. Karate
B. Kalaripayattu
C. Judo
D. Taekwondo

**Answer:** B. Kalaripayattu
**Explanation:** Kalaripayattu is considered one of the oldest fighting systems in the world, originating in Kerala. It includes strikes, kicks, grappling, and weapons. It is believed to have influenced other Asian martial arts.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kalaripayattu = Kerala (oldest martial art); Silambam = Tamil Nadu (staff fighting); Thang-Ta = Manipur (sword and spear).

### Q620 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who was the first Asian to receive the Nobel Prize?

A. C.V. Raman
B. Rabindranath Tagore
C. Amartya Sen
D. Yasunari Kawabata

**Answer:** B. Rabindranath Tagore
**Explanation:** Rabindranath Tagore was the first Asian to win the Nobel Prize (Literature, 1913) for his poetry collection "Gitanjali." He was also the first non-European Nobel laureate in Literature.
**Shortcut / rule:** First Asian Nobel = Tagore (1913); First Indian Nobel in Science = C.V. Raman (1930); First woman Nobel = Marie Curie (1903).

### Q621 · UN & World Organizations

INTERPOL is headquartered in:

A. Washington DC
B. London
C. Lyon, France
D. Geneva

**Answer:** C. Lyon, France
**Explanation:** INTERPOL (International Criminal Police Organization) was established in 1923 and is headquartered in Lyon, France. It has 195 member countries and facilitates international police cooperation.
**Shortcut / rule:** INTERPOL = Lyon, France; Europol = The Hague; FBI = Washington DC; CBI = New Delhi (India's apex investigating agency).

### Q622 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Bhopal Gas Tragedy (1984) involved leakage of which gas?

A. Carbon monoxide
B. Methyl Isocyanate (MIC)
C. Sulphur dioxide
D. Chlorine

**Answer:** B. Methyl Isocyanate (MIC)
**Explanation:** On 2-3 December 1984, methyl isocyanate (MIC) gas leaked from the Union Carbide plant in Bhopal, killing thousands. It is considered the world's worst industrial disaster and led to the EPA 1986.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bhopal = MIC (2-3 Dec 1984); Union Carbide; Led to EPA 1986; National Pollution Prevention Day = 2 December.

### Q623 · Computers & Applications

The shortcut key to save a file in Windows is:

A. Ctrl + P
B. Ctrl + S
C. Ctrl + D
D. Ctrl + N

**Answer:** B. Ctrl + S
**Explanation:** Ctrl+S is the universal keyboard shortcut for saving files in Windows applications. Ctrl+P = Print, Ctrl+N = New, Ctrl+D = Bookmark/Delete.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ctrl+S = Save; Ctrl+P = Print; Ctrl+N = New; Ctrl+O = Open; Ctrl+F = Find; Ctrl+H = Replace.

### Q624 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

BHIM app stands for:

A. Bharat Interface for Money
B. Basic Household Income Management
C. Banking Helpline for Indian Markets
D. Bharat Internal Money

**Answer:** A. Bharat Interface for Money
**Explanation:** BHIM (Bharat Interface for Money) app was launched on 30 December 2016 for UPI-based digital payments. It is named after Dr. B.R. Ambedkar ("Bhim" being his popular name).
**Shortcut / rule:** BHIM = UPI payments (2016); UPI = Unified Payments Interface; NPCI = National Payments Corporation of India.

### Q625 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The Pradhan Mantri Suraksha Bima Yojana (PMSBY) provides:

A. Life insurance
B. Accidental insurance
C. Health insurance
D. Crop insurance

**Answer:** B. Accidental insurance
**Explanation:** PMSBY provides accidental death and disability insurance cover of ₹2 lakh at a premium of ₹20 per year. It was launched on 9 May 2015. Ages 18-70 eligible.
**Shortcut / rule:** PMSBY = accident insurance (₹20/yr, ₹2L cover); PMJJBY = life insurance (₹436/yr, ₹2L cover); Both launched 9 May 2015.

### Q626 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The 'Quit India' resolution was passed at which session of INC?

A. Wardha Session
B. Bombay Session (AICC)
C. Lahore Session
D. Karachi Session

**Answer:** B. Bombay Session (AICC)
**Explanation:** The Quit India Resolution was passed on 8 August 1942 at the AICC session at Gowalia Tank Maidan (now August Kranti Maidan), Bombay. The working committee had drafted it at Wardha on 14 July 1942.
**Shortcut / rule:** QIM Resolution: Wardha (drafted) → Bombay (passed, 8 Aug 1942); "Do or Die" = Gandhi; Leaders arrested next day.

### Q627 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who was the Governor-General who abolished Sati?

A. Lord Cornwallis
B. Lord William Bentinck
C. Lord Dalhousie
D. Lord Curzon

**Answer:** B. Lord William Bentinck
**Explanation:** Lord William Bentinck abolished Sati (self-immolation of widows on the husband's funeral pyre) in 1829 through Regulation XVII. Raja Ram Mohan Roy's campaign was instrumental in this reform.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sati abolished = Bentinck (1829); Ram Mohan Roy campaigned; Bentinck = first GG of India (1833 Charter Act).

### Q628 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Communal Award was given by:

A. Lord Mountbatten
B. Ramsay MacDonald
C. Winston Churchill
D. Clement Attlee

**Answer:** B. Ramsay MacDonald
**Explanation:** The Communal Award (1932) by British PM Ramsay MacDonald provided separate electorates for Depressed Classes (Dalits). Gandhi opposed it and went on a fast unto death, leading to the Poona Pact.
**Shortcut / rule:** Communal Award 1932 = MacDonald (separate electorates for Dalits); Poona Pact 1932 = Gandhi-Ambedkar compromise (reserved seats, joint electorate).

### Q629 · Indian & World Geography

The Palk Bay lies between:

A. India and Maldives
B. India and Sri Lanka
C. India and Myanmar
D. India and Bangladesh

**Answer:** B. India and Sri Lanka
**Explanation:** Palk Bay is a semi-enclosed shallow bay between Tamil Nadu (India) and the northern coast of Sri Lanka. It is bounded by the Palk Strait on the northeast and Adam's Bridge on the southeast.
**Shortcut / rule:** Palk Bay/Strait = India-Sri Lanka; Adam's Bridge (Ram Setu) = chain of limestone shoals; Gulf of Mannar = south.

### Q630 · Indian & World Geography

Which Indian state is called the "Land of the Rising Sun"?

A. Manipur
B. Arunachal Pradesh
C. Nagaland
D. Mizoram

**Answer:** B. Arunachal Pradesh
**Explanation:** Arunachal Pradesh is called the "Land of the Rising Sun" (or "Land of the Dawn-Lit Mountains") because it is the easternmost state of India and receives the first rays of the sun.
**Shortcut / rule:** Arunachal = Land of Rising Sun; Meghalaya = Abode of Clouds; Mizoram = Land of Hill People; Nagaland = Land of Festivals.

### Q631 · Indian Polity & Governance

The 'Directive Principles' are contained in which Part of the Constitution?

A. Part II
B. Part III
C. Part IV
D. Part V

**Answer:** C. Part IV
**Explanation:** Directive Principles of State Policy (DPSP) are in Part IV (Articles 36-51). They are non-justiciable (not enforceable by courts) but fundamental in governance.
**Shortcut / rule:** Part III = FR (justiciable); Part IV = DPSP (non-justiciable); Part IV-A = FD; DPSP guide the State in policy making.

### Q632 · Indian Polity & Governance

Under which Article is President's Rule imposed in a state?

A. Article 352
B. Article 356
C. Article 360
D. Article 370

**Answer:** B. Article 356
**Explanation:** Article 356 empowers the President to impose President's Rule in a state if the Governor reports that constitutional machinery has failed. Maximum duration = 3 years (with Parliamentary approval every 6 months).
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 352 = National Emergency; Art 356 = State Emergency (President's Rule); Art 360 = Financial Emergency; Max 3 years.

### Q633 · Indian Economy

India's Green Revolution was primarily focused on increasing production of:

A. Pulses
B. Rice and Wheat
C. Cotton
D. Sugarcane

**Answer:** B. Rice and Wheat
**Explanation:** The Green Revolution (1960s-70s) focused on wheat and rice using High Yielding Variety (HYV) seeds, fertilizers, and irrigation. Punjab and Haryana became the "Granary of India."
**Shortcut / rule:** Green Revolution = wheat & rice; Started mid-1960s; HYV seeds = Norman Borlaug; India = M.S. Swaminathan; Punjab/Haryana = main beneficiaries.

### Q634 · Indian Economy

Which Indian industry is called the 'Sunrise Industry'?

A. Textile
B. Steel
C. Information Technology (IT)
D. Jute

**Answer:** C. Information Technology (IT)
**Explanation:** The IT industry is called a 'Sunrise Industry' because of its rapid growth, employment generation, and contribution to India's GDP and exports. India is a global leader in IT services and outsourcing.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sunrise industry = IT (rapidly growing); Sunset industry = declining (e.g., typewriter); India's IT hubs: Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Pune, Chennai.

### Q635 · Physics

Which law states that the volume of a fixed mass of gas at constant pressure is directly proportional to its absolute temperature?

A. Boyle's Law
B. Charles's Law
C. Gay-Lussac's Law
D. Avogadro's Law

**Answer:** B. Charles's Law
**Explanation:** Charles's Law: V ∝ T (at constant P). V₁/T₁ = V₂/T₂. This means gases expand when heated and contract when cooled at constant pressure.
**Shortcut / rule:** Charles = V ∝ T (P constant); Boyle = P ∝ 1/V (T constant); Gay-Lussac = P ∝ T (V constant); Ideal gas: PV = nRT.

### Q636 · Physics

Electromagnetic induction was discovered by:

A. James Clerk Maxwell
B. Michael Faraday
C. Nikola Tesla
D. Thomas Edison

**Answer:** B. Michael Faraday
**Explanation:** Michael Faraday discovered electromagnetic induction in 1831. He showed that a changing magnetic field induces an electric current in a conductor. This principle is the basis for generators and transformers.
**Shortcut / rule:** Faraday = electromagnetic induction (1831); Lenz's Law = direction of induced current; Used in generators, transformers, induction cooktops.

### Q637 · Chemistry

The gas used in balloons for flying is:

A. Nitrogen
B. Hydrogen
C. Helium
D. Oxygen

**Answer:** C. Helium
**Explanation:** Helium is used in modern balloons because it is lighter than air and inert (non-flammable). Earlier, hydrogen was used but it is highly flammable (Hindenburg disaster, 1937).
**Shortcut / rule:** Helium = safe, inert, lighter than air; Hydrogen = lighter but flammable; Helium discovered on Sun first (helios = sun).

### Q638 · Chemistry

Which compound is called 'Blue Vitriol'?

A. Iron Sulphate
B. Zinc Sulphate
C. Copper Sulphate
D. Sodium Sulphate

**Answer:** C. Copper Sulphate
**Explanation:** Copper Sulphate pentahydrate (CuSO₄·5H₂O) is called Blue Vitriol. It appears as blue crystals and is used as a fungicide (Bordeaux mixture) and in electroplating.
**Shortcut / rule:** Blue Vitriol = CuSO₄; Green Vitriol = FeSO₄; White Vitriol = ZnSO₄; Oil of Vitriol = H₂SO₄.

### Q639 · Biology / Life Science

The basic structural and functional unit of life is:

A. Atom
B. Cell
C. Tissue
D. Organ

**Answer:** B. Cell
**Explanation:** The cell is the basic structural and functional unit of all living organisms. It was discovered by Robert Hooke in 1665. Cell theory was proposed by Schleiden and Schwann (1838-39).
**Shortcut / rule:** Cell discovered = Robert Hooke (1665, cork); Cell theory = Schleiden & Schwann; Types: Prokaryotic (no nucleus) & Eukaryotic (nucleus).

### Q640 · Biology / Life Science

Which is the longest cell in the human body?

A. Muscle cell
B. Nerve cell (Neuron)
C. Red blood cell
D. White blood cell

**Answer:** B. Nerve cell (Neuron)
**Explanation:** Nerve cells (neurons) are the longest cells in the human body. Some neurons (like the sciatic nerve) can be over 1 meter long. The smallest human cell is the sperm (head only) or RBC.
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest cell = Neuron; Largest cell = Ovum (egg, 0.1mm); Smallest cell = Sperm (head); Largest cell in world = Ostrich egg.

### Q641 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Hampi' ruins are associated with which empire?

A. Mughal Empire
B. Chola Empire
C. Vijayanagara Empire
D. Maurya Empire

**Answer:** C. Vijayanagara Empire
**Explanation:** Hampi in Karnataka was the capital of the Vijayanagara Empire (1336-1646). The ruins include temples, royal enclosures, and market streets. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hampi = Vijayanagara; Fatehpur Sikri = Mughal (Akbar); Thanjavur = Chola; Pataliputra = Maurya.

### Q642 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Jama Masjid' in Delhi was built by:

A. Akbar
B. Jahangir
C. Shah Jahan
D. Aurangzeb

**Answer:** C. Shah Jahan
**Explanation:** Jama Masjid in Old Delhi was built by Shah Jahan between 1644-1656. It is one of the largest mosques in India. Shah Jahan also built the Taj Mahal and Red Fort.
**Shortcut / rule:** Shah Jahan's buildings: Taj Mahal, Red Fort (Delhi), Jama Masjid, Peacock Throne; "Builder Emperor."

### Q643 · Games and Sports

The 'Nehru Trophy Boat Race' is held in:

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Kerala
C. Goa
D. West Bengal

**Answer:** B. Kerala
**Explanation:** The Nehru Trophy Boat Race is held annually on Punnamada Lake in Alappuzha (Alleppey), Kerala. It features snake boats (Chundan Vallam) and is part of Kerala's harvest festival Onam celebrations.
**Shortcut / rule:** Nehru Trophy Boat Race = Kerala (Alappuzha, snake boats); Onam = harvest festival of Kerala.

### Q644 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who founded Infosys?

A. Azim Premji
B. N.R. Narayana Murthy
C. Ratan Tata
D. Mukesh Ambani

**Answer:** B. N.R. Narayana Murthy
**Explanation:** N.R. Narayana Murthy co-founded Infosys in 1981 along with 6 other engineers with an initial capital of ₹10,000. Infosys became one of India's largest IT companies and a global brand.
**Shortcut / rule:** Infosys = Narayana Murthy (1981, Pune); Wipro = Azim Premji; TCS = Tata Group (oldest, 1968); HCL = Shiv Nadar.

### Q645 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

India's first woman in space was:

A. Kalpana Chawla
B. Sunita Williams
C. Tessy Thomas
D. Ritu Karidhal

**Answer:** A. Kalpana Chawla
**Explanation:** Kalpana Chawla (Indian-born American) was the first Indian-origin woman in space (1997, Space Shuttle Columbia). Tragically, she died on 1 February 2003 when Columbia disintegrated during re-entry.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kalpana Chawla: first Indian-origin woman in space (1997); Died: Columbia disaster (1 Feb 2003); Rakesh Sharma = first Indian citizen in space (1984).

### Q646 · UN & World Organizations

UNICEF is primarily concerned with:

A. World trade
B. Children's welfare
C. Labour rights
D. Environmental protection

**Answer:** B. Children's welfare
**Explanation:** UNICEF works in over 190 countries to protect children's rights, provide education, healthcare, nutrition, and emergency relief. It was awarded the Nobel Peace Prize in 1965.
**Shortcut / rule:** UNICEF = children (1946, New York); Nobel Peace 1965; Polio eradication, immunization, child nutrition = key areas.

### Q647 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Olive Ridley' turtles nest mainly along which Indian coast?

A. Gujarat coast
B. Odisha coast (Gahirmatha)
C. Kerala coast
D. Goa coast

**Answer:** B. Odisha coast (Gahirmatha)
**Explanation:** Gahirmatha Beach in Odisha is the world's largest nesting site for Olive Ridley sea turtles. Mass nesting (Arribada) occurs between January and March. It was declared a marine sanctuary in 1997.
**Shortcut / rule:** Olive Ridley = Gahirmatha, Odisha (Arribada = mass nesting); Endangered species; Also nest at Rushikulya, Odisha.

### Q648 · Computers & Applications

What is a computer virus?

A. A hardware component
B. A malicious software program that replicates and spreads to other computers
C. A type of processor
D. An operating system update

**Answer:** B. A malicious software program that replicates and spreads to other computers
**Explanation:** A computer virus is a type of malware that attaches itself to a program or file and replicates when the host is executed. Types include: Trojans, Worms, Ransomware, Spyware.
**Shortcut / rule:** Virus = replicates via host; Worm = self-replicating; Trojan = disguised; Ransomware = encrypts files; Antivirus = protection.

### Q649 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

AIIMS stands for:

A. All India Institute of Medical Sciences
B. All India Institute of Management Studies
C. Association of Indian Industrial and Manufacturing Sectors
D. All India Information and Media Services

**Answer:** A. All India Institute of Medical Sciences
**Explanation:** AIIMS is a group of autonomous public medical universities. The first AIIMS was established in New Delhi in 1956. Currently, there are multiple AIIMS across India under the PMSSY scheme.
**Shortcut / rule:** AIIMS = premier medical institute (1956, Delhi); Multiple new AIIMS under PMSSY; Autonomous body under Health Ministry.

### Q650 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The National Food Security Act (NFSA) was enacted in:

A. 2011
B. 2013
C. 2015
D. 2017

**Answer:** B. 2013
**Explanation:** NFSA was enacted on 10 September 2013 to provide subsidized food grains to approximately 2/3 of India's population. It provides 5 kg of food grains per person per month at ₹1-3/kg.
**Shortcut / rule:** NFSA = 2013 (food security); 5 kg/person/month; Rice ₹3, Wheat ₹2, Coarse grains ₹1; Covers 75% rural, 50% urban.

### Q651 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The term 'Pakistan' was coined by:

A. Muhammad Ali Jinnah
B. Choudhry Rahmat Ali
C. Allama Iqbal
D. Liaquat Ali Khan

**Answer:** B. Choudhry Rahmat Ali
**Explanation:** Choudhry Rahmat Ali coined the name "Pakistan" in 1933 in his pamphlet "Now or Never." The name stands for P (Punjab), A (Afghania/NWFP), K (Kashmir), S (Sindh), TAN (Baluchistan).
**Shortcut / rule:** Pakistan coined = Rahmat Ali (1933); Allama Iqbal = first proposed idea of separate Muslim state (1930 Allahabad address); Jinnah = achieved it.

### Q652 · Indian & World Geography

Which winds bring winter rainfall to north India?

A. Southwest Monsoon
B. Northeast Monsoon
C. Western Disturbances
D. Trade Winds

**Answer:** C. Western Disturbances
**Explanation:** Western Disturbances are extratropical storms originating in the Mediterranean region that bring winter rainfall (and snowfall in the Himalayas) to north and northwest India (December-February).
**Shortcut / rule:** Western Disturbances = winter rain (NW India, Mediterranean origin); Important for Rabi crops (wheat); Cause fog in Indo-Gangetic plains.

### Q653 · Indian Polity & Governance

The first Fundamental Right in the Indian Constitution is:

A. Right to Freedom
B. Right to Equality
C. Right against Exploitation
D. Right to Freedom of Religion

**Answer:** B. Right to Equality
**Explanation:** Right to Equality (Articles 14-18) is the first Fundamental Right. Art 14 = Equality before law; Art 15 = No discrimination; Art 16 = Equal opportunity in public employment; Art 17 = Abolition of untouchability; Art 18 = Abolition of titles.
**Shortcut / rule:** 6 FRs: Equality (14-18), Freedom (19-22), Against Exploitation (23-24), Freedom of Religion (25-28), Cultural & Educational (29-30), Constitutional Remedies (32).

### Q654 · Indian Economy

India is the _____ largest economy in the world (by nominal GDP):

A. 3rd
B. 5th
C. 7th
D. 10th

**Answer:** B. 5th
**Explanation:** India is the 5th largest economy by nominal GDP (after USA, China, Germany, Japan) and 3rd largest by Purchasing Power Parity (PPP) after USA and China.
**Shortcut / rule:** Nominal GDP: USA > China > Germany > Japan > India (5th); PPP: USA > China > India (3rd).

### Q655 · Physics

The SI unit of magnetic flux is:

A. Tesla
B. Weber
C. Henry
D. Gauss

**Answer:** B. Weber
**Explanation:** Magnetic flux is measured in Weber (Wb). Magnetic flux density (or magnetic field strength) is measured in Tesla (T). 1 Weber = 1 Tesla × 1 m².
**Shortcut / rule:** Magnetic flux = Weber (Wb); Magnetic field = Tesla (T); Inductance = Henry (H); T = Wb/m².

### Q656 · Chemistry

Cement is made from a mixture of:

A. Sand and gravel
B. Limestone and clay
C. Iron and carbon
D. Aluminium and silicon

**Answer:** B. Limestone and clay
**Explanation:** Portland cement is made by heating a mixture of limestone (CaCO₃) and clay in a kiln at ~1500°C to form clinker, which is ground with gypsum (to control setting time).
**Shortcut / rule:** Cement = limestone + clay (heated); Gypsum added to slow setting; Concrete = cement + sand + gravel + water.

### Q657 · Biology / Life Science

Antibiotics are effective against:

A. Viruses
B. Bacteria
C. Both viruses and bacteria
D. Fungi only

**Answer:** B. Bacteria
**Explanation:** Antibiotics work against bacterial infections (e.g., Penicillin, Amoxicillin). They are NOT effective against viral infections (like common cold, flu, COVID-19). Antivirals are used for viruses.
**Shortcut / rule:** Antibiotics = bacteria; Antivirals = viruses; Antifungals = fungi; Antibiotic resistance = major global concern (overuse).

### Q658 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'India Gate' in New Delhi was built to commemorate:

A. India's independence
B. Soldiers who died in World War I
C. The Republic
D. The British Raj

**Answer:** B. Soldiers who died in World War I
**Explanation:** India Gate was designed by Edwin Lutyens and completed in 1931. It is a war memorial dedicated to 70,000 Indian soldiers who died in World War I and the Third Anglo-Afghan War.
**Shortcut / rule:** India Gate = WWI memorial (1931, Lutyens); Amar Jawan Jyoti = eternal flame (1972); National War Memorial nearby (2019).

### Q659 · Games and Sports

The 'Uber Cup' is associated with:

A. Cricket
B. Football
C. Badminton (Women's)
D. Tennis

**Answer:** C. Badminton (Women's)
**Explanation:** The Uber Cup is the premier women's international team badminton championship, equivalent to the Thomas Cup for men. Named after Betty Uber, it started in 1957.
**Shortcut / rule:** Uber Cup = Women's Badminton; Thomas Cup = Men's Badminton; Sudirman Cup = Mixed; India won Thomas Cup in 2022.

### Q660 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who wrote "Mein Kampf"?

A. Napoleon Bonaparte
B. Winston Churchill
C. Adolf Hitler
D. Joseph Stalin

**Answer:** C. Adolf Hitler
**Explanation:** "Mein Kampf" (My Struggle) was written by Adolf Hitler in 1925 while imprisoned. It outlined his political ideology, antisemitic views, and plans for German expansion.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mein Kampf = Hitler (1925); The Prince = Machiavelli; Das Kapital = Karl Marx; Wealth of Nations = Adam Smith.

### Q661 · Space & Nuclear Program of India

The Nuclear Power Corporation of India (NPCIL) operates nuclear power plants. India's latest large nuclear plant is:

A. Tarapur
B. Kudankulam
C. Kalpakkam
D. Narora

**Answer:** B. Kudankulam
**Explanation:** Kudankulam Nuclear Power Plant (KKNPP) in Tamil Nadu, built with Russian collaboration, is India's latest large nuclear plant. Unit 1 started commercial operation in 2014 and Unit 2 in 2017.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kudankulam = Tamil Nadu (Russian VVER reactors); Tarapur = oldest (1969); Kalpakkam = fast breeder reactor (IGCAR).

### Q662 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The 'Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change' (IPCC) was established in:

A. 1985
B. 1988
C. 1992
D. 1997

**Answer:** B. 1988
**Explanation:** IPCC was established in 1988 by WMO (World Meteorological Organization) and UNEP. It assesses climate change science and provides reports to policymakers. It won the Nobel Peace Prize in 2007 (shared with Al Gore).
**Shortcut / rule:** IPCC = 1988 (WMO + UNEP); Assessment Reports (AR); Nobel Peace 2007; R.K. Pachauri was IPCC chairman during Nobel.

### Q663 · Computers & Applications

What does SSD stand for?

A. Solid State Drive
B. Super Speed Device
C. System Storage Disk
D. Secure Software Device

**Answer:** A. Solid State Drive
**Explanation:** SSD uses flash memory (NAND) for data storage. It is much faster than traditional HDDs (hard disk drives) because it has no moving parts. It is more reliable, lighter, and uses less power.
**Shortcut / rule:** SSD = no moving parts (fast, reliable); HDD = spinning disk (slower, cheaper per GB); NVMe SSD > SATA SSD in speed.

### Q664 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

FSSAI stands for:

A. Food Safety and Standards Authority of India
B. Federal Science and Standards Authority of India
C. Food Security and Sanitation Authority of India
D. Food Supply and Storage Association of India

**Answer:** A. Food Safety and Standards Authority of India
**Explanation:** FSSAI was established under the Food Safety and Standards Act 2006. It regulates food safety, sets standards, and issues licenses for food businesses in India. HQ: New Delhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** FSSAI = food safety (2006, Delhi); FSSAI license required for food businesses; Logo on food packages = "Eat Right India."

### Q665 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The 'Smart Cities Mission' aims to develop how many cities?

A. 50
B. 75
C. 100
D. 200

**Answer:** C. 100
**Explanation:** The Smart Cities Mission was launched on 25 June 2015 to develop 100 cities with smart infrastructure, e-governance, and sustainable development. Cities were selected through a competition.
**Shortcut / rule:** Smart Cities = 100 cities (2015); AMRUT = 500 cities (urban infrastructure); HRIDAY = 12 heritage cities.

### Q666 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Indian Councils Act 1861 was significant because it:

A. Introduced separate electorates
B. Allowed Indian representation in legislative councils for the first time
C. Gave full self-governance
D. Abolished the EIC

**Answer:** B. Allowed Indian representation in legislative councils for the first time
**Explanation:** The Indian Councils Act 1861 allowed the Viceroy to nominate Indians to the legislative council. It decentralized legislative powers and allowed provinces to make their own laws on certain matters.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1861 Act = first Indian representation (nominated); 1892 = indirect elections; 1909 = direct elections (limited); 1919 = Diarchy.

### Q667 · Indian & World Geography

The Lakshadweep Islands are located in which sea?

A. Bay of Bengal
B. Arabian Sea
C. Indian Ocean
D. Pacific Ocean

**Answer:** B. Arabian Sea
**Explanation:** Lakshadweep is a Union Territory of 36 islands in the Arabian Sea, about 200-440 km off the Kerala coast. Only 10 islands are inhabited. The capital is Kavaratti.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lakshadweep = Arabian Sea; Andaman & Nicobar = Bay of Bengal; Lakshadweep = coral atoll; A&N = volcanic/tectonic.

### Q668 · Indian Polity & Governance

National Commission for Scheduled Castes is established under:

A. Article 335
B. Article 338
C. Article 340
D. Article 342

**Answer:** B. Article 338
**Explanation:** Article 338 provides for the National Commission for Scheduled Castes. It was established as a constitutional body by the 89th Amendment (2003), separating it from the combined SC/ST Commission.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 338 = SC Commission; Art 338A = ST Commission (89th Amendment); Art 340 = Backward Classes Commission.

### Q669 · Indian Economy

The term 'Sensex' is related to:

A. Reserve Bank of India
B. Bombay Stock Exchange (BSE)
C. National Stock Exchange (NSE)
D. SEBI

**Answer:** B. Bombay Stock Exchange (BSE)
**Explanation:** Sensex (Sensitive Index) is the benchmark index of BSE, comprising 30 of the largest and most actively traded stocks. BSE (est. 1875) is Asia's oldest stock exchange.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sensex = BSE (30 stocks); Nifty 50 = NSE (50 stocks); BSE = 1875 (oldest in Asia); NSE = 1992.

### Q670 · Physics

What is the first law of thermodynamics?

A. Heat cannot flow from cold to hot
B. Energy can neither be created nor destroyed, only converted
C. Entropy always increases
D. Absolute zero is unattainable

**Answer:** B. Energy can neither be created nor destroyed, only converted
**Explanation:** The First Law of Thermodynamics is essentially the law of conservation of energy applied to thermal systems. It states that the change in internal energy equals heat added minus work done by the system (ΔU = Q - W).
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Law = energy conservation; 2nd Law = entropy increases; 3rd Law = absolute zero unattainable; Zeroth Law = thermal equilibrium.

### Q671 · Chemistry

The chemical formula of Ozone is:

A. O₂
B. O₃
C. O₄
D. O

**Answer:** B. O₃
**Explanation:** Ozone (O₃) is an allotrope of oxygen with three atoms per molecule. The ozone layer in the stratosphere protects Earth from harmful UV radiation. At ground level, ozone is a pollutant.
**Shortcut / rule:** O₂ = oxygen (breathe); O₃ = ozone (UV shield in stratosphere, pollutant at ground); Ozone hole = Antarctica.

### Q672 · Biology / Life Science

Which mosquito transmits Dengue fever?

A. Anopheles
B. Aedes
C. Culex
D. Tsetse fly

**Answer:** B. Aedes
**Explanation:** Dengue fever is transmitted by the Aedes aegypti mosquito. It is a viral disease causing high fever, headache, joint pain, and rash. There is no specific antiviral treatment.
**Shortcut / rule:** Aedes = Dengue, Chikungunya, Zika; Anopheles = Malaria; Culex = Japanese Encephalitis, Filariasis.

### Q673 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Fatehpur Sikri' was built by which Mughal Emperor?

A. Babur
B. Humayun
C. Akbar
D. Shah Jahan

**Answer:** C. Akbar
**Explanation:** Akbar built Fatehpur Sikri near Agra in 1571 as his capital. It was abandoned after about 14 years due to water scarcity. It contains the Buland Darwaza (tallest gateway in the world).
**Shortcut / rule:** Fatehpur Sikri = Akbar (1571, abandoned ~1585); Buland Darwaza = 54m (victory over Gujarat); Panch Mahal, Jama Masjid, tomb of Salim Chishti.

### Q674 · Games and Sports

The 'Arjuna Award' is given for:

A. Excellence in coaching
B. Outstanding performance in sports
C. Lifetime achievement in sports
D. Best sports journalist

**Answer:** B. Outstanding performance in sports
**Explanation:** The Arjuna Award was instituted in 1961 for outstanding achievement in sports. It is named after the legendary archer Arjuna from the Mahabharata. Recipients get a statuette, certificate, and prize money.
**Shortcut / rule:** Arjuna = sports performance (1961); Dronacharya = coaching; Khel Ratna = highest sports; Dhyan Chand = lifetime achievement.

### Q675 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Who was the first President of the United States?

A. Abraham Lincoln
B. George Washington
C. Thomas Jefferson
D. John Adams

**Answer:** B. George Washington
**Explanation:** George Washington served as the first President of the United States from 1789 to 1797. He is called the "Father of His Country" and led the Continental Army during the American Revolution.
**Shortcut / rule:** Washington = 1st US President; Lincoln = 16th (abolished slavery); Obama = 44th (first African American); Current = check latest.

### Q676 · UN & World Organizations

The 'G-20' is a forum of:

A. 20 richest countries
B. 20 major economies (19 countries + EU)
C. 20 most populated countries
D. 20 democratic countries

**Answer:** B. 20 major economies (19 countries + EU)
**Explanation:** The G-20 comprises 19 countries plus the EU, representing about 85% of global GDP and 75% of world trade. India hosted the G-20 Summit in 2023 (New Delhi).
**Shortcut / rule:** G-20 = 19 countries + EU; G-7 = USA, UK, France, Germany, Italy, Canada, Japan; India hosted G-20 in 2023 (theme: "One Earth, One Family, One Future").

### Q677 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Tiger reserves in India are managed under:

A. Project Tiger / NTCA
B. Ministry of Environment
C. Wildlife Institute of India
D. IUCN

**Answer:** A. Project Tiger / NTCA
**Explanation:** Project Tiger (1973) and the National Tiger Conservation Authority (NTCA, 2005) manage India's 54+ tiger reserves. India has the largest population of wild tigers in the world.
**Shortcut / rule:** Project Tiger = 1973; NTCA = 2005 (statutory body); India has 54+ tiger reserves; Global tiger count: India has ~75% of wild tigers.

### Q678 · Computers & Applications

The term 'Phishing' refers to:

A. Online fishing game
B. Fraudulent attempt to obtain sensitive information by disguising as a trustworthy entity
C. A type of internet speed
D. A programming technique

**Answer:** B. Fraudulent attempt to obtain sensitive information by disguising as a trustworthy entity
**Explanation:** Phishing is a cybercrime where attackers send fraudulent emails, messages, or create fake websites impersonating legitimate organizations to steal passwords, credit card numbers, and personal data.
**Shortcut / rule:** Phishing = fake emails/sites for data theft; Vishing = voice phishing; Smishing = SMS phishing; Never click unknown links.

### Q679 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

Oil India Limited (OIL) is primarily engaged in:

A. Oil marketing
B. Oil exploration and production
C. Oil refining
D. Petrochemical manufacturing

**Answer:** B. Oil exploration and production
**Explanation:** Oil India Limited is a Navratna PSU primarily engaged in exploration, development, and production of crude oil and natural gas. It operates mainly in Assam and Arunachal Pradesh.
**Shortcut / rule:** OIL = exploration (Navratna, Assam); ONGC = exploration (Maharatna); IOCL = refining/marketing; BPCL/HPCL = marketing.

### Q680 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The 'Indian National Army' (INA) trials were held at:

A. Kolkata
B. Delhi (Red Fort)
C. Mumbai
D. Chennai

**Answer:** B. Delhi (Red Fort)
**Explanation:** The INA trials (1945-46) were held at the Red Fort, Delhi. The British tried INA officers (Shah Nawaz Khan, P.K. Sahgal, Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon) for treason. The trials aroused national sentiment and contributed to India's independence.
**Shortcut / rule:** INA trials = Red Fort, Delhi (1945-46); Defence lawyer = Bhulabhai Desai, Nehru, Asaf Ali; Trials = major catalyst for independence.

### Q681 · Indian & World Geography

The state of Telangana was carved out of which state?

A. Karnataka
B. Andhra Pradesh
C. Tamil Nadu
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** B. Andhra Pradesh
**Explanation:** Telangana became the 29th state of India on 2 June 2014 by bifurcation of Andhra Pradesh. Hyderabad serves as the joint capital for 10 years. T. region was earlier part of Nizam's dominion.
**Shortcut / rule:** Telangana = 29th state (2 June 2014); Carved from AP; Capital = Hyderabad (shared); Newest state = Telangana.

### Q682 · Indian Polity & Governance

Impeachment of the President requires:

A. Simple majority in both Houses
B. Two-thirds majority of total membership of each House
C. Three-fourths majority
D. Unanimous decision

**Answer:** B. Two-thirds majority of total membership of each House
**Explanation:** Under Article 61, impeachment charges can be initiated in either House by at least 1/4 of total members. The other House investigates and must pass the resolution by 2/3 of total membership to remove the President.
**Shortcut / rule:** Impeachment: 1/4 to initiate, 2/3 to pass (total membership); Only for "violation of Constitution"; Never used in India.

### Q683 · Indian Economy

The Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India (IRDAI) is headquartered in:

A. Mumbai
B. New Delhi
C. Hyderabad
D. Chennai

**Answer:** C. Hyderabad
**Explanation:** IRDAI is headquartered in Hyderabad. It was established in 1999 to protect policyholders' interests, regulate, and promote the insurance industry in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** IRDAI = Hyderabad; SEBI = Mumbai; RBI = Mumbai (established Kolkata); TRAI = New Delhi; PFRDA = New Delhi.

### Q684 · Physics

Which particle carries a positive charge?

A. Electron
B. Proton
C. Neutron
D. Photon

**Answer:** B. Proton
**Explanation:** A proton carries a positive charge (+1.6 × 10⁻¹⁹ C). It is found in the nucleus of an atom. Electrons carry negative charge, and neutrons have no charge.
**Shortcut / rule:** Proton = positive (nucleus); Electron = negative (orbits); Neutron = neutral (nucleus); Proton mass ≈ Neutron mass >> Electron mass.

### Q685 · Chemistry

Vinegar contains which acid?

A. Citric acid
B. Lactic acid
C. Acetic acid
D. Tartaric acid

**Answer:** C. Acetic acid
**Explanation:** Vinegar contains 4-8% acetic acid (CH₃COOH). It is produced by fermentation of ethanol by acetic acid bacteria. It is used in cooking, cleaning, and food preservation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vinegar = Acetic acid; Lemon = Citric acid; Curd/yogurt = Lactic acid; Grapes = Tartaric acid; Ant sting = Formic acid.

### Q686 · Biology / Life Science

Respiration in plants occurs through:

A. Roots only
B. Leaves only
C. All parts (stomata, lenticels, root hairs)
D. Flowers only

**Answer:** C. All parts (stomata, lenticels, root hairs)
**Explanation:** Plants respire through all living cells. Gas exchange occurs through stomata (leaves), lenticels (stems), and root hairs (roots). Unlike photosynthesis, respiration occurs 24/7.
**Shortcut / rule:** Stomata = leaf gas exchange; Lenticels = stem gas exchange; Root hairs = root absorption; Respiration = 24/7; Photosynthesis = only with light.

### Q687 · Games and Sports

Leander Paes is associated with which sport?

A. Cricket
B. Badminton
C. Tennis
D. Swimming

**Answer:** C. Tennis
**Explanation:** Leander Paes is an Indian tennis player who won a Bronze medal at the 1996 Atlanta Olympics (singles). He is also a Grand Slam doubles champion and has represented India in a record number of Davis Cup ties.
**Shortcut / rule:** Leander Paes = Tennis (Olympic Bronze 1996); 18 Grand Slam doubles titles; Davis Cup record (most ties).

### Q688 · Famous Personalities & Current Affairs

Albert Einstein is best known for:

A. Laws of Motion
B. Theory of Relativity (E = mc²)
C. Discovery of Penicillin
D. Periodic Table

**Answer:** B. Theory of Relativity (E = mc²)
**Explanation:** Albert Einstein developed the Theory of Relativity (Special, 1905; General, 1915). His famous equation E = mc² shows mass-energy equivalence. He won the Nobel Prize in Physics 1921 (Photoelectric Effect).
**Shortcut / rule:** Einstein = Relativity + E=mc² + Photoelectric Effect (Nobel 1921); Not for Relativity directly.

### Q689 · Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments

The 'Mysore Palace' is located in which state?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Kerala
C. Karnataka
D. Andhra Pradesh

**Answer:** C. Karnataka
**Explanation:** Mysore Palace (Amba Vilas Palace) in Mysuru, Karnataka is a historical palace and the seat of the Wodeyar dynasty. It is one of the most visited monuments in India after the Taj Mahal.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mysore Palace = Karnataka (Wodeyar); City Palace = Jaipur/Udaipur; Umaid Bhawan = Jodhpur; Lake Palace = Udaipur.

### Q690 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which is the national flower of India?

A. Rose
B. Sunflower
C. Lotus
D. Jasmine

**Answer:** C. Lotus
**Explanation:** Lotus (Nelumbo nucifera) is the national flower of India. It symbolizes purity, beauty, and divinity. It grows in shallow, murky waters and blooms above the surface.
**Shortcut / rule:** National flower = Lotus; National bird = Peacock; National animal = Bengal Tiger; National tree = Banyan; National fruit = Mango.

### Q691 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The 'August Offer' (1940) was made by:

A. Lord Mountbatten
B. Lord Linlithgow
C. Lord Wavell
D. Stafford Cripps

**Answer:** B. Lord Linlithgow
**Explanation:** The August Offer (8 August 1940) by Viceroy Lord Linlithgow proposed dominion status after WWII, expansion of executive council with Indian members, and setting up of a constituent assembly. It was rejected by both Congress and Muslim League.
**Shortcut / rule:** August Offer = 1940 (Linlithgow); Rejected; Followed by Cripps Mission 1942; Individual Satyagraha 1940 = Gandhi's response.

### Q692 · Indian & World Geography

Which dam is the tallest dam in India?

A. Bhakra Nangal
B. Hirakud
C. Tehri Dam
D. Nagarjuna Sagar

**Answer:** C. Tehri Dam
**Explanation:** Tehri Dam on the Bhagirathi River in Uttarakhand is the tallest dam in India (260.5 m) and one of the tallest in Asia. It is an earth and rockfill dam used for irrigation, power, and water supply to Delhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tallest dam = Tehri (260m, Uttarakhand); Longest dam = Hirakud (Odisha, 26 km); Bhakra = Sutlej (Punjab/HP).

### Q693 · Indian Polity & Governance

National Emergency has been proclaimed in India how many times?

A. Once
B. Twice
C. Three times
D. Four times

**Answer:** C. Three times
**Explanation:** National Emergency has been proclaimed 3 times: 1962 (Chinese aggression), 1971 (Indo-Pak War/Bangladesh), and 1975 (internal disturbance — controversial Emergency under Indira Gandhi).
**Shortcut / rule:** Emergency: 1962 (China war); 1971 (Pak war); 1975-77 (internal, Indira Gandhi); 44th Amendment (1978) restricted misuse.

### Q694 · Indian Economy

What is HDI?

A. Human Development Index
B. High Density Infrastructure
C. Healthcare and Development Indicator
D. Household Disposable Income

**Answer:** A. Human Development Index
**Explanation:** HDI measures a country's average achievement in health (life expectancy), education (years of schooling), and standard of living (GNI per capita). Published by UNDP since 1990. Created by Mahbub ul Haq and Amartya Sen.
**Shortcut / rule:** HDI = health + education + income; Published by UNDP; Scale: 0 to 1; Created by Mahbub ul Haq.

### Q695 · Physics

Who discovered X-rays?

A. Marie Curie
B. Wilhelm Conrad Röntgen
C. Max Planck
D. Niels Bohr

**Answer:** B. Wilhelm Conrad Röntgen
**Explanation:** Wilhelm Röntgen discovered X-rays in 1895 while working with cathode rays. He received the first Nobel Prize in Physics in 1901. X-rays are used in medical imaging, security, and industry.
**Shortcut / rule:** X-rays = Röntgen (1895); First Nobel Physics 1901; X-rays = short wavelength electromagnetic radiation; Used in CT scans.

### Q696 · Chemistry

Glass is primarily made from:

A. Calcium carbonate
B. Silicon dioxide (Silica/Sand)
C. Aluminium oxide
D. Iron oxide

**Answer:** B. Silicon dioxide (Silica/Sand)
**Explanation:** Glass is primarily made from silica (SiO₂, sand) mixed with soda (Na₂CO₃) and lime (CaO). It is heated to ~1700°C and cooled slowly. Glass is an amorphous solid (supercooled liquid).
**Shortcut / rule:** Glass = SiO₂ + Na₂CO₃ + CaO; Amorphous solid; Types: soda-lime, borosilicate (Pyrex), lead glass (crystal).

### Q697 · Biology / Life Science

Vitamins were discovered by:

A. Alexander Fleming
B. Casimir Funk
C. Louis Pasteur
D. Gregor Mendel

**Answer:** B. Casimir Funk
**Explanation:** Polish biochemist Casimir Funk coined the term "vitamine" (vital amine) in 1912 while studying beri-beri. The 'e' was later dropped when it was found not all vitamins contain amines.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vitamins named = Casimir Funk (1912); 13 essential vitamins; Fat-soluble: A, D, E, K; Water-soluble: B-complex, C.

### Q698 · Flagship Govt Programs, PSU & Organizations

The National Clean Air Programme (NCAP) targets reduction of PM2.5 by:

A. 10-15%
B. 20-30%
C. 40%
D. 50-60%

**Answer:** C. 40%
**Explanation:** NCAP was launched on 10 January 2019 targeting 40% reduction in PM2.5 and PM10 concentration by 2025-26 (base year 2017) in 131 non-attainment cities.
**Shortcut / rule:** NCAP = 40% PM reduction (2019); 131 cities targeted; PM2.5 = fine particles (most dangerous); WHO guidelines = 5 µg/m³ annual mean.

### Q699 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

What does UPI stand for?

A. Unified Payments Interface
B. Universal Payment Integration
C. United Payment of India
D. Unified Processing of Income

**Answer:** A. Unified Payments Interface
**Explanation:** UPI was developed by NPCI (National Payments Corporation of India) and launched in 2016. It enables instant real-time inter-bank transactions through mobile phones. India processes billions of UPI transactions monthly.
**Shortcut / rule:** UPI = instant mobile payments (2016, NPCI); Apps: Google Pay, PhonePe, Paytm; India = global leader in digital payments.

### Q700 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Kheda Satyagraha (1918) was related to:

A. Indigo farming
B. Revenue remission for crop failure
C. Salt tax
D. Labour rights

**Answer:** B. Revenue remission for crop failure
**Explanation:** Gandhi led the Kheda Satyagraha in 1918 when the government refused to remit land revenue despite crop failure (famine and plague). Sardar Patel played a key role in this movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kheda (1918) = revenue remission (Gujarat); Champaran (1917) = indigo (Bihar); Ahmedabad (1918) = mill workers; All = Gandhi's early Indian movements.

### Q701 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Panchayati Raj system in India was constitutionalized by which amendment?

A. 71st Amendment
B. 73rd Amendment
C. 74th Amendment
D. 76th Amendment

**Answer:** B. 73rd Amendment
**Explanation:** The 73rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992 gave constitutional status to Panchayati Raj institutions. It added Part IX and Schedule 11 to the Constitution. The 74th Amendment dealt with municipalities.
**Shortcut / rule:** 73rd = Panchayats (rural); 74th = Municipalities (urban); Both in 1992; Part IX & IX-A respectively.

### Q702 · Physics

The SI unit of electric current is:

A. Volt
B. Ohm
C. Ampere
D. Watt

**Answer:** C. Ampere
**Explanation:** Ampere (A) is the SI base unit of electric current, named after André-Marie Ampère. One ampere is defined as the flow of one coulomb of charge per second.
**Shortcut / rule:** 7 SI base units: metre (length), kilogram (mass), second (time), ampere (current), kelvin (temperature), mole (amount), candela (luminous intensity).

### Q703 · Chemistry

Which gas is known as "Laughing Gas"?

A. Nitric oxide
B. Nitrous oxide
C. Nitrogen dioxide
D. Carbon monoxide

**Answer:** B. Nitrous oxide
**Explanation:** Nitrous oxide (N₂O) is called laughing gas because inhaling it produces a euphoric effect. It is used as an anaesthetic in dentistry and surgery. It is also a greenhouse gas.
**Shortcut / rule:** N₂O = Laughing gas (anaesthetic); NO = Nitric oxide; NO₂ = Nitrogen dioxide (brown, pungent); CO = Carbon monoxide (silent killer).

### Q704 · Indian Economy

The headquarter of the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) is located in:

A. New Delhi
B. Kolkata
C. Mumbai
D. Chennai

**Answer:** C. Mumbai
**Explanation:** RBI was established on 1 April 1935 based on the Hilton Young Commission recommendations. Its headquarter is in Mumbai. The first governor was Sir Osborne Smith and the first Indian governor was C.D. Deshmukh.
**Shortcut / rule:** RBI = Mumbai (1935); SEBI = Mumbai (1992); NABARD = Mumbai (1982); SIDBI = Lucknow (1990).

### Q705 · Biology / Life Science

Which vitamin is essential for blood clotting?

A. Vitamin A
B. Vitamin C
C. Vitamin D
D. Vitamin K

**Answer:** D. Vitamin K
**Explanation:** Vitamin K (Phylloquinone) is essential for the synthesis of prothrombin, a protein required for blood clotting. Its deficiency causes excessive bleeding. Green leafy vegetables are rich sources.
**Shortcut / rule:** K = Koagulation (German for clotting); A = vision (Night blindness); C = scurvy; D = rickets; E = sterility; B1 = beriberi.

### Q706 · Indian Geography

The longest river in India is:

A. Godavari
B. Ganga
C. Brahmaputra
D. Krishna

**Answer:** B. Ganga
**Explanation:** The Ganga (2,525 km in India) is the longest river flowing entirely within India's territory. If we consider total length including outside India, the Brahmaputra is longer. Godavari (1,465 km) is the longest peninsular river.
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest in India: Ganga (2,525 km); Longest peninsular: Godavari (1,465 km); Longest tributary of Ganga: Yamuna.

### Q707 · Art, Culture & Literature

The famous Sun Temple is located at:

A. Madurai
B. Konark
C. Khajuraho
D. Thanjavur

**Answer:** B. Konark
**Explanation:** The Sun Temple at Konark (Odisha) was built by King Narasimhadeva I of the Eastern Ganga dynasty in the 13th century. It is designed as a giant chariot with 24 wheels. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Konark = Sun Temple (chariot shape, 24 wheels); Khajuraho = Chandela dynasty; Thanjavur = Brihadeeswarar (Chola); Madurai = Meenakshi Temple.

### Q708 · Games & Sports

In cricket, the term "Hat-trick" refers to:

A. Scoring 100 runs in a match
B. Taking 3 wickets in 3 consecutive balls
C. Hitting 3 sixes in a row
D. Winning 3 consecutive matches

**Answer:** B. Taking 3 wickets in 3 consecutive balls
**Explanation:** A hat-trick in cricket means a bowler takes 3 wickets on 3 consecutive deliveries. The term originated in cricket when a bowler was awarded a hat for this feat.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hat-trick = 3 wickets in 3 balls; First Test hat-trick: Fred Spofforth (1879); India's first: Harbhajan Singh (2001 vs Australia).

### Q709 · Famous Personalities

Who is known as the "Father of Indian Constitution"?

A. Jawaharlal Nehru
B. Mahatma Gandhi
C. B.R. Ambedkar
D. Sardar Patel

**Answer:** C. B.R. Ambedkar
**Explanation:** Dr. Bhimrao Ramji Ambedkar was the Chairman of the Drafting Committee of the Indian Constitution. He played a pivotal role in framing the Constitution, which was adopted on 26 November 1949. He was also the first Law Minister of independent India.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ambedkar = Father of Indian Constitution + Chairman Drafting Committee; Rajendra Prasad = President of Constituent Assembly; B.N. Rau = Constitutional Advisor.

### Q710 · Space & Nuclear Program

India's first satellite was:

A. Bhaskara
B. INSAT-1A
C. Aryabhata
D. Rohini

**Answer:** C. Aryabhata
**Explanation:** Aryabhata was India's first satellite, launched on 19 April 1975 from Kapustin Yar (USSR) by a Soviet Kosmos-3M rocket. It was named after the ancient Indian mathematician Aryabhata.
**Shortcut / rule:** Aryabhata (1975) = 1st satellite (USSR launch); Rohini (1980) = 1st by Indian SLV-3; Bhaskara (1979) = 1st earth observation; INSAT-1A (1982) = 1st communication.

### Q711 · UN & World Organizations

The headquarters of the United Nations is located in:

A. Geneva
B. London
C. New York
D. Vienna

**Answer:** C. New York
**Explanation:** The United Nations headquarters is in New York City, USA. The UN was established on 24 October 1945 after World War II. It has 6 principal organs and currently 193 member states.
**Shortcut / rule:** UN = New York; UNESCO = Paris; WHO = Geneva; IAEA = Vienna; ICJ = The Hague; FAO = Rome; IMF & World Bank = Washington DC.

### Q712 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The ozone layer is primarily found in which layer of the atmosphere?

A. Troposphere
B. Stratosphere
C. Mesosphere
D. Thermosphere

**Answer:** B. Stratosphere
**Explanation:** The ozone layer is found in the stratosphere at a height of 15-35 km. It absorbs harmful ultraviolet (UV) radiation from the Sun. Ozone depletion is caused by CFCs (chlorofluorocarbons).
**Shortcut / rule:** Layers (bottom to top): Troposphere → Stratosphere → Mesosphere → Thermosphere → Exosphere; Ozone = Stratosphere; Weather = Troposphere.

### Q713 · Computers & Applications

What is the full form of RAM?

A. Read Access Memory
B. Random Access Memory
C. Read All Memory
D. Run Access Memory

**Answer:** B. Random Access Memory
**Explanation:** RAM (Random Access Memory) is the primary volatile memory of a computer. Data stored in RAM is lost when power is switched off. It is faster than ROM and allows both read and write operations.
**Shortcut / rule:** RAM = volatile (temporary); ROM = non-volatile (permanent); Types of RAM: SRAM (Static), DRAM (Dynamic); Cache uses SRAM.

### Q714 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

ISRO stands for:

A. Indian Space Research Organization
B. Indian Science Research Organization
C. International Space Research Organization
D. Indian Space and Rocket Organization

**Answer:** A. Indian Space Research Organization
**Explanation:** ISRO (Indian Space Research Organisation) was established in 1969 with its headquarters in Bengaluru. Its first chairman was Vikram Sarabhai, known as the father of the Indian space programme.
**Shortcut / rule:** ISRO = Bengaluru (1969); First Chairman: Vikram Sarabhai; Current launch centre: Sriharikota (SDSC-SHAR), Andhra Pradesh.

### Q715 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana (PMJDY) was launched in:

A. 2012
B. 2014
C. 2016
D. 2018

**Answer:** B. 2014
**Explanation:** PMJDY was launched on 28 August 2014 for financial inclusion of the unbanked population. It provides zero-balance bank accounts with a RuPay debit card and insurance coverage of ₹1 lakh (later raised to ₹2 lakh).
**Shortcut / rule:** PMJDY = 28 Aug 2014 (financial inclusion, zero balance); PMSBY = ₹2 lakh accident insurance (₹12/year); PMJJBY = ₹2 lakh life insurance (₹436/year); APY = pension.

### Q716 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Battle of Plassey was fought in the year:

A. 1757
B. 1764
C. 1857
D. 1799

**Answer:** A. 1757
**Explanation:** The Battle of Plassey was fought on 23 June 1757 between the British East India Company (led by Robert Clive) and the Nawab of Bengal (Siraj-ud-Daulah). Mir Jafar's betrayal led to British victory. This established British political power in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** Plassey (1757) = British rule begins, Mir Jafar's betrayal; Buxar (1764) = consolidated British power; 1857 = First War of Independence.

### Q717 · Indian Polity & Governance

The minimum age for becoming President of India is:

A. 25 years
B. 30 years
C. 35 years
D. 40 years

**Answer:** C. 35 years
**Explanation:** Under Article 58 of the Constitution, a person must be at least 35 years old, an Indian citizen, and qualified to be a member of the Lok Sabha to become President of India.
**Shortcut / rule:** President/VP = 35 years; Lok Sabha/State Assembly = 25 years; Rajya Sabha/Legislative Council = 30 years; Governor = 35 years.

### Q718 · Physics

The unit of force in the SI system is:

A. Joule
B. Pascal
C. Newton
D. Watt

**Answer:** C. Newton
**Explanation:** Newton (N) is the SI unit of force, named after Sir Isaac Newton. One Newton is the force needed to accelerate a mass of 1 kg by 1 m/s². F = ma (Newton's second law).
**Shortcut / rule:** Force = Newton; Energy = Joule; Pressure = Pascal; Power = Watt; 1 N = 1 kg·m/s².

### Q719 · Chemistry

pH value of pure water is:

A. 0
B. 5
C. 7
D. 14

**Answer:** C. 7
**Explanation:** Pure water has a pH of 7, which is neutral. pH less than 7 is acidic and pH greater than 7 is basic/alkaline. pH scale ranges from 0 to 14.
**Shortcut / rule:** pH 7 = neutral (water); pH < 7 = acidic (lemon ~2, vinegar ~3, stomach acid ~1.5); pH > 7 = basic (soap ~10, bleach ~13, blood ~7.4).

### Q720 · Indian Economy

The term "Repo Rate" refers to:

A. Rate at which banks lend to public
B. Rate at which RBI lends to commercial banks
C. Rate at which banks deposit money with RBI
D. Rate of inflation in the economy

**Answer:** B. Rate at which RBI lends to commercial banks
**Explanation:** Repo Rate (Repurchase Rate) is the rate at which the RBI lends short-term money to commercial banks against securities. It is a key tool of monetary policy to control inflation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Repo = RBI lends TO banks; Reverse Repo = banks deposit WITH RBI; CRR = cash kept with RBI; SLR = liquid assets with RBI; Repo ↑ = less money supply = inflation control.

### Q721 · Biology / Life Science

The largest organ of the human body is:

A. Liver
B. Brain
C. Heart
D. Skin

**Answer:** D. Skin
**Explanation:** Skin is the largest organ of the human body, covering about 1.5-2 square metres. It has three layers: epidermis (outer), dermis (middle), and hypodermis (inner). The liver is the largest internal organ.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest organ = Skin; Largest internal organ = Liver; Largest gland = Liver; Smallest bone = Stapes (ear); Largest bone = Femur (thigh).

### Q722 · Indian Geography

The Tropic of Cancer passes through how many Indian states?

A. 6
B. 7
C. 8
D. 9

**Answer:** C. 8
**Explanation:** The Tropic of Cancer (23.5°N) passes through 8 Indian states: Gujarat, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tripura, and Mizoram.
**Shortcut / rule:** Remember: "GRiM CJW TM" — Gujarat, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tripura, Mizoram. Nagercoil (Tamil Nadu) is closest to the Equator.

### Q723 · Art, Culture & Literature

Bharatanatyam dance form originated in which state?

A. Kerala
B. Tamil Nadu
C. Andhra Pradesh
D. Karnataka

**Answer:** B. Tamil Nadu
**Explanation:** Bharatanatyam is a classical dance form that originated in Tamil Nadu. It was traditionally performed in temples (Devadasi system) and was revived by Rukmini Devi Arundale in the 20th century.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bharatanatyam = Tamil Nadu; Kathakali = Kerala; Kathak = North India (Lucknow/Jaipur); Odissi = Odisha; Manipuri = Manipur; Kuchipudi = Andhra Pradesh; Sattriya = Assam; Mohiniyattam = Kerala.

### Q724 · Games & Sports

The Olympic Games are held every:

A. 2 years
B. 3 years
C. 4 years
D. 5 years

**Answer:** C. 4 years
**Explanation:** The modern Olympic Games are held every 4 years. The first modern Olympics were held in Athens, Greece in 1896. The 2024 Olympics were held in Paris, France. The 2028 Olympics will be held in Los Angeles.
**Shortcut / rule:** Olympics = every 4 years; 2024 = Paris; 2028 = Los Angeles; 2032 = Brisbane; Winter Olympics also every 4 years but offset by 2 years from Summer.

### Q725 · Famous Personalities

Who is known as the "Missile Man of India"?

A. Vikram Sarabhai
B. Homi Bhabha
C. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
D. Satish Dhawan

**Answer:** C. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
**Explanation:** Dr. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam was known as the Missile Man of India for his work on the development of ballistic missile and launch vehicle technology (SLV-III, Agni, Prithvi). He served as the 11th President of India (2002-2007).
**Shortcut / rule:** Kalam = Missile Man + 11th President (2002-07); Vikram Sarabhai = Father of Indian Space Programme; Homi Bhabha = Father of Indian Nuclear Programme; Satish Dhawan = Father of Experimental Fluid Dynamics.

### Q726 · Space & Nuclear Program

The first Indian to go to space was:

A. Kalpana Chawla
B. Sunita Williams
C. Rakesh Sharma
D. Ravish Malhotra

**Answer:** C. Rakesh Sharma
**Explanation:** Squadron Leader Rakesh Sharma became the first Indian to travel to space on 3 April 1984 aboard the Soviet spacecraft Soyuz T-11. When asked by PM Indira Gandhi how India looked from space, he replied "Saare Jahan Se Achha."
**Shortcut / rule:** Rakesh Sharma (1984) = 1st Indian in space (Soviet mission); Kalpana Chawla (1997) = 1st Indian-origin woman in space (NASA); Sunita Williams = Indian-American astronaut.

### Q727 · UN & World Organizations

WHO stands for:

A. World Health Organization
B. World Heritage Organization
C. World Humanitarian Organization
D. World Housing Organization

**Answer:** A. World Health Organization
**Explanation:** WHO (World Health Organization) was established on 7 April 1948 with headquarters in Geneva, Switzerland. It is a specialized agency of the United Nations responsible for international public health.
**Shortcut / rule:** WHO = Geneva (1948); 7 April = World Health Day; Director-General elected for 5-year term; Current DG: Tedros Adhanom (since 2017).

### Q728 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which of the following is a renewable source of energy?

A. Coal
B. Petroleum
C. Natural gas
D. Solar energy

**Answer:** D. Solar energy
**Explanation:** Solar energy is a renewable source as it is inexhaustible and continuously available. Coal, petroleum, and natural gas are non-renewable fossil fuels that take millions of years to form and are finite.
**Shortcut / rule:** Renewable: Solar, Wind, Hydro, Biomass, Geothermal, Tidal; Non-renewable: Coal, Petroleum, Natural Gas, Nuclear (uranium); India's renewable target: 500 GW by 2030.

### Q729 · Computers & Applications

What is the brain of a computer?

A. Monitor
B. CPU
C. Hard Disk
D. RAM

**Answer:** B. CPU
**Explanation:** CPU (Central Processing Unit) is called the brain of the computer. It performs all processing operations including arithmetic, logic, control, and input/output operations. It has three main parts: ALU, CU, and Registers.
**Shortcut / rule:** CPU = brain; ALU = arithmetic & logic; CU = control unit; Types: CISC (Complex), RISC (Reduced); Intel & AMD = major CPU manufacturers.

### Q730 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

GPS stands for:

A. Global Positioning System
B. General Positioning System
C. Global Processing System
D. Geographic Positioning System

**Answer:** A. Global Positioning System
**Explanation:** GPS (Global Positioning System) is a satellite-based navigation system operated by the US Department of Defense. It uses a network of 24+ satellites to determine location. India's equivalent is NavIC (IRNSS).
**Shortcut / rule:** GPS = USA; GLONASS = Russia; Galileo = EU; BeiDou = China; NavIC (IRNSS) = India (7 satellites, regional coverage).

### Q731 · Flagship Govt Programs

Swachh Bharat Mission was launched on:

A. 15 August 2014
B. 2 October 2014
C. 26 January 2015
D. 2 October 2019

**Answer:** B. 2 October 2014
**Explanation:** Swachh Bharat Mission was launched on 2 October 2014 (Gandhi Jayanti) with the aim of making India clean and open-defecation free by 2 October 2019 (Gandhi's 150th birth anniversary). It has two components: Urban and Rural (Gramin).
**Shortcut / rule:** Swachh Bharat = 2 Oct 2014 (Gandhi Jayanti); Target: ODF by 2019; Phase 2: 2020-2025 (sustainability); Logo: spectacles of Mahatma Gandhi.

### Q732 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who gave the slogan "Inquilab Zindabad"?

A. Bhagat Singh
B. Subhash Chandra Bose
C. Hasrat Mohani
D. Bal Gangadhar Tilak

**Answer:** C. Hasrat Mohani
**Explanation:** The slogan "Inquilab Zindabad" (Long Live the Revolution) was coined by Maulana Hasrat Mohani in 1921. However, it was popularized by Bhagat Singh during the Indian freedom struggle, especially after the 1929 Assembly bombing.
**Shortcut / rule:** "Inquilab Zindabad" = coined by Hasrat Mohani (1921), popularized by Bhagat Singh; "Jai Hind" = Subhash Chandra Bose; "Swaraj is my birthright" = Tilak; "Do or Die" = Gandhi.

### Q733 · Indian Polity & Governance

How many Fundamental Rights are currently guaranteed by the Indian Constitution?

A. 5
B. 6
C. 7
D. 8

**Answer:** B. 6
**Explanation:** Originally there were 7 Fundamental Rights. After the 44th Amendment (1978) abolished the Right to Property (Article 31), there are now 6 Fundamental Rights: Right to Equality (14-18), Right to Freedom (19-22), Right against Exploitation (23-24), Right to Freedom of Religion (25-28), Cultural & Educational Rights (29-30), Right to Constitutional Remedies (32).
**Shortcut / rule:** 6 FRs: Equality, Freedom, Against Exploitation, Religion, Cultural/Educational, Constitutional Remedies; Art 32 = "Heart and Soul" of Constitution (Ambedkar); Right to Property = now legal right (Art 300A).

### Q734 · Physics

The speed of light in vacuum is approximately:

A. 3 × 10⁶ m/s
B. 3 × 10⁸ m/s
C. 3 × 10¹⁰ m/s
D. 3 × 10¹² m/s

**Answer:** B. 3 × 10⁸ m/s
**Explanation:** The speed of light in vacuum is approximately 3 × 10⁸ m/s (299,792,458 m/s exactly). It is denoted by 'c' and is the fastest speed possible in the universe according to Einstein's theory of relativity.
**Shortcut / rule:** c = 3 × 10⁸ m/s; Light year = distance light travels in 1 year (~9.46 × 10¹² km); Speed of sound in air ≈ 343 m/s (at 20°C).

### Q735 · Chemistry

The chemical formula of common salt is:

A. KCl
B. NaCl
C. CaCl₂
D. MgCl₂

**Answer:** B. NaCl
**Explanation:** Common salt (table salt) is sodium chloride (NaCl). It is an ionic compound formed by the reaction of sodium (Na) and chlorine (Cl). India's major salt-producing state is Gujarat (Rann of Kutch).
**Shortcut / rule:** NaCl = common salt; KCl = potassium chloride (sylvite); CaCl₂ = calcium chloride (deicing); NaHCO₃ = baking soda; Na₂CO₃ = washing soda; NaOH = caustic soda.

### Q736 · Indian Economy

Which of the following is NOT a direct tax?

A. Income Tax
B. Corporate Tax
C. GST
D. Capital Gains Tax

**Answer:** C. GST
**Explanation:** GST (Goods and Services Tax) is an indirect tax as it is levied on goods and services and the burden is passed to the final consumer. Income Tax, Corporate Tax, and Capital Gains Tax are direct taxes levied directly on income.
**Shortcut / rule:** Direct Tax = Income Tax, Corporate Tax, Capital Gains Tax, Wealth Tax (abolished 2016); Indirect Tax = GST, Customs Duty, Excise Duty; GST replaced multiple indirect taxes (1 July 2017).

### Q737 · Biology / Life Science

Photosynthesis takes place in which part of the plant cell?

A. Mitochondria
B. Nucleus
C. Chloroplast
D. Ribosome

**Answer:** C. Chloroplast
**Explanation:** Photosynthesis occurs in chloroplasts, which contain the green pigment chlorophyll. The process converts carbon dioxide and water into glucose and oxygen using sunlight: 6CO₂ + 6H₂O → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chloroplast = photosynthesis (food factory); Mitochondria = respiration (powerhouse); Nucleus = DNA (control centre); Ribosome = protein synthesis.

### Q738 · Indian Geography

Which state of India has the longest coastline?

A. Maharashtra
B. Tamil Nadu
C. Gujarat
D. Andhra Pradesh
