**Answer:** C. Gujarat
**Explanation:** Gujarat has the longest coastline among Indian states, measuring about 1,600 km. The total coastline of India is about 7,516 km (including islands). After Gujarat, Maharashtra has the second longest coastline.
**Shortcut / rule:** Coastline ranking: Gujarat > Maharashtra > Andhra Pradesh > Tamil Nadu > Kerala; Among UTs: Andaman & Nicobar has the longest; Total India coastline: 7,516 km.

### Q739 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Ajanta Caves are famous for:

A. Rock-cut architecture and paintings
B. Iron pillar
C. Stone inscriptions
D. Buddhist manuscripts

**Answer:** A. Rock-cut architecture and paintings
**Explanation:** The Ajanta Caves in Aurangabad, Maharashtra, are famous for their stunning Buddhist rock-cut cave architecture and mural paintings dating from the 2nd century BCE to the 6th century CE. They are a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ajanta = paintings (Buddhist, 30 caves); Ellora = sculptures (Buddhist + Hindu + Jain, 34 caves); Both in Maharashtra; Ajanta: 2nd c BCE – 6th c CE; Ellora: 6th – 11th c CE.

### Q740 · Games & Sports

Kabaddi is the national game of which country?

A. India
B. Bangladesh
C. Pakistan
D. Nepal

**Answer:** B. Bangladesh
**Explanation:** Kabaddi is the national game of Bangladesh. India does not have an officially declared national game (hockey is traditionally considered but not officially). Kabaddi originated in ancient India and is hugely popular in South Asia.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bangladesh = Kabaddi; Sri Lanka = Volleyball; Pakistan = Hockey (field); Nepal = Dandi Biyo; India = no official national game (hockey traditionally associated).

### Q741 · Famous Personalities

Rabindranath Tagore won the Nobel Prize in Literature in:

A. 1911
B. 1913
C. 1930
D. 1941

**Answer:** B. 1913
**Explanation:** Rabindranath Tagore won the Nobel Prize in Literature in 1913 for "Gitanjali" (Song Offerings). He was the first non-European and first Asian to win the Nobel Prize in Literature. He also composed the national anthems of India and Bangladesh.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tagore = Nobel 1913 (Literature, Gitanjali); C.V. Raman = 1930 (Physics); Mother Teresa = 1979 (Peace); Amartya Sen = 1998 (Economics); Kailash Satyarthi = 2014 (Peace).

### Q742 · Space & Nuclear Program

Chandrayaan-1, India's first lunar mission, was launched in:

A. 2006
B. 2008
C. 2013
D. 2019

**Answer:** B. 2008
**Explanation:** Chandrayaan-1 was launched on 22 October 2008 from Sriharikota using PSLV-C11. It discovered evidence of water molecules on the Moon's surface using its Moon Impact Probe (MIP). India became the 4th country to place its flag on the Moon.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chandrayaan-1 (2008) = found water on Moon; Chandrayaan-2 (2019) = Vikram lander crash; Chandrayaan-3 (2023) = successful soft landing (South Pole), India = 4th country to land on Moon.

### Q743 · UN & World Organizations

The International Court of Justice (ICJ) is located in:

A. New York
B. Geneva
C. The Hague
D. Brussels

**Answer:** C. The Hague
**Explanation:** The International Court of Justice (ICJ) is located in The Hague, Netherlands. Also known as the World Court, it is the principal judicial organ of the United Nations. It was established in 1945 and has 15 judges serving 9-year terms.
**Shortcut / rule:** ICJ = The Hague (15 judges, 9-year terms); ICC (International Criminal Court) = also The Hague; NATO = Brussels; EU Parliament = Strasbourg; INTERPOL = Lyon.

### Q744 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Red Data Book contains information about species that are:

A. Abundant
B. Endangered
C. Extinct
D. Invasive

**Answer:** B. Endangered
**Explanation:** The Red Data Book is maintained by IUCN (International Union for Conservation of Nature) and contains a list of endangered and threatened species. It categorizes species as Extinct, Critically Endangered, Endangered, Vulnerable, etc.
**Shortcut / rule:** IUCN Red List categories: Extinct (EX) > Extinct in Wild (EW) > Critically Endangered (CR) > Endangered (EN) > Vulnerable (VU) > Near Threatened (NT) > Least Concern (LC). IUCN HQ = Gland, Switzerland.

### Q745 · Computers & Applications

Which generation of computers used Integrated Circuits (ICs)?

A. First Generation
B. Second Generation
C. Third Generation
D. Fourth Generation

**Answer:** C. Third Generation
**Explanation:** Third generation computers (1965-1971) used Integrated Circuits (ICs), also called chips. This made computers smaller, faster, and more reliable. Jack Kilby and Robert Noyce independently invented the IC.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Gen = Vacuum tubes (1940-56); 2nd Gen = Transistors (1956-63); 3rd Gen = ICs (1964-71); 4th Gen = Microprocessors/VLSI (1971-present); 5th Gen = AI (ongoing).

### Q746 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

UNESCO stands for:

A. United Nations Educational, Social and Cultural Organization
B. United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization
C. Universal Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization
D. United Nations Economic, Social and Cultural Organization

**Answer:** B. United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization
**Explanation:** UNESCO (United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization) was established on 16 November 1945 with headquarters in Paris, France. It designates World Heritage Sites and promotes education, science, and culture globally.
**Shortcut / rule:** UNESCO = Paris (1945); World Heritage Sites in India: 42 (as of 2023); 33 cultural + 7 natural + 1 mixed; Most recent: Shantiniketan (2023).

### Q747 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Ayushman Bharat Yojana provides health insurance cover of:

A. ₹1 lakh per family per year
B. ₹3 lakh per family per year
C. ₹5 lakh per family per year
D. ₹10 lakh per family per year

**Answer:** C. ₹5 lakh per family per year
**Explanation:** Ayushman Bharat – Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (PM-JAY) was launched on 23 September 2018. It provides health insurance of ₹5 lakh per family per year for secondary and tertiary hospitalization to poor families. It is the world's largest health insurance scheme.
**Shortcut / rule:** PM-JAY = ₹5 lakh/family/year (23 Sep 2018); Covers: 10.74 crore families; Also called "Modicare"; e-Health card issued; Cashless & paperless treatment at empanelled hospitals.

### Q748 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Jallianwala Bagh massacre took place on:

A. 13 April 1919
B. 13 March 1919
C. 13 April 1920
D. 13 March 1920

**Answer:** A. 13 April 1919
**Explanation:** The Jallianwala Bagh massacre occurred on 13 April 1919 (Baisakhi day) in Amritsar, Punjab, when General Reginald Dyer ordered troops to fire on a peaceful gathering killing hundreds. This event led to the Non-Cooperation Movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** Jallianwala Bagh = 13 April 1919 (Baisakhi); General Dyer; Hunter Commission investigated; Rabindranath Tagore renounced knighthood; Udham Singh assassinated Michael O'Dwyer (1940) in revenge.

### Q749 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of Directive Principles of State Policy is borrowed from:

A. USA
B. UK
C. Ireland
D. Canada

**Answer:** C. Ireland
**Explanation:** The Directive Principles of State Policy (Part IV, Articles 36-51) are borrowed from the Irish Constitution. They are non-justiciable (not enforceable by courts) but are fundamental in the governance of the country.
**Shortcut / rule:** Borrowed features: DPSP = Ireland; Fundamental Rights = USA; Parliamentary system = UK; Federal structure = Canada; Emergency provisions = Germany; Concurrent List = Australia; Amendment procedure = South Africa.

### Q750 · Physics

Sound cannot travel through:

A. Air
B. Water
C. Steel
D. Vacuum

**Answer:** D. Vacuum
**Explanation:** Sound requires a medium (solid, liquid, or gas) to travel as it is a mechanical wave. It cannot travel through vacuum because there are no particles to vibrate. Sound travels fastest through solids and slowest through gases.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sound speed: Solid > Liquid > Gas; Sound in air ≈ 343 m/s; Sound in water ≈ 1,480 m/s; Sound in steel ≈ 5,960 m/s; Vacuum = no sound; Light can travel through vacuum.


### Q751 · Chemistry

The hardest natural substance is:

A. Iron
B. Platinum
C. Diamond
D. Quartz

**Answer:** C. Diamond
**Explanation:** Diamond is the hardest naturally occurring substance, scoring 10 on the Mohs scale of hardness. It is an allotrope of carbon. Other allotropes of carbon include graphite, fullerene, and graphene.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mohs scale: 1 (Talc) to 10 (Diamond); Diamond = hardest; Graphite = softest form of carbon (pencil lead); Both diamond & graphite = pure carbon, different crystal structures.

### Q752 · Indian Economy

The "Green Revolution" in India is associated with:

A. Verghese Kurien
B. M.S. Swaminathan
C. Sam Pitroda
D. Amartya Sen

**Answer:** B. M.S. Swaminathan
**Explanation:** Dr. M.S. Swaminathan is known as the "Father of Green Revolution in India." The Green Revolution (1960s-70s) introduced high-yielding varieties (HYV) of wheat and rice, modern irrigation, and fertilizers, making India self-sufficient in food grain production.
**Shortcut / rule:** Green Revolution = M.S. Swaminathan (India), Norman Borlaug (World); White Revolution (Milk) = Verghese Kurien (Operation Flood); Blue Revolution = Fish; Yellow Revolution = Oilseeds; Golden Revolution = Fruits/Horticulture.

### Q753 · Biology / Life Science

Which blood group is called the "Universal Donor"?

A. A
B. B
C. AB
D. O

**Answer:** D. O
**Explanation:** Blood group O negative (O−) is the universal donor because it has no A, B, or Rh antigens on red blood cells, so it can be donated to anyone without causing an immune reaction. AB positive (AB+) is the universal recipient.
**Shortcut / rule:** O = Universal Donor (no antigens); AB = Universal Recipient (no antibodies); Rh factor: discovered by Karl Landsteiner & Wiener; Blood groups discovered by Karl Landsteiner (1901, Nobel Prize 1930).

### Q754 · Indian Geography

The Chilika Lake is located in which state?

A. Kerala
B. Odisha
C. Tamil Nadu
D. West Bengal

**Answer:** B. Odisha
**Explanation:** Chilika Lake is the largest coastal lagoon in India and the second largest in the world. It is located in Odisha on the Mahanadi delta. It is a Ramsar site and home to the Irrawaddy dolphins and migratory birds from Central Asia.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chilika = Odisha (largest brackish water lagoon in India); Wular = J&K (largest freshwater lake in India); Vembanad = Kerala (longest lake); Loktak = Manipur (only floating lake, phumdis).

### Q755 · Art, Culture & Literature

"Meghadootam" was written by:

A. Valmiki
B. Kalidas
C. Vedavyasa
D. Tulsidas

**Answer:** B. Kalidas
**Explanation:** "Meghadootam" (The Cloud Messenger) is a lyric poem by the Sanskrit poet Kalidas. Other works of Kalidas include Shakuntala (Abhijnanasakuntalam), Raghuvamsha, and Kumarasambhavam. He is often called the "Shakespeare of India."
**Shortcut / rule:** Kalidas = Meghadootam, Shakuntala, Raghuvamsha, Kumarasambhavam; Valmiki = Ramayana; Vedavyasa = Mahabharata; Tulsidas = Ramcharitmanas; Banabhatta = Harshacharita, Kadambari.

### Q756 · Games & Sports

The term "Grand Slam" in tennis refers to winning:

A. 3 major tournaments
B. 4 major tournaments
C. 5 major tournaments
D. All matches in a season

**Answer:** B. 4 major tournaments
**Explanation:** A Grand Slam in tennis means winning all four major tournaments in a single calendar year: Australian Open, French Open (Roland Garros), Wimbledon, and US Open. Very few players have achieved this feat.
**Shortcut / rule:** 4 Grand Slams: Australian Open (Melbourne, hard court), French Open (Paris, clay), Wimbledon (London, grass), US Open (New York, hard court); Wimbledon = oldest (1877).

### Q757 · Famous Personalities

Who is known as the "Iron Man of India"?

A. Lal Bahadur Shastri
B. Bhagat Singh
C. Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
D. Jawaharlal Nehru

**Answer:** C. Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
**Explanation:** Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel is known as the "Iron Man of India" for his role in unifying 562 princely states into the Indian Union after independence. He was the first Deputy Prime Minister and Home Minister of India. The Statue of Unity in Gujarat (182 m) is built in his honour.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sardar Patel = Iron Man, united princely states; Statue of Unity = world's tallest (182 m), Kevadia, Gujarat; His birthday (31 Oct) = Rashtriya Ekta Diwas (National Unity Day).

### Q758 · Space & Nuclear Program

The nuclear power plant at Tarapur is located in which state?

A. Gujarat
B. Maharashtra
C. Rajasthan
D. Tamil Nadu

**Answer:** B. Maharashtra
**Explanation:** Tarapur Atomic Power Station (TAPS) in Maharashtra is India's first nuclear power plant, commissioned in 1969. It was built with US assistance. India's nuclear power plants are operated by NPCIL (Nuclear Power Corporation of India Limited).
**Shortcut / rule:** Tarapur = Maharashtra (1st, 1969); Rawatbhata = Rajasthan; Kalpakkam = Tamil Nadu (1st indigenous PHWR); Kudankulam = Tamil Nadu (Russian collaboration); Narora = Uttar Pradesh.

### Q759 · UN & World Organizations

The World Bank is headquartered in:

A. New York
B. Geneva
C. Washington DC
D. London

**Answer:** C. Washington DC
**Explanation:** The World Bank Group is headquartered in Washington DC, USA. It was established in 1944 at the Bretton Woods Conference. It provides loans and grants to developing countries for capital projects.
**Shortcut / rule:** World Bank = Washington DC (1944); IMF = Washington DC (1944); Both from Bretton Woods Conference; World Bank Group = IBRD, IDA, IFC, MIGA, ICSID; IMF's currency = SDR.

### Q760 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which day is celebrated as "World Environment Day"?

A. 22 April
B. 5 June
C. 21 March
D. 16 September

**Answer:** B. 5 June
**Explanation:** World Environment Day is celebrated on 5 June every year since 1973. It was established by the UN General Assembly in 1972 during the Stockholm Conference on Human Environment. Each year has a different theme and host country.
**Shortcut / rule:** 5 June = World Environment Day; 22 April = Earth Day; 22 March = World Water Day; 16 September = International Day for Preservation of Ozone Layer; 21 March = World Forestry Day.

### Q761 · Computers & Applications

Which of the following is an output device?

A. Keyboard
B. Mouse
C. Scanner
D. Printer

**Answer:** D. Printer
**Explanation:** A printer is an output device that produces a physical copy (hard copy) of digital information. Keyboard, mouse, and scanner are input devices that send data to the computer.
**Shortcut / rule:** Input devices: Keyboard, Mouse, Scanner, Microphone, Joystick, Webcam; Output devices: Monitor, Printer, Speaker, Projector; Both I/O: Touch screen, Modem, Headset.

### Q762 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

ATM stands for:

A. Automated Transfer Machine
B. Automatic Teller Machine
C. Any Time Money
D. Automated Transaction Machine

**Answer:** B. Automatic Teller Machine
**Explanation:** ATM (Automatic/Automated Teller Machine) is an electronic banking device that allows customers to perform financial transactions like cash withdrawal, balance inquiry, etc. without visiting a bank branch. The first ATM in India was set up by HSBC in Mumbai (1987).
**Shortcut / rule:** ATM = Automatic Teller Machine; First ATM: Barclays Bank, London (1967); First ATM in India: HSBC, Mumbai (1987); White Label ATMs = operated by non-bank entities (e.g., Tata Communications' Indicash).

### Q763 · Flagship Govt Programs

Make in India initiative was launched in:

A. 2013
B. 2014
C. 2015
D. 2016

**Answer:** B. 2014
**Explanation:** Make in India was launched on 25 September 2014 to encourage multinational and domestic companies to manufacture products in India. Its logo is a lion made of gears, symbolizing manufacturing, strength, and national pride.
**Shortcut / rule:** Make in India = 25 Sep 2014 (manufacturing); Digital India = 1 Jul 2015 (technology); Skill India = 15 Jul 2015 (employment); Start-up India = 16 Jan 2016 (entrepreneurship); Stand-up India = 5 Apr 2016 (SC/ST/Women entrepreneurs).

### Q764 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Quit India Movement was launched in:

A. 1940
B. 1942
C. 1944
D. 1946

**Answer:** B. 1942
**Explanation:** The Quit India Movement (Bharat Chhodo Andolan) was launched by Mahatma Gandhi on 8 August 1942 at the Bombay session of the All India Congress Committee. Gandhi gave the slogan "Do or Die" (Karenge ya Marenge). The movement demanded an end to British rule in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** Quit India = 8 Aug 1942, Bombay; "Do or Die" = Gandhi; Key leaders arrested: Gandhi, Nehru, Patel, Maulana Azad; Aruna Asaf Ali hoisted flag at Gowalia Tank Maidan.

### Q765 · Indian Polity & Governance

Who appoints the Chief Justice of India?

A. Prime Minister
B. President
C. Parliament
D. Law Minister

**Answer:** B. President
**Explanation:** The President of India appoints the Chief Justice of India under Article 124(2) of the Constitution. By convention, the senior-most judge of the Supreme Court is appointed as CJI. The CJI and other SC judges retire at the age of 65.
**Shortcut / rule:** CJI appointed by: President; SC judges retire: 65 years; HC judges retire: 62 years; CJI recommends SC judges (Collegium system); First CJI: H.J. Kania (1950).

### Q766 · Physics

Which colour of light has the longest wavelength?

A. Violet
B. Blue
C. Green
D. Red

**Answer:** D. Red
**Explanation:** In the visible spectrum, red light has the longest wavelength (~620-750 nm) and violet has the shortest (~380-450 nm). The order is VIBGYOR (Violet, Indigo, Blue, Green, Yellow, Orange, Red) from shortest to longest wavelength.
**Shortcut / rule:** VIBGYOR = shortest to longest wavelength; Red = longest wavelength, lowest frequency; Violet = shortest wavelength, highest frequency; Wavelength × Frequency = Speed of light (c).

### Q767 · Chemistry

The most abundant element in the Earth's crust is:

A. Iron
B. Silicon
C. Oxygen
D. Aluminium

**Answer:** C. Oxygen
**Explanation:** Oxygen (46.6%) is the most abundant element in the Earth's crust by mass, followed by Silicon (27.7%), Aluminium (8.1%), and Iron (5%). In the Earth's atmosphere, nitrogen (78%) is the most abundant gas.
**Shortcut / rule:** Crust: O > Si > Al > Fe; Atmosphere: N₂ (78%) > O₂ (21%) > Ar (0.93%); Universe: H > He; Human body: O > C > H > N; Sea water: O > H > Cl > Na.

### Q768 · Indian Economy

GST was implemented in India on:

A. 1 April 2017
B. 1 July 2017
C. 1 January 2018
D. 1 April 2018

**Answer:** B. 1 July 2017
**Explanation:** GST (Goods and Services Tax) was implemented on 1 July 2017 through the 101st Constitutional Amendment Act. It replaced multiple indirect taxes with a single unified tax. GST has a 4-tier tax structure: 5%, 12%, 18%, and 28%.
**Shortcut / rule:** GST = 1 July 2017 (101st Amendment); "One Nation, One Tax"; GST Council chaired by Union Finance Minister; Rates: 0%, 5%, 12%, 18%, 28%; GSTN = GST Network (IT backbone).

### Q769 · Biology / Life Science

Which organ produces insulin in the human body?

A. Liver
B. Kidney
C. Pancreas
D. Stomach

**Answer:** C. Pancreas
**Explanation:** Insulin is produced by the beta cells of the Islets of Langerhans in the pancreas. Insulin regulates blood sugar (glucose) levels. Deficiency or ineffective use of insulin causes diabetes mellitus.
**Shortcut / rule:** Insulin = Pancreas (beta cells); Glucagon = Pancreas (alpha cells); Bile = Liver; ADH = Pituitary; Thyroxine = Thyroid; Adrenaline = Adrenal glands. Insulin discovered by: Banting & Best (1921).

### Q770 · Indian Geography

Which river is known as the "Sorrow of Bihar"?

A. Ganga
B. Son
C. Kosi
D. Gandak

**Answer:** C. Kosi
**Explanation:** The Kosi River is called the "Sorrow of Bihar" because of its frequent and devastating floods. It shifts its course frequently, causing widespread destruction. The Damodar River is called the "Sorrow of Bengal."
**Shortcut / rule:** Kosi = Sorrow of Bihar; Damodar = Sorrow of Bengal; Mahanadi = Sorrow of Odisha (historically); Brahmaputra = Sorrow of Assam; Rivers named "sorrow" due to frequent flooding.

### Q771 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Khajuraho temples were built by which dynasty?

A. Chola
B. Pallava
C. Chandela
D. Chalukya

**Answer:** C. Chandela
**Explanation:** The Khajuraho temples in Madhya Pradesh were built by the Chandela dynasty between 950-1050 CE. They are famous for their nagara-style architecture and erotic sculptures. Originally 85 temples were built, of which 25 survive today. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Khajuraho = Chandela dynasty (MP); Konark = Eastern Ganga (Odisha); Brihadeeswarar = Chola (Tamil Nadu); Kailasanatha (Ellora) = Rashtrakuta; Dilwara = Solanki (Rajasthan, Jain temples).

### Q772 · Games & Sports

Which country has won the most FIFA World Cup titles?

A. Germany
B. Argentina
C. Brazil
D. Italy

**Answer:** C. Brazil
**Explanation:** Brazil has won the most FIFA World Cup titles — 5 times (1958, 1962, 1970, 1994, 2002). Germany and Italy have won 4 times each. Argentina has won 3 times (1978, 1986, 2022). The FIFA World Cup is held every 4 years.
**Shortcut / rule:** Brazil = 5 titles (most); Germany = 4; Italy = 4; Argentina = 3 (latest 2022); France = 2; England, Spain, Uruguay = 1 each; 2022 final = Argentina vs France (Qatar).

### Q773 · Famous Personalities

Who wrote "Discovery of India"?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Jawaharlal Nehru
C. Rabindranath Tagore
D. Subhash Chandra Bose

**Answer:** B. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Explanation:** "The Discovery of India" was written by Jawaharlal Nehru during his imprisonment at Ahmednagar Fort in 1944. Other books by Nehru include "Glimpses of World History" and "An Autobiography" (Toward Freedom).
**Shortcut / rule:** Nehru: Discovery of India, Glimpses of World History, An Autobiography; Gandhi: My Experiments with Truth, Hind Swaraj; Ambedkar: Annihilation of Caste; Bose: The Indian Struggle.

### Q774 · Space & Nuclear Program

ISRO's Geosynchronous Satellite Launch Vehicle is called:

A. PSLV
B. GSLV
C. ASLV
D. SLV

**Answer:** B. GSLV
**Explanation:** GSLV (Geosynchronous Satellite Launch Vehicle) is ISRO's launch vehicle designed to place satellites in Geosynchronous Transfer Orbit (GTO). The latest version GSLV Mk III (renamed LVM3) uses an indigenous cryogenic upper stage.
**Shortcut / rule:** SLV-3 (1980) → ASLV → PSLV (workhorse, polar orbit) → GSLV (GTO) → GSLV Mk III/LVM3 (heaviest, 4-tonne to GTO); PSLV used for Chandrayaan-1 & Mars Orbiter; LVM3 for Chandrayaan-3.

### Q775 · UN & World Organizations

The Universal Declaration of Human Rights was adopted in:

A. 1945
B. 1948
C. 1950
D. 1955

**Answer:** B. 1948
**Explanation:** The Universal Declaration of Human Rights (UDHR) was adopted by the UN General Assembly on 10 December 1948 in Paris. It has 30 articles outlining fundamental human rights. 10 December is celebrated as Human Rights Day.
**Shortcut / rule:** UDHR = 10 Dec 1948 (30 articles); 10 Dec = Human Rights Day; Drafted by committee chaired by Eleanor Roosevelt; Part III of Indian Constitution (Fundamental Rights) influenced by UDHR.

### Q776 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Jim Corbett National Park is located in which state?

A. Madhya Pradesh
B. Uttarakhand
C. Rajasthan
D. Karnataka

**Answer:** B. Uttarakhand
**Explanation:** Jim Corbett National Park in Uttarakhand (Nainital district) is India's oldest national park, established in 1936 as Hailey National Park. It was the first park to come under Project Tiger (1973). It is named after the hunter-turned-conservationist Jim Corbett.
**Shortcut / rule:** Corbett = Uttarakhand (1st NP, 1936, 1st Project Tiger 1973); Kanha = MP (Mowgli's land, tiger); Kaziranga = Assam (one-horned rhino); Gir = Gujarat (Asiatic lion); Ranthambore = Rajasthan (tiger).

### Q777 · Computers & Applications

What is the full form of HTML?

A. Hyper Text Markup Language
B. High Text Markup Language
C. Hyper Transfer Markup Language
D. Hyper Text Machine Language

**Answer:** A. Hyper Text Markup Language
**Explanation:** HTML (Hyper Text Markup Language) is the standard markup language used for creating web pages and web applications. It was created by Tim Berners-Lee in 1991. HTML uses tags to structure content on the web.
**Shortcut / rule:** HTML = web pages; CSS = styling; JavaScript = interactivity; Tim Berners-Lee = invented WWW (1989) & HTML; URL = Uniform Resource Locator; HTTP = Hyper Text Transfer Protocol.

### Q778 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The first railway line in India was established between:

A. Delhi and Agra
B. Bombay and Thane
C. Calcutta and Delhi
D. Madras and Bangalore

**Answer:** B. Bombay and Thane
**Explanation:** The first railway line in India was opened on 16 April 1853 between Bombay (Bori Bunder) and Thane, covering a distance of 34 km. It was operated by the Great Indian Peninsula Railway (GIPR) with 3 locomotives: Sahib, Sindh, and Sultan.
**Shortcut / rule:** First train: 16 Apr 1853, Bombay-Thane (34 km); 3 locos: Sahib, Sindh, Sultan; Indian Railways = largest employer (Asia); Railway zones: 18; Railway Board established: 1905.

### Q779 · Flagship Govt Programs

Under which scheme did the Indian government aim to provide electricity to all households?

A. Ujjwala Yojana
B. Saubhagya Yojana
C. Kusum Yojana
D. Deen Dayal Upadhyaya Gram Jyoti Yojana

**Answer:** B. Saubhagya Yojana
**Explanation:** Pradhan Mantri Sahaj Bijli Har Ghar Yojana (Saubhagya) was launched on 25 September 2017 to achieve universal household electrification. It aimed to provide electricity connections to all willing households in rural and urban areas.
**Shortcut / rule:** Saubhagya = electricity to all homes (2017); Ujjwala = free LPG to BPL women (2016); KUSUM = solar for farmers (2019); DDUGJY = rural electrification infrastructure (2015).

### Q780 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Simon Commission came to India in:

A. 1925
B. 1927
C. 1928
D. 1930

**Answer:** C. 1928
**Explanation:** The Simon Commission arrived in India on 3 February 1928. It was boycotted because it had no Indian member — all 7 members were British. The slogan "Simon Go Back" was raised. During protests in Lahore, Lala Lajpat Rai was injured by police lathi-charge and later died.
**Shortcut / rule:** Simon Commission = 1928 (all British, no Indian member); "Simon Go Back"; Lala Lajpat Rai died from injuries; Led to Nehru Report (1928) which demanded Dominion Status.

### Q781 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Speaker of the Lok Sabha is elected by:

A. The President
B. Members of Lok Sabha
C. Members of both Houses
D. The Prime Minister

**Answer:** B. Members of Lok Sabha
**Explanation:** The Speaker of the Lok Sabha is elected from among the members of the Lok Sabha. The Speaker presides over sessions, maintains order, and has a casting vote in case of a tie. The Speaker does not vote in the first instance.
**Shortcut / rule:** Speaker = elected by Lok Sabha members; Chairman of Rajya Sabha = Vice President (ex-officio); Speaker can be removed by a resolution passed by effective majority of Lok Sabha; First Speaker: G.V. Mavalankar (1952).

### Q782 · Physics

The device used to measure atmospheric pressure is:

A. Thermometer
B. Barometer
C. Hygrometer
D. Ammeter

**Answer:** B. Barometer
**Explanation:** A barometer is used to measure atmospheric pressure. The mercury barometer was invented by Evangelista Torricelli in 1643. Normal atmospheric pressure at sea level is 760 mm of Hg (1 atm = 101.325 kPa).
**Shortcut / rule:** Barometer = atmospheric pressure (Torricelli); Thermometer = temperature; Hygrometer = humidity; Ammeter = electric current; Voltmeter = voltage; Seismograph = earthquakes; Anemometer = wind speed.

### Q783 · Chemistry

Which acid is found in lemon?

A. Acetic acid
B. Citric acid
C. Hydrochloric acid
D. Oxalic acid

**Answer:** B. Citric acid
**Explanation:** Citric acid (C₆H₈O₇) is found in citrus fruits like lemon, orange, and grapefruit. Acetic acid is found in vinegar, formic acid in ant stings, lactic acid in sour milk, and oxalic acid in tomatoes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lemon/Orange = Citric acid; Vinegar = Acetic acid; Ant/Bee sting = Formic acid; Sour milk/Curd = Lactic acid; Tomato = Oxalic acid; Tamarind = Tartaric acid; Apple = Malic acid; Stomach = HCl.

### Q784 · Indian Economy

NABARD stands for:

A. National Bank for Agriculture and Regional Development
B. National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development
C. National Board for Agriculture and Rural Development
D. National Bureau for Agricultural and Rural Development

**Answer:** B. National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development
**Explanation:** NABARD was established on 12 July 1982 based on the recommendations of the Shivaraman Committee (CRAFICARD). It is headquartered in Mumbai and is the apex development bank for agriculture and rural development in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** NABARD = Mumbai (1982, Shivaraman Committee); RBI = Mumbai (1935, Hilton Young); SEBI = Mumbai (1992); SIDBI = Lucknow (1990); EXIM Bank = Mumbai (1982).

### Q785 · Biology / Life Science

The powerhouse of the cell is:

A. Nucleus
B. Ribosome
C. Mitochondria
D. Golgi body

**Answer:** C. Mitochondria
**Explanation:** Mitochondria are called the "powerhouse of the cell" because they produce ATP (adenosine triphosphate) through cellular respiration. They have their own DNA (maternal inheritance). They have a double membrane structure.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mitochondria = powerhouse (ATP); Nucleus = control centre (DNA); Ribosome = protein factory; Golgi body = packaging & secretion; ER = transport; Lysosome = suicide bags; Chloroplast = kitchen of plant cell.

### Q786 · Indian Geography

The highest peak in India is:

A. Nanda Devi
B. Kanchenjunga
C. K2 (Godwin-Austen)
D. Mount Everest

**Answer:** C. K2 (Godwin-Austen)
**Explanation:** K2 (Mount Godwin-Austen) at 8,611 m in the Karakoram Range (PoK) is the highest peak in India (and 2nd highest in the world). Kanchenjunga (8,586 m) is the highest peak within India's administered territory. Nanda Devi (7,816 m) is the highest peak entirely within India.
**Shortcut / rule:** K2 = highest in India (8,611 m, PoK, disputed); Kanchenjunga = highest in administered India (8,586 m, Sikkim border); Nanda Devi = highest entirely within India (7,816 m, Uttarakhand).

### Q787 · Art, Culture & Literature

The classical dance form "Kathakali" belongs to:

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Kerala
C. Karnataka
D. Andhra Pradesh

**Answer:** B. Kerala
**Explanation:** Kathakali is a classical dance-drama form from Kerala, known for its elaborate costumes, facial make-up (green face = noble characters), and hand gestures. Stories are mainly from the Ramayana and Mahabharata.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kerala dances: Kathakali (dance-drama, male) + Mohiniyattam (female); Green face (Pacha) = good; Red beard (Chuvanna Thaadi) = evil; Themes: epics; Musical instruments: Chenda, Maddalam.

### Q788 · Games & Sports

Who was the first Indian to win an individual Olympic gold medal?

A. Leander Paes
B. Abhinav Bindra
C. Sushil Kumar
D. Neeraj Chopra

**Answer:** B. Abhinav Bindra
**Explanation:** Abhinav Bindra won India's first individual Olympic gold medal in the 10m Air Rifle event at the 2008 Beijing Olympics. Before this, India had won gold medals only in hockey (team event). Neeraj Chopra won gold in Javelin at Tokyo 2020.
**Shortcut / rule:** Abhinav Bindra = 1st individual gold (Shooting, Beijing 2008); Neeraj Chopra = gold in Javelin (Tokyo 2020); K.D. Jadhav = 1st individual medal (bronze, Wrestling, Helsinki 1952).

### Q789 · Famous Personalities

Who is the author of "Wings of Fire"?

A. Vikram Seth
B. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
C. R.K. Narayan
D. Chetan Bhagat

**Answer:** B. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam
**Explanation:** "Wings of Fire" is the autobiography of Dr. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam, written with Arun Tiwari. It was published in 1999 and covers his life from childhood to his role in India's missile and space programmes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kalam: Wings of Fire (autobiography), India 2020, Ignited Minds; Gandhi: My Experiments with Truth; Nehru: Discovery of India; Ambedkar: Annihilation of Caste; Tagore: Gitanjali.

### Q790 · Space & Nuclear Program

India's first Mars mission (Mangalyaan) was launched in:

A. 2011
B. 2013
C. 2015
D. 2017

**Answer:** B. 2013
**Explanation:** Mars Orbiter Mission (Mangalyaan) was launched on 5 November 2013 and entered Mars orbit on 24 September 2014. India became the first Asian country and the first nation to succeed in its first attempt to reach Mars orbit. The mission cost only ₹450 crore (~$74 million).
**Shortcut / rule:** MOM/Mangalyaan = 5 Nov 2013 (launch), 24 Sep 2014 (Mars orbit); ISRO = 1st attempt success; Cost: ₹450 crore; Launched by PSLV-C25; India = 4th space agency to reach Mars (after NASA, Roscosmos, ESA).

### Q791 · UN & World Organizations

FAO is a specialized agency of the UN that deals with:

A. Aviation
B. Labour
C. Food and Agriculture
D. Education

**Answer:** C. Food and Agriculture
**Explanation:** FAO (Food and Agriculture Organization) is a specialized agency of the UN that leads international efforts to defeat hunger and improve nutrition. It was established on 16 October 1945, with headquarters in Rome, Italy.
**Shortcut / rule:** FAO = Rome (Food & Agriculture, 16 Oct = World Food Day); ILO = Geneva (Labour); ICAO = Montreal (Aviation); UNESCO = Paris (Education/Culture); WTO = Geneva (Trade); WIPO = Geneva (Intellectual Property).

### Q792 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Kyoto Protocol is related to:

A. Nuclear disarmament
B. Biodiversity conservation
C. Climate change and greenhouse gas reduction
D. Ozone layer protection

**Answer:** C. Climate change and greenhouse gas reduction
**Explanation:** The Kyoto Protocol was adopted in 1997 in Kyoto, Japan, and came into force in 2005. It committed industrialized countries to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. The USA did not ratify it. It was succeeded by the Paris Agreement (2015).
**Shortcut / rule:** Kyoto Protocol (1997) = greenhouse gas reduction; Montreal Protocol (1987) = ozone layer (CFC ban); Paris Agreement (2015) = limit warming to 1.5-2°C; Ramsar Convention (1971) = wetlands; CITES = endangered species trade.

### Q793 · Computers & Applications

Which of the following is an operating system?

A. Microsoft Word
B. Google Chrome
C. Linux
D. Adobe Photoshop

**Answer:** C. Linux
**Explanation:** Linux is an open-source operating system kernel created by Linus Torvalds in 1991. Other operating systems include Windows (Microsoft), macOS (Apple), Android (Google, based on Linux), and iOS (Apple). MS Word is word processing software, Chrome is a web browser, and Photoshop is image editing software.
**Shortcut / rule:** OS: Windows, macOS, Linux, Android, iOS; Linux = open source (Linus Torvalds, 1991); Android = based on Linux; Application software: Word, Excel, Chrome, Photoshop; System software: OS, compiler, assembler.

### Q794 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

BHIM app stands for:

A. Banking Hub for Indian Money
B. Bharat Interface for Money
C. Banking Highway for Indian Markets
D. Bharat Integrated Mobile

**Answer:** B. Bharat Interface for Money
**Explanation:** BHIM (Bharat Interface for Money) is a mobile payment app developed by NPCI based on the UPI platform. It was launched on 30 December 2016 and named after Dr. B.R. Ambedkar. It enables instant bank-to-bank transactions.
**Shortcut / rule:** BHIM = Bharat Interface for Money (NPCI, UPI-based, 2016); UPI = Unified Payments Interface; NPCI = National Payments Corporation of India; IMPS = Immediate Payment Service; NEFT = National Electronic Funds Transfer.

### Q795 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Beti Bachao Beti Padhao scheme was launched in:

A. 2014
B. 2015
C. 2016
D. 2017

**Answer:** B. 2015
**Explanation:** Beti Bachao Beti Padhao (Save Girl Child, Educate Girl Child) was launched on 22 January 2015 from Panipat, Haryana. It aims to address the declining Child Sex Ratio, ensure survival, protection, and education of the girl child.
**Shortcut / rule:** Beti Bachao Beti Padhao = 22 Jan 2015 (Panipat, Haryana); Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana = part of BBBP (savings for girl child); Ministries involved: WCD, Health, HRD (Education).

### Q796 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Indian National Army (INA) was founded by:

A. Subhash Chandra Bose
B. Rashbehari Bose
C. Mohan Singh
D. Shah Nawaz Khan

**Answer:** C. Mohan Singh
**Explanation:** The Indian National Army (Azad Hind Fauj) was first formed by Captain Mohan Singh in 1942 with Indian prisoners of war in Southeast Asia. Rashbehari Bose handed over its leadership to Subhash Chandra Bose in 1943, who reorganized and expanded it significantly.
**Shortcut / rule:** INA 1st formation: Captain Mohan Singh (1942); Reorganized by: Subhash Chandra Bose (1943); "Delhi Chalo" & "Jai Hind" = Bose's slogans; Azad Hind Government = formed by Bose in Singapore (1943).

### Q797 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Indian Constitution came into effect on:

A. 15 August 1947
B. 26 November 1949
C. 26 January 1950
D. 15 August 1950

**Answer:** C. 26 January 1950
**Explanation:** The Indian Constitution came into effect on 26 January 1950, celebrated as Republic Day. It was adopted on 26 November 1949 (celebrated as Constitution Day/Samvidhan Divas since 2015). India became independent on 15 August 1947.
**Shortcut / rule:** 15 Aug 1947 = Independence Day; 26 Nov 1949 = Constitution adopted (Constitution Day); 26 Jan 1950 = Constitution effective (Republic Day); 26 Jan chosen to commemorate 1930 Purna Swaraj declaration.

### Q798 · Physics

The phenomenon of bending of light when it passes from one medium to another is called:

A. Reflection
B. Refraction
C. Diffraction
D. Polarization

**Answer:** B. Refraction
**Explanation:** Refraction is the bending of light when it passes from one medium to another of different density (e.g., from air to water). It occurs due to the change in speed of light in different media. Snell's Law governs refraction: n₁sinθ₁ = n₂sinθ₂.
**Shortcut / rule:** Refraction = bending (medium change); Reflection = bouncing back; Diffraction = bending around obstacles; Dispersion = splitting (prism/rainbow); Examples of refraction: pencil appears bent in water, mirage, twinkling of stars.

### Q799 · Chemistry

Rust is chemically:

A. Ferrous oxide
B. Ferric oxide hydrated
C. Ferrous sulphate
D. Iron carbonate

**Answer:** B. Ferric oxide hydrated
**Explanation:** Rust is hydrated ferric oxide (Fe₂O₃·xH₂O), formed when iron reacts with oxygen and water (moisture). It is a reddish-brown flaky substance. Rusting can be prevented by galvanization, painting, or alloying (stainless steel).
**Shortcut / rule:** Rust = Fe₂O₃·xH₂O (hydrated iron oxide); Needs: iron + oxygen + moisture; Prevention: galvanizing (zinc coating), painting, oiling, alloying; Stainless steel = iron + chromium + nickel (corrosion resistant).

### Q800 · Indian Economy

The National Income of India is computed by:

A. RBI
B. NITI Aayog
C. Central Statistical Office (CSO)
D. Finance Commission

**Answer:** C. Central Statistical Office (CSO)
**Explanation:** The National Income of India is computed by the Central Statistical Office (CSO), now renamed as the National Statistical Office (NSO) under the Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation. It calculates GDP, GNP, NNP, and per capita income.
**Shortcut / rule:** NSO (formerly CSO) = National Income, GDP; RBI = monetary policy, currency; NITI Aayog = policy think tank (replaced Planning Commission, 2015); Finance Commission = tax distribution (federal).


### Q801 · Biology / Life Science

Which disease is caused by the deficiency of Vitamin D?

A. Scurvy
B. Rickets
C. Beriberi
D. Pellagra

**Answer:** B. Rickets
**Explanation:** Vitamin D deficiency causes Rickets in children (soft and weak bones) and Osteomalacia in adults. Vitamin D is synthesized in the skin when exposed to sunlight and helps in absorption of calcium and phosphorus.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vitamin D = Rickets/Osteomalacia (Sunshine vitamin); A = Night blindness; B1 = Beriberi; B3 = Pellagra; B12 = Anaemia; C = Scurvy; E = Sterility; K = Bleeding.

### Q802 · Indian Geography

The Thar Desert is located in which Indian state?

A. Gujarat
B. Rajasthan
C. Madhya Pradesh
D. Haryana

**Answer:** B. Rajasthan
**Explanation:** The Thar Desert (Great Indian Desert) is located mainly in Rajasthan and extends into Gujarat, Punjab, and Haryana. It covers about 200,000 sq km and is bordered by the Aravalli Range on the east. The Indira Gandhi Canal brings water from the Sutlej and Beas rivers to this region.
**Shortcut / rule:** Thar = Rajasthan (Great Indian Desert); Rann of Kutch = Gujarat (salt marsh); Aravalli = oldest fold mountains in India; Luni = only major river in Thar; Sam Sand Dunes = Jaisalmer (tourist spot).

### Q803 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Sangam literature belongs to which language?

A. Sanskrit
B. Tamil
C. Kannada
D. Telugu

**Answer:** B. Tamil
**Explanation:** Sangam literature is a body of classical Tamil literature created between the 3rd century BCE and 3rd century CE. The Sangam was an academy of Tamil poets held in Madurai under the patronage of Pandya kings. It includes Ettuthogai (Eight Anthologies) and Pattuppattu (Ten Idylls).
**Shortcut / rule:** Sangam = Tamil (Madurai, Pandya kings); 3 Sangams: 1st at South Madurai, 2nd at Kapatapuram, 3rd at Madurai; Tolkappiyam = oldest Tamil grammar; Tirukkural = by Thiruvalluvar (ethical text).

### Q804 · Games & Sports

The Dronacharya Award is given for excellence in:

A. Sports performance
B. Sports coaching
C. Lifetime achievement in sports
D. Sports journalism

**Answer:** B. Sports coaching
**Explanation:** The Dronacharya Award is given to outstanding coaches who have trained sportspersons who have achieved medals in international competitions. It is named after Dronacharya, the royal guru of Pandavas and Kauravas in the Mahabharata.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dronacharya = coaching; Arjuna Award = outstanding sports performance; Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna (now Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna) = highest sporting honour; Dhyan Chand Award = lifetime achievement in sports.

### Q805 · Famous Personalities

Who is known as the "Nightingale of India"?

A. Lata Mangeshkar
B. Sarojini Naidu
C. M.S. Subbulakshmi
D. Asha Bhosle

**Answer:** B. Sarojini Naidu
**Explanation:** Sarojini Naidu was called the "Nightingale of India" (Bharat Kokila) for her beautiful poetry. She was the first Indian woman to become the President of the Indian National Congress (1925) and the first woman Governor of an Indian state (United Provinces, 1947).
**Shortcut / rule:** Sarojini Naidu = Nightingale of India + 1st woman Congress President (1925) + 1st woman Governor (UP); Her poems: "The Golden Threshold", "The Bird of Time"; Born: 13 Feb (observed as National Women's Day).

### Q806 · Space & Nuclear Program

The Pokhran nuclear tests (Pokhran-II) were conducted in:

A. 1974
B. 1988
C. 1998
D. 2004

**Answer:** C. 1998
**Explanation:** India conducted five nuclear tests at Pokhran, Rajasthan on 11 and 13 May 1998 (Operation Shakti/Pokhran-II) under PM Atal Bihari Vajpayee. The first nuclear test (Pokhran-I/Smiling Buddha) was conducted on 18 May 1974 under PM Indira Gandhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** Pokhran-I = 18 May 1974 (Smiling Buddha, Indira Gandhi); Pokhran-II = 11 & 13 May 1998 (Operation Shakti, Vajpayee); 11 May = National Technology Day; Lead scientist Pokhran-II: A.P.J. Abdul Kalam & R. Chidambaram.

### Q807 · UN & World Organizations

The International Monetary Fund (IMF) was established in:

A. 1944
B. 1945
C. 1950
D. 1960

**Answer:** B. 1945
**Explanation:** The IMF was conceived at the Bretton Woods Conference in July 1944 and formally established on 27 December 1945 when 29 countries signed its Articles of Agreement. It is headquartered in Washington DC and aims to ensure global monetary stability.
**Shortcut / rule:** IMF = Washington DC (1945, Bretton Woods); 190 member countries; Currency: SDR (Special Drawing Rights); MD elected for 5-year term; India = founding member; Functions: balance of payments support, exchange rate stability.

### Q808 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The "Chipko Movement" is associated with:

A. Conservation of forests
B. Water conservation
C. Air pollution control
D. Wildlife protection

**Answer:** A. Conservation of forests
**Explanation:** The Chipko Movement (1973) was a forest conservation movement in Uttarakhand (then Uttar Pradesh). Villagers, especially women led by Gaura Devi, hugged trees to prevent them from being cut down. Sunderlal Bahuguna and Chandi Prasad Bhatt were key leaders.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chipko = tree-hugging (1973, Uttarakhand); Sunderlal Bahuguna (key propagator); Appiko Movement = Karnataka (similar, 1983); Narmada Bachao Andolan = dam opposition (Medha Patkar); Bishnoi community = earliest conservationists (Rajasthan).

### Q809 · Computers & Applications

1 Kilobyte (KB) is equal to:

A. 1000 bytes
B. 1024 bytes
C. 512 bytes
D. 2048 bytes

**Answer:** B. 1024 bytes
**Explanation:** In computing, 1 Kilobyte (KB) = 1024 bytes (2¹⁰). The sequence is: Byte → Kilobyte (KB) → Megabyte (MB) → Gigabyte (GB) → Terabyte (TB) → Petabyte (PB), each being 1024 times the previous unit.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1 KB = 1024 B; 1 MB = 1024 KB; 1 GB = 1024 MB; 1 TB = 1024 GB; 1 bit = smallest unit (0 or 1); 1 byte = 8 bits; 1 nibble = 4 bits.

### Q810 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

NATO stands for:

A. North American Treaty Organization
B. North Atlantic Treaty Organization
C. National Allied Treaty Organization
D. Northern Atlantic Territorial Organization

**Answer:** B. North Atlantic Treaty Organization
**Explanation:** NATO (North Atlantic Treaty Organization) is a military alliance established on 4 April 1949 by the Washington Treaty. Its headquarters is in Brussels, Belgium. It currently has 32 member countries. India is not a member of NATO.
**Shortcut / rule:** NATO = Brussels (1949); 32 members (Finland 2023, Sweden 2024); Article 5 = collective defense; QUAD = India, USA, Japan, Australia; BRICS = Brazil, Russia, India, China, South Africa + expanded.

### Q811 · Flagship Govt Programs

The MUDRA Yojana provides loans up to:

A. ₹5 lakh
B. ₹10 lakh
C. ₹15 lakh
D. ₹20 lakh

**Answer:** B. ₹10 lakh
**Explanation:** Pradhan Mantri MUDRA Yojana (PMMY) was launched on 8 April 2015 to provide loans up to ₹10 lakh to non-corporate, non-farm small and micro enterprises. MUDRA = Micro Units Development and Refinance Agency.
**Shortcut / rule:** MUDRA categories: Shishu (up to ₹50,000), Kishore (₹50,001-₹5 lakh), Tarun (₹5,00,001-₹10 lakh); No collateral required; Target: micro enterprises, street vendors, small businesses.

### Q812 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who presided over the Lahore Session of Congress (1929) where "Purna Swaraj" was declared?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Subhash Chandra Bose
C. Jawaharlal Nehru
D. Motilal Nehru

**Answer:** C. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Explanation:** Jawaharlal Nehru presided over the Lahore Session of the Indian National Congress in December 1929, where the demand for "Purna Swaraj" (Complete Independence) was adopted. The Indian flag was unfurled on the banks of the Ravi River on 31 December 1929. 26 January 1930 was celebrated as the first Independence Day.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lahore 1929 = Nehru (Purna Swaraj); Lucknow 1916 = Ambika Charan Majumdar (Congress-League Pact); Calcutta 1920 = Lala Lajpat Rai (special session, NCM); Karachi 1931 = Sardar Patel (Fundamental Rights resolution).

### Q813 · Indian Polity & Governance

The 42nd Amendment to the Indian Constitution is known as:

A. Mini Constitution
B. First Amendment
C. Right to Education Amendment
D. Anti-Defection Amendment

**Answer:** A. Mini Constitution
**Explanation:** The 42nd Amendment Act (1976), passed during the Emergency, is called the "Mini Constitution" because it made the most extensive changes: added "Socialist", "Secular" and "Integrity" to the Preamble, added Fundamental Duties (Part IV-A), and curtailed judicial review.
**Shortcut / rule:** 42nd (1976) = Mini Constitution (Emergency, Indira Gandhi); 44th (1978) = undid many 42nd changes; 73rd (1992) = Panchayats; 74th (1992) = Municipalities; 86th (2002) = Right to Education (Art 21A); 101st (2016) = GST.

### Q814 · Physics

Which mirror is used in the headlights of vehicles?

A. Plane mirror
B. Convex mirror
C. Concave mirror
D. Cylindrical mirror

**Answer:** C. Concave mirror
**Explanation:** Concave mirrors are used in vehicle headlights because they produce powerful parallel beams of light when the light source is placed at the focus. They converge light rays. Convex mirrors are used as rear-view mirrors because they give a wider field of view.
**Shortcut / rule:** Concave mirror: headlights, solar furnace, shaving mirror, dentist mirror (converging); Convex mirror: rear-view mirror, street lights, ATM security (diverging, wider view); Plane mirror: looking glass, periscope.

### Q815 · Chemistry

The gas used in fire extinguishers is:

A. Oxygen
B. Hydrogen
C. Carbon dioxide
D. Nitrogen

**Answer:** C. Carbon dioxide
**Explanation:** Carbon dioxide (CO₂) is used in fire extinguishers because it is heavier than air, does not support combustion, and blankets the fire by cutting off the oxygen supply. It is also called "dry ice" in solid form.
**Shortcut / rule:** CO₂ = fire extinguisher (heavier than air, non-combustible); Dry ice = solid CO₂ (−78.5°C); CO₂ is a greenhouse gas; Used in soda water (carbonation); Plants absorb CO₂ in photosynthesis.

### Q816 · Indian Economy

Which Five Year Plan in India focused on rapid industrialization?

A. First Five Year Plan
B. Second Five Year Plan
C. Third Five Year Plan
D. Fourth Five Year Plan

**Answer:** B. Second Five Year Plan
**Explanation:** The Second Five Year Plan (1956-61), also called the Mahalanobis Plan, focused on rapid industrialization and development of heavy industries. The First Plan (1951-56) focused on agriculture. India had 13 Five Year Plans before NITI Aayog replaced the Planning Commission.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Plan (1951-56) = Agriculture (Harrod-Domar Model); 2nd Plan (1956-61) = Industrialization (Mahalanobis Model); 12th Plan = last (2012-17); NITI Aayog replaced Planning Commission on 1 Jan 2015.

### Q817 · Biology / Life Science

The normal human body temperature is:

A. 36°C
B. 37°C (98.6°F)
C. 38°C
D. 39°C

**Answer:** B. 37°C (98.6°F)
**Explanation:** The normal human body temperature is approximately 37°C or 98.6°F. It is regulated by the hypothalamus in the brain. Body temperature above 38°C (100.4°F) indicates fever.
**Shortcut / rule:** Normal body temp = 37°C / 98.6°F; Hypothalamus = body thermostat; Conversion: °F = (9/5 × °C) + 32; °C = 5/9 × (°F − 32); 0°C = 32°F (water freezes); 100°C = 212°F (water boils).

### Q818 · Indian Geography

The longest dam in India is:

A. Bhakra Nangal Dam
B. Hirakud Dam
C. Tehri Dam
D. Nagarjuna Sagar Dam

**Answer:** B. Hirakud Dam
**Explanation:** Hirakud Dam on the Mahanadi River in Odisha is the longest dam in India (about 25.8 km including dykes). It was one of the first major multipurpose river valley projects in India. The tallest dam in India is Tehri Dam (260.5 m) on the Bhagirathi River in Uttarakhand.
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest dam: Hirakud (Mahanadi, Odisha, 25.8 km); Tallest dam: Tehri (Bhagirathi, Uttarakhand, 260.5 m); Highest: Bhakra Nangal (Sutlej, HP/Punjab, 225.5 m, gravity dam); Nagarjuna Sagar = Krishna, Telangana.

### Q819 · Art, Culture & Literature

Who wrote the national song "Vande Mataram"?

A. Rabindranath Tagore
B. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay
C. Muhammad Iqbal
D. Subramania Bharati

**Answer:** B. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay
**Explanation:** "Vande Mataram" was written by Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay in his novel "Anandamath" (1882). It was first sung at the 1896 session of the Indian National Congress by Rabindranath Tagore. It was adopted as the National Song on 24 January 1950.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vande Mataram = Bankim Chandra (Anandamath, 1882); National Song adopted: 24 Jan 1950; National Anthem "Jana Gana Mana" = Rabindranath Tagore (1911, adopted 24 Jan 1950); Both adopted on the same day.

### Q820 · Games & Sports

The Ranji Trophy is associated with which sport?

A. Hockey
B. Football
C. Cricket
D. Tennis

**Answer:** C. Cricket
**Explanation:** The Ranji Trophy is India's premier first-class cricket tournament, named after Ranjitsinhji (Maharaja of Nawanagar). It was started in 1934-35. Mumbai has won the most titles. BCCI organizes this tournament.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ranji Trophy = Cricket (1st class, since 1934); Duleep Trophy = Cricket (zonal); Irani Cup = Ranji champion vs Rest of India; Santosh Trophy = Football; Beighton Cup = Hockey; Davis Cup = Tennis (men's team).

### Q821 · Famous Personalities

Bhagat Singh, Rajguru, and Sukhdev were executed on:

A. 23 March 1929
B. 23 March 1931
C. 14 February 1931
D. 30 January 1931

**Answer:** B. 23 March 1931
**Explanation:** Bhagat Singh, Rajguru, and Sukhdev were hanged on 23 March 1931 in Lahore jail for the murder of British police officer J.P. Saunders (mistaken for Superintendent James Scott). 23 March is observed as Shaheed Diwas (Martyrs' Day).
**Shortcut / rule:** 23 March 1931 = Bhagat Singh, Rajguru, Sukhdev executed (Shaheed Diwas); They killed Saunders (1928) to avenge Lala Lajpat Rai; Bhagat Singh & Batukeshwar Dutt threw bombs in Assembly (8 Apr 1929).

### Q822 · Space & Nuclear Program

Which Indian satellite navigation system provides regional coverage?

A. GPS
B. GLONASS
C. NavIC (IRNSS)
D. Galileo

**Answer:** C. NavIC (IRNSS)
**Explanation:** NavIC (Navigation with Indian Constellation), also known as IRNSS (Indian Regional Navigation Satellite System), is India's autonomous regional satellite navigation system developed by ISRO. It has 7 satellites and provides coverage over India and the surrounding region (1,500 km).
**Shortcut / rule:** NavIC/IRNSS = India (7 satellites, regional); GPS = USA (31 satellites, global); GLONASS = Russia; Galileo = EU; BeiDou = China; QZSS = Japan; NavIC accuracy: 10-20 metres.

### Q823 · UN & World Organizations

The Security Council of the United Nations has how many permanent members?

A. 3
B. 5
C. 7
D. 10

**Answer:** B. 5
**Explanation:** The UN Security Council has 5 permanent members (P5): USA, UK, France, Russia, and China. Each has veto power. The Council also has 10 non-permanent members elected for 2-year terms. India has been a non-permanent member 8 times.
**Shortcut / rule:** P5 = USA, UK, France, Russia, China (veto power); 10 non-permanent (2-year terms, no veto); Total = 15 members; Decisions need 9 affirmative votes including all P5; India's demand: permanent seat in expanded UNSC.

### Q824 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which gas is primarily responsible for the depletion of the ozone layer?

A. Carbon dioxide
B. Methane
C. Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)
D. Sulphur dioxide

**Answer:** C. Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)
**Explanation:** CFCs (Chlorofluorocarbons), used in refrigerators, air conditioners, and aerosol sprays, are the primary cause of ozone layer depletion. When CFCs reach the stratosphere, UV radiation breaks them down, releasing chlorine atoms that destroy ozone molecules.
**Shortcut / rule:** CFCs = ozone depletion; Montreal Protocol (1987) = phased out CFCs; Ozone hole = largest over Antarctica; 1 Cl atom can destroy 100,000 O₃ molecules; Alternatives: HFCs, HCFCs (less damaging).

### Q825 · Computers & Applications

The first computer programmer in history is considered to be:

A. Charles Babbage
B. Ada Lovelace
C. Alan Turing
D. John von Neumann

**Answer:** B. Ada Lovelace
**Explanation:** Ada Lovelace (Augusta Ada King, Countess of Lovelace) is considered the first computer programmer for her work on Charles Babbage's Analytical Engine in the 1840s. She wrote the first algorithm intended to be processed by a machine.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ada Lovelace = 1st programmer; Charles Babbage = Father of Computer (Analytical Engine); Alan Turing = Father of AI & Computer Science; John von Neumann = stored-program concept; Tim Berners-Lee = WWW.

### Q826 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The Golden Quadrilateral connects:

A. 4 metro cities
B. 4 holy cities
C. 4 ports
D. 4 border states

**Answer:** A. 4 metro cities
**Explanation:** The Golden Quadrilateral is a network of highways connecting India's four major metros: Delhi, Mumbai, Chennai, and Kolkata. It is about 5,846 km long and was initiated by PM Atal Bihari Vajpayee under the NHDP (National Highways Development Project).
**Shortcut / rule:** Golden Quadrilateral: Delhi-Mumbai-Chennai-Kolkata (5,846 km); North-South corridor: Srinagar-Kanyakumari; East-West corridor: Silchar-Porbandar; NHAI (National Highways Authority of India) = implementing agency.

### Q827 · Flagship Govt Programs

Under PM Ujjwala Yojana, free LPG connections are provided to:

A. All households
B. BPL women
C. Senior citizens
D. Farmers

**Answer:** B. BPL women
**Explanation:** Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana (PMUY) was launched on 1 May 2016 from Ballia, UP to provide free LPG connections to women from Below Poverty Line (BPL) families. The aim was to replace unhealthy cooking fuels (wood, dung, coal) with clean LPG fuel.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ujjwala = 1 May 2016 (Ballia, UP); Free LPG for BPL women; Target: 8 crore connections (Ujjwala 1.0); Ujjwala 2.0 = expanded coverage; Reduces indoor air pollution; ₹1,600 subsidy per connection.

### Q828 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Dandi March was undertaken to protest against:

A. Land revenue
B. Salt tax
C. Income tax
D. Import duties

**Answer:** B. Salt tax
**Explanation:** The Dandi March (Salt March) was undertaken by Mahatma Gandhi from Sabarmati Ashram to Dandi (a coastal village in Gujarat) from 12 March to 6 April 1930 to protest against the British salt tax. Gandhi picked up a lump of natural salt, breaking the Salt Laws and launching the Civil Disobedience Movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dandi March = 12 Mar – 6 Apr 1930 (385 km, 24 days); 78 volunteers; Broke Salt Law → Civil Disobedience Movement; Gandhi-Irwin Pact (5 Mar 1931) ended movement temporarily.

### Q829 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of "Basic Structure" of the Constitution was established in which case?

A. Golaknath Case (1967)
B. Kesavananda Bharati Case (1973)
C. Minerva Mills Case (1980)
D. Maneka Gandhi Case (1978)

**Answer:** B. Kesavananda Bharati Case (1973)
**Explanation:** In the Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala case (1973), the Supreme Court established the "Basic Structure Doctrine," ruling that Parliament can amend any part of the Constitution but cannot alter its basic structure. This landmark judgment was decided by a 13-judge bench (7:6 majority).
**Shortcut / rule:** Kesavananda Bharati (1973) = Basic Structure doctrine; Golaknath (1967) = Parliament cannot amend FRs; Minerva Mills (1980) = reaffirmed basic structure; Basic structure includes: supremacy of Constitution, separation of powers, federalism, secularism, judicial review.

### Q830 · Physics

The SI unit of power is:

A. Joule
B. Newton
C. Watt
D. Pascal

**Answer:** C. Watt
**Explanation:** Watt (W) is the SI unit of power, named after James Watt. Power is the rate of doing work or the rate of energy transfer. 1 Watt = 1 Joule per second. The commercial unit of electrical energy is kilowatt-hour (kWh).
**Shortcut / rule:** Power = Watt (W) = J/s; Energy = Joule; 1 kWh = 3.6 × 10⁶ J = 1 unit of electricity; 1 HP (horse power) = 746 W; P = VI (electrical power) = V²/R = I²R.

### Q831 · Chemistry

Which element is found in all organic compounds?

A. Nitrogen
B. Oxygen
C. Carbon
D. Hydrogen

**Answer:** C. Carbon
**Explanation:** Carbon is the essential element present in all organic compounds. It can form four covalent bonds and can create long chains and ring structures. The branch of chemistry dealing with carbon compounds is called Organic Chemistry.
**Shortcut / rule:** Carbon = basis of all organic compounds; Can form 4 bonds; Allotropes: diamond, graphite, fullerene, graphene; Carbon dating = age determination of fossils (C-14); Organic chemistry = study of carbon compounds (except CO₂, CO, carbonates).

### Q832 · Indian Economy

The first bank to be nationalized in India was:

A. State Bank of India
B. Reserve Bank of India
C. Punjab National Bank
D. Imperial Bank of India

**Answer:** A. State Bank of India
**Explanation:** The Imperial Bank of India was nationalized and renamed the State Bank of India (SBI) on 1 July 1955 under the SBI Act, 1955 based on the recommendations of the All India Rural Credit Survey Committee. This was before the major bank nationalization of 14 banks on 19 July 1969.
**Shortcut / rule:** SBI = 1 Jul 1955 (1st nationalized, from Imperial Bank); 14 banks nationalized: 19 Jul 1969 (Indira Gandhi); 6 more banks: 15 Apr 1980; Total: 20 nationalized banks (now merged into fewer entities).

### Q833 · Biology / Life Science

DNA stands for:

A. Di-Nucleic Acid
B. Deoxyribonucleic Acid
C. Deoxyribose Nucleic Acid
D. Both B and C

**Answer:** D. Both B and C
**Explanation:** DNA stands for Deoxyribonucleic Acid. It carries the genetic instructions for the development, functioning, and reproduction of all known organisms. Its double-helix structure was discovered by Watson and Crick in 1953.
**Shortcut / rule:** DNA = double helix (Watson & Crick, 1953); RNA = single strand; DNA bases: A-T, G-C (Adenine-Thymine, Guanine-Cytosine); RNA bases: A-U, G-C; DNA in nucleus; Mitochondrial DNA = maternal inheritance.

### Q834 · Indian Geography

Which Indian state is the largest producer of tea?

A. Assam
B. Kerala
C. West Bengal
D. Tamil Nadu

**Answer:** A. Assam
**Explanation:** Assam is the largest producer of tea in India, contributing over 50% of India's total tea production. India is the second-largest tea producer in the world after China. Darjeeling tea (West Bengal) is famous for its unique flavour and is called the "Champagne of Teas."
**Shortcut / rule:** Tea: Assam (largest producer); Coffee: Karnataka (Coorg, Chikmagalur); Rubber: Kerala; Spices: Kerala (Spice Garden of India); Jute: West Bengal; Silk: Karnataka (largest); Cotton: Gujarat/Maharashtra.

### Q835 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Red Fort in Delhi was built by:

A. Akbar
B. Shah Jahan
C. Aurangzeb
D. Humayun

**Answer:** B. Shah Jahan
**Explanation:** The Red Fort (Lal Qila) in Delhi was built by Mughal Emperor Shah Jahan between 1638-1648. It is built of red sandstone and is located along the banks of the Yamuna River. The Indian PM hoists the national flag here on Independence Day.
**Shortcut / rule:** Shah Jahan built: Red Fort (Delhi), Taj Mahal (Agra), Jama Masjid (Delhi), Peacock Throne; Akbar built: Fatehpur Sikri, Buland Darwaza, Agra Fort; Humayun's Tomb = built by his wife Hamida Banu (1st Mughal garden tomb).

### Q836 · Games & Sports

Which country hosted the 2024 Summer Olympic Games?

A. Japan
B. USA
C. France
D. Australia

**Answer:** C. France
**Explanation:** The 2024 Summer Olympic Games were held in Paris, France from 26 July to 11 August 2024. It was the third time Paris hosted the Olympics (after 1900 and 1924). The 2028 Olympics will be held in Los Angeles, USA.
**Shortcut / rule:** 2024 = Paris (France); 2028 = Los Angeles (USA); 2032 = Brisbane (Australia); 2020 (held 2021) = Tokyo (Japan); 2016 = Rio (Brazil); India hosted Asian Games: 1951 & 1982 (New Delhi).

### Q837 · Famous Personalities

Who is the first woman Prime Minister of India?

A. Pratibha Patil
B. Indira Gandhi
C. Sarojini Naidu
D. Sushma Swaraj

**Answer:** B. Indira Gandhi
**Explanation:** Indira Gandhi served as the Prime Minister of India for two terms (1966-1977 and 1980-1984). She was the daughter of Jawaharlal Nehru and the first (and so far only) woman PM of India. Pratibha Patil was the first woman President of India (2007-2012).
**Shortcut / rule:** Indira Gandhi = 1st woman PM (1966); Pratibha Patil = 1st woman President (2007); Sarojini Naidu = 1st woman Governor (1947); Sucheta Kriplani = 1st woman CM (UP, 1963); Fathima Beevi = 1st woman SC judge (1989).

### Q838 · Space & Nuclear Program

DRDO stands for:

A. Defence Research and Development Organization
B. Defence Research and Design Organization
C. Department of Research and Development Operations
D. Defence Resource and Development Organization

**Answer:** A. Defence Research and Development Organization
**Explanation:** DRDO (Defence Research and Development Organisation) was established in 1958 and is headquartered in New Delhi. It is under the Ministry of Defence and works on military technology. Key projects include Agni missiles, Tejas aircraft, and Arjun tank.
**Shortcut / rule:** DRDO = New Delhi (1958); Key products: Agni (ballistic missile), Prithvi (tactical missile), Tejas (LCA), Arjun (tank), Akash (air defence), BrahMos (cruise missile, Indo-Russian); DRDO has 50+ labs across India.

### Q839 · UN & World Organizations

The WTO (World Trade Organization) was established in:

A. 1945
B. 1975
C. 1990
D. 1995

**Answer:** D. 1995
**Explanation:** The WTO was established on 1 January 1995, replacing GATT (General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade, 1948). It is headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland and deals with global trade rules. India is a founding member of the WTO.
**Shortcut / rule:** WTO = Geneva (1 Jan 1995, replaced GATT); 164 member countries; Ministerial Conference = highest body (every 2 years); DG appointed for 4-year term; Key agreements: TRIPS, TRIMS, AoA; India = founding member.

### Q840 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which of the following is a biodiversity hotspot in India?

A. Thar Desert
B. Western Ghats
C. Indo-Gangetic Plain
D. Deccan Plateau

**Answer:** B. Western Ghats
**Explanation:** The Western Ghats is one of the 36 global biodiversity hotspots. India has 4 biodiversity hotspots: Western Ghats, Eastern Himalayas, Indo-Burma Region, and Sundaland. The Western Ghats are home to numerous endemic species of flora and fauna.
**Shortcut / rule:** India's 4 biodiversity hotspots: Western Ghats, Eastern Himalayas, Indo-Burma, Sundaland (Nicobar Islands); Biodiversity hotspot criteria: at least 1,500 endemic plant species + lost ≥70% of original habitat; Concept by: Norman Myers.

### Q841 · Computers & Applications

Which programming language is known as the "backbone of the internet"?

A. Java
B. Python
C. HTML
D. C++

**Explanation:** HTML (HyperText Markup Language) is considered the backbone of the internet as every web page is built using HTML. While not a traditional programming language, it structures all web content. JavaScript adds interactivity and CSS adds styling.

### Q842 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The first metro rail in India was started in:

A. Delhi
B. Mumbai
C. Kolkata
D. Chennai

**Answer:** C. Kolkata
**Explanation:** The first metro rail in India was opened in Kolkata on 24 October 1984 between Esplanade and Bhowanipore (now Netaji Bhavan) stations. The Delhi Metro began operations in 2002. Currently, multiple Indian cities have operational metro rail systems.

### Q843 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Digital India programme was launched in:

A. 2014
B. 2015
C. 2016
D. 2017

### Q844 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who founded the Arya Samaj?

A. Raja Ram Mohan Roy
B. Swami Dayanand Saraswati
C. Swami Vivekananda
D. Ramakrishna Paramahamsa

**Answer:** B. Swami Dayanand Saraswati
**Explanation:** Swami Dayanand Saraswati founded the Arya Samaj in 1875 in Bombay. His motto was "Back to the Vedas" (Krinvanto Vishwam Aryam). He opposed idol worship, caste system, and advocated for the authority of the Vedas. His book "Satyarth Prakash" is a key text.
**Shortcut / rule:** Arya Samaj = Dayanand Saraswati (1875, Bombay); Brahmo Samaj = Raja Ram Mohan Roy (1828, Calcutta); Ramakrishna Mission = Swami Vivekananda (1897); Theosophical Society = Blavatsky & Olcott (1875, New York; India HQ = Adyar, Madras).

### Q845 · Indian Polity & Governance

Article 370 of the Indian Constitution was related to:

A. Right to Education
B. Special status of Jammu & Kashmir
C. Emergency provisions
D. Amendment procedure

**Answer:** B. Special status of Jammu & Kashmir
**Explanation:** Article 370 granted special autonomous status to the state of Jammu & Kashmir. It was abrogated on 5 August 2019, and J&K was reorganized into two Union Territories: Jammu & Kashmir (with legislature) and Ladakh (without legislature).
**Shortcut / rule:** Article 370 = J&K special status (abrogated 5 Aug 2019); Article 35A = special privileges to J&K residents (also revoked); J&K + Ladakh = 2 UTs; Bifurcation effective: 31 Oct 2019 (Sardar Patel's birthday).

### Q846 · Physics

Which of the following is a vector quantity?

A. Speed
B. Mass
C. Temperature
D. Velocity

**Answer:** D. Velocity
**Explanation:** Velocity is a vector quantity because it has both magnitude and direction. Speed has only magnitude (scalar). Other vector quantities include force, acceleration, displacement, momentum, and torque.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vectors (magnitude + direction): velocity, force, acceleration, displacement, momentum, torque; Scalars (magnitude only): speed, mass, temperature, time, energy, work, power, distance, volume.

### Q847 · Chemistry

Which metal is liquid at room temperature?

A. Iron
B. Aluminium
C. Mercury
D. Zinc

**Answer:** C. Mercury
**Explanation:** Mercury (Hg) is the only metal that is liquid at room temperature (melting point: −38.83°C). It is used in thermometers, barometers, and fluorescent lamps. Bromine is the only non-metal that is liquid at room temperature.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mercury (Hg) = only liquid metal (room temp); Bromine (Br) = only liquid non-metal; Gallium (Ga) melts at ~29.8°C (melts in hand); Mercury symbol Hg from Latin "Hydrargyrum" (liquid silver); Mercury is toxic.

### Q848 · Indian Economy

SEBI stands for:

A. Securities and Exchange Board of India
B. Stock Exchange Bureau of India
C. Securities and Economic Board of India
D. Stock and Exchange Bureau of India

**Answer:** A. Securities and Exchange Board of India
**Explanation:** SEBI (Securities and Exchange Board of India) was established on 12 April 1988 as a non-statutory body and given statutory powers on 30 January 1992. It is headquartered in Mumbai and regulates the Indian securities market to protect investors' interests.
**Shortcut / rule:** SEBI = Mumbai (1988/1992); Regulates: stock exchanges, mutual funds, brokers; BSE (Bombay Stock Exchange) = oldest in Asia (1875); NSE = National Stock Exchange (1992); BSE index = Sensex (30 stocks); NSE index = Nifty (50 stocks).

### Q849 · Biology / Life Science

Which part of the brain controls balance and coordination?

A. Cerebrum
B. Cerebellum
C. Medulla oblongata
D. Hypothalamus

**Answer:** B. Cerebellum
**Explanation:** The cerebellum (located at the back of the brain) controls balance, posture, coordination of voluntary movements, and motor learning. The cerebrum handles thinking, memory, and sensation. The medulla oblongata controls involuntary functions like breathing and heartbeat.
**Shortcut / rule:** Cerebrum = thinking, memory, intelligence (largest part); Cerebellum = balance, coordination; Medulla oblongata = involuntary actions (breathing, heartbeat); Hypothalamus = temperature, hunger, thirst; Pons = bridge between cerebrum & cerebellum.

### Q850 · Indian Geography

Which waterfall is the highest in India?

A. Jog Falls
B. Dudhsagar Falls
C. Kunchikal Falls
D. Athirapilly Falls

**Answer:** C. Kunchikal Falls
**Explanation:** Kunchikal Falls on the Varahi River in Karnataka is the highest waterfall in India at 455 metres. Jog Falls (also in Karnataka, on the Sharavathi River) is the second highest at 253 metres. Jog Falls has 4 cascades: Raja, Rani, Roarer, and Rocket.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kunchikal Falls = highest in India (455 m, Karnataka); Jog Falls = 2nd highest (253 m, Karnataka, 4 cascades); Dudhsagar = Goa/Karnataka (310 m); Angel Falls = world's highest (979 m, Venezuela); Niagara = USA-Canada border.


### Q851 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Hampi ruins are located in which state?

A. Maharashtra
B. Tamil Nadu
C. Karnataka
D. Andhra Pradesh

**Answer:** C. Karnataka
**Explanation:** Hampi in Karnataka was the capital of the Vijayanagara Empire (14th-16th century). The ruins include the Vittala Temple with its famous stone chariot and musical pillars. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site on the banks of the Tungabhadra River.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hampi = Vijayanagara Empire (Karnataka); Vittala Temple = stone chariot + musical pillars; Founded: Harihara & Bukka (1336); Greatest ruler: Krishnadevaraya; Battle of Talikota (1565) = destruction of Vijayanagara.

### Q852 · Games & Sports

The term "Deuce" is used in which sport?

A. Cricket
B. Hockey
C. Tennis
D. Football

**Answer:** C. Tennis
**Explanation:** In tennis, "Deuce" refers to a score of 40-40 (tied at 40 all). After deuce, a player must win two consecutive points (Advantage → Game) to win the game. The term comes from the French "à deux" meaning "at two" (two points from winning).
**Shortcut / rule:** Tennis scoring: 15 → 30 → 40 → Game; Deuce = 40-40; Advantage = point after deuce; Love = 0; LBW = Cricket; Penalty corner = Hockey; Free kick = Football; Birdie = Badminton (also golf).

### Q853 · Famous Personalities

Who established the Servants of India Society?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Bal Gangadhar Tilak
C. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
D. Dadabhai Naoroji

**Answer:** C. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
**Explanation:** Gopal Krishna Gokhale founded the Servants of India Society in 1905 in Pune. He was a moderate leader of the Indian National Congress and is known as Gandhi's political guru. He advocated for social reform and Indian self-governance through constitutional methods.

### Q854 · Space & Nuclear Program

India's reusable launch vehicle technology demonstrator (RLV-TD) was first tested in:

A. 2014
B. 2016
C. 2018
D. 2020

**Answer:** B. 2016
**Explanation:** ISRO's Reusable Launch Vehicle – Technology Demonstrator (RLV-TD) was first tested on 23 May 2016 from Sriharikota. It is a step towards developing a fully reusable two-stage-to-orbit (TSTO) launch vehicle, which could reduce launch costs significantly.
**Shortcut / rule:** RLV-TD = 23 May 2016 (1st test); Nicknamed "Swadeshi Space Shuttle"; Goal: reduce launch cost by 10x; ISRO's future: Gaganyaan (human spaceflight), RLV (reusable), SSLV (Small Satellite Launch Vehicle).

### Q855 · UN & World Organizations

UNICEF primarily works for:

A. World trade
B. Children's welfare
C. Labour rights
D. Environmental protection

**Answer:** B. Children's welfare
**Explanation:** UNICEF (United Nations Children's Fund) works for children's rights, survival, development, and protection. Originally called United Nations International Children's Emergency Fund, it was established on 11 December 1946. Its headquarters is in New York.
