### Q856 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which of the following is a greenhouse gas?

A. Nitrogen
B. Oxygen
C. Methane
D. Argon

**Answer:** C. Methane
**Explanation:** Methane (CH₄) is a potent greenhouse gas, about 80 times more effective at trapping heat than CO₂ over a 20-year period. Major sources include livestock, rice paddies, landfills, and fossil fuel extraction. Other greenhouse gases include CO₂, N₂O, and water vapour.
**Shortcut / rule:** Greenhouse gases: CO₂ (most abundant), CH₄ (most potent per molecule), N₂O, CFCs, O₃, water vapour; CO₂ = largest contributor to global warming; Greenhouse effect = natural (keeps Earth warm), enhanced = climate change.

### Q857 · Computers & Applications

What does URL stand for?

A. Uniform Resource Locator
B. Universal Resource Locator
C. Uniform Resource Link
D. Universal Reference Locator

**Answer:** A. Uniform Resource Locator
**Explanation:** URL (Uniform Resource Locator) is the address of a web page or resource on the internet. It typically consists of a protocol (http/https), domain name, and path. URLs were invented by Tim Berners-Lee as part of the World Wide Web.
**Shortcut / rule:** URL = web address; HTTP = HyperText Transfer Protocol; HTTPS = secure version (SSL/TLS); Domain types: .com (commercial), .org (organization), .edu (education), .gov (government), .in (India).

### Q858 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

BRICS originally included how many member countries?

A. 4
B. 5
C. 6
D. 7

**Answer:** B. 5
**Explanation:** BRICS originally consisted of 5 member countries: Brazil, Russia, India, China, and South Africa. It was initially BRIC (coined by Jim O'Neill of Goldman Sachs in 2001), and South Africa joined in 2010 to make it BRICS. In 2024, it expanded to include new members.
**Shortcut / rule:** BRIC = 2001 (concept by Jim O'Neill); BRICS = 2010 (South Africa joined); 2024 expansion: added Egypt, Ethiopia, Iran, UAE, Saudi Arabia; BRICS summits rotate; G7 = USA, UK, France, Germany, Italy, Canada, Japan.

### Q859 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Atal Pension Yojana (APY) guarantees a minimum pension of:

A. ₹500 per month
B. ₹1,000 per month
C. ₹3,000 per month
D. ₹1,000 to ₹5,000 per month

**Answer:** D. ₹1,000 to ₹5,000 per month
**Explanation:** Atal Pension Yojana was launched on 9 May 2015 to provide a defined pension (₹1,000 to ₹5,000 per month) to workers in the unorganized sector after the age of 60. The pension amount depends on the contribution and the age at which the person joins.
**Shortcut / rule:** APY = 9 May 2015 (pension for unorganized sector); Age: 18-40 years (can join); Pension at 60; Fixed pension: ₹1K/2K/3K/4K/5K per month based on contribution; Government co-contribution: 50% (for 5 years) for eligible subscribers.

### Q860 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Lucknow Pact of 1916 was an agreement between:

A. Congress and Muslim League
B. Congress and British
C. Muslim League and British
D. Hindu Mahasabha and Muslim League

**Answer:** A. Congress and Muslim League
**Explanation:** The Lucknow Pact (1916) was an agreement between the Indian National Congress and the All India Muslim League. It marked the first joint demand for self-governance and agreed on separate electorates for Muslims. The pact was facilitated by leaders like Tilak and Jinnah.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lucknow Pact (1916) = Congress + Muslim League unity; Facilitated by Tilak & Jinnah; Jinnah called "Ambassador of Hindu-Muslim Unity" by Sarojini Naidu; Led to: Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms (1919).

### Q861 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Inter-State Council is established under which article?

A. Article 260
B. Article 263
C. Article 267
D. Article 270

**Answer:** B. Article 263
**Explanation:** The Inter-State Council is established under Article 263 of the Constitution to investigate and advise on disputes between states, discuss common interests, and make recommendations for better coordination. It was set up in 1990 based on the Sarkaria Commission recommendation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 263 = Inter-State Council (1990, Sarkaria Commission); Art 262 = Inter-State water disputes; Zonal Councils = statutory (States Reorganisation Act, 1956, 5+1 zones); Art 263 = constitutional advisory body.

### Q862 · Physics

The instrument used to measure temperature is:

A. Barometer
B. Thermometer
C. Hygrometer
D. Pyrometer

**Answer:** B. Thermometer
**Explanation:** A thermometer is used to measure temperature. It was invented by Daniel Gabriel Fahrenheit (mercury thermometer, 1714). There are three main temperature scales: Celsius (°C), Fahrenheit (°F), and Kelvin (K). Kelvin is the SI unit of temperature.
**Shortcut / rule:** Thermometer = temperature; Pyrometer = very high temperatures (furnaces); Clinical thermometer range: 35-42°C; Scales: °C (water: 0-100), °F (water: 32-212), K (absolute, 0K = −273.15°C); K = °C + 273.15.

### Q863 · Chemistry

Bleaching powder is chemically:

A. Calcium hypochlorite
B. Sodium hypochlorite
C. Calcium chloride
D. Sodium chloride

**Answer:** A. Calcium hypochlorite
**Explanation:** Bleaching powder (CaOCl₂) is calcium hypochlorite. It is produced by the reaction of chlorine gas with slaked lime (Ca(OH)₂). It is used for bleaching cotton, linen, and wood pulp, and for disinfecting drinking water.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bleaching powder = CaOCl₂ (Ca hypochlorite); Slaked lime = Ca(OH)₂; Quick lime = CaO; Plaster of Paris = CaSO₄·½H₂O; Gypsum = CaSO₄·2H₂O; Bleaching powder used for: water purification + textile bleaching.

### Q864 · Indian Economy

The Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management (FRBM) Act was enacted in:

A. 2000
B. 2003
C. 2005
D. 2008

**Answer:** B. 2003
**Explanation:** The FRBM Act was enacted in 2003 to institutionalize fiscal discipline and reduce fiscal deficit. It set targets to eliminate revenue deficit and reduce fiscal deficit to 3% of GDP. The Act was based on the recommendations of the E.A.S. Sarma Committee.
**Shortcut / rule:** FRBM Act = 2003 (fiscal discipline); Target: Fiscal deficit ≤ 3% of GDP; Revenue deficit = 0; Fiscal deficit = total expenditure − total revenue (excluding borrowings); Revenue deficit = revenue expenditure − revenue receipts.

### Q865 · Biology / Life Science

Which enzyme is present in saliva?

A. Pepsin
B. Trypsin
C. Amylase (Ptyalin)
D. Lipase

**Answer:** C. Amylase (Ptyalin)
**Explanation:** Salivary amylase (ptyalin) is the enzyme present in saliva. It converts starch into maltose (sugar) in the mouth. This is why bread tastes sweet when chewed for a long time. Digestion of carbohydrates begins in the mouth.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mouth: Ptyalin/Amylase (starch → maltose); Stomach: Pepsin (protein), HCl; Small intestine: Trypsin (protein), Lipase (fats), Amylase; Liver: Bile (emulsifies fats); Carbohydrate digestion starts in mouth; Protein in stomach; Fat in small intestine.

### Q866 · Indian Geography

The state with the highest literacy rate in India is:

A. Mizoram
B. Kerala
C. Goa
D. Tripura

**Answer:** B. Kerala
**Explanation:** Kerala has the highest literacy rate in India at 96.2% (Census 2011). The national average literacy rate is 74.04%. Bihar has the lowest literacy rate. Kerala was declared the first fully literate state in India in 1991.
**Shortcut / rule:** Highest literacy: Kerala (96.2%); Lowest: Bihar (63.8%); Highest male literacy: Kerala; Highest female literacy: Kerala; National average: 74.04% (Census 2011); Gender gap in literacy is smallest in Kerala.

### Q867 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Charminar is located in:

A. Lucknow
B. Delhi
C. Hyderabad
D. Ahmedabad

**Answer:** C. Hyderabad
**Explanation:** The Charminar (Four Minarets) is located in Hyderabad, Telangana. It was built by Muhammad Quli Qutb Shah in 1591 to commemorate the end of a plague epidemic. It has four grand arches and minarets at each corner, with a mosque on the top floor.
**Shortcut / rule:** Charminar = Hyderabad (Qutb Shahi dynasty, 1591); Hawa Mahal = Jaipur (Maharaja Sawai Pratap Singh, 1799); Gateway of India = Mumbai (George Wittet, 1924); India Gate = Delhi (Edwin Lutyens, 1931, war memorial).

### Q868 · Games & Sports

The number of players in a hockey team (on field) is:

A. 9
B. 10
C. 11

### Q869 · Famous Personalities

Who was the first President of India?

### Q870 · Space & Nuclear Program

The Gaganyaan mission is India's:

A. First Mars mission
B. First human spaceflight programme

**Explanation:** Gaganyaan is ISRO's first crewed spaceflight programme, aiming to send Indian astronauts (Vyomanauts) to low Earth orbit for a 3-day mission. The mission includes development of a human-rated launch vehicle (GSLV Mk III/LVM3) and a crew module.

The Nobel Peace Prize is awarded in which city?

### Q874 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

LPG stands for:

A. Liquid Propane Gas

**Explanation:** NEP 2020 replaced the National Policy on Education, 1986 (modified in 1992). ### Q876 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Partition of Bengal took place in:

A. 1899
B. 1905
C. 1911
D. 1919

**Answer:** B. 1905
**Explanation:** The Partition of Bengal was announced by Lord Curzon on 20 July 1905 and implemented on 16 October 1905. It divided Bengal into East Bengal & Assam (Muslim majority) and West Bengal (Hindu majority). It was annulled in 1911 by Lord Hardinge at the Delhi Durbar.
**Shortcut / rule:** Partition of Bengal = 1905 (Curzon); Annulled = 1911 (Delhi Durbar, Hardinge); Swadeshi Movement started as protest; 16 Oct 1905 = Rakhi Bandhan Day (unity); "Amar Sonar Bangla" sung during Swadeshi movement (later Bangladesh national anthem).

The Election Commission of India was established in:

A. 1948
B. 1950
C. 1952
D. 1960

**Answer:** B. 1950
**Explanation:** The Election Commission of India (ECI) was established on 25 January 1950 under Article 324 of the Constitution. 25 January is celebrated as National Voters' Day. Sukumar Sen was the first Chief Election Commissioner. The ECI became a multi-member body with 3 members in 1993.
**Shortcut / rule:** ECI = 25 Jan 1950 (Art 324); 25 Jan = National Voters' Day; 1st CEC: Sukumar Sen; Multi-member (3) since 1993; CEC can be removed like SC judge (by Parliament); ECs can be removed on CEC's recommendation.

### Q878 · Physics

The phenomenon responsible for the blue colour of the sky is:

A. Reflection
B. Refraction
C. Scattering of light
D. Dispersion

**Answer:** C. Scattering of light
**Explanation:** The blue colour of the sky is due to Rayleigh scattering. Blue light has a shorter wavelength and is scattered more by atmospheric molecules than red light. At sunset/sunrise, the sky appears red because light travels a longer path through the atmosphere, scattering blue completely.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rayleigh scattering: intensity ∝ 1/λ⁴; Blue scattered most (short λ); Sky = blue (day); Sunset = red/orange (long path, only red reaches); Tyndall effect = scattering in colloids; Raman scattering = inelastic (Nobel 1930).

### Q879 · Chemistry

Baking soda is chemically:

A. Sodium carbonate
B. Sodium bicarbonate
C. Sodium chloride
D. Sodium hydroxide

**Answer:** B. Sodium bicarbonate
**Explanation:** Baking soda is sodium bicarbonate (NaHCO₃), also called sodium hydrogen carbonate. It is used in cooking (as a leavening agent), fire extinguishers, and as an antacid. It reacts with acids to produce CO₂ gas, which makes dough rise.
**Shortcut / rule:** NaHCO₃ = Baking soda; Na₂CO₃ = Washing soda; NaOH = Caustic soda; NaCl = Common salt; CaOCl₂ = Bleaching powder; CaSO₄·½H₂O = Plaster of Paris; KOH = Caustic potash.

### Q880 · Indian Economy

Who is considered the architect of India's economic reforms of 1991?

A. P.V. Narasimha Rao
B. Manmohan Singh
C. Pranab Mukherjee
D. Yashwant Sinha

**Answer:** B. Manmohan Singh
**Explanation:** Dr. Manmohan Singh, as Finance Minister under PM P.V. Narasimha Rao, is considered the architect of India's 1991 economic reforms (LPG reforms — Liberalization, Privatization, Globalization). The reforms were triggered by a balance of payments crisis.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1991 reforms = Manmohan Singh (FM) + P.V. Narasimha Rao (PM); LPG = Liberalization, Privatization, Globalization; Triggered by: BoP crisis, gold pledged to Bank of England; Abolished License Raj; Opened FDI; Reduced import tariffs.

### Q881 · Biology / Life Science

The functional unit of the kidney is:

A. Neuron
B. Nephron
C. Alveoli
D. Villi

**Answer:** B. Nephron
**Explanation:** The nephron is the basic functional unit of the kidney responsible for filtering blood and producing urine. Each kidney contains about 1 million nephrons. The nephron consists of Bowman's capsule, glomerulus, and renal tubules.
**Shortcut / rule:** Nephron = kidney (filtration); Neuron = nervous system; Alveoli = lungs (gas exchange); Villi = small intestine (absorption); Sarcomere = muscle; Hepatocyte = liver cell; Each kidney = ~1 million nephrons.

### Q882 · Indian Geography

The largest delta in the world is:

A. Nile Delta
B. Ganges-Brahmaputra Delta
C. Mississippi Delta
D. Mekong Delta

**Answer:** B. Ganges-Brahmaputra Delta
**Explanation:** The Ganges-Brahmaputra Delta (Sundarbans Delta) is the largest delta in the world, covering about 100,000 sq km across India (West Bengal) and Bangladesh. It is formed by the confluence of the Ganga and Brahmaputra rivers. The Sundarbans mangrove forest is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest delta: Ganges-Brahmaputra (Sundarbans); Sundarbans = Royal Bengal Tiger + largest mangrove forest; Nile Delta = Egypt; Mississippi = USA; Sundarbans shared by India + Bangladesh; UNESCO World Heritage Site.

### Q883 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Nalanda University was an ancient centre of learning located in:

A. Uttar Pradesh
B. Bihar
C. Madhya Pradesh
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** B. Bihar
**Explanation:** Nalanda University in Bihar was one of the oldest and most renowned centres of higher learning in the ancient world (5th-12th century CE). It was founded during the Gupta dynasty and attracted students from across Asia. It was destroyed by Bakhtiyar Khilji in 1193 CE. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Nalanda = Bihar (Gupta dynasty, 5th c); Destroyed: Bakhtiyar Khilji (1193); Taxila (Takshashila) = Pakistan (oldest, 6th c BCE); Vikramashila = Bihar (Pala dynasty); New Nalanda University established: 2010 (Rajgir, Bihar).

### Q884 · Games & Sports

Which Indian cricketer is known as the "God of Cricket"?

A. Virat Kohli
B. M.S. Dhoni
C. Sachin Tendulkar
D. Kapil Dev

**Answer:** C. Sachin Tendulkar
**Explanation:** Sachin Tendulkar is fondly called the "God of Cricket" and "Master Blaster." He holds numerous records including most runs in Test (15,921) and ODI (18,426) cricket, 100 international centuries, and was the first to score a double century in ODIs (200* vs SA, 2010). He received the Bharat Ratna in 2014.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tendulkar = God of Cricket (200 Tests, 463 ODIs, 100 centuries); Bharat Ratna 2014; Debut: 15 years old (1989 vs Pakistan); Retired: 2013 (200th Test vs WI); Don Bradman = 99.94 Test average (greatest ever).

### Q885 · Famous Personalities

Who was the first Indian to receive the Bharat Ratna?

A. Dr. S. Radhakrishnan
B. C. Rajagopalachari
C. Dr. C.V. Raman
D. All three received it in the same year

**Answer:** D. All three received it in the same year
**Explanation:** Dr. S. Radhakrishnan, C. Rajagopalachari, and Dr. C.V. Raman were the first three recipients of the Bharat Ratna in 1954. The Bharat Ratna is India's highest civilian award, established in 1954 by President Rajendra Prasad.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bharat Ratna (1954): 1st recipients — Radhakrishnan, Rajagopalachari, C.V. Raman (all 1954); No provision for posthumous award until 1966; Posthumous: Subhash Chandra Bose (1992, later withdrawn), Lal Bahadur Shastri (1966).

### Q886 · Space & Nuclear Program

The Bhabha Atomic Research Centre (BARC) is located in:

A. Hyderabad
B. Bengaluru
C. Mumbai (Trombay)
D. Chennai

**Answer:** C. Mumbai (Trombay)
**Explanation:** BARC is located at Trombay, Mumbai. It was established in 1954 as the Atomic Energy Establishment, Trombay (AEET) and renamed BARC in 1967 after Homi Jehangir Bhabha, the father of India's nuclear programme.
**Shortcut / rule:** BARC = Trombay, Mumbai (1954); Named after Homi Bhabha (1967); India's 1st nuclear reactor: Apsara (1956, BARC); TIFR (Tata Institute of Fundamental Research) = also founded by Bhabha (Mumbai, 1945); DAE = Department of Atomic Energy.

### Q887 · UN & World Organizations

The International Labour Organization (ILO) is headquartered in:

A. New York
B. Geneva
C. Paris
D. Vienna

**Answer:** B. Geneva
**Explanation:** The ILO (International Labour Organization) is headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland. It was established in 1919 as part of the Treaty of Versailles and became a UN specialized agency in 1946. It won the Nobel Peace Prize in 1969.
**Shortcut / rule:** ILO = Geneva (1919); Nobel Peace Prize 1969; Tripartite structure: governments + employers + workers; India = founding member; Key conventions: minimum wage, child labour, forced labour, working hours.

### Q888 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which act in India deals with the protection of wildlife?

A. Forest Conservation Act, 1980
B. Wildlife Protection Act, 1972
C. Environment Protection Act, 1986
D. Biodiversity Act, 2002

**Answer:** B. Wildlife Protection Act, 1972
**Explanation:** The Wildlife Protection Act, 1972 provides for the protection of wild animals, birds, and plants. It established the framework for national parks, wildlife sanctuaries, and tiger reserves. It also created schedules listing protected species.
**Shortcut / rule:** Wildlife Protection Act = 1972 (animals/plants protection); Forest Conservation Act = 1980 (forests); Environment Protection Act = 1986 (overall, Bhopal Gas Tragedy triggered); Water (Prevention) Act = 1974; Air (Prevention) Act = 1981.

### Q889 · Computers & Applications

What is the full form of PDF?

A. Portable Document Format
B. Personal Document File
C. Printed Document Format
D. Portable Digital Format

**Answer:** A. Portable Document Format
**Explanation:** PDF (Portable Document Format) was developed by Adobe Systems in 1993. It is designed to present documents consistently across different platforms and devices. It preserves formatting, fonts, and layout regardless of the viewing device.
**Shortcut / rule:** PDF = Adobe (1993); JPEG = image compression; PNG = lossless image; MP3 = audio; MP4 = video; DOC/DOCX = Word; XLS/XLSX = Excel; PPT/PPTX = PowerPoint; ZIP = compressed archive.

### Q890 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

IRDAI stands for:

A. Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India
B. Indian Regulatory and Development Authority for Insurance
C. Insurance Research and Development Authority of India
D. Indian Reserve and Development Authority for Insurance

**Answer:** A. Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India
**Explanation:** IRDAI regulates and promotes the insurance industry in India. It was established in 1999 based on the recommendations of the Malhotra Committee (1994). It is headquartered in Hyderabad.

### Q891 · Flagship Govt Programs

Poshan Abhiyaan (National Nutrition Mission) was launched in:

A. 2016
B. 2017
C. 2018
D. 2019

**Answer:** C. 2018
**Explanation:** POSHAN Abhiyaan (PM's Overarching Scheme for Holistic Nourishment) was launched on 8 March 2018 (International Women's Day) from Jhunjhunu, Rajasthan. It aims to reduce stunting, undernutrition, anaemia, and low birth weight among children, adolescent girls, pregnant women, and lactating mothers.
**Shortcut / rule:** POSHAN Abhiyaan = 8 Mar 2018; Targets: reduce stunting by 2% per year; Anaemia reduction: 3% per year; September = Poshan Maah (Nutrition Month); Integrated with: ICDS, Anganwadi services; National Nutrition Month.

### Q892 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Rowlatt Act was passed in:

A. 1917
B. 1919
C. 1920
D. 1922

**Answer:** B. 1919
**Explanation:** The Rowlatt Act was passed on 18 March 1919 by the Imperial Legislative Council. It allowed the government to imprison any person without trial for up to 2 years. Gandhi called it a "Black Act" and launched a nationwide hartal (strike) on 6 April 1919. It led to the Jallianwala Bagh massacre.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rowlatt Act = March 1919 (arrest without trial); "Black Act" — Gandhi; Hartal: 6 April 1919; Led to: Jallianwala Bagh (13 April 1919); Committee: Justice Sidney Rowlatt; Also called "Black Bills" or "Anarchical and Revolutionary Crimes Act."

### Q893 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Rajya Sabha can have a maximum of how many members?

A. 238
B. 245
C. 250
D. 275

**Answer:** C. 250
**Explanation:** The Rajya Sabha (Council of States) can have a maximum of 250 members — 238 elected by state and UT legislative assemblies, and 12 nominated by the President from experts in literature, science, art, and social service. Currently it has 245 members.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rajya Sabha: max 250 (238 elected + 12 nominated); Lok Sabha: max 552 (530 states + 20 UTs + 2 Anglo-Indians, now 543); Rajya Sabha = permanent (not dissolved); 1/3 members retire every 2 years; Term = 6 years.

### Q894 · Physics

Radar is used for:

A. Detecting the speed and location of distant objects
B. Measuring atmospheric pressure
C. Recording earthquakes
D. Measuring rainfall

**Answer:** A. Detecting the speed and location of distant objects
**Explanation:** RADAR (Radio Detection And Ranging) uses radio waves to detect the position, speed, altitude, and direction of objects such as aircraft, ships, and weather formations. It was developed during World War II. It works on the principle of reflection of radio waves.
**Shortcut / rule:** RADAR = Radio Detection and Ranging (radio waves); SONAR = Sound Navigation and Ranging (sound waves, underwater); LIDAR = Light Detection and Ranging (laser); RADAR uses: aviation, military, weather, traffic speed detection.

### Q895 · Chemistry

The gas responsible for the pungent smell of garlic is:

A. Methane
B. Hydrogen sulphide
C. Allyl methyl sulphide
D. Carbon dioxide

**Answer:** C. Allyl methyl sulphide
**Explanation:** The pungent smell of garlic is primarily due to allicin and allyl methyl sulphide, sulphur-containing compounds. When garlic is crushed, the enzyme alliinase converts alliin to allicin. These organosulfur compounds also have antibacterial properties.
**Shortcut / rule:** Garlic smell = allicin/allyl methyl sulphide; Rotten egg = H₂S (hydrogen sulphide); Mustard gas = dichlorodiethyl sulphide (chemical weapon); Sulphur compounds = pungent smells; Garlic: antimicrobial + heart-healthy properties.

### Q896 · Indian Economy

What is the minimum denomination of currency note issued by RBI?

A. ₹1
B. ₹2
C. ₹5
D. ₹10

**Answer:** B. ₹2
**Explanation:** The minimum denomination of currency note issued by the RBI is ₹2. The ₹1 note is issued by the Ministry of Finance (Government of India) and bears the signature of the Finance Secretary. All other notes (₹2, ₹5, ₹10, ₹20, ₹50, ₹100, ₹200, ₹500, ₹2000) are issued by the RBI.
**Shortcut / rule:** ₹1 note = Govt of India (Finance Secretary signs); ₹2 onwards = RBI (Governor signs); RBI issues under Section 22 of RBI Act; Coins = Govt of India; ₹2000 note = introduced during demonetization (2016), withdrawn 2023.

### Q897 · Biology / Life Science

The largest cell in the human body is:

A. Red blood cell
B. White blood cell
C. Ovum (egg cell)
D. Neuron

**Answer:** C. Ovum (egg cell)
**Explanation:** The ovum (egg cell) is the largest cell in the human body, approximately 0.1 mm (100 micrometres) in diameter and visible to the naked eye. The smallest cell in the human body is the sperm cell (head only about 5 µm).
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest human cell = Ovum (female egg, ~0.1 mm); Smallest human cell = Sperm (male); Longest cell = Neuron (nerve cell, up to 1 metre); Largest cell in world = Ostrich egg; RBC = no nucleus (mammals).

### Q898 · Indian Geography

The Palk Strait separates India from:

A. Pakistan
B. Sri Lanka
C. Bangladesh
D. Myanmar

**Answer:** B. Sri Lanka
**Explanation:** The Palk Strait is a narrow strait separating Tamil Nadu (India) from northern Sri Lanka. It is about 53-80 km wide. Adam's Bridge (Ram Setu) is a chain of limestone shoals connecting Rameswaram (India) to Mannar (Sri Lanka) across the Palk Strait.
**Shortcut / rule:** Palk Strait = India-Sri Lanka; Gulf of Mannar = south of Palk Strait; Adam's Bridge (Ram Setu) = Rameswaram to Mannar; Strait of Malacca = Malaysia-Indonesia; Strait of Hormuz = Iran-Oman (oil route); Bering Strait = Alaska-Russia.

### Q899 · Art, Culture & Literature

The famous painting "Bharat Mata" was painted by:

A. Raja Ravi Varma
B. Abanindranath Tagore
C. Nandalal Bose
D. Jamini Roy

**Answer:** B. Abanindranath Tagore
**Explanation:** The painting "Bharat Mata" was created by Abanindranath Tagore in 1905 during the Swadeshi Movement. It depicts a saffron-clad woman holding a book, sheaves of paddy, a piece of cloth, and a garland, symbolizing education, food, clothing, and spirituality.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bharat Mata = Abanindranath Tagore (1905); Raja Ravi Varma = "Father of Modern Indian Art" (Shakuntala, Nair Woman); Nandalal Bose = illustrated Constitution of India; Amrita Sher-Gil = "Indian Frida Kahlo"; M.F. Husain = "Picasso of India."

### Q900 · Games & Sports

The term "Checkmate" is used in:

A. Carrom
B. Ludo
C. Chess
D. Billiards

**Answer:** C. Chess
**Explanation:** "Checkmate" in chess means the king is under attack (in check) and has no legal move to escape. The game ends immediately with a win for the player delivering checkmate. The term comes from the Persian "shah mat" meaning "the king is dead."
**Shortcut / rule:** Chess: 64 squares, 16 pieces per player; King = most important; Queen = most powerful; Checkmate = game over; FIDE = governing body (Lausanne); India's greatest: Viswanathan Anand (5-time World Champion); Chess originated in India (Chaturanga).


### Q901 · Famous Personalities

Who is known as the "Father of the Indian Nation"?

A. Jawaharlal Nehru
B. Sardar Patel
C. Mahatma Gandhi
D. Bhimrao Ambedkar

**Answer:** C. Mahatma Gandhi
**Explanation:** Mahatma Gandhi is known as the "Father of the Nation" (Rashtrapita). The title was first used by Subhash Chandra Bose in a radio broadcast from Singapore on 6 July 1944. Officially, the Government of India does not give this title constitutional recognition.
**Shortcut / rule:** Gandhi = Father of the Nation; Nehru = Chacha Nehru (children); Patel = Iron Man; Ambedkar = Father of Constitution; Tagore gave Gandhi the title "Mahatma"; Gandhi called Gokhale his political guru.

### Q902 · Space & Nuclear Program

India's first indigenous nuclear submarine is:

A. INS Vikrant
B. INS Arihant
C. INS Chakra
D. INS Sindhughosh

**Answer:** B. INS Arihant
**Explanation:** INS Arihant is India's first indigenously built nuclear-powered ballistic missile submarine (SSBN). It was commissioned in 2016 under the Advanced Technology Vessel (ATV) project. With its induction, India completed its nuclear triad (land, air, sea-based nuclear capability).
**Shortcut / rule:** INS Arihant = 1st indigenous nuclear sub (SSBN, 2016); INS Vikrant = 1st indigenous aircraft carrier (2022); INS Chakra = leased nuclear sub from Russia; Nuclear triad: Agni (land) + Rafale/Su-30 (air) + Arihant (sea).

### Q903 · UN & World Organizations

The headquarters of ASEAN is located in:

A. Bangkok
B. Singapore
C. Jakarta
D. Manila

**Answer:** C. Jakarta
**Explanation:** ASEAN (Association of Southeast Asian Nations) is headquartered in Jakarta, Indonesia. It was established on 8 August 1967 by 5 founding members: Indonesia, Malaysia, Philippines, Singapore, and Thailand. It currently has 10 member states. India is not a member but is part of ASEAN+3 dialogue.
**Shortcut / rule:** ASEAN = Jakarta (1967); 10 members; ASEAN+3 = ASEAN + China, Japan, South Korea; India = ASEAN dialogue partner (1992); SAARC = Kathmandu (1985, 8 members); BIMSTEC = Dhaka (1997, 7 members, includes India).

### Q904 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The National Green Tribunal (NGT) was established in:

A. 2008
B. 2010
C. 2012
D. 2014

**Answer:** B. 2010
**Explanation:** The National Green Tribunal (NGT) was established on 18 October 2010 under the National Green Tribunal Act, 2010 for effective and expeditious disposal of environmental cases. India is the third country after Australia and New Zealand to set up a specialized environmental tribunal.
**Shortcut / rule:** NGT = 18 Oct 2010; Principal bench: New Delhi; 4 regional benches: Bhopal, Pune, Kolkata, Chennai; Deals with: environmental disputes, forest conservation, water/air pollution; Chairperson = retired SC/HC judge; India = 3rd country with environmental tribunal.

### Q905 · Computers & Applications

Which of the following is NOT a web browser?

A. Google Chrome
B. Mozilla Firefox
C. Microsoft Outlook

**Explanation:** Microsoft Outlook is an email client and personal information manager, not a web browser. Google Chrome, Mozilla Firefox, Safari (Apple), Microsoft Edge, and Opera are popular web browsers used to access websites and web applications.

### Q906 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

NHAI stands for:

A. National Highway Authority of India
B. National Highways Authority of India
C. National Highway Association of India

**Answer:** B. National Highways Authority of India
**Explanation:** NHAI (National Highways Authority of India) was established in 1988 and became operational in 1995. It is responsible for the development, maintenance, and management of national highways. It implements the NHDP (National Highways Development Project) including the Golden Quadrilateral.

### Q907 · Flagship Govt Programs

The PM-KISAN scheme provides:

A. Free insurance to farmers
B. ₹6,000 per year in three instalments
C. Free seeds and fertilizers
D. Loan waiver for farmers

**Answer:** B. ₹6,000 per year in three instalments
**Explanation:** Pradhan Mantri Kisan Samman Nidhi (PM-KISAN) was launched on 24 February 2019. It provides ₹6,000 per year (in 3 instalments of ₹2,000 each) directly to the bank accounts of small and marginal farmers through Direct Benefit Transfer (DBT).
**Shortcut / rule:** PM-KISAN = ₹6,000/year (3 × ₹2,000); Launched: 24 Feb 2019; For: all landholder farmer families; DBT (Direct Benefit Transfer); PM Fasal Bima Yojana = crop insurance (2016); Kisan Credit Card (KCC) = short-term credit for agriculture.

### Q908 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The first session of the Indian National Congress was held in:

A. Calcutta, 1885
B. Bombay, 1885
C. Madras, 1884
D. Allahabad, 1886

**Answer:** B. Bombay, 1885
**Explanation:** The first session of the Indian National Congress was held in Bombay (at Gokuldas Tejpal Sanskrit College) on 28 December 1885. It was presided over by Womesh Chunder Bonnerjee (W.C. Bonnerjee). 72 delegates attended. A.O. Hume is called the founder of INC.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Congress session: Bombay, 28 Dec 1885; President: W.C. Bonnerjee; Founder: A.O. Hume; 72 delegates; 2nd session: Calcutta (1886, Dadabhai Naoroji); 1st Muslim president: Badruddin Tyabji (1887, Madras).

### Q909 · Indian Polity & Governance

The writ of "Habeas Corpus" literally means:

A. We command
B. To have the body
C. What is your authority
D. To forbid

**Answer:** B. To have the body
**Explanation:** Habeas Corpus (Latin: "to have the body") is a writ issued to produce a detained person before the court to examine the legality of detention. It protects against unlawful detention. It can be issued by both the Supreme Court (Art 32) and High Courts (Art 226).
**Shortcut / rule:** 5 Writs: Habeas Corpus (produce body), Mandamus (we command), Prohibition (forbid lower court), Certiorari (quash order), Quo Warranto (by what authority); HC issues all 5 for FRs + other rights; SC issues for FRs only.

### Q910 · Physics

What is the unit of frequency?

A. Watt
B. Hertz
C. Joule
D. Ampere

**Answer:** B. Hertz
**Explanation:** Hertz (Hz) is the SI unit of frequency, named after Heinrich Hertz. One Hertz equals one cycle per second. Audible frequency range for humans is 20 Hz to 20,000 Hz. Ultrasound is above 20,000 Hz, and infrasound is below 20 Hz.
**Shortcut / rule:** Frequency = Hertz (Hz) = cycles/second; Human hearing: 20-20,000 Hz; Ultrasound > 20 kHz (medical imaging, SONAR); Infrasound < 20 Hz (earthquakes); Radio waves: kHz-GHz; Microwave: GHz.

### Q911 · Chemistry

The process of converting sugar into alcohol is called:

A. Distillation
B. Fermentation
C. Pasteurization
D. Crystallization

**Answer:** B. Fermentation
**Explanation:** Fermentation is the biochemical process in which sugar (glucose) is converted to ethanol (alcohol) and carbon dioxide by yeast (enzyme zymase). C₆H₁₂O₆ → 2C₂H₅OH + 2CO₂. It is used in making wine, beer, bread, and curd.
**Shortcut / rule:** Fermentation = sugar → alcohol + CO₂ (yeast/zymase); Pasteurization = killing microbes by heating (Louis Pasteur); Distillation = separation by boiling points; Crystallization = purification of solids; Fermentation = anaerobic process.

### Q912 · Indian Economy

Which committee recommended the introduction of GST in India?

A. Kelkar Committee
B. Narasimham Committee
C. Rangarajan Committee
D. Raja Chelliah Committee

**Answer:** A. Kelkar Committee
**Explanation:** The Vijay Kelkar Task Force on implementation of the FRBM Act (2004) first recommended GST. The Asim Dasgupta Committee (2007) designed the GST model. The Empowered Committee of State Finance Ministers further developed the framework.
**Shortcut / rule:** GST recommendation: Kelkar Committee; Narasimham Committee = banking reforms (1991, 1998); Rangarajan = disinvestment + capital account convertibility; Raja Chelliah = tax reforms (1991); FRBM = fiscal discipline.

### Q913 · Biology / Life Science

Malaria is caused by which organism?

A. Bacteria
B. Virus
C. Protozoa (Plasmodium)
D. Fungus

**Answer:** C. Protozoa (Plasmodium)
**Explanation:** Malaria is caused by the protozoan parasite Plasmodium, transmitted through the bite of the female Anopheles mosquito. There are 5 species: P. vivax, P. falciparum (most dangerous), P. malariae, P. ovale, and P. knowlesi. Ronald Ross discovered the malaria parasite in mosquitoes (Nobel 1902).
**Shortcut / rule:** Malaria = Plasmodium (protozoa) via female Anopheles mosquito; Dengue = virus via Aedes aegypti; Typhoid = Salmonella (bacteria); TB = Mycobacterium (bacteria); Ringworm = fungus; COVID-19 = SARS-CoV-2 (virus).

### Q914 · Indian Geography

The highest rainfall in India is received at:

A. Cherrapunji
B. Mawsynram
C. Amboli
D. Agumbe

**Answer:** B. Mawsynram
**Explanation:** Mawsynram in Meghalaya receives the highest average annual rainfall in India (and the world) at about 11,871 mm. Cherrapunji (Sohra), also in Meghalaya, holds the record for the highest rainfall in a single year (26,471 mm in 1860-61).
**Shortcut / rule:** Mawsynram = highest average annual rainfall (Meghalaya); Cherrapunji = highest single-year record (1860-61); Both in Khasi Hills; Jaisalmer (Rajasthan) = lowest rainfall in India; SW Monsoon = major source of Indian rainfall (June-Sep).

### Q915 · Art, Culture & Literature

The famous Konark Sun Temple was built in which century?

A. 10th century
B. 11th century
C. 13th century
D. 15th century

**Answer:** C. 13th century
**Explanation:** The Konark Sun Temple in Odisha was built in the 13th century (around 1250 CE) by King Narasimhadeva I of the Eastern Ganga dynasty. It is designed as a giant chariot with 24 elaborately carved stone wheels and pulled by 7 horses. The temple is also called the "Black Pagoda."
**Shortcut / rule:** Konark = 13th century (1250 CE), Narasimhadeva I, Eastern Ganga; "Black Pagoda" (by sailors); 24 wheels = 24 hours; 7 horses = 7 days of week; UNESCO World Heritage Site (1984); Dedicated to Sun God (Surya).

### Q916 · Games & Sports

The 2023 Cricket World Cup final was played between:

A. India and Australia
B. India and England
C. Australia and New Zealand
D. India and South Africa

**Answer:** A. India and Australia
**Explanation:** The 2023 ICC Cricket World Cup final was played between India and Australia on 19 November 2023 at the Narendra Modi Stadium in Ahmedabad. Australia won by 6 wickets. Travis Head was the Player of the Match with 137 runs.
**Shortcut / rule:** 2023 WC final: India vs Australia (Australia won, Ahmedabad); 2019: England vs NZ (England won, Lord's); India won WC: 1983 (Kapil Dev) & 2011 (Dhoni); T20 WC India won: 2007 & 2024; Australia = most ODI WC titles (6).

### Q917 · Famous Personalities

Who gave the theory of evolution by natural selection?

A. Gregor Mendel
B. Charles Darwin
C. Louis Pasteur
D. Alexander Fleming

**Answer:** B. Charles Darwin
**Explanation:** Charles Darwin proposed the theory of evolution by natural selection in his book "On the Origin of Species" (1859). He observed that organisms with favourable traits are more likely to survive and reproduce. He traveled on HMS Beagle and studied finches on the Galapagos Islands.
**Shortcut / rule:** Darwin = evolution by natural selection (Origin of Species, 1859); HMS Beagle voyage; Mendel = genetics (Father of Genetics, pea plants); Pasteur = germ theory, pasteurization; Fleming = penicillin (1928); Lamarck = inheritance of acquired characters.

### Q918 · Space & Nuclear Program

Which planet is known as the "Red Planet"?

A. Venus
B. Jupiter
C. Mars
D. Saturn

**Answer:** C. Mars
**Explanation:** Mars is called the "Red Planet" because of iron oxide (rust) on its surface, giving it a reddish appearance. It has two moons: Phobos and Deimos. Mars has the largest volcano in the solar system: Olympus Mons. India's Mangalyaan successfully orbited Mars in 2014.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mercury = closest to Sun; Venus = hottest + Morning/Evening Star; Earth = Blue Planet; Mars = Red Planet; Jupiter = largest; Saturn = rings; Uranus = tilted axis; Neptune = farthest; Pluto = dwarf planet (since 2006).

### Q919 · UN & World Organizations

The World Health Organization declared COVID-19 a pandemic on:

A. 30 January 2020
B. 11 March 2020
C. 25 March 2020
D. 1 April 2020

**Answer:** B. 11 March 2020
**Explanation:** WHO declared COVID-19 a pandemic on 11 March 2020. The disease was caused by SARS-CoV-2 virus, first identified in Wuhan, China in December 2019. India imposed a nationwide lockdown on 25 March 2020. WHO declared the end of COVID-19 as a public health emergency on 5 May 2023.
**Shortcut / rule:** COVID-19 timeline: Dec 2019 (Wuhan) → 30 Jan 2020 (PHEIC) → 11 Mar 2020 (Pandemic) → 25 Mar 2020 (India lockdown) → 5 May 2023 (emergency ended); Vaccines in India: Covishield (AstraZeneca/SII) & Covaxin (Bharat Biotech).

### Q920 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The concept of "Carbon Credit" is linked to:

A. Banking reforms
B. Climate change mitigation
C. Agricultural subsidies
D. Industrial licensing

**Answer:** B. Climate change mitigation
**Explanation:** Carbon credits are tradable permits that allow the holder to emit one tonne of CO₂ or equivalent greenhouse gas. The concept was established under the Kyoto Protocol's Clean Development Mechanism (CDM). Companies can trade carbon credits to meet emission reduction targets.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1 Carbon credit = 1 tonne CO₂ equivalent; Kyoto Protocol → CDM → carbon trading; Paris Agreement → Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs); India's NDC: 45% emission intensity reduction by 2030; Net Zero target: 2070.

### Q921 · Computers & Applications

Which of the following is a programming language?

A. HTML
B. HTTP
C. Python
D. FTP

**Answer:** C. Python
**Explanation:** Python is a high-level, interpreted programming language created by Guido van Rossum in 1991. It is widely used in AI, machine learning, data science, and web development. HTML is a markup language, HTTP and FTP are protocols.
**Shortcut / rule:** Programming languages: Python, Java, C, C++, JavaScript, Ruby, Go, Rust; Python creator: Guido van Rossum (1991); Java: James Gosling (1995); C: Dennis Ritchie (1972); C++: Bjarne Stroustrup (1979); JavaScript: Brendan Eich (1995).

### Q922 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The first Indian satellite dedicated to education is:

A. INSAT-1A
B. EDUSAT (GSAT-3)
C. IRS-1A
D. Cartosat

**Answer:** B. EDUSAT (GSAT-3)
**Explanation:** EDUSAT (GSAT-3) was India's first satellite dedicated exclusively to education, launched on 20 September 2004 by GSLV-F01. It provided connectivity for distance education through satellite-based interactive classrooms.
**Shortcut / rule:** EDUSAT (2004) = 1st education satellite; GSAT series = communication; IRS series = remote sensing; IRNSS = navigation (NavIC); Cartosat = cartography/mapping; RISAT = radar imaging; Oceansat = ocean studies.

### Q923 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Jal Jeevan Mission aims to provide:

A. Irrigation to all farms
B. Tap water to every rural household
C. River interlinking
D. Flood control

**Answer:** B. Tap water to every rural household
**Explanation:** Jal Jeevan Mission was launched on 15 August 2019 to provide Functional Household Tap Connection (FHTC) to every rural household by 2024. The target was to supply 55 litres per person per day (lpcd) of potable water through individual household tap connections.
**Shortcut / rule:** Jal Jeevan Mission = 15 Aug 2019 (rural tap water); 55 lpcd target; Jal Shakti Ministry; Har Ghar Jal = every home with water; Nal Se Jal = piped water; Swachh Bharat = sanitation; Namami Gange = Ganga cleaning.

### Q924 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Moplah Rebellion (1921) took place in:

A. Punjab
B. Kerala
C. Tamil Nadu
D. Karnataka

**Answer:** B. Kerala
**Explanation:** The Moplah (Mappila) Rebellion of 1921 took place in the Malabar region of Kerala. It began as part of the Khilafat and Non-Cooperation movements but turned into a peasant revolt against Hindu landlords (jenmi) and the British. Variyankunnathu Kunjahammed Haji was a key leader.
**Shortcut / rule:** Moplah Rebellion = 1921, Malabar (Kerala); Started as Khilafat/NCM → peasant revolt; Chauri Chaura (1922) = UP (Gandhi withdrew NCM); Eka Movement (1921-22) = UP (peasant); Tebhaga (1946) = Bengal (tenant farmers).

### Q925 · Indian Polity & Governance

Which amendment introduced the anti-defection law?

A. 42nd Amendment
B. 44th Amendment
C. 52nd Amendment
D. 61st Amendment

**Answer:** C. 52nd Amendment
**Explanation:** The 52nd Amendment Act, 1985 introduced the anti-defection law by adding the Tenth Schedule to the Constitution. It was based on the recommendations of the Dinesh Goswami Committee. A member can be disqualified if they voluntarily give up party membership or vote against the party whip.
**Shortcut / rule:** 52nd Amendment (1985) = Anti-Defection (10th Schedule); Speaker/Chairman decides disqualification; Exception: merger of 2/3 of party; Kihoto Hollohan case (1992) = upheld validity; 91st Amendment (2003) = removed "split" exception, only "merger" allowed.

### Q926 · Physics

What is the principle of an electric motor?

A. Electromagnetic induction
B. Heating effect of current
C. A current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field experiences a force
D. Electrostatic attraction

**Answer:** C. A current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field experiences a force
**Explanation:** An electric motor works on the principle that a current-carrying conductor placed in a magnetic field experiences a mechanical force (Lorentz force). This force causes the coil to rotate, converting electrical energy into mechanical energy. The direction of force is given by Fleming's Left-Hand Rule.
**Shortcut / rule:** Motor = electrical → mechanical (Fleming's Left-Hand Rule); Generator = mechanical → electrical (Fleming's Right-Hand Rule, electromagnetic induction); Faraday's law = EMF induced by changing magnetic flux.

### Q927 · Chemistry

The chemical name of Vitamin C is:

A. Retinol
B. Ascorbic acid
C. Thiamine
D. Calciferol

**Answer:** B. Ascorbic acid
**Explanation:** Vitamin C is chemically called Ascorbic acid. It prevents scurvy (hence "a-scorbutic acid"). It is a water-soluble vitamin found abundantly in citrus fruits (lemon, orange), amla (Indian gooseberry), guava, and tomatoes.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vit A = Retinol; B1 = Thiamine; B2 = Riboflavin; B3 = Niacin; B6 = Pyridoxine; B12 = Cyanocobalamin; C = Ascorbic acid; D = Calciferol; E = Tocopherol; K = Phylloquinone; Water-soluble: B & C; Fat-soluble: A, D, E, K.

### Q928 · Indian Economy

Demonetization of ₹500 and ₹1000 notes was announced on:

A. 8 November 2015
B. 8 November 2016
C. 8 December 2016
D. 1 January 2017

**Answer:** B. 8 November 2016
**Explanation:** PM Narendra Modi announced the demonetization of ₹500 and ₹1000 currency notes on 8 November 2016 at 8 PM. The objectives were to curb black money, counterfeit currency, and terror financing. New ₹500 and ₹2000 notes were introduced.
**Shortcut / rule:** Demonetization: 8 Nov 2016 (₹500 & ₹1000 old notes); Previous: 1946 (₹1000 & ₹10,000) & 1978 (₹1000, ₹5000, ₹10,000); Cashless economy push; Digital payment surge post-demonetization; ₹2000 notes later withdrawn (2023).

### Q929 · Biology / Life Science

Which blood cells are responsible for immunity?

A. Red Blood Cells (RBC)
B. White Blood Cells (WBC)
C. Platelets
D. Plasma

**Answer:** B. White Blood Cells (WBC)
**Explanation:** White Blood Cells (Leucocytes) are responsible for the body's immune defence against infections and diseases. Types include Neutrophils, Lymphocytes, Monocytes, Eosinophils, and Basophils. RBCs carry oxygen, and Platelets help in blood clotting.
**Shortcut / rule:** WBC (Leucocytes) = immunity; RBC (Erythrocytes) = oxygen transport (haemoglobin); Platelets (Thrombocytes) = clotting; WBC types: Neutrophils (most common), Lymphocytes (B & T cells), Monocytes; WBC count ↑ = infection; ↓ = immunodeficiency.

### Q930 · Indian Geography

The Siachen Glacier is located in:

A. Himachal Pradesh
B. Uttarakhand
C. Eastern Ladakh
D. Karakoram Range (Ladakh)

**Answer:** D. Karakoram Range (Ladakh)
**Explanation:** The Siachen Glacier in the Karakoram Range (Ladakh, India) is the highest battleground in the world. It is about 76 km long, making it the second-longest glacier in the non-polar world. The Indian Army has been deployed there since Operation Meghdoot (1984).
**Shortcut / rule:** Siachen = Karakoram (highest battlefield, 76 km); Operation Meghdoot = 1984 (Indian Army captured Siachen); Largest glacier in India: Siachen; Gangotri glacier = source of Ganga (Uttarakhand); Nubra Valley = near Siachen.

### Q931 · Art, Culture & Literature

Who composed the famous devotional song "Ragupati Raghav Raja Ram"?

A. Rabindranath Tagore
B. Meerabai
C. V.D. Paluskar (popularized by Gandhi)
D. Surdas

**Answer:** C. V.D. Paluskar (popularized by Gandhi)
**Explanation:** The bhajan "Raghupati Raghav Raja Ram" was based on a composition attributed to Vishnu Digambar Paluskar and was widely popularized by Mahatma Gandhi during his prayer meetings and the Dandi March. It became closely associated with the Indian independence movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** Raghupati Raghav = associated with Gandhi (prayer meetings, Dandi March); Vaishnav Jan To = Gandhi's favourite bhajan (by Narsinh Mehta); Meerabai = devotee of Krishna; Surdas = Sur Sagar (Krishna); Tulsidas = Ramcharitmanas.

### Q932 · Games & Sports

The Olympics motto is:

A. "Faster, Higher, Stronger"
B. "Faster, Higher, Stronger – Together"
C. "Unity, Peace, Progress"
D. "Fair Play, Sportsmanship, Friendship"

**Answer:** B. "Faster, Higher, Stronger – Together"
**Explanation:** The Olympic motto was updated in 2021 to "Citius, Altius, Fortius – Communiter" (Faster, Higher, Stronger – Together). The original motto "Citius, Altius, Fortius" was proposed by Pierre de Coubertin, founder of the modern Olympics.
**Shortcut / rule:** Olympic motto: Faster, Higher, Stronger – Together (updated 2021); 5 Olympic rings = 5 continents (blue, yellow, black, green, red); Pierre de Coubertin = Father of Modern Olympics; IOC HQ = Lausanne, Switzerland.

### Q933 · Famous Personalities

Who invented the telephone?

A. Thomas Edison
B. Alexander Graham Bell
C. Guglielmo Marconi
D. Nikola Tesla

**Answer:** B. Alexander Graham Bell
**Explanation:** Alexander Graham Bell invented the telephone in 1876. His first words over the phone were "Mr. Watson, come here. I want to see you." Thomas Edison invented the phonograph and improved the light bulb. Marconi invented wireless telegraphy (radio).
**Shortcut / rule:** Bell = telephone (1876); Edison = phonograph, light bulb; Marconi = radio/wireless telegraphy (Nobel 1909); Tesla = AC electricity; Wright Brothers = aeroplane (1903); Gutenberg = printing press (~1440); Tim Berners-Lee = WWW (1989).

### Q934 · Space & Nuclear Program

The full form of NASA is:

A. National Aeronautics and Space Association
B. National Aeronautics and Space Administration
C. North American Space Agency
D. National Aerospace and Science Administration

**Answer:** B. National Aeronautics and Space Administration
**Explanation:** NASA (National Aeronautics and Space Administration) is the US space agency, established on 29 July 1958. Its headquarters is in Washington DC. Key missions include Apollo 11 (first Moon landing, 1969), Mars rovers, Hubble Space Telescope, and James Webb Space Telescope.
**Shortcut / rule:** NASA = USA (1958); ESA = European Space Agency; JAXA = Japan; CNSA = China; Roscosmos = Russia; ISRO = India; Apollo 11 (1969) = Neil Armstrong (1st on Moon); "One small step for man, one giant leap for mankind."

### Q935 · UN & World Organizations

The G20 summit was hosted by India in:

A. 2022
B. 2023
C. 2024
D. 2025

**Answer:** B. 2023
**Explanation:** India hosted the G20 Leaders' Summit on 9-10 September 2023 in New Delhi under the presidency theme "One Earth, One Family, One Future" (Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam). The African Union was admitted as a permanent member during India's presidency.
**Shortcut / rule:** India's G20 presidency: Dec 2022 – Nov 2023; Summit: 9-10 Sep 2023 (Delhi); Theme: Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam; Logo: lotus + earth; African Union = G20 permanent member; G20 = G7 + BRICS + others (19 countries + EU + AU).

### Q936 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Sundarbans is famous for which species?

A. Snow Leopard
B. One-horned Rhinoceros
C. Royal Bengal Tiger
D. Asiatic Lion

**Answer:** C. Royal Bengal Tiger
**Explanation:** The Sundarbans, the world's largest mangrove forest spanning India (West Bengal) and Bangladesh, is famous for the Royal Bengal Tiger. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site and a Tiger Reserve. The Sundarbans tigers are the only ones known to swim in salt water.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sundarbans = Royal Bengal Tiger + largest mangrove; Snow Leopard = Hemis (Ladakh); Rhino = Kaziranga (Assam); Asiatic Lion = Gir (Gujarat); Elephant = Periyar (Kerala), Corbett (Uttarakhand); Red Panda = Sikkim/Arunachal.

### Q937 · Computers & Applications

The first generation of computers used which technology?

A. Transistors
B. Integrated Circuits
C. Vacuum Tubes
D. Microprocessors

**Answer:** C. Vacuum Tubes
**Explanation:** First generation computers (1940-1956) used vacuum tubes for circuitry and magnetic drums for memory. They were enormous, consumed huge power, and generated excessive heat. Examples include ENIAC, UNIVAC, and EDVAC. Machine language was used for programming.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Gen: Vacuum tubes (ENIAC, UNIVAC); 2nd Gen: Transistors (IBM 7094); 3rd Gen: ICs (IBM 360); 4th Gen: Microprocessors (Intel 4004, Apple); 5th Gen: AI/parallel processing; ENIAC (1946) = first general-purpose electronic computer.

### Q938 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The Konkan Railway connects:

A. Mumbai to Goa
B. Mumbai to Mangalore
C. Mumbai to Kochi
D. Goa to Chennai

**Answer:** B. Mumbai to Mangalore
**Explanation:** The Konkan Railway connects Mumbai (Roha) to Mangalore, covering about 741 km along the western coast. It passes through Maharashtra, Goa, and Karnataka. It was completed in 1998 and is an engineering marvel with 2,000 bridges and 91 tunnels.
**Shortcut / rule:** Konkan Railway: Mumbai-Mangalore (741 km, 1998); 2,000 bridges + 91 tunnels; Passes through: Maharashtra, Goa, Karnataka; E. Sreedharan = key figure in Konkan Railway construction (before Delhi Metro); Western Ghats route.

### Q939 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Startup India initiative was launched in:

A. 2014
B. 2015
C. 2016
D. 2017

**Answer:** C. 2016
**Explanation:** Startup India was launched on 16 January 2016 to promote entrepreneurship and innovation. Key benefits include tax exemptions for 3 years, easy compliance, self-certification, funding through Fund of Funds, and simplified bankruptcy norms. It aims to make India a hub for startups.
**Shortcut / rule:** Startup India = 16 Jan 2016; Stand-up India = 5 Apr 2016 (SC/ST/Women, ₹10L-₹1Cr loans); Make in India = 25 Sep 2014; Skill India = 15 Jul 2015; Mudra = 8 Apr 2015; Atal Innovation Mission = incubation centres.

### Q940 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Indian Independence Act was passed in:

A. 1945
B. 1946
C. 1947
D. 1948

**Answer:** C. 1947
**Explanation:** The Indian Independence Act was passed by the British Parliament on 18 July 1947. It provided for the partition of British India into two independent dominions — India and Pakistan — effective from 15 August 1947. Lord Mountbatten was the last Viceroy who oversaw the transition.
**Shortcut / rule:** Indian Independence Act = 18 July 1947; Independence: 15 Aug 1947; Partition: India + Pakistan; Last Viceroy: Lord Mountbatten (also 1st Governor-General of India); C. Rajagopalachari = last Indian Governor-General; Radcliffe Line = India-Pakistan border.

### Q941 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of Public Interest Litigation (PIL) was introduced by:

A. Justice V.R. Krishna Iyer
B. Justice P.N. Bhagwati
C. Both A and B
D. Justice Y.V. Chandrachud

**Answer:** C. Both A and B
**Explanation:** Public Interest Litigation (PIL) in India was pioneered by Justice V.R. Krishna Iyer and Justice P.N. Bhagwati in the late 1970s and 1980s. PIL allows any public-spirited citizen to file a case in the interest of the public, even if they are not directly affected.
**Shortcut / rule:** PIL pioneers: Justice Krishna Iyer + Justice Bhagwati; Hussainara Khatoon case (1979) = landmark PIL (undertrial prisoners); PIL can be filed in SC (Art 32) or HC (Art 226); PIL relaxes "locus standi" rule; Even a postcard to CJI can be treated as PIL.

### Q942 · Physics

The process by which a solid directly converts to gas without becoming liquid is:

A. Evaporation
B. Condensation
C. Sublimation
D. Vaporization

**Answer:** C. Sublimation
**Explanation:** Sublimation is the process where a solid converts directly to gas without passing through the liquid state. Examples include dry ice (solid CO₂), naphthalene balls (camphor), and iodine crystals. The reverse process (gas to solid) is called deposition.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sublimation = solid → gas; Deposition = gas → solid; Evaporation = liquid → gas (surface); Boiling = liquid → gas (throughout); Condensation = gas → liquid; Freezing = liquid → solid; Melting = solid → liquid.

### Q943 · Chemistry

Washing soda is chemically:

A. Sodium bicarbonate
B. Sodium carbonate
C. Calcium carbonate
D. Potassium carbonate

**Answer:** B. Sodium carbonate
**Explanation:** Washing soda is sodium carbonate decahydrate (Na₂CO₃·10H₂O). It is used for cleaning, softening hard water, and in the manufacture of glass, paper, and soap. It is produced by the Solvay process.
**Shortcut / rule:** Na₂CO₃·10H₂O = Washing soda; NaHCO₃ = Baking soda; NaCl = Common salt; NaOH = Caustic soda; CaCO₃ = Limestone/Marble/Chalk; Na₂CO₃ uses: glass, soap, paper, water softening; Solvay process = industrial production.

### Q944 · Indian Economy

Who was the first Governor of the Reserve Bank of India?

A. C.D. Deshmukh
B. Sir Osborne Smith
C. Sir Benegal Rama Rau
D. James Braid Taylor

**Answer:** B. Sir Osborne Smith
**Explanation:** Sir Osborne Smith was the first Governor of the RBI (1935-1937). C.D. Deshmukh was the first Indian Governor of RBI (1943-1949). RBI was established on 1 April 1935 based on the Hilton Young Commission (1926) recommendations, initially as a private shareholders' bank, nationalized in 1949.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st RBI Governor: Sir Osborne Smith (1935); 1st Indian Governor: C.D. Deshmukh (1943); RBI nationalized: 1 Jan 1949; RBI functions: monetary policy, currency issue, banker's bank, lender of last resort; RBI Act = 1934.

### Q945 · Biology / Life Science

The Rh factor in blood was named after:

A. Rhesus monkey
B. Rhodium element
C. Rhine River
D. Dr. Rh. Fischer

**Answer:** A. Rhesus monkey
**Explanation:** The Rh factor was named after the Rhesus monkey, in which it was first discovered in 1940 by Karl Landsteiner and Alexander Wiener. About 85% of humans are Rh-positive (Rh+). Rh incompatibility between mother and foetus can cause erythroblastosis fetalis.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rh factor = Rhesus monkey (Landsteiner & Wiener, 1940); Rh+ = 85% people; Rh− = 15%; Rh incompatibility: Rh− mother + Rh+ foetus = risk; ABO blood groups = Landsteiner (1901, Nobel 1930); O− = universal donor; AB+ = universal recipient.

### Q946 · Indian Geography

Which Indian state has the maximum number of districts?

A. Madhya Pradesh
B. Uttar Pradesh
C. Maharashtra
D. Rajasthan

**Answer:** B. Uttar Pradesh
**Explanation:** Uttar Pradesh has the maximum number of districts in India (75 districts). It is also the most populous state. UP is followed by Madhya Pradesh and Maharashtra in terms of number of districts. UP's capital is Lucknow.
**Shortcut / rule:** Most districts: UP (75); Most populous state: UP; Largest state by area: Rajasthan; Smallest state by area: Goa; Most literate: Kerala; Largest city: Mumbai; National capital: New Delhi; UP = most Lok Sabha seats (80).

### Q947 · Art, Culture & Literature

The famous Dilwara Temples are located in:

A. Rajasthan
B. Gujarat
C. Madhya Pradesh
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** A. Rajasthan
**Explanation:** The Dilwara Temples are located at Mount Abu in Rajasthan. They are a group of 5 Jain temples built between the 11th and 13th centuries by the Solanki dynasty rulers. They are famous for their exquisite marble carvings, considered among the finest in the world.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dilwara = Mount Abu, Rajasthan (Jain temples, marble, Solanki dynasty); Ranakpur = Rajasthan (Jain, 1,444 pillars); Palitana = Gujarat (Jain, Shatrunjaya hill); Mount Abu = only hill station in Rajasthan; Vimal Vasahi temple = oldest Dilwara temple (1031 CE).

### Q948 · Games & Sports

The duration of a football match is:

A. 60 minutes
B. 80 minutes
C. 90 minutes
D. 120 minutes

**Answer:** C. 90 minutes
**Explanation:** A standard football (soccer) match consists of two halves of 45 minutes each, totaling 90 minutes, with a 15-minute half-time break. Extra time (30 minutes: two halves of 15 minutes) is played in knockout matches if the score is tied.
**Shortcut / rule:** Football: 90 min (2×45), 11 players; Cricket T20: ~3 hours; Hockey: 60 min (4×15); Basketball: 48 min (4×12, NBA); Tennis: no time limit (sets); Kabaddi: 40 min (2×20); Volleyball: no time limit (sets to 25 points).

### Q949 · Famous Personalities

Who discovered penicillin?

A. Louis Pasteur
B. Alexander Fleming
C. Joseph Lister
D. Edward Jenner

**Answer:** B. Alexander Fleming
**Explanation:** Sir Alexander Fleming discovered penicillin in 1928 from the mould Penicillium notatum. It was the first antibiotic and revolutionized medicine. He shared the Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine (1945) with Howard Florey and Ernst Boris Chain, who developed it for clinical use.
**Shortcut / rule:** Fleming = Penicillin (1928, Nobel 1945); Pasteur = Rabies vaccine, Pasteurization; Jenner = Smallpox vaccine (1796); Lister = antiseptic surgery; Robert Koch = TB bacterium (Koch's postulates); Jonas Salk = Polio vaccine.

### Q950 · Space & Nuclear Program

India's space launch centre is located at:

A. Thumba
B. Sriharikota
C. Bengaluru
D. Chandipur

**Answer:** B. Sriharikota
**Explanation:** Satish Dhawan Space Centre (SDSC-SHAR) at Sriharikota, Andhra Pradesh is India's main spaceport for launching satellites and rockets. Thumba (Kerala) is the Thumba Equatorial Rocket Launching Station (TERLS), used for sounding rockets. ISRO HQ is in Bengaluru.
**Shortcut / rule:** Sriharikota = main launch centre (SDSC-SHAR, AP); Thumba = sounding rockets (Kerala); ISRO HQ = Bengaluru; VSSC = Thiruvananthapuram (rocket development); ISTRAC = Bengaluru (tracking); SAC = Ahmedabad (Space Applications Centre).


### Q951 · UN & World Organizations

The International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA) is headquartered in:

A. New York
B. Geneva
C. Vienna
D. Brussels

**Answer:** C. Vienna
**Explanation:** The IAEA is headquartered in Vienna, Austria. It was established in 1957 to promote the peaceful use of nuclear energy and inhibit military use. It won the Nobel Peace Prize in 2005. India is a member of the IAEA but not of the NPT (Non-Proliferation Treaty).
**Shortcut / rule:** IAEA = Vienna (1957, Nobel Peace 2005); OPCW = The Hague (Chemical Weapons); CTBTO = Vienna (Nuclear Test Ban); NPT = 1968 (India not a signatory); India = member of IAEA; NSG = Nuclear Suppliers Group (India seeking membership).

### Q952 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Ramsar Convention is related to:

A. Climate change
B. Ozone depletion
C. Conservation of wetlands
D. Marine pollution

**Answer:** C. Conservation of wetlands
**Explanation:** The Ramsar Convention (1971) is an international treaty for the conservation and sustainable use of wetlands. It was signed in Ramsar, Iran. India has 80+ Ramsar sites including Chilika Lake, Loktak Lake, Vembanad-Kol, and Sundarbans. UP has the most Ramsar sites in India.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ramsar Convention = 1971, Iran (wetlands); 2 Feb = World Wetlands Day; India's 1st Ramsar sites: Chilika (Odisha) & Keoladeo (Rajasthan), designated 1981; Most Ramsar sites in India: UP/Tamil Nadu; World: UK has the most.

### Q953 · Computers & Applications

What is Artificial Intelligence (AI)?

A. A type of computer hardware
B. Simulation of human intelligence by machines
C. A programming language

**Answer:** B. Simulation of human intelligence by machines
**Explanation:** Artificial Intelligence (AI) is the simulation of human intelligence processes by computer systems, including learning, reasoning, problem-solving, and self-correction. The term was coined by John McCarthy in 1956. AI includes machine learning, deep learning, natural language processing, and computer vision.

### Q954 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

FSSAI stands for:

A. Food Safety and Standards Authority of India
B. Food and Sanitation Standards Authority of India

**Explanation:** FSSAI was established under the Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006 and became operational in 2011. It is headquartered in New Delhi and regulates the manufacture, storage, distribution, sale, and import of food to ensure food safety.

### Q955 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Namami Gange programme is related to:

A. Solar energy promotion
B. Cleaning and conservation of River Ganga

**Explanation:** Namami Gange Programme was launched in June 2014 as a flagship programme of the central government with a budget of ₹20,000 crore for the conservation, rejuvenation, and cleaning of the Ganga River. It is under the National Mission for Clean Ganga (NMCG).
**Shortcut / rule:** Namami Gange = June 2014 (₹20,000 crore); Clean Ganga Mission; NMCG = implementing body; National Ganga Council = chaired by PM; Previous: Ganga Action Plan (1986, Rajiv Gandhi); Ganga flows through: Uttarakhand, UP, Bihar, Jharkhand, WB.

### Q956 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who is known as "Lal-Bal-Pal"?

A. Lala Lajpat Rai, Bal Gangadhar Tilak, Bipin Chandra Pal
B. Lal Bahadur Shastri, Bal Gangadhar Tilak, Sardar Patel
C. Lala Lajpat Rai, Balagangadhara Tilak, Sardar Patel
D. Lal Bahadur Shastri, Balgangadhar Tilak, Bipin Chandra Pal

**Answer:** A. Lala Lajpat Rai, Bal Gangadhar Tilak, Bipin Chandra Pal
**Explanation:** Lal-Bal-Pal refers to Lala Lajpat Rai, Bal Gangadhar Tilak, and Bipin Chandra Pal — the triumvirate of extremist leaders in the Indian National Congress during the early 20th century. They advocated for Swaraj (self-rule) through militant methods rather than moderate petitions.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lal = Lala Lajpat Rai (Punjab Kesari); Bal = Bal Gangadhar Tilak (Lokmanya, Swaraj birthright); Pal = Bipin Chandra Pal (Father of Revolutionary Thought); All three were extremists; Surat Split (1907) = Moderates vs Extremists.

### Q957 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Finance Commission is constituted under which article?

A. Article 275
B. Article 280
C. Article 281
D. Article 300

**Answer:** B. Article 280
**Explanation:** The Finance Commission is constituted under Article 280 of the Constitution every 5 years by the President. It recommends the distribution of net proceeds of taxes between the Centre and states, and grants-in-aid to states. The first FC was chaired by K.C. Neogy (1951).
**Shortcut / rule:** Finance Commission: Art 280 (every 5 years); 1st FC: K.C. Neogy (1951); 16th FC: Arvind Panagariya (2024); Functions: tax distribution (Centre-State), grants-in-aid; Recommendations are advisory; NITI Aayog ≠ Finance Commission.

### Q958 · Physics

Nuclear energy is based on which principle?

A. Nuclear fusion
B. Nuclear fission
C. Both fission and fusion
D. Radioactive decay

**Answer:** C. Both fission and fusion
**Explanation:** Nuclear energy can be generated by both fission (splitting heavy nuclei like Uranium-235 or Plutonium-239) and fusion (combining light nuclei like hydrogen isotopes). Current nuclear power plants use fission. The Sun generates energy through fusion. E = mc² (Einstein) governs nuclear energy.
**Shortcut / rule:** Fission = splitting heavy atoms (U-235, Pu-239, used in reactors & atomic bombs); Fusion = combining light atoms (H → He, used in Sun & hydrogen bombs); E = mc² (mass-energy equivalence); Fission = current power plants; Fusion = future clean energy (ITER project).

### Q959 · Chemistry

The chemical formula of methane is:

A. CH₃
B. CH₄
C. C₂H₆
D. CO₂

**Answer:** B. CH₄
**Explanation:** Methane (CH₄) is the simplest hydrocarbon and a major component of natural gas. It is a potent greenhouse gas. Methane is produced in marshes, landfills, and by livestock (ruminants). It is also called "marsh gas."
**Shortcut / rule:** CH₄ = Methane (marsh gas, simplest alkane); C₂H₆ = Ethane; C₃H₈ = Propane; C₄H₁₀ = Butane; CNG = mainly methane; LPG = propane + butane; Alkanes: CₙH₂ₙ₊₂ (saturated hydrocarbons).

### Q960 · Indian Economy

The concept of "Hindu Rate of Growth" refers to:

A. High economic growth
B. Slow economic growth (around 3.5% GDP)
C. Negative economic growth
D. Industrial growth rate

**Answer:** B. Slow economic growth (around 3.5% GDP)
**Explanation:** The term "Hindu Rate of Growth" was coined by economist Raj Krishna to describe India's slow GDP growth rate of about 3.5% per annum during 1950s-1980s, which barely exceeded population growth. After the 1991 economic reforms, India's growth rate increased significantly.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hindu Rate of Growth = ~3.5% (1950s-80s); Coined by: Raj Krishna; Post-1991 reforms: growth accelerated to 6-9%; India's current GDP growth: ~6-7%; Causes of slow growth: License Raj, inward-looking policies, limited private sector role.

### Q961 · Biology / Life Science

Which organ is known as the "graveyard of RBCs"?

A. Liver
B. Kidney
C. Spleen
D. Bone marrow

**Answer:** C. Spleen
**Explanation:** The spleen is called the "graveyard of RBCs" because it filters out and destroys old, damaged, or abnormal red blood cells. The lifespan of an RBC is about 120 days. The liver also helps in breaking down old RBCs. New RBCs are produced in the bone marrow.
**Shortcut / rule:** Spleen = graveyard of RBCs; Bone marrow = factory of blood cells (RBC, WBC, platelets); RBC lifespan = 120 days; WBC lifespan = 5-21 days; Platelet lifespan = 8-10 days; Liver = detoxification + bile production.

### Q962 · Indian Geography

The Indian Standard Meridian passes through:

A. Ahmedabad
B. Allahabad (Prayagraj)
C. Kolkata
D. Mumbai

**Answer:** B. Allahabad (Prayagraj)
**Explanation:** The Indian Standard Meridian (82°30' E longitude) passes through Mirzapur near Allahabad (Prayagraj) in Uttar Pradesh. Indian Standard Time (IST) is 5 hours 30 minutes ahead of Greenwich Mean Time (GMT/UTC). IST is uniform across India.
**Shortcut / rule:** IST = 82°30'E (Mirzapur, near Prayagraj, UP); IST = GMT + 5:30; India has single time zone; States it passes: UP, MP, Chhattisgarh, Odisha, AP; International Date Line = 180° longitude; Prime Meridian = 0° (Greenwich, London).

### Q963 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Mysore Dasara festival is famous in which state?

A. Tamil Nadu
B. Kerala
C. Karnataka
D. Andhra Pradesh

**Answer:** C. Karnataka
**Explanation:** The Mysore Dasara (Dussehra) is a 10-day festival celebrated in Mysuru, Karnataka. It has been the state festival since 2008. The highlight is the grand Jamboo Savari procession on Vijayadashami day, where a golden howdah carrying the idol of Goddess Chamundeshwari is carried atop a decorated elephant.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mysore Dasara = Karnataka (10 days, Jamboo Savari); Pongal = Tamil Nadu (harvest); Onam = Kerala (harvest, Mahabali); Bihu = Assam; Baisakhi = Punjab; Hornbill Festival = Nagaland; Pushkar Fair = Rajasthan (camel fair).

### Q964 · Games & Sports

PV Sindhu is associated with which sport?

A. Tennis
B. Table Tennis
C. Badminton

**Answer:** C. Badminton
**Explanation:** PV Sindhu is an Indian badminton player who won a silver medal at the 2016 Rio Olympics and a bronze medal at the 2020 Tokyo Olympics. She won the BWF World Championship gold medal in 2019. She is the first Indian woman to win an Olympic silver medal.

### Q965 · Famous Personalities

Who wrote "Arthashastra"?

A. Chanakya (Kautilya)
B. Kalidasa

**Answer:** A. Chanakya (Kautilya)
**Explanation:** "Arthashastra" was written by Kautilya (Chanakya/Vishnugupta) in the 4th century BCE. It is an ancient Indian treatise on statecraft, economic policy, and military strategy. Chanakya was the chief advisor to Emperor Chandragupta Maurya.

### Q966 · Space & Nuclear Program

Which country was the first to land on the Moon?

A. Russia (Soviet Union)
B. USA
C. China
D. India

**Answer:** B. USA
**Explanation:** The USA's Apollo 11 mission successfully landed the first humans on the Moon on 20 July 1969. Neil Armstrong was the first person to step on the Moon, followed by Buzz Aldrin. Michael Collins orbited the Moon in the command module. Armstrong's famous words: "That's one small step for man, one giant leap for mankind."
**Shortcut / rule:** Apollo 11 (1969) = 1st Moon landing (USA); Neil Armstrong = 1st person on Moon; USSR = 1st to orbit Earth (Yuri Gagarin, 1961); China Chang'e = Moon; India Chandrayaan-3 (2023) = soft landing on Moon's south pole (4th country to land).

**Explanation:** SAARC (South Asian Association for Regional Cooperation) was established on 8 December 1985 with its secretariat in Kathmandu, Nepal. ### Q968 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The Central Pollution Control Board (CPCB) was established under which act?

A. Environment Protection Act, 1986
B. Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974
C. Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1981
D. Wildlife Protection Act, 1972

**Answer:** B. Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974
**Explanation:** CPCB was established in September 1974 under the Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974. It also functions under the Air Act, 1981 and EP Act, 1986. CPCB is headquartered in New Delhi and monitors air and water quality nationwide.

### Q969 · Computers & Applications

The shortcut key for "Copy" in Windows is:

A. Ctrl + V
B. Ctrl + C
C. Ctrl + X
D. Ctrl + Z

**Answer:** B. Ctrl + C
**Explanation:** Ctrl + C is the keyboard shortcut for copying selected text or files in Windows. Ctrl + V is for paste, Ctrl + X is for cut, and Ctrl + Z is for undo. These shortcuts are universal across most Windows applications.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ctrl+C = Copy; Ctrl+V = Paste; Ctrl+X = Cut; Ctrl+Z = Undo; Ctrl+A = Select All; Ctrl+S = Save; Ctrl+P = Print; Ctrl+F = Find; Alt+F4 = Close window; Alt+Tab = Switch windows.

### Q970 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The full form of Wi-Fi is:

A. Wireless Fidelity
B. Wireless Frequency
C. Wide Fidelity
D. It has no full form (brand name)

**Answer:** D. It has no full form (brand name)
**Explanation:** Contrary to popular belief, Wi-Fi does not stand for "Wireless Fidelity." It is a brand name created by the Wi-Fi Alliance for wireless networking technology based on IEEE 802.11 standards. The name "Wi-Fi" was chosen as a catchy marketing term.
**Shortcut / rule:** Wi-Fi = brand name (not "Wireless Fidelity"); IEEE 802.11 = standard; Wi-Fi Alliance = certification body; Bluetooth = short-range wireless (named after Harald Bluetooth); 4G/5G = mobile data; Li-Fi = Light Fidelity (uses visible light for data).

### Q971 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Aspirational Districts Programme was launched in:

A. 2016
B. 2018
C. 2019
D. 2020

**Explanation:** The Aspirational Districts Programme was launched in January 2018 by NITI Aayog to rapidly transform 112 most underdeveloped districts across India. It focuses on 5 themes: Health & Nutrition, Education, Agriculture & Water Resources, Financial Inclusion & Skill Development, and Basic Infrastructure.
