### Q972 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Morley-Minto Reforms introduced:

A. Dyarchy
B. Separate electorates for Muslims
C. Universal adult franchise
D. Provincial autonomy

**Answer:** B. Separate electorates for Muslims
**Explanation:** The Indian Councils Act, 1909 (Morley-Minto Reforms) introduced separate electorates for Muslims for the first time, based on religion. Lord Minto is called the "Father of Communal Electorate." It also increased the size of legislative councils.
**Shortcut / rule:** Morley-Minto (1909) = separate electorates; Montagu-Chelmsford (1919) = Dyarchy in provinces; GoI Act 1935 = Provincial autonomy, All India Federation; Indian Independence Act 1947 = partition, independence; Key: M-M (1909) → M-C (1919) → GoI (1935) → Independence (1947).

### Q973 · Indian Polity & Governance

The first woman Chief Minister of an Indian state was:

A. Sarojini Naidu
B. Sucheta Kriplani
C. Indira Gandhi
D. Jayalalithaa

**Answer:** B. Sucheta Kriplani
**Explanation:** Sucheta Kriplani became the first woman Chief Minister of an Indian state when she was appointed CM of Uttar Pradesh on 2 October 1963. She served until 1967. She was also an active participant in the Indian freedom struggle.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st woman CM: Sucheta Kriplani (UP, 1963); 1st woman PM: Indira Gandhi (1966); 1st woman President: Pratibha Patil (2007); 1st woman Governor: Sarojini Naidu (UP, 1947); 1st woman SC Judge: M. Fathima Beevi (1989); 1st woman Speaker: Meira Kumar (2009).

### Q974 · Physics

Which of the following rays is used in a TV remote?

A. Ultraviolet rays
B. X-rays
C. Infrared rays
D. Gamma rays

**Answer:** C. Infrared rays
**Explanation:** TV remotes use infrared (IR) rays to transmit signals to the TV receiver. Infrared radiation has a wavelength longer than visible light but shorter than microwaves. It is also used in night vision devices, thermal imaging, and heat therapy.
**Shortcut / rule:** EM spectrum (increasing wavelength): Gamma → X-rays → UV → Visible → Infrared → Microwave → Radio; IR uses: TV remote, night vision, thermal imaging, heat therapy; UV uses: sterilization, vitamin D synthesis; Microwave uses: cooking, communication.

### Q975 · Chemistry

Which of the following is the most reactive metal?

A. Sodium
B. Potassium
C. Caesium
D. Lithium

**Answer:** C. Caesium
**Explanation:** Caesium (Cs) is the most reactive metal in the reactivity series (excluding Francium, which is radioactive and rare). It reacts explosively with water. Reactivity of alkali metals increases down the group: Li < Na < K < Rb < Cs.
**Shortcut / rule:** Reactivity series (metals): K > Na > Ca > Mg > Al > Zn > Fe > Ni > Sn > Pb > H > Cu > Hg > Ag > Pt > Au; Alkali metals (Group 1): most reactive; Noble metals (Au, Pt): least reactive; Cs = most reactive stable metal.

### Q976 · Indian Economy

The Goods and Services Tax (GST) Council is chaired by:

A. Prime Minister
B. Finance Minister of India
C. RBI Governor
D. Chief Economic Adviser

**Answer:** B. Finance Minister of India
**Explanation:** The GST Council is chaired by the Union Finance Minister and includes Finance Ministers of all states and UTs. It is a constitutional body under Article 279A. It makes recommendations on tax rates, exemptions, threshold limits, and dispute resolution.
**Shortcut / rule:** GST Council: Art 279A; Chair: Union FM; Members: State FMs; Decisions by 3/4 majority; Centre = 1/3 vote; States = 2/3 vote; Secretariat in New Delhi; Key decisions: rate changes, exemptions, compliance rules.

### Q977 · Biology / Life Science

The study of bones is called:

A. Osteology
B. Oncology
C. Ophthalmology
D. Ornithology

**Answer:** A. Osteology
**Explanation:** Osteology is the study of bones. The human body has 206 bones in adults (270 in babies, which fuse as they grow). The smallest bone is the stapes (ear) and the largest is the femur (thigh).
**Shortcut / rule:** Osteology = bones; Oncology = cancer; Ophthalmology = eyes; Ornithology = birds; Cardiology = heart; Dermatology = skin; Neurology = nervous system; Nephrology = kidneys; Herpetology = reptiles; Entomology = insects.

### Q978 · Indian Geography

Which river is known as "Vridha Ganga" (Old Ganga)?

A. Yamuna
B. Godavari
C. Krishna
D. Narmada

**Answer:** B. Godavari
**Explanation:** The Godavari River is known as "Vridha Ganga" (Old Ganga) or "Dakshin Ganga" (Ganga of the South). It is the longest peninsular river (1,465 km), originating from Trimbakeshwar near Nasik, Maharashtra, and flowing through Maharashtra, Telangana, and Andhra Pradesh.
**Shortcut / rule:** Godavari = Vridha Ganga / Dakshin Ganga (longest peninsular river, 1,465 km, Nasik); Krishna = 2nd longest peninsular (1,400 km, Mahabaleshwar); Narmada = longest west-flowing peninsular river (rift valley); Kaveri = Ganga of the South (also).

### Q979 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Elephanta Caves are located near:

A. Delhi
B. Mumbai
C. Chennai
D. Kolkata

**Answer:** B. Mumbai
**Explanation:** The Elephanta Caves are located on Elephanta Island (Gharapuri) in Mumbai Harbour. They contain rock-cut cave temples dating to the 5th-8th centuries, primarily dedicated to Lord Shiva. The main attraction is the Trimurti Sadashiva sculpture. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Elephanta Caves = Mumbai (Shiva, Trimurti sculpture, 5th-8th c, UNESCO); Ajanta = Aurangabad (Buddhist paintings); Ellora = Aurangabad (Buddhist+Hindu+Jain); Badami = Karnataka (Chalukya); Undavalli = AP (Vishnu, Gupta period).

### Q980 · Games & Sports

The term "Slam Dunk" is associated with:

A. Volleyball
B. Basketball
C. Tennis
D. Badminton

**Answer:** B. Basketball
**Explanation:** A "Slam Dunk" in basketball is a shot where a player jumps and forcefully pushes the ball down through the basket from above. The NBA (National Basketball Association) is the premier professional basketball league in the world.
**Shortcut / rule:** Basketball: Slam Dunk, Alley-oop, Three-pointer; Tennis: Ace, Deuce, Volley; Cricket: Googly, Yorker, Maiden; Hockey: Penalty Corner, Bully; Football: Header, Penalty Kick; Basketball court = 28.7m × 15.2m; Hoop height = 10 feet (3.05 m).

### Q981 · Famous Personalities

Who is known as the "Lady with the Lamp"?

A. Mother Teresa
B. Florence Nightingale
C. Marie Curie
D. Helen Keller

**Answer:** B. Florence Nightingale
**Explanation:** Florence Nightingale (1820-1910) is known as the "Lady with the Lamp" for her nighttime rounds caring for wounded soldiers during the Crimean War (1854-56). She is the founder of modern nursing. Her birthday (12 May) is celebrated as International Nurses Day.
**Shortcut / rule:** Florence Nightingale = Lady with the Lamp (nursing, Crimean War); 12 May = International Nurses Day; Mother Teresa = "Saint of the Gutters" (Nobel Peace 1979); Marie Curie = radioactivity (2 Nobels); Helen Keller = deaf-blind activist and author.

### Q982 · Space & Nuclear Program

The James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) was launched in:

A. 2018
B. 2020
C. 2021
D. 2023

**Answer:** C. 2021
**Explanation:** The James Webb Space Telescope was launched on 25 December 2021 by NASA, ESA, and CSA. It is the most powerful space telescope ever built, designed to observe the universe in infrared. It orbits the Sun-Earth L2 Lagrange point, about 1.5 million km from Earth. It succeeded the Hubble Space Telescope.
**Shortcut / rule:** JWST = 25 Dec 2021 (infrared, L2 point, 1.5M km); Hubble = 1990 (visible/UV/near-IR, low Earth orbit); JWST mirror: 6.5m diameter (gold-coated beryllium); NASA+ESA+CSA collaboration; Named after NASA's 2nd administrator.

### Q983 · UN & World Organizations

The International Criminal Court (ICC) is located in:

A. Geneva
B. The Hague
C. Brussels
D. Vienna

**Answer:** B. The Hague
**Explanation:** The International Criminal Court (ICC) is located in The Hague, Netherlands. It was established in 2002 under the Rome Statute (1998) to prosecute individuals for genocide, crimes against humanity, war crimes, and crimes of aggression. It is separate from the ICJ.
**Shortcut / rule:** ICC = The Hague (2002, Rome Statute 1998); ICJ = The Hague (1945, UN principal organ); ICC ≠ ICJ; ICC = criminal (individuals); ICJ = disputes (states); USA, Russia, China, India = NOT ICC members; ICC has 124 member states.

### Q984 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which Indian state has the maximum forest cover?

A. Madhya Pradesh
B. Arunachal Pradesh
C. Chhattisgarh
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** A. Madhya Pradesh
**Explanation:** Madhya Pradesh has the maximum forest cover in India in terms of absolute area (about 77,414 sq km as per India State of Forest Report). However, in terms of percentage of geographical area, Mizoram has the highest forest cover (84.53%), followed by Arunachal Pradesh.
**Shortcut / rule:** Maximum forest cover (area): Madhya Pradesh > Arunachal Pradesh > Chhattisgarh; Maximum forest cover (%): Mizoram > Arunachal Pradesh > Meghalaya; FSI publishes biennial report (ISFR); Total forest cover: ~21.71% of India's area.

### Q985 · Computers & Applications

What is Cloud Computing?

A. Computing done on clouds
B. Delivery of computing services over the internet

**Explanation:** Cloud computing is the delivery of computing services (servers, storage, databases, networking, software, analytics, intelligence) over the internet ("the cloud") to offer faster innovation, flexible resources, and economies of scale. Major providers: AWS (Amazon), Azure (Microsoft), GCP (Google).

### Q986 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

IRCTC stands for:

A. Indian Railway Catering and Tourism Corporation
B. Indian Railway Central Transport Corporation

**Explanation:** IRCTC (Indian Railway Catering and Tourism Corporation) was established on 27 September 1999. It is a subsidiary of Indian Railways that handles catering, tourism, and online ticketing services. The IRCTC website is one of the most visited e-commerce sites in Asia.

### Q987 · Flagship Govt Programs

The One Nation One Ration Card scheme allows:

A. Free ration to all citizens
B. Portability of ration card across states

**Answer:** B. Portability of ration card across states
**Explanation:** One Nation One Ration Card (ONORC) scheme enables migrant workers and their families to access their entitled food grains from any Fair Price Shop (FPS) in the country using their existing ration card with biometric authentication, ensuring food security portability.

### Q988 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Cripps Mission came to India in:

A. 1940
B. 1942
C. 1944
D. 1946

**Answer:** B. 1942
**Explanation:** The Cripps Mission came to India in March 1942, led by Sir Stafford Cripps. It proposed Dominion Status after WWII and the right for provinces to secede. It was rejected by both Congress and Muslim League. Gandhi called it a "post-dated cheque on a crashing bank."
**Shortcut / rule:** Cripps Mission = 1942 ("post-dated cheque" — Gandhi); Simon Commission = 1928; August Offer = 1940; Cabinet Mission = 1946 (3-tier federal structure); Mountbatten Plan = June 1947 (partition); Wavell Plan = 1945 (Shimla Conference).

### Q989 · Indian Polity & Governance

The first General Elections in India were held in:

A. 1950
B. 1951-52
C. 1953
D. 1955

**Answer:** B. 1951-52
**Explanation:** The first General Elections in India were held from 25 October 1951 to 21 February 1952. The Indian National Congress won 364 out of 489 seats in Lok Sabha under the leadership of Jawaharlal Nehru. Sukumar Sen was the first Chief Election Commissioner who conducted these elections.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st elections: 1951-52; Congress won 364/489 seats; Nehru = PM; Sukumar Sen = CEC; Universal adult franchise (21 years, later 18 by 61st Amendment 1988); Largest democracy; 2024 = 18th Lok Sabha elections.

### Q990 · Physics

Diamonds shine due to:

A. Reflection
B. Refraction
C. Total internal reflection
D. Diffraction

**Answer:** C. Total internal reflection
**Explanation:** Diamonds shine (brilliance) due to total internal reflection. Diamond has a very high refractive index (2.42), giving it a small critical angle (~24.4°). Light entering the diamond undergoes multiple total internal reflections before emerging, creating the characteristic sparkle.
**Shortcut / rule:** Diamond brilliance = total internal reflection (high RI = 2.42, low critical angle); Dispersion = fire/rainbow colours in diamond; Cut shape maximizes reflections; Total internal reflection: also used in optical fibre, mirage, sparkling of gemstones.

### Q991 · Chemistry

Which gas is released during the process of photosynthesis?

A. Carbon dioxide
B. Nitrogen
C. Oxygen
D. Hydrogen

**Answer:** C. Oxygen
**Explanation:** During photosynthesis, plants absorb carbon dioxide (CO₂) and water (H₂O) in the presence of sunlight and chlorophyll, and release oxygen (O₂) as a byproduct. The chemical equation is: 6CO₂ + 6H₂O → C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6O₂.
**Shortcut / rule:** Photosynthesis: CO₂ + H₂O → Glucose + O₂ (sunlight, chlorophyll); Respiration: Glucose + O₂ → CO₂ + H₂O + Energy (reverse); Photosynthesis = anabolic (builds); Respiration = catabolic (breaks down); Green plants = producers in food chain.

### Q992 · Indian Economy

Who is the current chairman of NITI Aayog?

A. Finance Minister
B. Prime Minister
C. President
D. Vice President

**Explanation:** The Prime Minister of India is the chairman of NITI Aayog (National Institution for Transforming India). NITI Aayog replaced the Planning Commission on 1 January 2015. It has a CEO appointed by the PM and a Vice Chairman with the rank of Cabinet Minister.
**Shortcut / rule:** NITI Aayog: Chairman = PM; replaced Planning Commission (1 Jan 2015); CEO = appointed by PM; Vice Chairman = Cabinet rank; Functions: policy think tank, cooperative federalism, monitoring SDGs; Does NOT allocate funds (unlike erstwhile Planning Commission).

### Q993 · Biology / Life Science

Penicillin is obtained from:

A. Bacteria
B. Algae
C. Fungus
D. Virus

**Answer:** C. Fungus
**Explanation:** Penicillin is obtained from the fungus Penicillium notatum (now reclassified as Penicillium chrysogenum). It was discovered by Alexander Fleming in 1928. Penicillin was the first antibiotic and revolutionized the treatment of bacterial infections.

### Q994 · Indian Geography

Which is the smallest state of India by area?

A. Sikkim
B. Tripura
C. Goa
D. Mizoram

**Explanation:** Goa is the smallest Indian state by area (3,702 sq km). It is located on the western coast and was liberated from Portuguese rule on 19 December 1961 (Goa Liberation Day). It became a full state on 30 May 1987 (previously a Union Territory).

### Q995 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Warli painting is a folk art from:

A. Rajasthan
B. Maharashtra
C. Madhya Pradesh

**Explanation:** Warli painting is a tribal art form originating from the Warli tribe in the Sahyadri Range of Maharashtra. The first Indian woman to win an Olympic medal is:

A. PV Sindhu

**Shortcut / rule:** Karnam Malleswari = 1st Indian woman Olympic medalist (bronze, Weightlifting, Sydney 2000); Saina Nehwal = Badminton (bronze, London 2012); PV Sindhu = Badminton (silver, Rio 2016 + bronze, Tokyo 2020); Mary Kom = Boxing (bronze, London 2012).

### Q997 · Famous Personalities

Who is the first Indian woman in space?

A. Sunita Williams
B. Kalpana Chawla
C. Sirisha Bandla
D. Tessy Thomas

**Answer:** B. Kalpana Chawla
**Explanation:** Kalpana Chawla was the first Indian-origin woman to go to space. She first flew on the Space Shuttle Columbia (STS-87) in 1997. She tragically died on 1 February 2003 when the Space Shuttle Columbia (STS-107) disintegrated during re-entry.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kalpana Chawla = 1st Indian-origin woman in space (1997); Died: 1 Feb 2003 (Columbia disaster); Rakesh Sharma = 1st Indian in space (1984); Sunita Williams = Indian-American astronaut (record-holder for spacewalks by a woman).

### Q998 · Space & Nuclear Program

The headquarter of the Indian Space Research Organisation (ISRO) is located in:

A. New Delhi
B. Sriharikota
C. Bengaluru
D. Thiruvananthapuram

**Answer:** C. Bengaluru
**Explanation:** ISRO headquarters is located in Bengaluru, Karnataka. It was established on 15 August 1969 by Dr. Vikram Sarabhai, who is known as the Father of the Indian Space Programme. ISRO operates under the Department of Space (DOS), which reports directly to the Prime Minister.
**Shortcut / rule:** ISRO HQ = Bengaluru (1969); SDSC-SHAR = Sriharikota (launch centre); VSSC = Thiruvananthapuram (rockets); SAC = Ahmedabad (applications); ISTRAC = Bengaluru (tracking); NRSC = Hyderabad (remote sensing); DOS reports to PM.

### Q999 · UN & World Organizations

Which organization publishes the Human Development Index (HDI)?

A. World Bank
B. IMF
C. UNDP
D. WHO

**Answer:** C. UNDP
**Explanation:** The Human Development Index (HDI) is published by the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP) in its annual Human Development Report. HDI measures development based on three dimensions: life expectancy, education (mean and expected years of schooling), and per capita income (GNI). It was developed by Mahbub ul Haq and Amartya Sen.
**Shortcut / rule:** HDI = UNDP (since 1990); Created by: Mahbub ul Haq & Amartya Sen; 3 dimensions: health (life expectancy), education (schooling years), income (GNI per capita); Scale: 0-1; India's HDI rank: ~130-134; Top: Norway/Switzerland; World Bank publishes: Ease of Doing Business.

### Q1000 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

India celebrates its Independence Day on 15 August because:

A. The Indian Independence Act came into effect on that date
B. Lord Mountbatten chose this date as the anniversary of Japan's surrender in WWII
C. The last British soldier left India on this date
D. The Constituent Assembly passed a resolution on this date

**Answer:** B. Lord Mountbatten chose this date as the anniversary of Japan's surrender in WWII
**Explanation:** Lord Mountbatten chose 15 August 1947 as Independence Day because it was the second anniversary of Japan's surrender to the Allied Forces on 15 August 1945, ending World War II. At midnight on 14-15 August 1947, Jawaharlal Nehru delivered the famous "Tryst with Destiny" speech in the Constituent Assembly.
**Shortcut / rule:** 15 Aug 1947 = Independence Day (Mountbatten chose it); "Tryst with Destiny" = Nehru (midnight speech); 1st flag hoisting: Red Fort, 16 Aug 1947; 26 Jan 1950 = Republic Day; Mountbatten = last Viceroy & 1st Governor-General of independent India.


### Q801 · Biology / Life Science

Which disease is caused by the deficiency of Vitamin D?

A. Scurvy
B. Rickets
C. Beriberi
D. Pellagra

**Answer:** B. Rickets
**Explanation:** Vitamin D deficiency causes Rickets in children (soft and weak bones) and Osteomalacia in adults. Vitamin D is synthesized in the skin when exposed to sunlight and helps in absorption of calcium and phosphorus.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vitamin D = Rickets/Osteomalacia (Sunshine vitamin); A = Night blindness; B1 = Beriberi; B3 = Pellagra; B12 = Anaemia; C = Scurvy; E = Sterility; K = Bleeding.

### Q802 · Indian Geography

The Thar Desert is located in which Indian state?

A. Gujarat
B. Rajasthan
C. Madhya Pradesh
D. Haryana

**Answer:** B. Rajasthan
**Explanation:** The Thar Desert (Great Indian Desert) is located mainly in Rajasthan and extends into Gujarat, Punjab, and Haryana. It covers about 200,000 sq km and is bordered by the Aravalli Range on the east. The Indira Gandhi Canal brings water from the Sutlej and Beas rivers to this region.
**Shortcut / rule:** Thar = Rajasthan (Great Indian Desert); Rann of Kutch = Gujarat (salt marsh); Aravalli = oldest fold mountains in India; Luni = only major river in Thar; Sam Sand Dunes = Jaisalmer (tourist spot).

### Q803 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Sangam literature belongs to which language?

A. Sanskrit
B. Tamil
C. Kannada
D. Telugu

**Answer:** B. Tamil
**Explanation:** Sangam literature is a body of classical Tamil literature created between the 3rd century BCE and 3rd century CE. The Sangam was an academy of Tamil poets held in Madurai under the patronage of Pandya kings. It includes Ettuthogai (Eight Anthologies) and Pattuppattu (Ten Idylls).
**Shortcut / rule:** Sangam = Tamil (Madurai, Pandya kings); 3 Sangams: 1st at South Madurai, 2nd at Kapatapuram, 3rd at Madurai; Tolkappiyam = oldest Tamil grammar; Tirukkural = by Thiruvalluvar (ethical text).

### Q804 · Games & Sports

The Dronacharya Award is given for excellence in:

A. Sports performance
B. Sports coaching
C. Lifetime achievement in sports
D. Sports journalism

**Answer:** B. Sports coaching
**Explanation:** The Dronacharya Award is given to outstanding coaches who have trained sportspersons who have achieved medals in international competitions. It is named after Dronacharya, the royal guru of Pandavas and Kauravas in the Mahabharata.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dronacharya = coaching; Arjuna Award = outstanding sports performance; Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna (now Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna) = highest sporting honour; Dhyan Chand Award = lifetime achievement in sports.

### Q805 · Famous Personalities

Who is known as the "Nightingale of India"?

A. Lata Mangeshkar
B. Sarojini Naidu
C. M.S. Subbulakshmi
D. Asha Bhosle

**Answer:** B. Sarojini Naidu
**Explanation:** Sarojini Naidu was called the "Nightingale of India" (Bharat Kokila) for her beautiful poetry. She was the first Indian woman to become the President of the Indian National Congress (1925) and the first woman Governor of an Indian state (United Provinces, 1947).
**Shortcut / rule:** Sarojini Naidu = Nightingale of India + 1st woman Congress President (1925) + 1st woman Governor (UP); Her poems: "The Golden Threshold", "The Bird of Time"; Born: 13 Feb (observed as National Women's Day).

### Q806 · Space & Nuclear Program

The Pokhran nuclear tests (Pokhran-II) were conducted in:

A. 1974
B. 1988
C. 1998
D. 2004

**Answer:** C. 1998
**Explanation:** India conducted five nuclear tests at Pokhran, Rajasthan on 11 and 13 May 1998 (Operation Shakti/Pokhran-II) under PM Atal Bihari Vajpayee. The first nuclear test (Pokhran-I/Smiling Buddha) was conducted on 18 May 1974 under PM Indira Gandhi.
**Shortcut / rule:** Pokhran-I = 18 May 1974 (Smiling Buddha, Indira Gandhi); Pokhran-II = 11 & 13 May 1998 (Operation Shakti, Vajpayee); 11 May = National Technology Day; Lead scientist Pokhran-II: A.P.J. Abdul Kalam & R. Chidambaram.

### Q807 · UN & World Organizations

The International Monetary Fund (IMF) was established in:

A. 1944
B. 1945
C. 1950
D. 1960

**Answer:** B. 1945
**Explanation:** The IMF was conceived at the Bretton Woods Conference in July 1944 and formally established on 27 December 1945 when 29 countries signed its Articles of Agreement. It is headquartered in Washington DC and aims to ensure global monetary stability.
**Shortcut / rule:** IMF = Washington DC (1945, Bretton Woods); 190 member countries; Currency: SDR (Special Drawing Rights); MD elected for 5-year term; India = founding member; Functions: balance of payments support, exchange rate stability.

### Q808 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The "Chipko Movement" is associated with:

A. Conservation of forests
B. Water conservation
C. Air pollution control
D. Wildlife protection

**Answer:** A. Conservation of forests
**Explanation:** The Chipko Movement (1973) was a forest conservation movement in Uttarakhand (then Uttar Pradesh). Villagers, especially women led by Gaura Devi, hugged trees to prevent them from being cut down. Sunderlal Bahuguna and Chandi Prasad Bhatt were key leaders.
**Shortcut / rule:** Chipko = tree-hugging (1973, Uttarakhand); Sunderlal Bahuguna (key propagator); Appiko Movement = Karnataka (similar, 1983); Narmada Bachao Andolan = dam opposition (Medha Patkar); Bishnoi community = earliest conservationists (Rajasthan).

### Q809 · Computers & Applications

1 Kilobyte (KB) is equal to:

A. 1000 bytes
B. 1024 bytes
C. 512 bytes
D. 2048 bytes

**Answer:** B. 1024 bytes
**Explanation:** In computing, 1 Kilobyte (KB) = 1024 bytes (2¹⁰). The sequence is: Byte → Kilobyte (KB) → Megabyte (MB) → Gigabyte (GB) → Terabyte (TB) → Petabyte (PB), each being 1024 times the previous unit.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1 KB = 1024 B; 1 MB = 1024 KB; 1 GB = 1024 MB; 1 TB = 1024 GB; 1 bit = smallest unit (0 or 1); 1 byte = 8 bits; 1 nibble = 4 bits.

### Q810 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

NATO stands for:

A. North American Treaty Organization
B. North Atlantic Treaty Organization
C. National Allied Treaty Organization
D. Northern Atlantic Territorial Organization

**Answer:** B. North Atlantic Treaty Organization
**Explanation:** NATO (North Atlantic Treaty Organization) is a military alliance established on 4 April 1949 by the Washington Treaty. Its headquarters is in Brussels, Belgium. It currently has 32 member countries. India is not a member of NATO.
**Shortcut / rule:** NATO = Brussels (1949); 32 members (Finland 2023, Sweden 2024); Article 5 = collective defense; QUAD = India, USA, Japan, Australia; BRICS = Brazil, Russia, India, China, South Africa + expanded.

### Q811 · Flagship Govt Programs

The MUDRA Yojana provides loans up to:

A. ₹5 lakh
B. ₹10 lakh
C. ₹15 lakh
D. ₹20 lakh

**Answer:** B. ₹10 lakh
**Explanation:** Pradhan Mantri MUDRA Yojana (PMMY) was launched on 8 April 2015 to provide loans up to ₹10 lakh to non-corporate, non-farm small and micro enterprises. MUDRA = Micro Units Development and Refinance Agency.
**Shortcut / rule:** MUDRA categories: Shishu (up to ₹50,000), Kishore (₹50,001-₹5 lakh), Tarun (₹5,00,001-₹10 lakh); No collateral required; Target: micro enterprises, street vendors, small businesses.

### Q812 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who presided over the Lahore Session of Congress (1929) where "Purna Swaraj" was declared?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Subhash Chandra Bose
C. Jawaharlal Nehru
D. Motilal Nehru

**Answer:** C. Jawaharlal Nehru
**Explanation:** Jawaharlal Nehru presided over the Lahore Session of the Indian National Congress in December 1929, where the demand for "Purna Swaraj" (Complete Independence) was adopted. The Indian flag was unfurled on the banks of the Ravi River on 31 December 1929. 26 January 1930 was celebrated as the first Independence Day.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lahore 1929 = Nehru (Purna Swaraj); Lucknow 1916 = Ambika Charan Majumdar (Congress-League Pact); Calcutta 1920 = Lala Lajpat Rai (special session, NCM); Karachi 1931 = Sardar Patel (Fundamental Rights resolution).

### Q813 · Indian Polity & Governance

The 42nd Amendment to the Indian Constitution is known as:

A. Mini Constitution
B. First Amendment
C. Right to Education Amendment
D. Anti-Defection Amendment

**Answer:** A. Mini Constitution
**Explanation:** The 42nd Amendment Act (1976), passed during the Emergency, is called the "Mini Constitution" because it made the most extensive changes: added "Socialist", "Secular" and "Integrity" to the Preamble, added Fundamental Duties (Part IV-A), and curtailed judicial review.
**Shortcut / rule:** 42nd (1976) = Mini Constitution (Emergency, Indira Gandhi); 44th (1978) = undid many 42nd changes; 73rd (1992) = Panchayats; 74th (1992) = Municipalities; 86th (2002) = Right to Education (Art 21A); 101st (2016) = GST.

### Q814 · Physics

Which mirror is used in the headlights of vehicles?

A. Plane mirror
B. Convex mirror
C. Concave mirror
D. Cylindrical mirror

**Answer:** C. Concave mirror
**Explanation:** Concave mirrors are used in vehicle headlights because they produce powerful parallel beams of light when the light source is placed at the focus. They converge light rays. Convex mirrors are used as rear-view mirrors because they give a wider field of view.
**Shortcut / rule:** Concave mirror: headlights, solar furnace, shaving mirror, dentist mirror (converging); Convex mirror: rear-view mirror, street lights, ATM security (diverging, wider view); Plane mirror: looking glass, periscope.

### Q815 · Chemistry

The gas used in fire extinguishers is:

A. Oxygen
B. Hydrogen
C. Carbon dioxide
D. Nitrogen

**Answer:** C. Carbon dioxide
**Explanation:** Carbon dioxide (CO₂) is used in fire extinguishers because it is heavier than air, does not support combustion, and blankets the fire by cutting off the oxygen supply. It is also called "dry ice" in solid form.
**Shortcut / rule:** CO₂ = fire extinguisher (heavier than air, non-combustible); Dry ice = solid CO₂ (−78.5°C); CO₂ is a greenhouse gas; Used in soda water (carbonation); Plants absorb CO₂ in photosynthesis.

### Q816 · Indian Economy

Which Five Year Plan in India focused on rapid industrialization?

A. First Five Year Plan
B. Second Five Year Plan
C. Third Five Year Plan
D. Fourth Five Year Plan

**Answer:** B. Second Five Year Plan
**Explanation:** The Second Five Year Plan (1956-61), also called the Mahalanobis Plan, focused on rapid industrialization and development of heavy industries. The First Plan (1951-56) focused on agriculture. India had 13 Five Year Plans before NITI Aayog replaced the Planning Commission.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1st Plan (1951-56) = Agriculture (Harrod-Domar Model); 2nd Plan (1956-61) = Industrialization (Mahalanobis Model); 12th Plan = last (2012-17); NITI Aayog replaced Planning Commission on 1 Jan 2015.

### Q817 · Biology / Life Science

The normal human body temperature is:

A. 36°C
B. 37°C (98.6°F)
C. 38°C
D. 39°C

**Answer:** B. 37°C (98.6°F)
**Explanation:** The normal human body temperature is approximately 37°C or 98.6°F. It is regulated by the hypothalamus in the brain. Body temperature above 38°C (100.4°F) indicates fever.
**Shortcut / rule:** Normal body temp = 37°C / 98.6°F; Hypothalamus = body thermostat; Conversion: °F = (9/5 × °C) + 32; °C = 5/9 × (°F − 32); 0°C = 32°F (water freezes); 100°C = 212°F (water boils).

### Q818 · Indian Geography

The longest dam in India is:

A. Bhakra Nangal Dam
B. Hirakud Dam
C. Tehri Dam
D. Nagarjuna Sagar Dam

**Answer:** B. Hirakud Dam
**Explanation:** Hirakud Dam on the Mahanadi River in Odisha is the longest dam in India (about 25.8 km including dykes). It was one of the first major multipurpose river valley projects in India. The tallest dam in India is Tehri Dam (260.5 m) on the Bhagirathi River in Uttarakhand.
**Shortcut / rule:** Longest dam: Hirakud (Mahanadi, Odisha, 25.8 km); Tallest dam: Tehri (Bhagirathi, Uttarakhand, 260.5 m); Highest: Bhakra Nangal (Sutlej, HP/Punjab, 225.5 m, gravity dam); Nagarjuna Sagar = Krishna, Telangana.

### Q819 · Art, Culture & Literature

Who wrote the national song "Vande Mataram"?

A. Rabindranath Tagore
B. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay
C. Muhammad Iqbal
D. Subramania Bharati

**Answer:** B. Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay
**Explanation:** "Vande Mataram" was written by Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay in his novel "Anandamath" (1882). It was first sung at the 1896 session of the Indian National Congress by Rabindranath Tagore. It was adopted as the National Song on 24 January 1950.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vande Mataram = Bankim Chandra (Anandamath, 1882); National Song adopted: 24 Jan 1950; National Anthem "Jana Gana Mana" = Rabindranath Tagore (1911, adopted 24 Jan 1950); Both adopted on the same day.

### Q820 · Games & Sports

The Ranji Trophy is associated with which sport?

A. Hockey
B. Football
C. Cricket
D. Tennis

**Answer:** C. Cricket
**Explanation:** The Ranji Trophy is India's premier first-class cricket tournament, named after Ranjitsinhji (Maharaja of Nawanagar). It was started in 1934-35. Mumbai has won the most titles. BCCI organizes this tournament.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ranji Trophy = Cricket (1st class, since 1934); Duleep Trophy = Cricket (zonal); Irani Cup = Ranji champion vs Rest of India; Santosh Trophy = Football; Beighton Cup = Hockey; Davis Cup = Tennis (men's team).

### Q821 · Famous Personalities

Bhagat Singh, Rajguru, and Sukhdev were executed on:

A. 23 March 1929
B. 23 March 1931
C. 14 February 1931
D. 30 January 1931

**Answer:** B. 23 March 1931
**Explanation:** Bhagat Singh, Rajguru, and Sukhdev were hanged on 23 March 1931 in Lahore jail for the murder of British police officer J.P. Saunders (mistaken for Superintendent James Scott). 23 March is observed as Shaheed Diwas (Martyrs' Day).
**Shortcut / rule:** 23 March 1931 = Bhagat Singh, Rajguru, Sukhdev executed (Shaheed Diwas); They killed Saunders (1928) to avenge Lala Lajpat Rai; Bhagat Singh & Batukeshwar Dutt threw bombs in Assembly (8 Apr 1929).

### Q822 · Space & Nuclear Program

Which Indian satellite navigation system provides regional coverage?

A. GPS
B. GLONASS
C. NavIC (IRNSS)
D. Galileo

**Answer:** C. NavIC (IRNSS)
**Explanation:** NavIC (Navigation with Indian Constellation), also known as IRNSS (Indian Regional Navigation Satellite System), is India's autonomous regional satellite navigation system developed by ISRO. It has 7 satellites and provides coverage over India and the surrounding region (1,500 km).
**Shortcut / rule:** NavIC/IRNSS = India (7 satellites, regional); GPS = USA (31 satellites, global); GLONASS = Russia; Galileo = EU; BeiDou = China; QZSS = Japan; NavIC accuracy: 10-20 metres.

### Q823 · UN & World Organizations

The Security Council of the United Nations has how many permanent members?

A. 3
B. 5
C. 7
D. 10

**Answer:** B. 5
**Explanation:** The UN Security Council has 5 permanent members (P5): USA, UK, France, Russia, and China. Each has veto power. The Council also has 10 non-permanent members elected for 2-year terms. India has been a non-permanent member 8 times.
**Shortcut / rule:** P5 = USA, UK, France, Russia, China (veto power); 10 non-permanent (2-year terms, no veto); Total = 15 members; Decisions need 9 affirmative votes including all P5; India's demand: permanent seat in expanded UNSC.

### Q824 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which gas is primarily responsible for the depletion of the ozone layer?

A. Carbon dioxide
B. Methane
C. Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)
D. Sulphur dioxide

**Answer:** C. Chlorofluorocarbons (CFCs)
**Explanation:** CFCs (Chlorofluorocarbons), used in refrigerators, air conditioners, and aerosol sprays, are the primary cause of ozone layer depletion. When CFCs reach the stratosphere, UV radiation breaks them down, releasing chlorine atoms that destroy ozone molecules.
**Shortcut / rule:** CFCs = ozone depletion; Montreal Protocol (1987) = phased out CFCs; Ozone hole = largest over Antarctica; 1 Cl atom can destroy 100,000 O₃ molecules; Alternatives: HFCs, HCFCs (less damaging).

### Q825 · Computers & Applications

The first computer programmer in history is considered to be:

A. Charles Babbage
B. Ada Lovelace
C. Alan Turing
D. John von Neumann

**Answer:** B. Ada Lovelace
**Explanation:** Ada Lovelace (Augusta Ada King, Countess of Lovelace) is considered the first computer programmer for her work on Charles Babbage's Analytical Engine in the 1840s. She wrote the first algorithm intended to be processed by a machine.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ada Lovelace = 1st programmer; Charles Babbage = Father of Computer (Analytical Engine); Alan Turing = Father of AI & Computer Science; John von Neumann = stored-program concept; Tim Berners-Lee = WWW.

### Q826 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The Golden Quadrilateral connects:

A. 4 metro cities
B. 4 holy cities

**Explanation:** The Golden Quadrilateral is a network of highways connecting India's four major metros: Delhi, Mumbai, Chennai, and Kolkata. It is about 5,846 km long and was initiated by PM Atal Bihari Vajpayee under the NHDP (National Highways Development Project).
**Shortcut / rule:** Golden Quadrilateral: Delhi-Mumbai-Chennai-Kolkata (5,846 km); North-South corridor: Srinagar-Kanyakumari; East-West corridor: Silchar-Porbandar; NHAI (National Highways Authority of India) = implementing agency.

### Q827 · Flagship Govt Programs

Under PM Ujjwala Yojana, free LPG connections are provided to:

A. All households

**Explanation:** Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana (PMUY) was launched on 1 May 2016 from Ballia, UP to provide free LPG connections to women from Below Poverty Line (BPL) families. The aim was to replace unhealthy cooking fuels (wood, dung, coal) with clean LPG fuel.
**Shortcut / rule:** Ujjwala = 1 May 2016 (Ballia, UP); Free LPG for BPL women; Target: 8 crore connections (Ujjwala 1.0); Ujjwala 2.0 = expanded coverage; Reduces indoor air pollution; ₹1,600 subsidy per connection.

### Q828 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Dandi March was undertaken to protest against:

A. Land revenue
B. Salt tax
C. Income tax
D. Import duties

**Answer:** B. Salt tax
**Explanation:** The Dandi March (Salt March) was undertaken by Mahatma Gandhi from Sabarmati Ashram to Dandi (a coastal village in Gujarat) from 12 March to 6 April 1930 to protest against the British salt tax. Gandhi picked up a lump of natural salt, breaking the Salt Laws and launching the Civil Disobedience Movement.
**Shortcut / rule:** Dandi March = 12 Mar – 6 Apr 1930 (385 km, 24 days); 78 volunteers; Broke Salt Law → Civil Disobedience Movement; Gandhi-Irwin Pact (5 Mar 1931) ended movement temporarily.

### Q829 · Indian Polity & Governance

The concept of "Basic Structure" of the Constitution was established in which case?

A. Golaknath Case (1967)
B. Kesavananda Bharati Case (1973)
C. Minerva Mills Case (1980)
D. Maneka Gandhi Case (1978)

**Answer:** B. Kesavananda Bharati Case (1973)
**Explanation:** In the Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala case (1973), the Supreme Court established the "Basic Structure Doctrine," ruling that Parliament can amend any part of the Constitution but cannot alter its basic structure. This landmark judgment was decided by a 13-judge bench (7:6 majority).
**Shortcut / rule:** Kesavananda Bharati (1973) = Basic Structure doctrine; Golaknath (1967) = Parliament cannot amend FRs; Minerva Mills (1980) = reaffirmed basic structure; Basic structure includes: supremacy of Constitution, separation of powers, federalism, secularism, judicial review.

### Q830 · Physics

The SI unit of power is:

A. Joule
B. Newton
C. Watt
D. Pascal

**Answer:** C. Watt
**Explanation:** Watt (W) is the SI unit of power, named after James Watt. Power is the rate of doing work or the rate of energy transfer. 1 Watt = 1 Joule per second. The commercial unit of electrical energy is kilowatt-hour (kWh).
**Shortcut / rule:** Power = Watt (W) = J/s; Energy = Joule; 1 kWh = 3.6 × 10⁶ J = 1 unit of electricity; 1 HP (horse power) = 746 W; P = VI (electrical power) = V²/R = I²R.

### Q831 · Chemistry

Which element is found in all organic compounds?

A. Nitrogen
B. Oxygen
C. Carbon
D. Hydrogen

**Answer:** C. Carbon
**Explanation:** Carbon is the essential element present in all organic compounds. It can form four covalent bonds and can create long chains and ring structures. The branch of chemistry dealing with carbon compounds is called Organic Chemistry.
**Shortcut / rule:** Carbon = basis of all organic compounds; Can form 4 bonds; Allotropes: diamond, graphite, fullerene, graphene; Carbon dating = age determination of fossils (C-14); Organic chemistry = study of carbon compounds (except CO₂, CO, carbonates).

### Q832 · Indian Economy

The first bank to be nationalized in India was:

A. State Bank of India
B. Reserve Bank of India
C. Punjab National Bank
D. Imperial Bank of India

**Answer:** A. State Bank of India
**Explanation:** The Imperial Bank of India was nationalized and renamed the State Bank of India (SBI) on 1 July 1955 under the SBI Act, 1955 based on the recommendations of the All India Rural Credit Survey Committee. This was before the major bank nationalization of 14 banks on 19 July 1969.
**Shortcut / rule:** SBI = 1 Jul 1955 (1st nationalized, from Imperial Bank); 14 banks nationalized: 19 Jul 1969 (Indira Gandhi); 6 more banks: 15 Apr 1980; Total: 20 nationalized banks (now merged into fewer entities).

### Q833 · Biology / Life Science

DNA stands for:

A. Di-Nucleic Acid
B. Deoxyribonucleic Acid
C. Deoxyribose Nucleic Acid
D. Both B and C

**Answer:** D. Both B and C
**Explanation:** DNA stands for Deoxyribonucleic Acid. It carries the genetic instructions for the development, functioning, and reproduction of all known organisms. Its double-helix structure was discovered by Watson and Crick in 1953.
**Shortcut / rule:** DNA = double helix (Watson & Crick, 1953); RNA = single strand; DNA bases: A-T, G-C (Adenine-Thymine, Guanine-Cytosine); RNA bases: A-U, G-C; DNA in nucleus; Mitochondrial DNA = maternal inheritance.

Which Indian state is the largest producer of tea?

A. Assam
B. Kerala
C. West Bengal

**Explanation:** Assam is the largest producer of tea in India, contributing over 50% of India's total tea production. India is the second-largest tea producer in the world after China. Darjeeling tea (West Bengal) is famous for its unique flavour and is called the "Champagne of Teas."

### Q835 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Red Fort in Delhi was built by:

A. Akbar
B. Shah Jahan
C. Aurangzeb
D. Humayun

**Answer:** B. Shah Jahan
**Explanation:** The Red Fort (Lal Qila) in Delhi was built by Mughal Emperor Shah Jahan between 1638-1648. It is built of red sandstone and is located along the banks of the Yamuna River. The Indian PM hoists the national flag here on Independence Day.

Which country hosted the 2024 Summer Olympic Games?

A. Japan
B. USA
C. France
D. Australia

### Q837 · Famous Personalities

Who is the first woman Prime Minister of India?

A. Pratibha Patil
B. Indira Gandhi
C. Sarojini Naidu
D. Sushma Swaraj

**Answer:** B. Indira Gandhi
**Explanation:** Indira Gandhi served as the Prime Minister of India for two terms (1966-1977 and 1980-1984). She was the daughter of Jawaharlal Nehru and the first (and so far only) woman PM of India. Pratibha Patil was the first woman President of India (2007-2012).
**Shortcut / rule:** Indira Gandhi = 1st woman PM (1966); Pratibha Patil = 1st woman President (2007); Sarojini Naidu = 1st woman Governor (1947); Sucheta Kriplani = 1st woman CM (UP, 1963); Fathima Beevi = 1st woman SC judge (1989).

### Q838 · Space & Nuclear Program

DRDO stands for:

A. Defence Research and Development Organization
B. Defence Research and Design Organization
C. Department of Research and Development Operations
D. Defence Resource and Development Organization

**Answer:** A. Defence Research and Development Organization
**Explanation:** DRDO (Defence Research and Development Organisation) was established in 1958 and is headquartered in New Delhi. It is under the Ministry of Defence and works on military technology. Key projects include Agni missiles, Tejas aircraft, and Arjun tank.
**Shortcut / rule:** DRDO = New Delhi (1958); Key products: Agni (ballistic missile), Prithvi (tactical missile), Tejas (LCA), Arjun (tank), Akash (air defence), BrahMos (cruise missile, Indo-Russian); DRDO has 50+ labs across India.

### Q839 · UN & World Organizations

The WTO (World Trade Organization) was established in:

A. 1945
B. 1975
C. 1990
D. 1995

**Answer:** D. 1995
**Explanation:** The WTO was established on 1 January 1995, replacing GATT (General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade, 1948). It is headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland and deals with global trade rules. India is a founding member of the WTO.
**Shortcut / rule:** WTO = Geneva (1 Jan 1995, replaced GATT); 164 member countries; Ministerial Conference = highest body (every 2 years); DG appointed for 4-year term; Key agreements: TRIPS, TRIMS, AoA; India = founding member.

### Q840 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which of the following is a biodiversity hotspot in India?

A. Thar Desert
B. Western Ghats
C. Indo-Gangetic Plain
D. Deccan Plateau

**Answer:** B. Western Ghats
**Explanation:** The Western Ghats is one of the 36 global biodiversity hotspots. India has 4 biodiversity hotspots: Western Ghats, Eastern Himalayas, Indo-Burma Region, and Sundaland. The Western Ghats are home to numerous endemic species of flora and fauna.
**Shortcut / rule:** India's 4 biodiversity hotspots: Western Ghats, Eastern Himalayas, Indo-Burma, Sundaland (Nicobar Islands); Biodiversity hotspot criteria: at least 1,500 endemic plant species + lost ≥70% of original habitat; Concept by: Norman Myers.

### Q841 · Computers & Applications

Which programming language is known as the "backbone of the internet"?

A. Java
B. Python
C. HTML
D. C++

**Explanation:** HTML (HyperText Markup Language) is considered the backbone of the internet as every web page is built using HTML. While not a traditional programming language, it structures all web content. JavaScript adds interactivity and CSS adds styling.

### Q842 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

The first metro rail in India was started in:

A. Delhi
B. Mumbai
C. Kolkata

**Answer:** C. Kolkata
**Explanation:** The first metro rail in India was opened in Kolkata on 24 October 1984 between Esplanade and Bhowanipore (now Netaji Bhavan) stations. The Delhi Metro began operations in 2002. Currently, multiple Indian cities have operational metro rail systems.

### Q843 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Digital India programme was launched in:

A. 2014
B. 2015
C. 2016
D. 2017

### Q844 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

Who founded the Arya Samaj?

A. Raja Ram Mohan Roy
B. Swami Dayanand Saraswati
C. Swami Vivekananda
D. Ramakrishna Paramahamsa

**Answer:** B. Swami Dayanand Saraswati
**Explanation:** Swami Dayanand Saraswati founded the Arya Samaj in 1875 in Bombay. His motto was "Back to the Vedas" (Krinvanto Vishwam Aryam). He opposed idol worship, caste system, and advocated for the authority of the Vedas. His book "Satyarth Prakash" is a key text.
**Shortcut / rule:** Arya Samaj = Dayanand Saraswati (1875, Bombay); Brahmo Samaj = Raja Ram Mohan Roy (1828, Calcutta); Ramakrishna Mission = Swami Vivekananda (1897); Theosophical Society = Blavatsky & Olcott (1875, New York; India HQ = Adyar, Madras).

### Q845 · Indian Polity & Governance

Article 370 of the Indian Constitution was related to:

A. Right to Education
B. Special status of Jammu & Kashmir
C. Emergency provisions
D. Amendment procedure

**Answer:** B. Special status of Jammu & Kashmir
**Explanation:** Article 370 granted special autonomous status to the state of Jammu & Kashmir. It was abrogated on 5 August 2019, and J&K was reorganized into two Union Territories: Jammu & Kashmir (with legislature) and Ladakh (without legislature).
**Shortcut / rule:** Article 370 = J&K special status (abrogated 5 Aug 2019); Article 35A = special privileges to J&K residents (also revoked); J&K + Ladakh = 2 UTs; Bifurcation effective: 31 Oct 2019 (Sardar Patel's birthday).

### Q846 · Physics

Which of the following is a vector quantity?

A. Speed
B. Mass
C. Temperature
D. Velocity

**Answer:** D. Velocity
**Explanation:** Velocity is a vector quantity because it has both magnitude and direction. Speed has only magnitude (scalar). Other vector quantities include force, acceleration, displacement, momentum, and torque.
**Shortcut / rule:** Vectors (magnitude + direction): velocity, force, acceleration, displacement, momentum, torque; Scalars (magnitude only): speed, mass, temperature, time, energy, work, power, distance, volume.

### Q847 · Chemistry

Which metal is liquid at room temperature?

A. Iron
B. Aluminium
C. Mercury
D. Zinc

**Answer:** C. Mercury
**Explanation:** Mercury (Hg) is the only metal that is liquid at room temperature (melting point: −38.83°C). It is used in thermometers, barometers, and fluorescent lamps. Bromine is the only non-metal that is liquid at room temperature.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mercury (Hg) = only liquid metal (room temp); Bromine (Br) = only liquid non-metal; Gallium (Ga) melts at ~29.8°C (melts in hand); Mercury symbol Hg from Latin "Hydrargyrum" (liquid silver); Mercury is toxic.

### Q848 · Indian Economy

SEBI stands for:

A. Securities and Exchange Board of India
B. Stock Exchange Bureau of India
C. Securities and Economic Board of India
D. Stock and Exchange Bureau of India

**Answer:** A. Securities and Exchange Board of India
**Explanation:** SEBI (Securities and Exchange Board of India) was established on 12 April 1988 as a non-statutory body and given statutory powers on 30 January 1992. It is headquartered in Mumbai and regulates the Indian securities market to protect investors' interests.
**Shortcut / rule:** SEBI = Mumbai (1988/1992); Regulates: stock exchanges, mutual funds, brokers; BSE (Bombay Stock Exchange) = oldest in Asia (1875); NSE = National Stock Exchange (1992); BSE index = Sensex (30 stocks); NSE index = Nifty (50 stocks).

### Q849 · Biology / Life Science

Which part of the brain controls balance and coordination?

A. Cerebrum
B. Cerebellum
C. Medulla oblongata
D. Hypothalamus

**Answer:** B. Cerebellum
**Explanation:** The cerebellum (located at the back of the brain) controls balance, posture, coordination of voluntary movements, and motor learning. The cerebrum handles thinking, memory, and sensation. The medulla oblongata controls involuntary functions like breathing and heartbeat.
**Shortcut / rule:** Cerebrum = thinking, memory, intelligence (largest part); Cerebellum = balance, coordination; Medulla oblongata = involuntary actions (breathing, heartbeat); Hypothalamus = temperature, hunger, thirst; Pons = bridge between cerebrum & cerebellum.

### Q850 · Indian Geography

Which waterfall is the highest in India?

A. Jog Falls
B. Dudhsagar Falls
C. Kunchikal Falls
D. Athirapilly Falls

**Answer:** C. Kunchikal Falls
**Explanation:** Kunchikal Falls on the Varahi River in Karnataka is the highest waterfall in India at 455 metres. Jog Falls (also in Karnataka, on the Sharavathi River) is the second highest at 253 metres. Jog Falls has 4 cascades: Raja, Rani, Roarer, and Rocket.
**Shortcut / rule:** Kunchikal Falls = highest in India (455 m, Karnataka); Jog Falls = 2nd highest (253 m, Karnataka, 4 cascades); Dudhsagar = Goa/Karnataka (310 m); Angel Falls = world's highest (979 m, Venezuela); Niagara = USA-Canada border.


### Q851 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Hampi ruins are located in which state?

A. Maharashtra
B. Tamil Nadu
C. Karnataka
D. Andhra Pradesh

**Answer:** C. Karnataka
**Explanation:** Hampi in Karnataka was the capital of the Vijayanagara Empire (14th-16th century). The ruins include the Vittala Temple with its famous stone chariot and musical pillars. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site on the banks of the Tungabhadra River.
**Shortcut / rule:** Hampi = Vijayanagara Empire (Karnataka); Vittala Temple = stone chariot + musical pillars; Founded: Harihara & Bukka (1336); Greatest ruler: Krishnadevaraya; Battle of Talikota (1565) = destruction of Vijayanagara.

### Q852 · Games & Sports

The term "Deuce" is used in which sport?

A. Cricket
B. Hockey
C. Tennis
D. Football

**Answer:** C. Tennis
**Explanation:** In tennis, "Deuce" refers to a score of 40-40 (tied at 40 all). After deuce, a player must win two consecutive points (Advantage → Game) to win the game. The term comes from the French "à deux" meaning "at two" (two points from winning).
**Shortcut / rule:** Tennis scoring: 15 → 30 → 40 → Game; Deuce = 40-40; Advantage = point after deuce; Love = 0; LBW = Cricket; Penalty corner = Hockey; Free kick = Football; Birdie = Badminton (also golf).

### Q853 · Famous Personalities

Who established the Servants of India Society?

A. Mahatma Gandhi
B. Bal Gangadhar Tilak
C. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
D. Dadabhai Naoroji

**Answer:** C. Gopal Krishna Gokhale
**Explanation:** Gopal Krishna Gokhale founded the Servants of India Society in 1905 in Pune. He was a moderate leader of the Indian National Congress and is known as Gandhi's political guru. He advocated for social reform and Indian self-governance through constitutional methods.

### Q854 · Space & Nuclear Program

India's reusable launch vehicle technology demonstrator (RLV-TD) was first tested in:

A. 2014
B. 2016
C. 2018
D. 2020

**Answer:** B. 2016
**Explanation:** ISRO's Reusable Launch Vehicle – Technology Demonstrator (RLV-TD) was first tested on 23 May 2016 from Sriharikota. It is a step towards developing a fully reusable two-stage-to-orbit (TSTO) launch vehicle, which could reduce launch costs significantly.
**Shortcut / rule:** RLV-TD = 23 May 2016 (1st test); Nicknamed "Swadeshi Space Shuttle"; Goal: reduce launch cost by 10x; ISRO's future: Gaganyaan (human spaceflight), RLV (reusable), SSLV (Small Satellite Launch Vehicle).

### Q855 · UN & World Organizations

UNICEF primarily works for:

A. World trade
B. Children's welfare
C. Labour rights
D. Environmental protection

**Answer:** B. Children's welfare
**Explanation:** UNICEF (United Nations Children's Fund) works for children's rights, survival, development, and protection. Originally called United Nations International Children's Emergency Fund, it was established on 11 December 1946. Its headquarters is in New York.

### Q856 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which of the following is a greenhouse gas?

A. Nitrogen
B. Oxygen
C. Methane
D. Argon

**Answer:** C. Methane
**Explanation:** Methane (CH₄) is a potent greenhouse gas, about 80 times more effective at trapping heat than CO₂ over a 20-year period. Major sources include livestock, rice paddies, landfills, and fossil fuel extraction. Other greenhouse gases include CO₂, N₂O, and water vapour.
**Shortcut / rule:** Greenhouse gases: CO₂ (most abundant), CH₄ (most potent per molecule), N₂O, CFCs, O₃, water vapour; CO₂ = largest contributor to global warming; Greenhouse effect = natural (keeps Earth warm), enhanced = climate change.

### Q857 · Computers & Applications

What does URL stand for?

A. Uniform Resource Locator
B. Universal Resource Locator
C. Uniform Resource Link
D. Universal Reference Locator

**Answer:** A. Uniform Resource Locator
**Explanation:** URL (Uniform Resource Locator) is the address of a web page or resource on the internet. It typically consists of a protocol (http/https), domain name, and path. URLs were invented by Tim Berners-Lee as part of the World Wide Web.
**Shortcut / rule:** URL = web address; HTTP = HyperText Transfer Protocol; HTTPS = secure version (SSL/TLS); Domain types: .com (commercial), .org (organization), .edu (education), .gov (government), .in (India).

### Q858 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

BRICS originally included how many member countries?

A. 4
B. 5
C. 6
D. 7

**Answer:** B. 5
**Explanation:** BRICS originally consisted of 5 member countries: Brazil, Russia, India, China, and South Africa. It was initially BRIC (coined by Jim O'Neill of Goldman Sachs in 2001), and South Africa joined in 2010 to make it BRICS. In 2024, it expanded to include new members.
**Shortcut / rule:** BRIC = 2001 (concept by Jim O'Neill); BRICS = 2010 (South Africa joined); 2024 expansion: added Egypt, Ethiopia, Iran, UAE, Saudi Arabia; BRICS summits rotate; G7 = USA, UK, France, Germany, Italy, Canada, Japan.

### Q859 · Flagship Govt Programs

The Atal Pension Yojana (APY) guarantees a minimum pension of:

A. ₹500 per month
B. ₹1,000 per month
C. ₹3,000 per month
D. ₹1,000 to ₹5,000 per month

**Answer:** D. ₹1,000 to ₹5,000 per month
**Explanation:** Atal Pension Yojana was launched on 9 May 2015 to provide a defined pension (₹1,000 to ₹5,000 per month) to workers in the unorganized sector after the age of 60. The pension amount depends on the contribution and the age at which the person joins.
**Shortcut / rule:** APY = 9 May 2015 (pension for unorganized sector); Age: 18-40 years (can join); Pension at 60; Fixed pension: ₹1K/2K/3K/4K/5K per month based on contribution; Government co-contribution: 50% (for 5 years) for eligible subscribers.

### Q860 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Lucknow Pact of 1916 was an agreement between:

A. Congress and Muslim League
B. Congress and British
C. Muslim League and British
D. Hindu Mahasabha and Muslim League

**Answer:** A. Congress and Muslim League
**Explanation:** The Lucknow Pact (1916) was an agreement between the Indian National Congress and the All India Muslim League. It marked the first joint demand for self-governance and agreed on separate electorates for Muslims. The pact was facilitated by leaders like Tilak and Jinnah.
**Shortcut / rule:** Lucknow Pact (1916) = Congress + Muslim League unity; Facilitated by Tilak & Jinnah; Jinnah called "Ambassador of Hindu-Muslim Unity" by Sarojini Naidu; Led to: Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms (1919).

### Q861 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Inter-State Council is established under which article?

A. Article 260
B. Article 263
C. Article 267
D. Article 270

**Answer:** B. Article 263
**Explanation:** The Inter-State Council is established under Article 263 of the Constitution to investigate and advise on disputes between states, discuss common interests, and make recommendations for better coordination. It was set up in 1990 based on the Sarkaria Commission recommendation.
**Shortcut / rule:** Art 263 = Inter-State Council (1990, Sarkaria Commission); Art 262 = Inter-State water disputes; Zonal Councils = statutory (States Reorganisation Act, 1956, 5+1 zones); Art 263 = constitutional advisory body.

### Q862 · Physics

The instrument used to measure temperature is:

A. Barometer
B. Thermometer
C. Hygrometer
D. Pyrometer

**Answer:** B. Thermometer
**Explanation:** A thermometer is used to measure temperature. It was invented by Daniel Gabriel Fahrenheit (mercury thermometer, 1714). There are three main temperature scales: Celsius (°C), Fahrenheit (°F), and Kelvin (K). Kelvin is the SI unit of temperature.
**Shortcut / rule:** Thermometer = temperature; Pyrometer = very high temperatures (furnaces); Clinical thermometer range: 35-42°C; Scales: °C (water: 0-100), °F (water: 32-212), K (absolute, 0K = −273.15°C); K = °C + 273.15.

### Q863 · Chemistry

Bleaching powder is chemically:

A. Calcium hypochlorite
B. Sodium hypochlorite
C. Calcium chloride
D. Sodium chloride

**Answer:** A. Calcium hypochlorite
**Explanation:** Bleaching powder (CaOCl₂) is calcium hypochlorite. It is produced by the reaction of chlorine gas with slaked lime (Ca(OH)₂). It is used for bleaching cotton, linen, and wood pulp, and for disinfecting drinking water.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bleaching powder = CaOCl₂ (Ca hypochlorite); Slaked lime = Ca(OH)₂; Quick lime = CaO; Plaster of Paris = CaSO₄·½H₂O; Gypsum = CaSO₄·2H₂O; Bleaching powder used for: water purification + textile bleaching.

### Q864 · Indian Economy

The Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management (FRBM) Act was enacted in:

A. 2000
B. 2003
C. 2005
D. 2008

**Answer:** B. 2003
**Explanation:** The FRBM Act was enacted in 2003 to institutionalize fiscal discipline and reduce fiscal deficit. It set targets to eliminate revenue deficit and reduce fiscal deficit to 3% of GDP. The Act was based on the recommendations of the E.A.S. Sarma Committee.
**Shortcut / rule:** FRBM Act = 2003 (fiscal discipline); Target: Fiscal deficit ≤ 3% of GDP; Revenue deficit = 0; Fiscal deficit = total expenditure − total revenue (excluding borrowings); Revenue deficit = revenue expenditure − revenue receipts.

### Q865 · Biology / Life Science

Which enzyme is present in saliva?

A. Pepsin
B. Trypsin
C. Amylase (Ptyalin)
D. Lipase

**Answer:** C. Amylase (Ptyalin)
**Explanation:** Salivary amylase (ptyalin) is the enzyme present in saliva. It converts starch into maltose (sugar) in the mouth. This is why bread tastes sweet when chewed for a long time. Digestion of carbohydrates begins in the mouth.
**Shortcut / rule:** Mouth: Ptyalin/Amylase (starch → maltose); Stomach: Pepsin (protein), HCl; Small intestine: Trypsin (protein), Lipase (fats), Amylase; Liver: Bile (emulsifies fats); Carbohydrate digestion starts in mouth; Protein in stomach; Fat in small intestine.

### Q866 · Indian Geography

The state with the highest literacy rate in India is:

A. Mizoram
B. Kerala
C. Goa
D. Tripura

**Answer:** B. Kerala
**Explanation:** Kerala has the highest literacy rate in India at 96.2% (Census 2011). The national average literacy rate is 74.04%. Bihar has the lowest literacy rate. Kerala was declared the first fully literate state in India in 1991.
**Shortcut / rule:** Highest literacy: Kerala (96.2%); Lowest: Bihar (63.8%); Highest male literacy: Kerala; Highest female literacy: Kerala; National average: 74.04% (Census 2011); Gender gap in literacy is smallest in Kerala.

### Q867 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Charminar is located in:

A. Lucknow
B. Delhi
C. Hyderabad
D. Ahmedabad

**Answer:** C. Hyderabad
**Explanation:** The Charminar (Four Minarets) is located in Hyderabad, Telangana. It was built by Muhammad Quli Qutb Shah in 1591 to commemorate the end of a plague epidemic. It has four grand arches and minarets at each corner, with a mosque on the top floor.
**Shortcut / rule:** Charminar = Hyderabad (Qutb Shahi dynasty, 1591); Hawa Mahal = Jaipur (Maharaja Sawai Pratap Singh, 1799); Gateway of India = Mumbai (George Wittet, 1924); India Gate = Delhi (Edwin Lutyens, 1931, war memorial).

### Q868 · Games & Sports

The number of players in a hockey team (on field) is:

A. 9
B. 10
C. 11
D. 12

### Q869 · Famous Personalities

Who was the first President of India?

A. Jawaharlal Nehru

### Q870 · Space & Nuclear Program

The Gaganyaan mission is India's:

A. First Mars mission
B. First human spaceflight programme

**Answer:** B. First human spaceflight programme
**Explanation:** Gaganyaan is ISRO's first crewed spaceflight programme, aiming to send Indian astronauts (Vyomanauts) to low Earth orbit for a 3-day mission. The mission includes development of a human-rated launch vehicle (GSLV Mk III/LVM3) and a crew module.

The Nobel Peace Prize is awarded in which city?

A. Stockholm

**Explanation:** The Nobel Peace Prize is awarded in Oslo, Norway by the Norwegian Nobel Committee. All other Nobel Prizes (Physics, Chemistry, Medicine, Literature, Economics) are awarded in Stockholm, Sweden. The prizes are given on 10 December (Alfred Nobel's death anniversary).

### Q872 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

The National Park with the highest population of one-horned rhinoceros is:

A. Jim Corbett

**Explanation:** Kaziranga National Park in Assam has the highest population of one-horned rhinoceros (Indian rhinoceros) in the world, housing about 2/3 of the world's population. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site on the banks of the Brahmaputra River.

### Q874 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

LPG stands for:

A. Liquid Propane Gas
B. Liquefied Petroleum Gas

### Q875 · Flagship Govt Programs

**Explanation:** NEP 2020 replaced the National Policy on Education, 1986 (modified in 1992). ### Q876 · Indian History & Freedom Struggle

The Partition of Bengal took place in:

A. 1899
B. 1905
C. 1911
D. 1919

**Answer:** B. 1905
**Explanation:** The Partition of Bengal was announced by Lord Curzon on 20 July 1905 and implemented on 16 October 1905. It divided Bengal into East Bengal & Assam (Muslim majority) and West Bengal (Hindu majority). It was annulled in 1911 by Lord Hardinge at the Delhi Durbar.
**Shortcut / rule:** Partition of Bengal = 1905 (Curzon); Annulled = 1911 (Delhi Durbar, Hardinge); Swadeshi Movement started as protest; 16 Oct 1905 = Rakhi Bandhan Day (unity); "Amar Sonar Bangla" sung during Swadeshi movement (later Bangladesh national anthem).

### Q877 · Indian Polity & Governance

The Election Commission of India was established in:

A. 1948
B. 1950
C. 1952
D. 1960

**Answer:** B. 1950
**Explanation:** The Election Commission of India (ECI) was established on 25 January 1950 under Article 324 of the Constitution. 25 January is celebrated as National Voters' Day. Sukumar Sen was the first Chief Election Commissioner. The ECI became a multi-member body with 3 members in 1993.
**Shortcut / rule:** ECI = 25 Jan 1950 (Art 324); 25 Jan = National Voters' Day; 1st CEC: Sukumar Sen; Multi-member (3) since 1993; CEC can be removed like SC judge (by Parliament); ECs can be removed on CEC's recommendation.

### Q878 · Physics

The phenomenon responsible for the blue colour of the sky is:

A. Reflection
B. Refraction
C. Scattering of light
D. Dispersion

**Answer:** C. Scattering of light
**Explanation:** The blue colour of the sky is due to Rayleigh scattering. Blue light has a shorter wavelength and is scattered more by atmospheric molecules than red light. At sunset/sunrise, the sky appears red because light travels a longer path through the atmosphere, scattering blue completely.
**Shortcut / rule:** Rayleigh scattering: intensity ∝ 1/λ⁴; Blue scattered most (short λ); Sky = blue (day); Sunset = red/orange (long path, only red reaches); Tyndall effect = scattering in colloids; Raman scattering = inelastic (Nobel 1930).

### Q879 · Chemistry

Baking soda is chemically:

A. Sodium carbonate
B. Sodium bicarbonate
C. Sodium chloride
D. Sodium hydroxide

**Answer:** B. Sodium bicarbonate
**Explanation:** Baking soda is sodium bicarbonate (NaHCO₃), also called sodium hydrogen carbonate. It is used in cooking (as a leavening agent), fire extinguishers, and as an antacid. It reacts with acids to produce CO₂ gas, which makes dough rise.
**Shortcut / rule:** NaHCO₃ = Baking soda; Na₂CO₃ = Washing soda; NaOH = Caustic soda; NaCl = Common salt; CaOCl₂ = Bleaching powder; CaSO₄·½H₂O = Plaster of Paris; KOH = Caustic potash.

### Q880 · Indian Economy

Who is considered the architect of India's economic reforms of 1991?

A. P.V. Narasimha Rao
B. Manmohan Singh
C. Pranab Mukherjee
D. Yashwant Sinha

**Answer:** B. Manmohan Singh
**Explanation:** Dr. Manmohan Singh, as Finance Minister under PM P.V. Narasimha Rao, is considered the architect of India's 1991 economic reforms (LPG reforms — Liberalization, Privatization, Globalization). The reforms were triggered by a balance of payments crisis.
**Shortcut / rule:** 1991 reforms = Manmohan Singh (FM) + P.V. Narasimha Rao (PM); LPG = Liberalization, Privatization, Globalization; Triggered by: BoP crisis, gold pledged to Bank of England; Abolished License Raj; Opened FDI; Reduced import tariffs.

### Q881 · Biology / Life Science

The functional unit of the kidney is:

A. Neuron
B. Nephron
C. Alveoli
D. Villi

**Answer:** B. Nephron
**Explanation:** The nephron is the basic functional unit of the kidney responsible for filtering blood and producing urine. Each kidney contains about 1 million nephrons. The nephron consists of Bowman's capsule, glomerulus, and renal tubules.
**Shortcut / rule:** Nephron = kidney (filtration); Neuron = nervous system; Alveoli = lungs (gas exchange); Villi = small intestine (absorption); Sarcomere = muscle; Hepatocyte = liver cell; Each kidney = ~1 million nephrons.

### Q882 · Indian Geography

The largest delta in the world is:

A. Nile Delta
B. Ganges-Brahmaputra Delta
C. Mississippi Delta
D. Mekong Delta

**Answer:** B. Ganges-Brahmaputra Delta
**Explanation:** The Ganges-Brahmaputra Delta (Sundarbans Delta) is the largest delta in the world, covering about 100,000 sq km across India (West Bengal) and Bangladesh. It is formed by the confluence of the Ganga and Brahmaputra rivers. The Sundarbans mangrove forest is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Largest delta: Ganges-Brahmaputra (Sundarbans); Sundarbans = Royal Bengal Tiger + largest mangrove forest; Nile Delta = Egypt; Mississippi = USA; Sundarbans shared by India + Bangladesh; UNESCO World Heritage Site.

### Q883 · Art, Culture & Literature

The Nalanda University was an ancient centre of learning located in:

A. Uttar Pradesh
B. Bihar
C. Madhya Pradesh
D. Maharashtra

**Answer:** B. Bihar
**Explanation:** Nalanda University in Bihar was one of the oldest and most renowned centres of higher learning in the ancient world (5th-12th century CE). It was founded during the Gupta dynasty and attracted students from across Asia. It was destroyed by Bakhtiyar Khilji in 1193 CE. It is a UNESCO World Heritage Site.
**Shortcut / rule:** Nalanda = Bihar (Gupta dynasty, 5th c); Destroyed: Bakhtiyar Khilji (1193); Taxila (Takshashila) = Pakistan (oldest, 6th c BCE); Vikramashila = Bihar (Pala dynasty); New Nalanda University established: 2010 (Rajgir, Bihar).

### Q884 · Games & Sports

Which Indian cricketer is known as the "God of Cricket"?

A. Virat Kohli
B. M.S. Dhoni
C. Sachin Tendulkar
D. Kapil Dev

**Answer:** C. Sachin Tendulkar
**Explanation:** Sachin Tendulkar is fondly called the "God of Cricket" and "Master Blaster." He holds numerous records including most runs in Test (15,921) and ODI (18,426) cricket, 100 international centuries, and was the first to score a double century in ODIs (200* vs SA, 2010). He received the Bharat Ratna in 2014.
**Shortcut / rule:** Tendulkar = God of Cricket (200 Tests, 463 ODIs, 100 centuries); Bharat Ratna 2014; Debut: 15 years old (1989 vs Pakistan); Retired: 2013 (200th Test vs WI); Don Bradman = 99.94 Test average (greatest ever).

### Q885 · Famous Personalities

Who was the first Indian to receive the Bharat Ratna?

A. Dr. S. Radhakrishnan
B. C. Rajagopalachari
C. Dr. C.V. Raman
D. All three received it in the same year

**Answer:** D. All three received it in the same year
**Explanation:** Dr. S. Radhakrishnan, C. Rajagopalachari, and Dr. C.V. Raman were the first three recipients of the Bharat Ratna in 1954. The Bharat Ratna is India's highest civilian award, established in 1954 by President Rajendra Prasad.
**Shortcut / rule:** Bharat Ratna (1954): 1st recipients — Radhakrishnan, Rajagopalachari, C.V. Raman (all 1954); No provision for posthumous award until 1966; Posthumous: Subhash Chandra Bose (1992, later withdrawn), Lal Bahadur Shastri (1966).

### Q886 · Space & Nuclear Program

The Bhabha Atomic Research Centre (BARC) is located in:

A. Hyderabad
B. Bengaluru
C. Mumbai (Trombay)
D. Chennai

**Answer:** C. Mumbai (Trombay)
**Explanation:** BARC is located at Trombay, Mumbai. It was established in 1954 as the Atomic Energy Establishment, Trombay (AEET) and renamed BARC in 1967 after Homi Jehangir Bhabha, the father of India's nuclear programme.
**Shortcut / rule:** BARC = Trombay, Mumbai (1954); Named after Homi Bhabha (1967); India's 1st nuclear reactor: Apsara (1956, BARC); TIFR (Tata Institute of Fundamental Research) = also founded by Bhabha (Mumbai, 1945); DAE = Department of Atomic Energy.

### Q887 · UN & World Organizations

The International Labour Organization (ILO) is headquartered in:

A. New York
B. Geneva
C. Paris
D. Vienna

**Answer:** B. Geneva
**Explanation:** The ILO (International Labour Organization) is headquartered in Geneva, Switzerland. It was established in 1919 as part of the Treaty of Versailles and became a UN specialized agency in 1946. It won the Nobel Peace Prize in 1969.
**Shortcut / rule:** ILO = Geneva (1919); Nobel Peace Prize 1969; Tripartite structure: governments + employers + workers; India = founding member; Key conventions: minimum wage, child labour, forced labour, working hours.

### Q888 · Environmental Issues & Ecology

Which act in India deals with the protection of wildlife?

A. Forest Conservation Act, 1980
B. Wildlife Protection Act, 1972
C. Environment Protection Act, 1986
D. Biodiversity Act, 2002

**Answer:** B. Wildlife Protection Act, 1972
**Explanation:** The Wildlife Protection Act, 1972 provides for the protection of wild animals, birds, and plants. It established the framework for national parks, wildlife sanctuaries, and tiger reserves. It also created schedules listing protected species.
**Shortcut / rule:** Wildlife Protection Act = 1972 (animals/plants protection); Forest Conservation Act = 1980 (forests); Environment Protection Act = 1986 (overall, Bhopal Gas Tragedy triggered); Water (Prevention) Act = 1974; Air (Prevention) Act = 1981.

### Q889 · Computers & Applications

What is the full form of PDF?

A. Portable Document Format
B. Personal Document File
C. Printed Document Format
D. Portable Digital Format

**Answer:** A. Portable Document Format
**Explanation:** PDF (Portable Document Format) was developed by Adobe Systems in 1993. It is designed to present documents consistently across different platforms and devices. It preserves formatting, fonts, and layout regardless of the viewing device.
**Shortcut / rule:** PDF = Adobe (1993); JPEG = image compression; PNG = lossless image; MP3 = audio; MP4 = video; DOC/DOCX = Word; XLS/XLSX = Excel; PPT/PPTX = PowerPoint; ZIP = compressed archive.

### Q890 · Transport Systems & Abbreviations

IRDAI stands for:

A. Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India
B. Indian Regulatory and Development Authority for Insurance
C. Insurance Research and Development Authority of India
D. Indian Reserve and Development Authority for Insurance

**Answer:** A. Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India
**Explanation:** IRDAI regulates and promotes the insurance industry in India. It was established in 1999 based on the recommendations of the Malhotra Committee (1994). It is headquartered in Hyderabad.
